The disturbance system does NOT validate or clamp this parameter; values much above 512 studs degrade query cost as `O((searchRadius / SPATIAL_GRID_CELL_SIZE)²)` cells traversed. Predator AI GDD owns the operational range; G.6 publishes a default of 200 studs.
- **Path stability rule.** Roblox `PathfindingService:FindPathAsync()` is async-yielding and recompute-expensive (concept doc technical risk). Predator AI MUST NOT re-invoke pathfinding unless the new `bestPosition` differs from the previously navigated-to position by more than `PREDATOR_PATH_STABILITY_MIN_DELTA` (G.6, default 8 studs). This knob is decoupled from `HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE` so spatial-grid tuning does not silently affect chase responsiveness.
- **Attribution refresh on source-set change.** `topContributingEmissionIds` is what the predator stores for death attribution per C.1.11. Predator AI MUST refresh its locked attribution whenever the returned `topContributingEmissionIds` list differs from its previously stored list **by set-equality (order-insensitive comparison of `emissionId` strings)** — reordering within the same membership set does NOT trigger refresh. This catches the "same position, different sources" case where the underlying contributors decayed and were replaced by new ones at the same grid cell, while avoiding churn from rank-reorderings as decay shifts contribution scores within a stable membership set.

Predator AI also queries `DisturbanceService:GetSquadAggregateT(excludePlayer: Player?): number` — a server-internal accessor returning the same `squadT` value pushed to HUDs via D.5 when called with `nil`, and the **exclusion form** (max over connected players MINUS `excludePlayer`) when a player is supplied. **The exclusion form is the round-23 B1 closure of PA's R5/OQ.10 obligation:** PA's Hunt→Disengage quiet-check reads `squadQuietT = GetSquadAggregateT(excludeLockedPlayer)` (PA Core Rule 2 / PA H.25/H.29/H.36/H.57) — without exclusion, the locked target's own forced-survival sprint would keep the unfiltered `squadT` loud and make Hunt→Disengage structurally unreachable, inverting Pillar 1's "going quiet is always a lever." Semantics pinned at D.5. Used for the Retreat-trigger logic above. Cost: O(P) where P is connected player count (4 max in MVP) — cheap enough to call at predator cadence without budget concerns. This is server-internal only; not exposed via Knit `Service.Client` (H.39c applies).

**Squad-aggregate band-crossing notification — `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` (Session C, 2026-06-19 — closes Player Controller's F.4 producer obligation).** Distinct from `TierCrossedEvent`: `TierCrossedEvent` is *position-keyed* (a single position's cumulative `t` crosses a threshold; consumed by Predator AI for spatial navigation). `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` is *squad-aggregate* — it fires when the **squad-collective tier** (the `squadT = GetSquadAggregateT()` value, classified by the D.4 tier thresholds) crosses an escalation boundary. It is the producer Player Controller subscribes to (PC GDD F.4 / C.9) and re-broadcasts to the living squad as the non-positional "world-response cue" (`OnWorldResponseCue`).
- **Signal + arity:** a **server-internal** Knit signal `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed(direction: "up" | "down", newTier: Tier, timestamp: number)`. `timestamp` uses the same server clock as D.1 (deterministic in tests via the `_setTestClock` seam — Test Infrastructure Prerequisites, `ecological-disturbance-verification.md`; round-23 stray-cite fix of the nonexistent "C.11" anchor, second site).
- **Producer logic:** the field-update pass maintains a `previousSquadTier` state variable. **First-pass rule (round-22 B10 — mirrors D.4's First-call rule / H.17b–H.17c; CD-ruled):** on the very first field-update pass of a session, `previousSquadTier` is initialised to that pass's freshly-classified squad tier via **flat-threshold dispatch** (the D.4 first-call convention — classify with `previousTier = "Calm"`, no hysteresis), and **NO `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` fires on that pass** regardless of the classified tier. This categorically prevents a spurious boot-time squad-wide "up" cue (which Player Controller would re-broadcast as a false world-response cue at session start — a Pillar-3-inverting failure); under Pillar 4's session-scoped field the first pass classifies Calm in every normal boot, so the rule only bites on abnormal starts (hot-join into a pre-elevated field, test fixtures). Verified by H.28d clause (d). Every subsequent pass classifies the current `squadT` into a tier via the **same D.4 tier-classification + ±0.03 hysteresis** used for positional crossings (no new thresholds — Calm/Tense at 0.30, Tense/Hunt at 0.65, Hunt/Retreat at 1.00; hysteresis prevents boundary chatter). When the classified squad tier differs from `previousSquadTier`, fire `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` once with `direction` (`"up"` if escalating, `"down"` if de-escalating) and the new tier, then update `previousSquadTier`. **Steady-state: at most one fire per field-update pass** (5 Hz cadence is the natural debounce). **Post-hitch multi-tier cascade (round-23 SD-3 — D.4 parity, previously unstated):** the squad classifier inherits D.4's post-hitch settle loop. If a single pass moves `squadT` across MULTIPLE boundaries (server hitch, mass simultaneous source expiry), the classifier converges through the full ladder within that pass (≤ 4 settle iterations) and fires one `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` per crossed boundary, in ladder order, all with the same `direction` — never a single skip-level fire (a Retreat→Calm hitch produces three "down" fires: Retreat→Hunt, Hunt→Tense, Tense→Calm) and never a phantom intermediate classification persisting across passes. Mirrors the positional cascade rule (D.4 / H.34b); PC's consumer receives every intermediate crossing. Verified by H.28d clause (e).
- **Both directions fire; the consumer filters.** ED fires on BOTH up and down crossings (the signal is a faithful squad-tier-change notification); Player Controller's handler acts on `direction == "up"` only (its world-response cue is one-directional, R23-1(a)). ED does not assume the consumer's filter.
- **Security (binding — server-internal, no client forge):** `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` MUST be a **server-internal signal — never declared on `DisturbanceService.Client`, never a RemoteEvent/RemoteFunction.** `squadT` derives entirely from server-authoritative player positions + the server-owned emission field (C.1.8 authority rule); no client-supplied value can drive the disturbance level or forge a band-crossing. This mirrors `TierCrossedEvent`'s server-internal/zero-network property and satisfies PC's binding security constraint end-to-end (PC's consume side is forge-proof on its own; this is the matching producer-side guarantee).
- **D.7 coupling (consistent):** the band thresholds are unchanged by the 2026-06-19 D.7 ED-yields ruling — that ruling changed the *magnitude/lifetime* of stationary-sprint pulses (so `squadT` reaches each tier faster: Hunt ~15 s, Retreat ~28 s under a held stationary sprint), but the tier boundaries (0.30/0.65/1.00) are untouched, so `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` fires earlier in real time — working as designed (louder → faster escalation).
- **AC LANDED (round-21, H.28d):** `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed`'s AC verifies (i) exactly one fire per field-update pass per squad-tier transition, with correct `direction`; (ii) hysteresis suppresses chatter within a band; (iii) the signal has no `Service.Client` surface and no client-reachable input path. Placed in Acceptance Criteria → Cross-System Wiring as **H.28d** (the squad-aggregate sibling of the position-keyed `TierCrossedEvent` ACs H.28/H.28b). ED tracks no canonical AC tally, so the AC-count reconciliation is satisfied by the unique non-colliding `H.28d` id.

**Return contract:**

```luau
type HotspotResult = {
    position: Vector3,                       -- world-space target for navigation
    fieldValue: number,                      -- t at bestPosition; in [0, 1]
    hotspotId: string,                       -- ROUND-22 B3 (closes Predator AI OQ.11 / FIX-13): stable hotspot
                                             -- identity — the D.6 dedup cellKey of bestPosition
                                             -- (`string.format("%d_%d", floor(X/k), floor(Z/k))`,
                                             -- k = HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE; see D.6 "Dedup snap-method").
                                             -- Stable across successive queries while the winning dedup
                                             -- cell is unchanged, even when the representative position
                                             -- within the cell shifts (first-encountered-source turnover).
                                             -- Predator AI's tie-break + challenger-debounce logic
                                             -- (PA Core Rules 3/4, tested AC H.8) keys off this field —
                                             -- raw `position` is NOT a sound identity proxy. H.25d verifies
                                             -- stability + format.
    topContributingEmissionIds: {string},    -- 1–3 emissionIds, ordered by contribution descending; for attribution
    hotspotCount: number,                    -- count of distinct candidate positions ≥ minimumTier within searchRadius
                                             -- (post-deduplication via HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE);
                                             -- 1 = single hotspot, ≥ 2 = multiple distinct loci (squad spread or multi-cluster).
                                             -- Caveat: at HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE = 4 studs, this cannot reliably distinguish
                                             -- "two players" from "one player + two nodes" — informational only.
                                             -- ROUND-5 / TIER-2 #21 — earlier "MAY use to deprioritize flanked positions"
                                             -- note is REVOKED. The caveat above contradicts the use case it was suggesting.
                                             -- Predator AI GDD MUST NOT use hotspotCount for flank-deprioritization logic
                                             -- until that GDD has explicitly verified the caveat is acceptable for that use.
                                             -- See F.2a row for Predator AI's evaluation obligation.
                                             -- Implementation cost is one counter increment in the D.6 loop — kept in MVP contract for future use.
    oldestContributorAge: number,            -- age in seconds of the oldest live source within INFLUENCE_RADIUS of bestPosition.
                                             -- Informational only. No threshold semantics defined here.
                                             -- Anticipated consumer (round-3 note): Predator AI MAY delay Hunt transition when
                                             -- oldestContributorAge > STALE_AGE_THRESHOLD AND fieldValue is near tierFloor
                                             -- (avoids chasing decaying signals). Implementation cost is a max-age scan in
                                             -- topContributorsAt — re-evaluate inclusion if the Predator AI GDD does not consume this field.
}
```

All six fields (`position`, `fieldValue`, `hotspotId`, `topContributingEmissionIds`, `hotspotCount`, `oldestContributorAge`) are required (non-optional) when `HotspotResult` is non-nil — round-7a R7-IMPORTANT gameplay-programmer F7 prose-fix (round-22 B3 raises the count from five with the `hotspotId` addition); the round-1 → round-3 incremental field additions left the prose stale at "all four." `topContributingEmissionIds` is guaranteed `#list >= 1` per the round-7a R7-B10 contract closure (D.6 `topContributorsAt` contract + H.25b). Returns nil only when no candidate meets `minimumTier` (E.5/E.5b) — never returns a partially populated result.

`secondBestPosition` was published in the round-1 enriched contract but has been **removed for MVP** — the concept doc commits to one predator at launch with no flank/distract mechanic, so it has no consumer. Reserved for post-launch multi-predator spec; if reintroduced, it will be added through a separate query method (`GetSecondaryHotspot`) rather than enriching the primary return.

#### C.3.4 — Disturbance System → HUD (subscriber)

The HUD receives three distinct data streams, each with its own update contract.

**Meter value**:
- Server pushes per-player `t` and squad collective `t` (max of all connected players' `t`) at the field-update cadence (5 Hz default) via `DisturbanceService.Client.OnMeterUpdate` (RemoteSignal in Knit terms — declared with `Knit.CreateSignal()`). Payload: `MeterUpdatePayload` (see Luau types below). HUD applies 0.25s `Sine/InOut` tween to the displayed meter — the world's `t` is the target, the meter is always catching up.
- **Routing (REQUIRED).** Server fires the signal **per-player** using `OnMeterUpdate:Fire(player, {playerT = thisPlayerT, squadT = currentSquadT})`. The `playerT` field MUST carry that specific player's individual field value, not a shared value. **Knit `RemoteSignal:FireAll(...)` (the broadcast variant) is forbidden** for this signal — using it would either leak per-player `t` to all clients (violating C.1.9) or force the signal to drop the per-player field (breaking the HUD contract). H.29b verifies routing. **Knit RemoteSignal API note (round-5 correction):** Knit RemoteSignal fires to a specific client via `:Fire(player, ...)` and to all clients via `:FireAll(...)`; the raw RemoteEvent API names `:FireClient` / `:FireAllClients` are NOT methods on Knit RemoteSignal objects. Earlier revisions of this GDD cited `:FireClient` — that text is corrected here. Implementing modules MUST call `:Fire` / `:FireAll` on Knit RemoteSignal handles. (The descriptive prose elsewhere in this GDD that uses "FireClient-per-player" as a routing label is referring to the wire-level effect, not the Knit method name.)
- **Send-on-change gate (advisory).** The implementation MAY skip a per-player FireClient on a tick if BOTH `playerT` and `squadT` are unchanged from the previous tick by less than 0.001. This is an optional optimization; the contract permits but does not require it. Always-push is the default, and the contract is correct under always-push semantics.
- Client never receives raw field data, only the derived `t` scalar. Per-player payloads do not reveal other players' `t` values.
- **Initial sync on player join: server-pushed.** When `Players.PlayerAdded` fires, the server registers the player and fires `OnMeterUpdate` to that player on the next field-update cycle (≤ 200 ms after join). No client-initiated RemoteFunction is used — the previous `GetCurrentMeterValue` RemoteFunction was **removed in round-1 revision**, eliminating thundering-herd risk on simultaneous 4-player join and unifying the code path with ongoing meter pushes.
- **Pre-character-spawn race (NEW edge case).** If `CharacterAdded` has not yet fired for the joining player when the first `OnMeterUpdate` would push, the server pushes `{playerT = 0.0, squadT = currentSquadT}`. Position-based `playerT` is correctly populated on the first update cycle after `CharacterAdded` fires. See E.17.

**Spike alerts (two sub-types) — event-driven, fire-once-on-trigger**:
- **ACTIVE alert**: a new emission's *cumulative* contribution, at the moment of emission, pushes the field at the emission position above a tier threshold. Fired at emission time. HUD displays as "you caused this."
- **PASSIVE alert**: a player walks into a position whose existing field is above a tier threshold. Fired once on entry; suppressed until the player exits and re-enters (D.4 hysteresis is the exit definition).
- Server pushes via `DisturbanceService.Client.OnDisturbanceAlert` (RemoteSignal in Knit terms — declared with `Knit.CreateSignal()`). HUD distinguishes the sub-types visually per art bible §7.3.
- **Routing.** `OnDisturbanceAlert` is fired only to the player whose tier crossing triggered the alert (`OnDisturbanceAlert:Fire(player, payload)` — Knit RemoteSignal API per round-5 correction above), never `:FireAll(...)`. Other players are not alerted to a player's crossings on their HUD (the squad meter handles that via `OnMeterUpdate`).

**Persistent zone-presence indicator (NEW — resolves PASSIVE alert dead-spot, R-block-#13)**:
The PASSIVE alert is a single-fire event. Sustained presence in a high-tier zone is communicated via a *separate* HUD indicator that the disturbance system pushes alongside `OnMeterUpdate`. The `playerT` field already carries the per-player current `t` value; the HUD GDD MUST render a continuous zone-presence indicator (distinct from the spike-alert flash) while `playerT` corresponds to tier ≥ Tense. This means a player who entered a Tense zone, received the PASSIVE alert, and remained in the zone continues to see the zone indicator on their HUD as long as they remain at tier ≥ Tense. No new RemoteSignal is required — the indicator derives from the existing 5 Hz `playerT` push. **Adds requirement to HUD GDD; the disturbance system owns no additional surface for this.**

**Death attribution**:
- On player death, server pushes `DeathAttributionPayload` via `DisturbanceService.Client.OnDeathAttributionPushed` (RemoteSignal in Knit terms; **`OnDeathAttributionPushed:Fire(deadPlayer, payload)` only — never `:FireAll(...)`** per the round-5 Knit API correction above). Up to 3 entries in `killingEmissions`, ordered by contribution score; `attributionMayBeIncomplete` is true if any `MAX_LIVE_SOURCES` cap drop occurred during the dead player's contribution window (E.12 / C.1.11), or if any contributing `emissionId` could not be resolved against the archive. **`predatorWasLockedAtDeath` (round-7c B-7b-3; round-9 snapshot-pattern revision; round-11 DR-4 = Option B subscription-monopoly update)** is computed by reading from DisturbanceService's `_deathLockSnapshot[deadPlayer]` table (captured atomically by DisturbanceService's `KnitInit`-registered `Humanoid.Died` handler per C.1.11 step 5): true if the dead player's UserId was the `targetedPlayer` for any predator with a non-empty `RegisterPredatorLock` chain at the moment of the first `Humanoid.Died` fire on that humanoid, false otherwise. The snapshot is captured BEFORE any other Knit service learns about the death (per C.1.11 step 5's `Humanoid.Died` subscription monopoly under round-11 DR-4 = Option B — DisturbanceService is the SOLE subscriber, so other services receive death notification only via the `OnPlayerDied` BindableEvent fan-out at sub-step (5c) AFTER the snapshot is already captured at sub-step (5a); the round-9 KnitInit-vs-KnitStart connection-order rule that previously enforced ordering is superseded by the structural single-subscriber model), so the value reflects the pre-transition registry state regardless of any subsequent `:ReleasePredatorLock` invocation triggered by `OnPlayerDied:Connect` subscribers' death-driven cleanup; see C.1.11 steps 1, 4, and 5 for the full ordering rule.
- HUD uses this to populate the death screen per art bible §2.6. Disturbance system owns the data; HUD owns the display.
- Resolution reads from the **attribution archive** (C.1.11), not the live-source list. Unresolvable `emissionId`s (e.g., from cap drops or expired archive entries) are skipped silently from `killingEmissions` and the `attributionMayBeIncomplete` flag is set.

**Published Luau types** (canonical for cross-system consumers):

```luau
type AlertSubtype = "Active" | "Passive"

type DisturbanceAlertPayload = {
    alertSubtype: AlertSubtype,
    emissionType: EmissionType,
    emissionId: string?,       -- nil for Passive alerts (no single emission caused entry)
    crossedTier: Tier,
    sourcePlayerId: number?,   -- nil for Passive alerts
}

type DeathAttributionEntry = {
    emissionId: string,
    sourcePlayerId: number,
    emissionType: EmissionType,
    emissionTime: number,
    positionLabel: string,     -- map-marker label resolved server-side; HUD does not derive
}

type DeathAttributionPayload = {
    killingEmissions: {DeathAttributionEntry},  -- 0–3 entries, ordered by contribution desc
    attributionMayBeIncomplete: boolean,
    predatorWasLockedAtDeath: boolean,          -- (round-7c B-7b-3) true iff at the moment of death the dead player's UserId was the targeted-player of an active predator's RegisterPredatorLock chain (i.e., the predator was actively hunting THIS player at death). HUD uses this to disambiguate the 5 s grace-window (G.4 PREDATOR_LOCK_RELEASE_GRACE_WINDOW): when `predatorWasLockedAtDeath == false` AND `killingEmissions` is non-empty, the death screen MUST present attribution as "decayed history" framing rather than "actively-hunted" framing, because in the grace window a predator may have already disengaged (OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(false) fired) yet `killingEmissions` resolves from the still-live archive. Without this flag, the 5 s window admits incoherent death-screen displays that misattribute decayed-history kills to ongoing predator engagement, breaking the squad-trust read of "we know exactly when the predator gave up."
}

type BeaconCapState = "A" | "B" | "C"

type MeterUpdatePayload = {
    playerT: number,                -- this player's per-position fieldValue, in [0, 1]
    squadT: number,                 -- max across all connected players' fieldValues, in [0, 1]
    -- Round-9 DR-1 = Option A additions for Cluster 3 / B-CC3-A HUD-data-surface closure.
    -- These three fields are jointly populated when ANY beacon's cap rule is currently
    -- evaluating against this player's nearest beacon (within INFLUENCE_RADIUS = 24 studs);
    -- they are nil when no beacon is in range or the cap rule is short-circuited (E.25
    -- denominator-zero / E.15 zero-connected-players / sub-mode (iii) near-floor cases).
    beaconCapState: BeaconCapState?,   -- discriminant for the three F.2a row 7 cap-cue states (see below)
    nearby: number?,                   -- count of connected players within BEACON_STATIONARY_PROXIMITY_RADIUS
                                       -- of the affecting beacon (per C.3.6 step 1; the same denominator
                                       -- used in step 5 stationary_nearby/nearby ratio)
    stationary_nearby: number?,        -- count of nearby players whose cumulativeXZ < BEACON_CUMULATIVE_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD
                                       -- (per C.3.6 step 4; under-filled-buffer players are excluded per round-7c B-7b-4)
}
```

**Beacon cap-state discriminant computation (round-9 DR-1 = Option A; closes Cluster 3 / B-CC3-A HUD-data-surface gap).** F.2a row 7's HUD-cue obligation requires the HUD to render three visually-distinct beacon cap states (A / B / C per the round-7c B-7b-1 sub-mode definitions). Diagnosing the state on the client requires server-supplied fields that the round-7c `OnMeterUpdate` payload did not carry — round-9 DR-1 = Option A extends `MeterUpdatePayload` with the three optional fields above (`beaconCapState`, `nearby`, `stationary_nearby`). The server computes the discriminant on every field-update pass per the following rules:

1. **Identify the affecting beacon (per-player).** For the player whose payload is being constructed, scan the live-source list for any beacon emission whose `B.position` is within `INFLUENCE_RADIUS = 24` studs of the player's XZ-position. If no beacon is in range, set `beaconCapState = nil`, `nearby = nil`, `stationary_nearby = nil`. If multiple beacons are in range, select the one whose contribution to `fieldValue(player.position)` is highest (the "dominant" beacon for legibility) — ties broken by lowest `emissionId` lexicographically for determinism.
2. **Read the affecting beacon's cap-evaluation state from this pass.** The C.3.6 step 6 cap-evaluation produces, for each beacon B, three observable values: `non_beacon_contribution_at_B` (per the round-7c NC-c all-beacons-excluded rule, computed by C.3.6 step 6 as `sum(contribution(B.position, S) for S in liveSources if S.emissionType != "Beacon")`), `beacon_native_contribution_at_B` (B's own decayed contribution at B.position), and `scaleFactor` (the clamped-to-`[0.0, 1.0]` multiplicative scale factor; always non-nil per the round-15 R14-B6 two-field encoding — defaults to 0.0 when the cap rule was short-circuited per E.25 denominator-zero protection OR when the cap rule did not engage, distinguished by the companion `cap_engaged: boolean` field per the round-15 R14-B6 read-site audit rule below). The implementation MUST cache these three values per-beacon per-pass in the field-update pass's per-source decay cache (no extra cost — they are already computed during the cap-evaluation step 6). (Round-15 red-team I-CS-1 cross-section closure: this enumeration's pre-round-15 wording referenced `scaleFactor` as nilable per the round-9–round-13 nilable-type pattern; the round-15 R14-B6 closure rewrites the field name to `scaleFactor` AND removes the nilable typing — the rename now reaches consistently into step 2's enumeration, the pseudocode at step 3, the State A/B prose at step 3 sub-bullets, and downstream H.41 ACs + D.7 worked examples.) **Write-discipline (round-16 I I18-4 closure):** the `scaleFactor` value is *produced* by the C.3.6 step 6 cap-evaluation and `cap_engaged` records whether the cap rule actually engaged on that pass — the cache MUST set `cap_engaged = false, scaleFactor = 0.0` whenever the cap did not engage (no in-range beacon, or short-circuit per E.25), and set `cap_engaged = true` with the real clamped `scaleFactor` ONLY when the cap rule ran. Reading `scaleFactor` without checking `cap_engaged` first is the type-safe-but-semantically-wrong pattern the R15-I3 read-site grep (H.39c) is designed to catch.
3. **Discriminate state (round-11 B-R10-2 — strict if/elseif/else evaluation order; State C ordered first; ambient-only HUD-read defect closure; round-13 R12-B8 `--!strict` nil-guard + `cap_engaged` definition).** Apply the following decision tree as a strict `if / elseif / elseif / else` chain — no two branches may evaluate true on the same pass for the same player. **Evaluation order is normative: State C MUST be evaluated FIRST**, then State A, then State B, then the No-cap-cue branch. The ordering is binding because State C's short-circuit (the cap-rule denominator-zero protection per E.25) MAY co-occur with `stationary_nearby / nearby > 0.50` cap-engagement preconditions on the same pass — without strict ordering, both State A and State C predicates could evaluate true and the implementation's branch selection becomes language-of-implementation-dependent rather than design-contract-controlled.

   **Definition of `cap_engaged` (round-13 R12-B8 closure of round-11 R11-I1 type-deferral; supersedes the round-11 pseudocode's undefined free variable).** `cap_engaged` is the cached **per-beacon** boolean flag set by C.3.6 step 5 — specifically: `cap_engaged := (stationary_nearby / nearby > 0.50)` evaluated for the affecting-beacon at C.3.6 step 5, with the engagement-precondition guards from C.3.6 steps 2 (`connected > 0`) and 3 (`nearby / connected > 0.50`) folded in (`cap_engaged` is `false` whenever any precondition fails). The flag is per-beacon, NOT per-player; a single beacon's cap-engagement state is computed once per pass at C.3.6 step 5 and reused for every player whose affecting-beacon is that beacon. The flag MUST NOT be re-derived per-player from the per-beacon `nearby` / `stationary_nearby` counts (which would expose the divide-by-zero error path when `nearby == 0`); the flag is read from the per-beacon entry in the per-source decay cache (the `BeaconCapEvalEntry` type per R11-I1 / round-13 R12-B8 elevation — see ADR for full type declaration; for this prose's purposes the flag's memberism is `cap_engaged: boolean`). When the affecting-beacon is in State C (short-circuit per `beacon_native_contribution_at_B < MAGNITUDE_FLOOR × 1.5`), `cap_engaged` is `false` (the cap rule did NOT engage; the short-circuit happens BEFORE the cap-engagement evaluation runs).

   **Two-field encoding for `BeaconCapEvalEntry` (round-15 R14-B6 closure of round-13 R12-B8 sentinel-safety unsoundness; supersedes the round-13 `-1.0` sentinel; updates the R11-I1 forward obligation on the DisturbanceService ADR).** Round-13 introduced a `-1.0` sentinel value for the cap-formula's scale factor (then named `beaconScaleFactor`) to close the round-11 R11-I1 nilable-type defect; round-14 surfaced (CD-elevated round-15 R14-B6) that the sentinel is unsound without exhaustive read-site audit — if any code path reads cached `beaconScaleFactor` for a State-C beacon and applies it as a multiplier directly (without recognising the `-1.0` sentinel), the field value gets multiplied by `-1.0`, producing a negative contribution that would silently invert the cap-rule's design intent. The ADR-deferred type does not enforce read-site safety mechanically. Round-15 design ruling chose Option A (two-field encoding; recommended) over Option B (sum-typed `BeaconScale = Active(number) | Inert`) and Option C (read-site audit only) because Option A delivers compile-time read-site safety via Luau's existing primitive types without introducing union-type machinery that Luau's `--!strict` solver does not natively pattern-match.

   The per-beacon `BeaconCapEvalEntry` cache replaces the nilable `beaconScaleFactor: number?` + sentinel pattern (round-9–round-13 historical name) with **two always-defined non-nilable fields** (round-15 R14-B6 closure also renames the field from `beaconScaleFactor` to `scaleFactor` for terminology hygiene; downstream H.41 ACs + D.7 worked examples updated by the same round-15 cross-section consistency closure):

   - `cap_engaged: boolean` — per-beacon flag set at C.3.6 step 5 (preserved from round-13; semantics unchanged). True iff `stationary_nearby / nearby > 0.50` AND the engagement-precondition guards from C.3.6 steps 2 + 3 hold AND the C.3.6 step 6 cap-rule math actually executed. False whenever any precondition fails OR the State C short-circuit fires (since State C short-circuits BEFORE step 5 runs). The flag is per-beacon, NOT per-player; computed once per pass per beacon at C.3.6 step 5 and reused for every player whose affecting beacon is that beacon.
   - `scaleFactor: number` — per-beacon clamped multiplicative scale factor, **always non-nil** (no sentinel, no nilable type). Default value `0.0`. Meaningful only when `cap_engaged == true` AND the beacon is in State A (in `(0.0, 1.0)` per the cap-formula's clamp range). When `cap_engaged == false` (State C short-circuit, or cap not engaged) the field is `0.0` by default. When `cap_engaged == true` AND State B (clamped from below by cap-formula numerator going negative), the field is exactly `0.0`. Read-sites MUST guard with `if cap_engaged then ... scaleFactor ... end` before applying the value as a multiplier — the `cap_engaged` boolean is the mechanical gate that ensures `scaleFactor` is meaningful. If `cap_engaged == false`, the value is meaningless (defaulted to `0.0`) and any read-site that applies it as a multiplier produces `0.0 × X = 0.0`, which is benign (the State C short-circuit case already collapses the beacon's contribution to its native value via the unmodified D.1/D.2 path; the cap formula's `0.95 × scaleFactor` evaluation is bypassed entirely on State C).

   The `cap_engaged` boolean's role as the gate is the same as the round-13 sentinel's role, but mechanically enforced by `--!strict` because read-sites that try to use `scaleFactor` without first checking `cap_engaged` are NOT type-errors in Luau (both fields are non-nilable `boolean` and `number`); the gate is enforced by the AC's read-site rule below + H.39 lint coverage, NOT by the type system directly. The two-field encoding's compile-time guarantee is narrower than a sum type would deliver: it eliminates the nilable-type defect AND the sentinel-misread defect, but does not enforce the gate at the type-system level. A future round MAY upgrade to sum-typed encoding if the Luau team ships native pattern-match support for sum types; for round-15 the two-field encoding is the chosen-canonical surface.

   **Read-site audit rule (binding contract on the DisturbanceService ADR + all consumers).** Any code path that reads cached `BeaconCapEvalEntry.scaleFactor` MUST be one of:
   - **(i)** Inside an `if cap_engaged then ... end` lexical guard at the same call site, OR
   - **(ii)** Inside the C.3.4 step 3 discriminant tree itself OR a function extracted from the discriminant tree (where the State A / State B branches' predicates structurally include `cap_engaged` as a leading conjunct). **Round-15 red-team I-RT-2 lexical-scope clarification:** if the discriminant tree is extracted from C.3.4 step 3 into a helper function (`function DisturbanceService:_evaluateCapStateDiscriminant(beaconCapCache: BeaconCapEvalEntry, ...): BeaconCapState?` or equivalent), the helper's body is also a permitted read-site context — the structural `cap_engaged` gating is preserved by the discriminant tree's predicate logic regardless of whether the tree lives inline at C.3.4 step 3 or in an extracted helper. The R15-I3 grep extension's anchor is the leading-conjunct pattern `cap_engaged and ...` (or equivalent boolean-AND chain starting with `cap_engaged`) — not a fixed line range; the AST-tooling pass authoring R15-I3 MUST recognise extracted discriminant helpers by their predicate-shape, NOT by lexical location.
   - **(iii)** A test seam (`_setLiveSourceState`, `_runFieldUpdatePass`, etc.) that constructs cache state for harness assertions; test-only reads are exempt because they are assertion-paths, not multiplier-paths.

   Read-sites NOT in (i)–(iii) are a regression class: H.39c's grep MUST be expanded to detect any `<expr>.scaleFactor` access that is NOT inside an `if cap_engaged ...` guard or inside the C.3.4 discriminant tree's lexical scope. The grep can run as a static pattern check (the `scaleFactor` field's name is unique to `BeaconCapEvalEntry` per the round-15 architectural commitment; collisions with other fields named `scaleFactor` in unrelated modules are forbidden by H.39c's expanded scope — round-15 R14-B6 forward-obligation tracking entry: R15-I3, recorded in F.2a-IMPORTANT round-15 IMPORTANT cluster). The R11-I1 forward obligation is updated to mandate the two-field encoding in the BeaconCapEvalEntry type alias declaration: `type BeaconCapEvalEntry = {non_beacon: number, beacon_native: number, cap_engaged: boolean, scaleFactor: number}` (no nilable types, no sentinels — the sentinel-removal IS the structural guarantee).

   Pseudocode (round-13 R12-B8 update; round-15 R14-B8 explicit-read preamble; round-15 R14-B6 two-field encoding update):

   ```
   -- Read cached per-beacon state set during this pass by C.3.6 steps 5+6
   -- (BeaconCapEvalEntry cache; see ADR for full type per round-15 R14-B6 closure).
   -- These reads MUST precede the if/elseif chain — round-15 R14-B8 closure of the
   -- round-14 finding that the if/elseif chain referenced `cap_engaged` and
   -- `beaconScaleFactor` (the round-13 field name; renamed to `scaleFactor`
   -- in round-15 R14-B6 two-field encoding) as undeclared free variables in the pseudocode block,
   -- breaking implementer copy-paste under --!strict. The cache lookup uses
   -- `beaconCapCache` as the per-beacon entry's local-binding name (the field-update
   -- pass's outer scope assigns `local beaconCapCache = self._beaconCapEvalCache[B.emissionId]`
   -- once per affecting-beacon resolution per Step 1; the assignment is implementation
   -- not contract — any binding name that resolves to the BeaconCapEvalEntry for
   -- this player's affecting beacon is acceptable).
   --
   -- Round-15 R14-B6 two-field encoding: scaleFactor is now `number` (non-nilable),
   -- defaults to 0.0 when cap_engaged is false. The round-13 -1.0 sentinel is REMOVED.
   -- Read-sites that apply scaleFactor as a multiplier MUST guard on cap_engaged first
   -- (per the read-site audit rule above); the discriminant tree below structurally
   -- guards via cap_engaged in the State A / State B branches' leading conjuncts.
   local cap_engaged: boolean = beaconCapCache.cap_engaged
   local scaleFactor: number = beaconCapCache.scaleFactor
   -- Round-20 GP19-2 closure of round-19 gameplay-programmer free-variable extraction
   -- finding: the non_beacon_contribution_at_B and beacon_native_contribution_at_B reads
   -- are hoisted explicitly above the if/elseif chain to eliminate free-variable
   -- references in the State A / State C branch predicates below. Per R11-I1 field-name
   -- mapping (round-17 I-CS-R17-1 closure in F.2b row R11-I1): the prose long-form
   -- names map to the type-alias short-form field names — non_beacon_contribution_at_B
   -- reads beaconCapCache.non_beacon; beacon_native_contribution_at_B reads
   -- beaconCapCache.beacon_native. Both fields are `number`, always non-nilable per
   -- round-15 R14-B6 two-field encoding (no nilable types; default 0.0 when cap rule
   -- short-circuited or did not engage; cap_engaged is the mechanical gate).
   local non_beacon_contribution_at_B: number = beaconCapCache.non_beacon
   local beacon_native_contribution_at_B: number = beaconCapCache.beacon_native

   if beacon_native_contribution_at_B < MAGNITUDE_FLOOR * 1.5 then
       -- State C path (short-circuit BEFORE any cap-rule math)
       -- Per-beacon cache: cap_engaged := false; scaleFactor := 0.0 (default — non-nilable)
   elseif cap_engaged and scaleFactor > 0.0 and non_beacon_contribution_at_B < BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE then
       -- State A path
       -- (cap_engaged is the per-beacon flag from C.3.6 step 5; scaleFactor is non-nilable
       --  number per round-15 R14-B6 two-field encoding — no nil-guard needed because
       --  the field is always defined. The State C short-circuit (above) ensures this
       --  branch is unreachable when the cap rule was short-circuited; cap_engaged = false
       --  in that case AND the leading conjunct mechanically gates the branch.)
   elseif cap_engaged and scaleFactor == 0.0 then
       -- State B path
       -- (Both predicates are well-typed under --!strict — cap_engaged is boolean,
       --  scaleFactor is number; no nilable-type residue. State C ordering precedence
       --  ensures this branch is reached only when the cap rule's scaleFactor was
       --  actually computed and clamped to 0.0 by C.3.6 step 6 (non-default 0.0 from
       --  cap-formula clamp, distinguishable from the State C default-0.0 because
       --  cap_engaged is true here AND false in State C).)
   else
       -- No-cap-cue branch (cap not engaged, beacon not near-floor)
       -- (cap_engaged = false here; scaleFactor is the default 0.0 but never read.)
   end
   ```

   - **State C (beacon near expiry — sub-mode (iii)):** `beacon_native_contribution_at_B < MAGNITUDE_FLOOR × 1.5 = 0.03` (the cap rule is short-circuited per E.25 denominator-zero protection). The beacon is decaying naturally; cap is NOT engaged AND CANNOT engage on this pass. Set `beaconCapState = "C"`. **`nearby` and `stationary_nearby` are nil on State C unless the F.2a row 7 persistence rule applies** (a prior pass within the past `math.ceil(1.0 × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ)` passes had State A or State B for this player) — in that persistence-window case, populate `nearby` / `stationary_nearby` from C.3.6 step 1 / step 4 per the persistence rule's post-hoc-attribution path; otherwise both fields are nil. **Round-11 B-R10-2 closure rationale:** the round-9 wording populated `nearby` / `stationary_nearby` unconditionally on State C, which produced an HUD legibility defect — the cap visibly showed "we're capping it" denominators (proximity pips highlighted as stationary) on a State that signals "the beacon expired naturally, the cap NEVER engaged." Players observing State C with populated denominators would learn the wrong cause-effect ("the cap engaged but the beacon faded out" instead of "the beacon faded out, no cap involvement"). Nil-ing the populations on State C aligns the HUD read with the actual mechanic. State C still takes evaluation-order priority over A and B because the short-circuit happens BEFORE the cap rule's `scaleFactor` math runs — but the fields populated to the HUD now reflect the cap-engagement reality, not the mid-pass intermediate state.
   - **State A (cap-suppressing beacon — sub-mode (i)):** cap engaged (`stationary_nearby / nearby > 0.50` per C.3.6 step 5) AND `scaleFactor > 0.0` AND `non_beacon_contribution_at_B < BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE = 0.65`. The cap is meaningfully reducing the beacon's contribution; `scaleFactor ∈ (0.0, 1.0)`. Set `beaconCapState = "A"`, populate `nearby` and `stationary_nearby` from C.3.6 step 1 / step 4. (Field name `scaleFactor` per round-15 R14-B6 two-field encoding closure; supersedes the round-9–round-13 `scaleFactor: number?` nilable + `-1.0` sentinel pattern.)
   - **State B (cap fired but ambient dominates — sub-mode (ii)):** cap engaged AND `scaleFactor` clamped to `0.0` per C.3.6 step 6 (the formula's numerator went negative because `non_beacon_contribution_at_B > BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE = 0.65`). The beacon's contribution is fully zeroed but ambient alone elevates `fieldValue(B.position)` above the cap. Set `beaconCapState = "B"`, populate `nearby` and `stationary_nearby` from C.3.6 step 1 / step 4. (State B's `scaleFactor == 0.0` is distinguishable from State C's default-0.0 because `cap_engaged == true` here and `false` there — the discriminant tree's State A / B branches' leading `cap_engaged` conjunct + State C's evaluation-order precedence ensure mechanical disambiguation per round-15 R14-B6 read-site audit rule.)
   - **No cap-cue (cap not engaged AND beacon not near-floor):** `stationary_nearby / nearby ≤ 0.50` (or `nearby / connected ≤ 0.50` per C.3.6 step 3, or `connected == 0` per E.25 zero-connected-players short-circuit). The beacon is operating normally. Set `beaconCapState = nil`, `nearby` and `stationary_nearby` populated from C.3.6 step 1 / step 4 only IF F.2a row 7's "past 1.0 s" persistence rule applies (a previous pass within the past `math.ceil(1.0 × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ)` passes had a non-nil `beaconCapState` for this player); otherwise nil.
4. **Persistence rule (F.2a row 7 "past 1.0 s" requirement; round-11 B-R10-3 cadence-derived counter).** When `beaconCapState` transitions from non-nil to nil for a given player (e.g., cap disengaged because a teammate moved), the implementation MUST continue populating `nearby` and `stationary_nearby` with their CURRENT live values for **`math.ceil(1.0 × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ)` field-update passes** after the transition AND set `beaconCapState = nil` immediately on the transition pass. This admits a per-player decay-counter `_capCueDecayPassesRemaining[player]: number` initialised to **`math.ceil(1.0 × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ)`** on transition-to-nil and decremented every pass; `nearby` / `stationary_nearby` populate while counter > 0. After the counter hits 0, all three fields go fully nil. The counter resets to `math.ceil(1.0 × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ)` on any transition back to a non-nil `beaconCapState`. **Why cadence-derived, not hardcoded 5 (round-11 B-R10-3 / round-10 systems-designer F2 + performance-analyst F5 convergent finding):** the round-9 wording initialised the counter to a literal `5` anchored to the default 5 Hz cadence — at non-default tunings (e.g., the G section's safe-range lower bound `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ = 2 Hz`) the literal-5 counter persists for `5 / 2 = 2.5 s` instead of the F.2a row 7-mandated 1.0 s, breaking the persistence rule's contract whenever the tuner deviates from the default cadence. The cadence-derived expression `math.ceil(1.0 × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ)` produces 5 at 5 Hz (unchanged from round-9 behaviour at default tunings), 2 at 2 Hz, 4 at 4 Hz, 10 at 10 Hz — always honouring the 1.0 s persistence target ± one pass. **`math.ceil` (not `math.floor`) is binding** because rounding down at non-integer cadences would underrun the 1.0 s target (e.g., at hypothetical 3 Hz: `floor(3) = 3` passes = 1.0 s exactly, but at 3.5 Hz the 1.0 s persistence requires ceil(3.5) = 4 passes, not floor = 3). At all G-row safe-range tunings the ceil-vs-floor distinction does not actually differ (FIELD_UPDATE_HZ values are integer Hz in the safe range), but the contract is anchored to ceil for forward-compatibility with future fractional-Hz tunings. This implements F.2a row 7's "disengagement events attributable post-hoc to the squadmate motion that caused them" requirement without extending the data-contract — the HUD reads the persisting `nearby` / `stationary_nearby` to render the post-disengage attribution UI.
5. **Bandwidth budget verification.** The three new fields add ~20 bytes per `OnMeterUpdate` payload (`beaconCapState` is a 1-byte-encoded enum or nil; `nearby` and `stationary_nearby` are small integers, ~4 bytes each + framing). At default 5 Hz × 4 players × 4 chunks (the H.37 worst-case bandwidth scenario) the additional outbound bandwidth is `20 bytes × 5 Hz × 4 players = 0.4 KB/s per client` — well within H.37's 20 KB/s sub-budget for disturbance-signal traffic. H.37 desk-calc updated to fold in the increment.

The HUD GDD's responsibility is to read these three fields and render the three-state cue per F.2a row 7's State A / State B / State C visual specifications. Verified by H.41m (round-9 addition; see Section H Beacon Stationary-Squad Cap).

#### C.3.5 — Disturbance System → Bioluminescent Flora (subscriber)

Flora are passive consumers — they do not query the field. Server pushes saturation targets via **chunk-batched broadcast**, not per-flora updates.

- Server evaluates local `t` at each loaded flora node's position on the field-update pass (5 Hz). The flora-target evaluation is performed inside the `DisturbanceService_Update` profilebegin scope (see H.36) — it counts toward the 2 ms cycle budget.
- **Chunk-batched broadcast**: the server fires one `FloraChunkUpdate` RemoteEvent per active chunk per tick (max 5/sec), carrying `{chunkId, [{floraNodeId, targetT}, ...]}` for every flora in that chunk whose `t` has changed by more than `FLORA_UPDATE_DELTA_THRESHOLD` since the last push. **Empty arrays are not fired** — if no flora in a chunk crossed the threshold this tick, no `FloraChunkUpdate` for that chunk fires (eliminates dead-fire overhead).
- "Active chunk" = any chunk that contains at least one player or is within the `StreamingTarget` radius of any connected player. The server maintains a per-chunk last-pushed-t cache for each flora; the cache is reset to current `t` after a successful `FloraChunkUpdate` push or after the snapshot path below.
- Client owns a `chunkId → floraList` table. On receiving a chunk update, the client applies `targetT` to each named flora locally and starts the visual tween: `Color3.Lerp(dull, full, targetT)`, `PointLight.Brightness Lerp(0.3, 2.0, targetT)`, pulse-period selection per tier per art bible §4.3.
- When a chunk streams out on a client, the client drops the chunk's flora from its local table. No server notification needed; the server's field state for that chunk continues to exist and decay (C.1.10). The server's per-flora last-pushed-t cache for that chunk is also retained until no client is rendering it; once all clients drop the chunk, the cache for those flora is reset so the next stream-in sees a fresh delta-tracking baseline.

**Initial snapshot on stream-in: server-pushed via server-tracked chunk membership (round-4 P0 mitigation — Option A).**

The previous round-1 design used a client-initiated `GetChunkFieldSnapshot` RemoteFunction; round-2 removed it and replaced with a server-push triggered by `Player:GetPropertyChangedSignal("ReplicationFocus")`. **Round-4 review identified a P0 security regression**: `ReplicationFocus` is a client-writable Roblox property, so a malicious client could drive the property-changed signal to pull `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` payloads for arbitrary chunks — reintroducing the chunk-enumeration / cross-map data-leak exploit that the round-2 redesign was specifically intended to eliminate. **Round-4 ruling (Option A): the server tracks chunk membership using its own streaming knowledge as authority; `ReplicationFocus` is NOT used as the snapshot trigger.**

- The server maintains a per-player `playerId → loadedChunks: Set<chunkId>` membership table, populated server-side from the same StreamingEnabled chunk-load events the server already needs for the chunk-update push path (the existing C.3.5 line above) — no new authority surface, just reuse of the existing server-streaming knowledge.
- When the server's own streaming logic newly adds a chunk to a player's `loadedChunks` set, the server fires a one-shot **`FloraChunkInitialSnapshot`** RemoteEvent (raw, not Knit) to that player only, carrying `{chunkId, [{floraNodeId, currentT}, ...]}` for every flora in that chunk. The payload format is identical to `FloraChunkUpdate` — the client handler can route both signals through the same code path; the only difference is that the initial snapshot includes ALL flora in the chunk, not just those that crossed `FLORA_UPDATE_DELTA_THRESHOLD`.
- **Every `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` and `FloraChunkUpdate` fire MUST gate its target-player resolution against the server-side `loadedChunks` membership set for that player.** A push attempt for a `(player, chunkId)` pair where `chunkId ∉ loadedChunks[player]` is silently dropped — never delivered. This invariant holds even during chunk-stream-out / chunk-stream-in race windows; the membership set is authoritative.
- After delivering the snapshot, the server resets the per-player per-flora last-pushed-t cache to `currentT` so subsequent `FloraChunkUpdate` pushes apply the delta-threshold gate against the snapshot's values.
- **`ReplicationFocus` is NEVER read or watched by `DisturbanceService`.** The property is client-writable and therefore not a trustworthy signal. Any future PR that reintroduces a `Player:GetPropertyChangedSignal("ReplicationFocus")` subscription on the snapshot path is an exploit-class regression and reviewers MUST reject it. H.31b verifies this.
- **No client RemoteFunction is exposed for this purpose.** The round-1 `GetChunkFieldSnapshot` RemoteFunction is **removed** (and stays removed). Combined with the server-tracked membership rule above, the chunk-enumeration / cross-map data-leak exploit surface is closed: a malicious client cannot request snapshots for chunks they have not actually streamed in (no client-callable surface) AND cannot induce the server to fire snapshots for chunks the server itself does not believe the client should have loaded (server membership is authoritative; client signals are not in the trust path).
- Flora outside any client's loaded region receive no update and need none — they are not rendering.
- **Cross-system action item: the `DisturbanceService` ADR MUST specify the chunk-membership data structure (recommended: `{[Player]: {[string]: true}}` keyed by chunkId), the StreamingEnabled chunk-load detection API choice (per F.4 row 5), and the security-property assertion that the membership set is *never* mutated in response to any client-driven signal (RemoteEvent, RemoteFunction, BindableEvent, property-changed signal, or any other client-influenced wire path).**

This pattern mirrors the round-1 fix that removed `GetCurrentMeterValue` and replaced it with a server-pushed `OnMeterUpdate` on `PlayerAdded`. Architectural consistency: clients never initiate snapshot requests, AND server fires are gated by server-tracked authority — no client signal is in the trust path.

#### C.3.6 — Crafting & Items → Disturbance System (publisher, partially TBD)

The game concept specifies that beacon activation is a guaranteed large disturbance spike. Confirmed in principle; the full Crafting & Items GDD has not been authored yet.

**Confirmed**:
- Beacon activation publishes a `BeaconEmission` with `emissionType="Beacon"` and `initialMagnitude` in [0.85, 1.00] (exact value TBD pending Crafting GDD). Only emission type with `initialMagnitude` above the 0.85 reserved threshold.
- Position is the beacon's world position, not the placing player's position.
- `attributionChain` for a BeaconEmission includes the placing player's `sourcePlayerId` and action label `"BeaconPlaced"`.

**TBD (deferred to Crafting & Items GDD)**:
- Whether other crafted items (decoys, noise-makers) publish their own emission types. This document reserves `emissionType` enum slots for up to 3 additional item-driven types (1 of original 4 consumed 2026-04-30 by `Craft` — see C.1.2).
- If Crafting adds new emission types, the disturbance system's tier-threshold notification (C.3.3) and spike-alert system (C.3.4) handle them without modification — new types are additive (downstream branches on type categorically).

**Beacon stationary-squad Hunt-floor cap (round-5 / Tier-1 #10 — Option A — REQUIRED).** Round-5 game-designer review identified a degenerate strategy at the win condition: squad members fan out and stand still while the beacon decoys the predator (the beacon's ~49 s Hunt-floor window protects the squad even if no member is moving), violating Pillar 1 (Quiet Is Power) in spirit while gaming it in letter. The disturbance system MUST apply a Hunt-floor cap to the beacon's effective contribution when the squad is stationary near the beacon, forcing movement at activation rather than rewarding standing still.

**Cap rule.** On every field-update pass, for each currently-active Beacon emission B (defined as: `B.emissionType == "Beacon"` AND B is in the live-source list — not yet decayed below `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR`):

1. Count the number of connected players whose XZ-position is within `BEACON_STATIONARY_PROXIMITY_RADIUS` studs (G.5; default 16) of `B.position`. Call this `nearby`.
2. Compute `connected` = count of players in `Players:GetPlayers()` **whose `Character` has spawned** (i.e., `player.Character ~= nil` at the field-update pass, equivalent to "CharacterAdded has fired and `Character` has not since been removed"). Players in the pre-CharacterAdded race window per E.17 are EXCLUDED from `connected` because they have no XZ position (handle the empty case per E.15 — if 0, the cap rule does not apply). **Round-7c B-7b-5 rationale:** the `nearby` count at step 1 is already gated on a player having a queryable XZ position (its 16-stud proximity test cannot evaluate against a nil Character), so a characterless player is silently excluded from `nearby`. Including characterless players in `connected` while excluding them from `nearby` creates a denominator asymmetry that makes the cap unreachable in pathological cases (e.g., `connected = 1` characterless solo player + active beacon → `nearby = 0`, ratio `0/1 = 0 NOT > 0.50`, cap NEVER engages regardless of any other signal). Defining `connected` as character-spawned-only restores the precondition that `nearby ⊆ connected` and makes the cap's denominator semantics symmetric. Verified by H.41j (round-7c addition).
3. If `nearby / connected > 0.50` (more than half the squad is near the beacon — uses STRICT greater-than, so 2-of-4 does NOT trigger but 3-of-4 does), check stationary status:
4. For each player counted as `nearby`, evaluate stationary-status via a **cumulative-distance integral** over the previous `BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW` seconds (G.5; default 3.0 s) — NOT a sampled boolean (round-7a R7-B6 / CD adjudication D2). Maintain a server-side per-player XZ-position-history ring buffer at the 5 Hz field-update cadence; on each pass, compute `cumulativeXZ = sum(magnitude(P_{i+1}.XZ - P_i.XZ))` summed across all consecutive sample pairs in the buffer (sum of inter-sample 2D displacements, NOT first-to-last displacement). Treat the player as **stationary** if `cumulativeXZ < BEACON_CUMULATIVE_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD` (G.5; default 6.0 studs over the 3.0 s window — equivalent to ~2 studs/sec average velocity, well below natural walking speed of ~16 studs/sec). Count nearby-AND-stationary players as `stationary_nearby`. **Why cumulative-distance, not sampled-boolean (round-7a binding rationale):** a boolean lookback (the round-5–round-6 design — "was player stationary at sample times within window?") admits a "shuffle-game" exploit where a player oscillates back and forth such that any sampled instant reads near-stationary while their integrated path covers significant distance per second. Tightening `BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW` within its safe range (1.0–8.0 s) does not close the exploit because the underlying problem is sampled-boolean topology, not window length (round-7 game-designer F5). The cumulative integral closes the exploit because every per-sample displacement contributes to the sum; oscillation accumulates distance even when sample-boundaries align with apparent rest positions. This is Pillar 1 (Quiet Is Power) made uncheesable. **Per-pass buffer-write contract (round-9 B-NS6 — `--!strict` nil-guard on Character.PrimaryPart).** On each field-update pass, for each `connected` player (per C.3.6 step 2, `connected` requires `player.Character ~= nil`), the implementation MUST sample the player's XZ-position via the following nil-guarded idiom:

```luau
--!strict
-- Round-9 B-NS6 nil-guard: Character is Model? (nilable) and PrimaryPart is BasePart? (nilable);
-- both nil-checks are required to satisfy --!strict and to avoid a runtime nil-indexing crash
-- in the rare-but-reachable window where Character has been assigned but PrimaryPart has not
-- yet replicated to the server (Roblox CharacterAdded fires when the model loads but
-- PrimaryPart assignment is a separate replication step).
local character = player.Character
if not character then return end  -- redundant with C.3.6 step 2 connected-filter, but defensive
local primaryPart = character.PrimaryPart
if not primaryPart then
    -- PrimaryPart not yet replicated; treat as if Character is nil for this pass.
    -- Buffer is NOT appended this pass (the player effectively has no queryable XZ).
    -- On the next pass after PrimaryPart replicates, the buffer resumes appending.
    return
end
local position: Vector3 = primaryPart.Position
-- Append (position.X, position.Z) to the ring buffer; evict oldest if at capacity.
self:_appendRingBufferSample(player, position)
```

The `primaryPart` nil-check is REQUIRED for `--!strict` compliance — without it, `character.PrimaryPart.Position` is a `BasePart?:Position` access that the Luau type checker rejects, AND a runtime `attempt to index nil with 'Position'` error is reachable in the narrow window where Character has been assigned but PrimaryPart has not (Roblox replicates Character model loading and PrimaryPart-assignment as separate signals). When `primaryPart` is nil, the buffer is NOT appended this pass; the player's `nearby` membership at C.3.6 step 1 is also affected (the 16-stud proximity check cannot evaluate against a nil position) — both step 1 and step 4 fall through to the "characterless" treatment per C.3.6 step 2's round-7c B-7b-5 rule. On the next pass after PrimaryPart replicates, the buffer resumes appending normally; under-filled-buffer rules from round-7c B-7b-4 apply. Verified by H.41o (round-9 addition).

**Buffer-fill precondition (round-7c B-7b-4 / round-7b systems-designer adversarial finding).** A player's `cumulativeXZ` is used for cap engagement ONLY when the position-history ring buffer for that player contains at least `floor(BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ)` samples — at default tunings (3.0 s × 5 Hz), `floor(15) = 15` samples. Under-filled buffers (the player joined within the past 3.0 s, the server just started, or a teleport-discontinuity reset purged the buffer per E.25) trivially yield small `cumulativeXZ` values (a 0-sample buffer evaluates to `cumulativeXZ = 0`, a 5-sample buffer can only accumulate 4 inter-sample displacements) and would falsely classify a moving newcomer as stationary. The implementation MUST treat under-filled players as **moving** for cap-engagement purposes (per-pass `cumulativeXZ` for that player is treated as `+∞`, parallel to the teleport-discontinuity rule in E.25) and exclude them from the `stationary_nearby` total until the buffer fills. This precondition closes the false-stationary read at server start, mid-session player-join, and post-teleport-recovery windows. Verified by H.41k (round-7c addition).
5. If `stationary_nearby / nearby > 0.50` (round-7a R7-B4 / CD adjudication D1 — strict majority of NEARBY players are stationary; denominator is `nearby` (the count from step 1), NOT `connected`. Using `connected` would falsely engage the cap during legitimate active-defender play where some squad members are far from the beacon by design — e.g., 2 nearby + 2 active-defenders far away with the 2 nearby both stationary would give `stationary_nearby/connected = 2/4 = 0.50, NOT > 0.50` and the cap would not engage, but the design-intended trigger is "do the players who are at the beacon stand still?" which is exactly `stationary_nearby/nearby`. With `nearby = 2` stationary the ratio is `2/2 = 1.0 > 0.50` and the cap correctly engages on the passive-hide-by-beacon attempt). The cap engages: the contribution of beacon B at its own position is reduced such that `fieldValue(B.position)` is bounded at `BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE` (G.5; default `BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE = HUNT_THRESHOLD = 0.65`) — the beacon's effective contribution is reduced to make total field at the beacon position no higher than the Hunt floor.
6. Implementation note: the cap is applied as a per-beacon **multiplicative scaling factor** computed once per field-update pass: `scaleFactor = (BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE - non_beacon_contribution_at_B) / beacon_native_contribution_at_B`, clamped to `[0.0, 1.0]`. The beacon's underlying `m(t, age)` and decay track are NOT modified — D.1 still uses raw `initialMagnitude`. Only the contribution-summation path applies the scale factor when reading the beacon's contribution to `fieldValue(B.position)` AND to `fieldValue(P)` for any P within `INFLUENCE_RADIUS` of `B.position`. Without the cap, the beacon's per-source contribution is unchanged. **All-beacons-excluded rule (round-7c NC-c codification; promoted from E.25 two-beacons prose to step-6 pseudocode contract). Round-9 DR-2 = Option B amendment for co-located beacons (closes B-NS1):** `non_beacon_contribution_at_B` for a given beacon `B_i` MUST be computed as the field value at `B_i.position` summed over all live sources, with the following inclusion/exclusion rule:
- **All non-beacon live sources** (`S.emissionType != "Beacon"`) ARE INCLUDED.
- **Beacon `B_i` itself** is EXCLUDED (the cap formula's purpose is to bound `B_i`'s own contribution; including it would create a circular dependency).
- **Other live beacons `B_j` (where `j != i`)**: INCLUDED IF `dist2D(B_i.position, B_j.position) < INFLUENCE_RADIUS = 24` studs (co-located case — the round-9 DR-2 = Option B amendment); EXCLUDED IF `dist2D(B_i.position, B_j.position) >= INFLUENCE_RADIUS` (non-co-located case — the round-7c NC-c rule preserved).

The non-co-located case (NC-c rule, `dist >= INFLUENCE_RADIUS`) is correct as round-7c specified: any other live Beacon's contribution at `B_i.position` is itself capped (or capped-to-zero) under its own per-beacon cap evaluation, and including it in `non_beacon_contribution_at_B(B_i)` would couple the two beacons' cap-evaluation order and break the order-independence verified by H.41i. The co-located case (round-9 DR-2 = Option B amendment, `dist < INFLUENCE_RADIUS`) overrides this exclusion because the round-7c NC-c rule produced a silent design-intent violation when two beacons were placed within mutual influence range: `B_j`'s contribution at `B_i.position` (per D.3 live-source-list summation) DID elevate `fieldValue(B_i.position)` regardless of `non_beacon_contribution_at_B(B_i)`, so the cap clamped `B_i.contribution_at_B_i` to a value such that capped-`B_i` + `B_j.contribution` exceeded `BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE` and pushed `fieldValue` to Retreat tier (1.0 clamped) — the cap's Hunt-floor design intent was silently defeated. Including `B_j` in `non_beacon_contribution_at_B(B_i)` at co-location closes this defect: the cap formula now accounts for the second beacon's elevation when computing `B_i`'s scale factor.

The field-value formula otherwise (D.3) sums all sources without exclusion; both the round-7c NC-c rule and the round-9 co-location amendment apply ONLY to the cap formula's denominator-input. Implementer pseudocode (round-9 update):

```luau
-- Round-9 update to step 6's non_beacon_at_B_i computation.
-- The boolean predicate distinguishes co-located (within INFLUENCE_RADIUS) from non-co-located other-beacons.
local non_beacon_at_B_i = 0
for _, S in ipairs(liveSources) do
    if S.emissionId == B_i.emissionId then continue end  -- always exclude B_i itself
    if S.emissionType == "Beacon" then
        -- Other beacon: include only if co-located
        local d2sq = dist2DSquared(B_i.position, S.position)
        if d2sq < INFLUENCE_RADIUS * INFLUENCE_RADIUS then
            non_beacon_at_B_i = non_beacon_at_B_i + contribution(B_i.position, S)
        end
    else
        -- Non-beacon source: always include (D.3-style summation, scoped to denominator-input)
        non_beacon_at_B_i = non_beacon_at_B_i + contribution(B_i.position, S)
    end
end
```

The single-beacon case (only one `Beacon`-typed live source in the world) reduces to: `non_beacon_at_B_i = sum( contribution(B_i.position, S) for S in liveSources if S.emissionType != "Beacon" )` — identical to the pre-round-9 NC-c formulation. The order-independence verified by H.41i is preserved for non-co-located beacons; the co-located case has its own AC (H.41l, round-9 addition) that asserts the new formulation correctly produces a Hunt-floor cap (not a Retreat-tier overshoot) when two beacons are placed within `INFLUENCE_RADIUS` of each other.
7. The cap clears as soon as any one of the criteria fails on a subsequent field-update pass (squad disperses such that `nearby/connected ≤ 0.50`; ≥1 nearby player accumulates `cumulativeXZ ≥ BEACON_CUMULATIVE_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD` over the window such that `stationary_nearby/nearby ≤ 0.50`; or the beacon expires per D.1). No hysteresis on the cap engagement / disengagement — sub-second flapping at the 50% boundary is bounded by the `BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW` smoothing on the cumulative-distance integral (the 3.0 s window itself low-pass-filters per-frame jitter into a single stationary verdict).

**Design intent.** The cap closes the degenerate strategy by shifting the beacon's effective protection: when activated and the squad disperses to draw the predator across the map (active-defender behaviour), the beacon's full contribution applies — Retreat tier, ~49 s Hunt-floor lure window. When activated and the squad stands still nearby (gaming the protection), the beacon's contribution caps at Hunt floor — predator still pursues at Hunt tier without the field-driven Retreat-tier commitment (attack remains melee/LOS-gated per PA's H.47 — round-23 NP-1). Stand-still-near-beacon strategy is reduced from "guaranteed survival" to "uncertain investigation that probably doesn't escalate to Retreat-tier commit." Pillar 1 (Quiet Is Power) is preserved because the optimal play remains an active-decoy maneuver, not a passive hide-by-beacon.

**Cross-system implications.** This rule has two cross-GDD implications:
- **Predator AI GDD**: when the cap engages, `GetHottestHotspot(callerPosition, searchRadius, "Hunt")` will continue to return the beacon's position (`fieldValue == HUNT_THRESHOLD = 0.65` exactly), but will NOT return Retreat-tier (`fieldValue ≥ 1.0`) at the beacon position. Predator's locked-target logic MUST treat the beacon as a Hunt-tier hotspot under the cap: the predator locks and pursues in FSM-Hunt, but the cap withholds the field-driven Retreat-tier escalation — attack remains melee-range/LOS-gated per PA Core Rule 6 / PA's H.47, never field-value-gated (round-23 NP-1 correction of the retired "investigation-grade vs attack-grade" framing; see F.2a row 6). No state-machine change needed; this is an emergent property of the field's reduced amplitude.
- **HUD GDD (round-7a R7-IMPORTANT — game-designer F7 + qa-lead F6 elevated to MUST)**: a beacon-cap-active visual cue (e.g., a dimmed beacon icon, a "stand still" warning indicator on the active-defender player's HUD) **MUST** be implemented to make the cap engagement legible to players (round-7 game-designer F7 / qa-lead F6 finding: under the round-5 "MAY" wording, players who experienced the cap engaging would observe the predator behaviour anomaly — beacon doesn't pull predator into Retreat — without any HUD signal explaining it; the reduced protection is misread as a system bug or non-deterministic predator AI, breaking the player-facing contract that the world-state is legible). The HUD GDD MUST author this indicator with the same urgency as the predator-locked-on indicator (Q11 ruling). F.2a row 7 carries the binding obligation; the round-7a "MAY → MUST" change is also reflected in F.2a row 7 prose.

## Formulas

### D.1 — Magnitude Decay

Implements C.1.4 (emissions are temporary) and C.1.12 (expiry at `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR`).

`m(t, age) = initialMagnitude × exp(−λ × age)`

where `age = math.max(0, serverTime − emissionTime)`, and `λ = ln(2) / t½`.

**Server clock specification (REQUIRED).** All `emissionTime` writes and all `serverTime` reads in `m(age)` evaluations MUST sample from the **same** Roblox monotonic-time API — mixing sources produces wrong ages. The mandated source is `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` — Roblox's documented monotonic server clock guaranteed not to jump backward and synced to the Roblox time epoch. `os.time()` is forbidden (NTP-correctable; backward jumps drive negative ages). `tick()` is forbidden (deprecated). `os.clock()` is **forbidden as the canonical source** because Roblox does not formally guarantee `os.clock()` returns wall-clock time on game-server processes (it may return CPU time, which would diverge from `RunService.Heartbeat`'s `dt` parameter and produce systematically wrong ages). Implementations MUST capture both `emissionTime` and `serverTime` via `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` to guarantee consistency. The `math.max(0, age)` guard remains a defense-in-depth — even with a monotonic clock, the guard ensures the formula is total over all float inputs and guards against same-Heartbeat-frame equal-timestamp edge cases. Verified by H.7b (see Section H).

**Variables:**

| Symbol | Type | Range | Description |
|--------|------|-------|-------------|
| `initialMagnitude` | float | 0.08 – 1.00 **at default tunings** | Strength at `age = 0`; assigned per emission type (D.7) |
| `age` | float | 0.0 – ∞ s | Elapsed seconds since `emissionTime`; clamped to ≥ 0 by the formula's `math.max(0, ...)` guard |
| `λ` (lambda) | float | 0.0077 – 0.0231 s⁻¹ **at default tunings** | Decay rate; `ln(2) / t½`; two values below |
| `m(t, age)` | float | 0.0 – `initialMagnitude` | Remaining magnitude; drives D.2; range is enforced by the `age ≥ 0` guard |

> **Range-column scope note (round-22 I2):** the `initialMagnitude` and `λ` ranges above reflect the **current default** emission types and half-lives, NOT hard formula bounds. Under currently-published-safe tunings the true envelope is wider: `initialMagnitude` can reach 0.04 (`MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE` safe-range floor, G.5) or 0.024 for attenuated stationary-Light pulses (C.1.1), and `λ` spans `ln(2)/180 ≈ 0.00385 s⁻¹` (Beacon half-life ceiling, G.2) to `ln(2)/15 ≈ 0.0462 s⁻¹` (standard half-life floor, G.2). Do not use the table's range column as a validation bound — E.16/E.24 own input validation; G.2/G.5 own the tunable envelope.

**Half-life table:**

| Emission types | `t½` | Derived `λ` |
|---|---|---|
| Gather, Sprint, Light | 30 s | `ln(2) / 30 ≈ 0.02310 s⁻¹` |
| Beacon | 90 s | `ln(2) / 90 ≈ 0.00770 s⁻¹` |

**Output range:** `(0.0, initialMagnitude]`. Source expires when `m(t, age) < MAGNITUDE_FLOOR = 0.02` (strict less-than per C.1.12; H.9 / H.10 verify). A source created with `initialMagnitude = MAGNITUDE_FLOOR` survives at least one query pass at age=0 because 0.02 is NOT strictly less than 0.02.

**Expiry age examples:**
- Standard `λ = 0.02310`, `initialMagnitude = 0.40`: expires at `age ≈ 130 s`
- Beacon `λ = 0.00770`, `initialMagnitude = 0.95`: expires at `age ≈ 501 s` (~8.4 min — intentional long-tail lure)

**Performance note:** `exp()` is transcendental. Cache `age` once per update pass per source (not per query position); skip exp evaluation entirely once magnitude is below `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR` (lazy expiry).

**Worked example** (Gather/Sprint/Light, `initialMagnitude = 0.25`; expiry row cross-checks against D.7's lifetime table — Gather-medium 0.25 → ~109 s):

| `age` | `exp(−λ × age)` | `m(t, age)` |
|-------|-----------------|--------------|
| 0 s | 1.0000 | 0.2500 |
| 10 s | 0.7937 | 0.1984 |
| 30 s | 0.5000 | 0.1250 (half-life ✓) |
| 60 s | 0.2500 | 0.0625 |
| 90 s | 0.1250 | 0.0313 |
| ~109 s | — | 0.0200 → **EXPIRES** |

### D.2 — Spatial Falloff Contribution

Single source `S` at position `S.position` contributing to field at query position `P`. Implements C.1.3 (proximity weighting; XZ-only). Quadratic falloff chosen over inverse-square: bounded by construction, no singularity, exactly 0 at the cutoff radius.

**Two helper functions** to enable the cheap-then-expensive optimization mandated below:

```luau
function dist2DSquared(a: Vector3, b: Vector3): number
    -- Cheap radius cull. No sqrt; no transcendentals.
    -- Parameters are named `a`/`b` (not `P`/`S`) so callers passing a source struct
    -- cannot accidentally collide the parameter name with the source-struct identifier `S`
    -- used throughout D.2/D.3/D.6 — pass `S.position`, not `S`. Compile-time `--!strict`
    -- enforces the Vector3 type, but the rename closes a readability trap that
    -- round-4 review flagged: at call sites `dist2DSquared(P, S.position)` reads cleanly
    -- and never confuses the helper with the surrounding scope's `S` identifier.
    local dx = a.X - b.X
    local dz = a.Z - b.Z
    return dx * dx + dz * dz
end

function dist2D(a: Vector3, b: Vector3): number
    -- True Euclidean distance; only call after dist2DSquared passed the radius gate.
    return math.sqrt(dist2DSquared(a, b))
end
```

**Contribution formula:**

`contribution(P, S) = m(age) × max(0, 1 − dist2D(P, S) / INFLUENCE_RADIUS)²`

**Implementation pattern (REQUIRED).** Use `dist2DSquared` for the radius cull (`if dist2DSquared(P, S.position) > INFLUENCE_RADIUS * INFLUENCE_RADIUS then continue end`); only invoke `dist2D` (i.e., `math.sqrt`) on sources that pass the cull. The squared form `(1 − d/r)*(1 − d/r)` MUST be used for the falloff square — `math.pow` is forbidden (some platforms implement `pow(x, 2)` via `exp(2 * log(x))`, a transcendental that defeats the no-pow rule and is not caught by H.9b's exp-counter). H.9c verifies neither `math.pow` nor `math.sqrt` is invoked in the inner loop beyond the radius-passing sources.

**Variables:**

| Symbol | Type | Range | Description |
|--------|------|-------|-------------|
| `m(t, age)` | float | 0.0 – 1.00 | Current decayed magnitude from D.1 |
| `dist2D(P, S)` | float | 0.0 – ∞ studs | 2D Euclidean distance, XZ plane |
| `INFLUENCE_RADIUS` | float | const = 24 studs | Hard cutoff. Intentionally ~2× the player lantern radius (8–12 studs per art bible §4.3) — disturbance ripples wider than the gathering player's own visible zone. |
| `contribution(P, S)` | float | 0.0 – 1.00 | Source's scalar contribution to field at P |

**Output range:** `[0.0, m(t, age)]`. Zero outside `INFLUENCE_RADIUS` or for expired sources.

**Performance note:** Avoid `sqrt()` in the cull check — compare squared distances `(ΔX² + ΔZ²) > INFLUENCE_RADIUS²`. Only compute `sqrt()` for sources passing the radius gate.

**Worked example** (`m = 0.25`, `INFLUENCE_RADIUS = 24`):

| `dist2D` | falloff `(1 − d/24)²` | contribution |
|----------|------------------------|--------------|
| 0 studs | 1.0000 | 0.2500 |
| 12 studs | 0.2500 | 0.0625 |
| 18 studs | 0.0625 | 0.0156 |
| 24 studs | 0.0000 | 0.0000 |

The rapid mid-distance falloff (75% loss at 50% radius) reinforces the "local cluster" read: nearby gathered nodes compound; distant gathers are nearly invisible.

### D.3 — Field Value at Position

Implements C.1.3 + C.1.5: sum of live contributions, clamped at 1.0.

`fieldValue(P) = min(1.0, Σ contribution(P, Sᵢ))` for all live sources `Sᵢ`

**Variables:**

| Symbol | Type | Range | Description |
|--------|------|-------|-------------|
| `P` | Vector3 | world-space (XZ used) | Query position — player, flora, or predator sample |
| `Sᵢ` | point source | — | i-th live source (`m(t, age) > MAGNITUDE_FLOOR`) |
| `fieldValue(P)` | float | 0.0 – 1.00 | Normalized disturbance at P; input to D.4 |

**Output range:** `[0.0, 1.0]`, hard clamped. Cannot be negative. Ceiling at 1.0 is the Retreat tier per C.1.7.

**Implementation note:** With the C.1.6 grid (32×32 stud cells), a query reads only cells whose center is within `INFLUENCE_RADIUS + 16 studs` of P — typically 4–9 cells.

**Worked example** (three live sources within range of P):

| Source | dist2D | `m(t, age)` | contribution |
|--------|--------|-------------|--------------|
| S1 (Gather, age=5s) | 4 studs | 0.236 | 0.236 × (1−4/24)² = **0.164** |
| S2 (Sprint, age=12s) | 8 studs | 0.083 | 0.083 × (1−8/24)² = **0.037** |
| S3 (Sprint, age=3s) | 14 studs | 0.098 | 0.098 × (1−14/24)² = **0.017** |
| **Sum** | | | **0.218** → fieldValue(P) = 0.218 (Calm) |

One more Gather completing nearby would push toward Tense.

### D.4 — Tier Classification with Hysteresis

Implements C.1.7. Stateful — requires previous tier at P to apply hysteresis on downward transitions only.

**Published Luau type:**

```luau
type Tier = "Calm" | "Tense" | "Hunt" | "Retreat"
```

`Tier` is the canonical type for all tier strings across the codebase. Use this type in `--!strict` modules — it catches mis-spelled tier strings at compile time.

**First-call rule.** For any position whose tier has never been classified before (server start, newly-loaded chunk, freshly-spawned predator), callers MUST pass `previousTier = "Calm"`. **On the Calm branch, the function dispatches by flat-threshold (no hysteresis applied) so that a first-call with `t = 0.85` correctly returns `"Hunt"` — not `"Tense"` (round-3 ruling).** Hysteresis applies only on subsequent calls when `previousTier` is non-default. The function never returns nil for a valid `Tier` input. Verified by H.17 (boundary), H.17b (first-call Hunt), and H.17c (first-call Retreat).

```luau
--!strict
function tierAt(P: Vector3, previousTier: Tier): Tier
    local t = fieldValue(P)

    if previousTier == "Calm" then
        -- First-call / default-state flat dispatch: report current tier directly.
        -- Hysteresis applies only after a non-default previousTier has been observed.
        if     t >= 1.00 then return "Retreat"
        elseif t >= 0.65 then return "Hunt"
        elseif t >= 0.30 then return "Tense"
        else                  return "Calm" end

    elseif previousTier == "Tense" then
        if     t >= 0.65 then return "Hunt"
        elseif t <  0.27 then return "Calm"   -- hysteresis: 0.30 - 0.03
        else                  return "Tense" end

    elseif previousTier == "Hunt" then
        if     t >= 1.00 then return "Retreat"
```
