```luau
        elseif t <  0.62 then return "Tense"  -- hysteresis: 0.65 - 0.03
        else                  return "Hunt" end

    elseif previousTier == "Retreat" then
        if t < 0.97 then return "Hunt"        -- hysteresis: 1.00 - 0.03
        else             return "Retreat" end
    end

    -- Unreachable for valid Tier inputs; defensive guard catches typed-string violations
    error(("tierAt: invalid previousTier '%s'"):format(tostring(previousTier)))
    return "Calm" -- unreachable; satisfies `--!strict` return-path check after defensive `error()` (round-7a R7-B6d fix)
end
```

**Tier boundary table:**

| Transition | Direction | Threshold |
|---|---|---|
| Calm → Tense | Up | t ≥ 0.30 (no hysteresis) |
| Tense → Calm | Down | t < 0.27 (−0.03) |
| Tense → Hunt | Up | t ≥ 0.65 (no hysteresis) |
| Hunt → Tense | Down | t < 0.62 (−0.03) |
| Hunt → Retreat | Up | t ≥ 1.00 |
| Retreat → Hunt | Down | t < 0.97 (−0.03) |

**Post-hitch multi-tier cascade (REQUIRED — round-4 addition).** A single `tierAt()` call resolves at most one boundary in the hysteresis direction it's biased to (Retreat→Hunt, Hunt→Tense, Tense→Calm — one step per call). After a server hitch, `t` may drop multiple tiers in a single field-update pass (e.g., `t` falls 1.00 → 0.20 between consecutive 5 Hz passes because predator-attribution releases caused mass source-cull). Calling `tierAt(P, "Retreat")` with `t = 0.20` returns `"Hunt"` (single-step transition); the actual current tier should be `"Calm"`. The field-update pass MUST therefore loop classification until it converges:

```luau
-- Post-hitch settle loop (caller's responsibility — performed once per position per pass).
-- IMPORTANT (round-5 fix): the previous-tier table MUST be keyed by a canonical
-- string encoding of (X, Z), NOT by the Vector3 itself. Luau userdata uses
-- reference equality for table lookups, so a freshly-constructed Vector3 with
-- the same coordinates as a previous one will MISS the lookup — silent
-- always-Calm bug (no `previousTier` is ever found, so first-call flat
-- dispatch fires every pass and the post-hitch settle loop is never needed
-- because `prev` is always reset to the default).
local key = string.format("%d_%d", math.floor(P.X), math.floor(P.Z))
local prev = previousTierAtPosition[key] or "Calm"
local result = tierAt(P, prev)
while result ~= prev do
    prev = result
    result = tierAt(P, prev)
end
previousTierAtPosition[key] = result
-- Each transition observed during the loop is a boundary crossing — fire one
-- TierCrossedEvent per boundary crossed, subject to round-3 server-side coalescing
-- (at most one TierCrossedEvent per `crossedTier` value per pass — a Retreat→Calm
-- cascade fires three events, one each for crossedTier ∈ {"Hunt", "Tense", "Calm"},
-- collapsed across positions sharing the same boundary in the same pass).
```

**Canonical key encoding (REQUIRED).** The `previousTierAtPosition` table MUST be keyed by `string.format("%d_%d", math.floor(P.X), math.floor(P.Z))` (the same X / Z floor-snap used for the spatial grid), NOT by the Vector3 `P` directly. Luau userdata equality is reference-based, not value-based, and successive calls into `tierAt` typically receive a freshly-constructed Vector3 (e.g., from `Player.Character.PrimaryPart.Position` or from a flora node's `WorldPosition`), so a Vector3-keyed lookup always misses. The string-key encoding ensures stable cross-pass lookup at the same XZ coordinate. The implementation MUST NOT use `tostring(P)` or any other Vector3-stringification idiom — only the canonical floor-snap encoding above. Verified by H.34b setup (the harness mutates the live-source list for a fixed XZ position; the post-hitch cascade must observe the prior `Retreat` tier at that key on the next pass).

The loop terminates in at most four iterations (worst-case Retreat→Calm cascade executes the loop body 4 times: Retreat→Hunt→Tense→Calm→stop), so it is O(1) per position. The TierCrossedEvent for `crossedTier="Calm"` is intentionally fired on downward transitions even though Calm is not a "threat" tier — it is the signal that consumers (Predator AI, HUD persistent-zone-presence indicator) use to leave their elevated state on this position. Verified by H.34b. Without this loop, post-hitch tier classification would silently misreport "Hunt" when a position is actually Calm and the Predator AI / HUD would respond to a phantom tier — a correctness defect, not just a cosmetic one.

**Worked example** (`t` oscillating near Tense boundary):

| Tick | t | previousTier | Result | Reason |
|------|---|--------------|--------|--------|
| 1 | 0.28 | Calm | Calm | 0.28 < 0.30 |
| 2 | 0.31 | Calm | Tense | 0.31 ≥ 0.30 — upward, no hysteresis |
| 3 | 0.29 | Tense | Tense | 0.29 ≥ 0.27 — still in Tense band |
| 4 | 0.26 | Tense | Calm | 0.26 < 0.27 — hysteresis crossed |

Without hysteresis, ticks 2/3/4 each fire transition events. With hysteresis, only ticks 2 and 4 do.

### D.5 — Squad Aggregate `t` (HUD Display Value)

Implements C.3.4 (server pushes squad collective `t` = max).

```
squadT =
    if connectedPlayers is empty then 0.0
    else max(fieldValue(pᵢ.position)) for all connected players pᵢ
```

**Empty-player guard (REQUIRED).** Luau's `math.max()` raises an error when called with zero arguments. The empty-player branch is reachable at server start (before any `Players.PlayerAdded` fires), during a frame where all connected players are simultaneously transitioning (e.g., simultaneous disconnect; respawn frame gap), and during single-player reconnect windows. The implementation MUST short-circuit to `0.0` when no players are connected — never invoke `math.max()` on an empty array. Verified by H.22b. See E.15.

**Justification of max over mean/sum:** Max-aggregation surfaces the worst case. Mean would under-read when one player is in Hunt while others are Calm. Sum grows unboundedly with squad size. Max matches the emotional truth: *the squad is only as safe as its most exposed member.*

**NaN-guard defense-in-depth (round-3 delegation note; UPDATED round-22 I1 for consistency with D.6's in-loop guard).** E.16 schema validation at the emission layer prevents NaN from ever entering the live-source list (rejecting any emission whose `position.X/Y/Z` is NaN or non-finite). Round-22 made D.6 belt-and-braces against E.16 ever being weakened (in-loop non-finite disqualification — the predator-targeting seam); the SAME principle applies to this formula's output, which feeds the HUD meter (player-visible) and `GetSquadAggregateT()` (Predator AI's Retreat trigger — equally consequential consumers). **Normative (round-22):** the implementation MUST apply a finiteness check to the computed `squadT` before publishing — if `squadT ~= squadT or squadT == math.huge`, publish `0.0` instead (fail-quiet: a corrupted aggregate reads as Calm rather than as a spurious Retreat). D.6b inherits the guard for free (it returns the same per-pass cached value). Verified by H.25c's squad-aggregate assertion. **Round-23 SD-2 extension — `playerT` parity:** the SAME finiteness check MUST be applied to each per-player `playerT = fieldValue(player.position)` before the C.3.4 `OnMeterUpdate` publish (fail-quiet `0.0` + ops-log entry) — `playerT` rides the same payload to the same player-visible meter round-22's rationale named, and the guarded read is also what feeds D.4 tier classification (`tierAt()` performs NO finiteness check of its own: a NaN input silently freezes the ladder because every NaN comparison evaluates false). With this extension, every read site feeding D.4 is guard-protected. Verified by H.25c clause (d).

**Exclusion form — `GetSquadAggregateT(excludePlayer: Player?): number` (round-23 B1 — closes PA R5/OQ.10; PA GDD unchanged).** When `excludePlayer` is supplied, the accessor computes the same `max(fieldValue(pᵢ.position))` over connected players EXCLUDING `excludePlayer` — PA's Hunt→Disengage quiet-check must not let the locked target's forced-survival emissions poison the squad quiet read (PA Core Rule 2; PA H.25/H.36 [P0]). Pinned semantics: **(a)** `nil` → the unfiltered D.5 max (the HUD/D.5 publish path is unchanged); **(b)** empty remainder (the excluded player is the ONLY connected player) → return the UNFILTERED max, i.e. the lone player's own `T` — per PA H.57's solo semantics, never `0`/`nil` on a non-empty server; **(c)** zero connected players → `0.0` per the empty-player guard / E.15, regardless of the argument; **(d)** the D.5 finiteness guard applies identically to the exclusion form's output. Computed from the same per-pass cache (O(P), P ≤ 4); server-internal only, never on `Service.Client` (H.39c). Verified by H.22c.

**Worked example** (4-player squad):

| Player | Position | `fieldValue` |
|--------|----------|--------------|
| P1 | Node cluster | 0.71 |
| P2 | Cave entrance | 0.34 |
| P3 | Open field | 0.09 |
| P4 | Spawn area | 0.02 |
| **squadT** | | **max = 0.71** (Hunt — P1 is the reason) |

### D.6 — Predator Hottest-Hotspot Score

Procedure for `GetHottestHotspot(callerPosition, searchRadius, minimumTier)` per C.3.3 (round-17 I16-11 closure of round-16 IMPORTANT — F.1 violation removal: the round-15 wording invoked `PredatorService:GetCurrentPosition()` inside `GetHottestHotspot`'s body, creating an implicit DisturbanceService→PredatorService hard dependency that violated F.1 "no MVP-internal hard dependencies"; round-17 elevates the position to a parameter supplied by the caller, eliminating the dependency entirely).

**Note on the algorithm body below.** The function signatures (`GetHottestHotspot`, `topContributorsAt`) are normative `--!strict` Luau. The bodies are **pseudocode** illustrating the algorithm — they are not parseable Luau. Production implementation lives in the future `DisturbanceService` ADR; this GDD specifies behaviour, not implementation.

```luau
--!strict
function GetHottestHotspot(callerPosition: Vector3, searchRadius: number, minimumTier: Tier): HotspotResult?
    --[[ pseudocode begins
    local tierFloor = tierLowerBound(minimumTier)
        -- Calm: 0.00, Tense: 0.30, Hunt: 0.65, Retreat: 1.00

    -- 1. Enumerate candidate positions from the spatial grid (C.1.6),
    --    not the full live-source list. Cells are queried by Region2D
    --    intersection with a circle of radius `searchRadius` around the
    --    `callerPosition` parameter (round-17 I16-11: position is supplied
    --    by the caller — typically Predator AI passes its own current
    --    world-space position via Predator AI's internal state read; this
    --    GDD does NOT pull the position via Knit.GetService("PredatorAI"),
    --    preserving F.1 no-MVP-internal-hard-dependencies). The caller's
    --    contract: `callerPosition` is a finite Vector3; non-finite
    --    components are caller error and produce undefined behaviour
    --    (the caller's own validation layer is responsible).
    local candidatePositions = {}
    for source in liveSourcesInGridCellsIntersecting(callerPosition, searchRadius) do
        if dist2DSquared(callerPosition, source.position) <= searchRadius * searchRadius then
            table.insert(candidatePositions, source.position)
        end
    end

    -- 2. De-duplicate by floor-snapping each position to a HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE-stud
    --    cell origin. Snap method is canonical: floor-to-nearest-cell-origin
    --    (not centroid, not first-in-cell). Retain one position per unique cell key
    --    in enumeration order. Per-query precompute, not persistent.
    candidatePositions = deduplicateByFloorSnap(candidatePositions, HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE)

    -- 3. Score each unique candidate; track best and count those above tierFloor.
    --    Round-22 in-loop non-finite disqualification (SUPERSEDES the round-21 I-GD18-1
    --    post-loop `bestScore` reset — that formulation left `bestPosition`/`hotspotCount`
    --    corrupted when a +Inf candidate won selection, returning a non-nil HotspotResult
    --    with a fabricated 0.0 fieldValue below the caller's minimumTier; round-21 panel B11).
    --    Defense-in-depth parallel to E.16's Emit-layer NaN/Infinity rejection: fieldValue is
    --    finite by construction (E.16 + D.3's [0,1] clamp), but if that is ever weakened, a
    --    NaN/+Inf candidate MUST NOT reach the tierFloor gate, win bestPosition, or increment
    --    hotspotCount — it is disqualified from selection entirely. Verified by H.25c.
    local bestPosition, bestScore = nil, -1
    local hotspotCount = 0
    for _, C in ipairs(candidatePositions) do
        local score = fieldValue(C)
        if score ~= score or score == math.huge then continue end  -- non-finite: disqualify this candidate (H.25c)
        if score >= tierFloor then
            hotspotCount = hotspotCount + 1
            if score > bestScore then
                bestScore, bestPosition = score, C
            end
        end
    end

    if bestPosition == nil then return nil end

    -- 4. Top-3 contributors at bestPosition: sort live sources within INFLUENCE_RADIUS
    --    of bestPosition by contribution(bestPosition, S) descending; take first 3.
    --    Source set scoped to grid cells around bestPosition (NOT the full live-source list).
    local topEmissions, oldestContributorAge = topContributorsAt(bestPosition, 3)

    return {
        position = bestPosition,
        fieldValue = bestScore,
        hotspotId = dedupCellKey(bestPosition),  -- round-22 B3: the canonical floor-snap cellKey of
                                                 -- bestPosition (same string the step-2 dedup already
                                                 -- computes — see "Dedup snap-method" below); the
                                                 -- stable hotspot identity PA's tie-break/debounce
                                                 -- keys off (PA Core Rules 3/4, AC H.8, OQ.11/FIX-13)
        topContributingEmissionIds = topEmissions,
        hotspotCount = hotspotCount,
        oldestContributorAge = oldestContributorAge,
    }
    pseudocode ends ]]
    return nil  -- normative-signature-only stub; production body in DisturbanceService ADR
end

function topContributorsAt(P: Vector3, n: number): ({string}, number)
    --[[ pseudocode begins
    -- Iterate live sources in grid cells within INFLUENCE_RADIUS of P.
    -- Track top-n by contribution(P, S) descending and the maximum age across that set.
    -- Returns: (top-n emissionId list ordered by contribution desc, oldest contributor's age in seconds)
    -- Contract (round-7a R7-B10 — gameplay-programmer F5 contract gap closure):
    --   * If `P` has ZERO live contributors, returns ({}, 0) — caller must handle the empty list.
    --   * If `P` has ≥ 1 live contributors, returns at least 1 emissionId in the first slot;
    --     callers MAY assume `#result_emissionIds >= 1 when P has any live contributor in range`.
    --   * GetHottestHotspot only invokes topContributorsAt(bestPosition, 3) AFTER bestScore
    --     has been determined to be ≥ tierFloor, which requires bestScore >= 0 (the only
    --     non-nil bestScore comes from a candidate that scored above tierFloor; a position
    --     achieving fieldValue > 0 implies at least one live source within INFLUENCE_RADIUS).
    --   * Therefore, when GetHottestHotspot returns a non-nil HotspotResult, the
    --     `topContributingEmissionIds` field is guaranteed to have length >= 1
    --     (NEVER empty alongside a non-nil HotspotResult). H.25b verifies this invariant.
    pseudocode ends ]]
    return {}, 0  -- normative-signature-only stub
end
```

**Sampling rationale:** Candidates are emission source positions, not a uniform grid sample. Cheaper than uniform sampling (O(k) where k = live sources in `searchRadius`) and a good approximation — field peaks are *typically* near source positions given the quadratic falloff. **Caveat:** when sources cluster tightly (e.g., 10 sprint pulses within a 4-stud radius), the field's true peak is approximately at the cluster centroid rather than any individual source position. The HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE de-duplication mitigates this — sources within the same dedup cell are merged.

**Dedup snap-method (canonical).** Floor-snap each candidate's `(X, Z)` to a `HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE`-stud grid origin and produce a string key:

```luau
local k = HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE
local cellKey: string = string.format("%d_%d", math.floor(P.X / k), math.floor(P.Z / k))
-- IMPORTANT (round-5 fix): the form `cellKey = (math.floor(X / k), math.floor(Z / k))`
-- is INVALID Luau — parentheses around two values produce a multi-return tuple, not a
-- table key. Luau truncates a multi-return to its first value when assigned to a
-- `local`, so the previous formulation silently used only `math.floor(X / k)` as the
-- key — collapsing all candidates with the same X-floor regardless of Z.
-- IMPORTANT (round-7a unification): use `string.format("%d_%d", ...)` as shown above.
-- The previous concat-form `math.floor(P.X/k) .. "_" .. math.floor(P.Z/k)` is functionally
-- equivalent for integer-valued inputs in current Luau, but `string.format("%d", n)` forces
-- integer formatting unambiguously and is the canonical encoding aligned with D.4 line 543's
-- `previousTierAtPosition` key. Aligning eliminates any cross-section divergence under
-- future Luau `tostring(number)` representation changes.
```

The retained position for each unique `cellKey` is the **first candidate encountered** in enumeration order (deterministic given a deterministic source iteration order — the spatial grid's internal source list ordering, owned by the `DisturbanceService` ADR, MUST be deterministic across calls). **Round-22 B3: the winning candidate's `cellKey` is additionally exposed to the caller as `HotspotResult.hotspotId`** — the stable cross-tick hotspot identity Predator AI's tie-break/debounce requires (PA Core Rules 3/4 + AC H.8; PA OQ.11/FIX-13 asked for exactly this primitive). Because the representative *position* within a cell can legitimately shift when the first-encountered source expires and a co-cell source becomes representative, `position` is NOT a stable identity — `hotspotId` is. The id is already computed by this dedup step; exposing it costs one table field. H.25d verifies stability + format. The approximation error in `bestPosition` is bounded by `HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE` (default 4 studs). The Predator AI GDD MUST specify a navigation arrival-radius ≥ `HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE` to absorb this approximation; if the Predator AI uses a tighter arrival radius, the dedup grid size must be reviewed jointly. H.25 test setup MUST use a source configuration where snap ambiguity cannot affect the outcome (e.g., a single dominant source, no co-cell competitors).

**`searchRadius = 0` edge case.** Enumerate sources within 0 studs of the predator → only sources at the predator's exact XZ. If a source exists there: 1 candidate. Otherwise: returns nil (E.5/E.5b path). This is informational; production callers should use `searchRadius` ≥ 32 studs per C.3.3 safe range.

**Variables:**

| Symbol | Type | Range | Description |
|--------|------|-------|-------------|
| `searchRadius` | number | recommended 32 – 512 studs (Predator AI owned) | See C.3.3 safe-range note. Disturbance system does not validate. |
| `minimumTier` | Tier (string union) | {"Calm", "Tense", "Hunt", "Retreat"} | Floor filter |
| `tierFloor` | number | {0.00, 0.30, 0.65, 1.00} | Cutoff score |
| `HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE` | number | const = 4 studs (G.3) | Per-query dedup cell |

**Output:** `HotspotResult?` per C.3.3 — all six fields (`position`, `fieldValue`, `hotspotId`, `topContributingEmissionIds`, `hotspotCount`, `oldestContributorAge`) required when non-nil. Returns nil when no candidate meets `minimumTier` — Predator AI handles nil (falls back to patrol or remains in current state per the C.3.3 nil-recovery rule). **Non-finite output prohibition (round-22, normative — the in-loop disqualification's contract, not just a code comment):** a returned non-nil `HotspotResult` ALWAYS satisfies `fieldValue == fieldValue and fieldValue ~= math.huge` AND meets the caller's `minimumTier`; a candidate whose score evaluates non-finite is disqualified from selection entirely (never returned, never counted in `hotspotCount`, never sets `bestPosition`). Verified by H.25c.

**Performance note:** Runs at predator cadence (fixed 4 Hz per the APPROVED PA GDD Core Rule 2 — round-22 I7 refresh of the concept doc's 1–4 Hz estimate), not field-update cadence. With expected steady-state live-source count of **~207 sources at the 60-second mark** under the worst-case continuous-sprint + active-lantern scenario across all 4 players (round-5 point estimate; derivation in H.36), grid-bounded cost is O(local cells × local sources). The lower-bound case — fully stationary 4-player squad with sprint held — settles around **~138 sources** (UPDATED 2026-06-19; see full derivation in H.36 lower-bound scenario note and D.7). Because Player Controller's `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR = 0.09` overrides the Q9 `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR` attenuation for Sprint, stationary-sprint pulses now have `initialMagnitude = 0.09` (not `0.030`), and their lifetime rises from `~17.55 s` to `ln(0.09/0.02) / (ln(2)/30) ≈ 65.1 s` (Sprint stream: 4 players × ~65.1s/2s ≈ 130 sources). Light remains attenuated at `0.024` with `≈ 7.89 s` lifetime (Light stream: 4 players × ~7.89s/4s ≈ 8 sources). Combined lower-bound: Sprint + Light ≈ 130 + 8 ≈ **~138 sources**. **Performance budget is UNAFFECTED**: the H.36/H.38 PASS criterion and `MAX_LIVE_SOURCES` (500) are anchored to the 207-source upper-bound (continuous-MOVING-sprint + lantern, all 4 players), NOT the stationary lower-bound. The stationary lower-bound rising from ~40 to ~138 changes the informational corridor but does NOT gate any AC — ~138 < 207, so H.36/H.38 PASS criteria are unchanged. The prior ~40-source lower-bound assumed Sprint was attenuated to 0.030; the hold-floor removes that attenuation for Sprint. Naturalistic 4-player play falls in the corridor between ~138 (stationary-sprint) and ~207 (continuous moving-sprint + lantern). H.38 verifies bounded cost at `MAX_LIVE_SOURCES`; H.38b verifies the 2 ms cycle budget when a query coincides with the field-update pass.

### D.6b — Squad Aggregate Server Accessor

```luau
function GetSquadAggregateT(excludePlayer: Player?): number
    -- Server-internal; identical math to D.5; not exposed via Service.Client.
    -- Round-23 B1 (PA R5/OQ.10): optional exclusion form for PA's Hunt→Disengage
    -- quiet-check. nil → the unfiltered D.5 squadT (per-pass cache, unchanged path).
    if excludePlayer == nil then
        return squadT -- as computed by D.5 using the same per-pass cache
    end
    -- max over connected players MINUS excludePlayer, from the same per-pass
    -- fieldValue cache; empty remainder → fall back to the unfiltered max
    -- (PA H.57 solo semantics: the lone player's own T, never 0/nil).
    -- D.5 finiteness guard applies to the result identically.
    return maxOverConnectedExcluding(excludePlayer) -- O(P), P ≤ 4
end
```

Predator AI uses this for the Retreat trigger (sustained-Calm logic per C.3.3). Cost: O(P) where P ≤ 4. Returns the value computed by the most recent field-update pass; predator should call at its own cadence — fresh-call-per-query is fine since P is small.

### D.7 — Per-Emission-Type Initial Magnitudes

Design constants assigned by publishers (C.3.1, C.3.2, C.3.6). Primary tuning levers for difficulty.

| Emission type | `initialMagnitude` | Half-life | Lifetime |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gather — light node | 0.15 | 30 s | ~87 s |
| Gather — medium node | 0.25 | 30 s | ~109 s |
| Gather — heavy/loud node | 0.40 | 30 s | ~130 s |
| Sprint (single 2s pulse) | **0.10** | 30 s | ~70 s |
| Light (single 4s pulse) | 0.08 | 30 s | ~60 s |
| Beacon | 0.95 | 90 s | ~501 s |

**Lifetime formula** (canonical for the column above): `Lifetime = ln(initialMagnitude / MAGNITUDE_FLOOR) / λ` where `λ = ln(2) / t½`. Worked check: Sprint = `ln(0.10/0.02) / (ln(2)/30) ≈ 69.65 s` (rounded to ~70 s); Light = `ln(0.08/0.02) / (ln(2)/30) ≈ 60.0 s`; Gather-medium = `ln(0.25/0.02) / (ln(2)/30) ≈ 109.3 s`. H.36 derivation MUST use the same arithmetic.

**Beacon reservation:** values above 0.85 reserved for Beacon per C.1.2.

**Sprint stress test — moving (4 pulses, sprint covers ~16 studs, pulses at non-overlapping positions):**

In normal play, a sprinting player moves significantly between 2-second pulses (a sprinting player covers ~12-16 studs per pulse interval depending on Player Controller speed). Pulse positions are spread out; falloff at any single query position attenuates non-self contributions. Per-pulse contribution at the player's *current* position (dist2D ≈ 0 for the latest pulse, ~12+ studs for older pulses — likely outside `INFLUENCE_RADIUS = 24`):

| Pulse | age | m | contribution at current position |
|-------|-----|---|----------------------------------|
| S1 (16 studs back) | 6 s | 0.0871 | partial — dist2D = 16, falloff = (1-16/24)² ≈ 0.111 → 0.0097 |
| S2 (12 studs back) | 4 s | 0.0912 | dist2D = 12, falloff = 0.25 → 0.0228 |
| S3 (4 studs back) | 2 s | 0.0955 | dist2D = 4, falloff = 0.694 → 0.0663 |
| S4 (current) | 0 s | 0.100 | dist2D = 0 → 0.100 |
| **Sum** | | | **~0.199 → Calm-tier** (well below Tense 0.30) |

**Design intent**: Sprinting while *moving* is loud but not catastrophic — a sprinting player who keeps moving ripples a trail through the field rather than building a single hot spot. Squad learns to pace; sprinting in straight lines through territory is acceptable.

**Sprint stress test — STATIONARY (4 pulses, same position, with Q9 stationary-decay floor applied per C.1.1):**

> **RESOLVED 2026-06-19 (user ruling: ED yields — PC's `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR` stands; design intent rewritten to Retreat-ward).** The prior stale-flag (PC round-20 / 2026-06-14) has been closed. Player Controller's `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR = 0.09` ([R13-F1], PC C.8.1 / D.4 Step 2 / G.7) dominates ED's `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR` attenuation for Sprint. A stationary Sprint pulse is published as `max(SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR, MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE × STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR) = max(0.09, 0.030) = **0.09**`. ED's stationary attenuation factor is effectively overridden for Sprint (the hold-floor wins); `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR` still applies to Light (no hold-floor). PC's `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR` is not reopened. The table, asymptote, and series formula below are fully re-derived at the published 0.09.

A player sprinting in place — by grinding against terrain, standing still while the sprint key is held, or **(on touch, the primary platform) leaving the tap-toggle sprint on while stationary (PC C.1.3 — sprint is a toggle, not a hold, on touch)** — triggers C.1.1's stationary-emitter attenuation **on recurring pulses only**. (Round-22 B12: the unattended cases — forgotten touch toggle, AFK key-weight — are bounded by the PC-owned stationary sprint auto-release forward obligation, F.2a row 9 in `ecological-disturbance-forward-obligations.md`; RATIFIED by user 2026-07-03.) Per C.1.1, **the first pulse on sprint-state-entry always fires at full magnitude** (`MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE = 0.10`); subsequent recurring pulses without sufficient movement-delta are subject to `max(SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR, MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE × STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR) = max(0.09, 0.030) = **0.09**`. The hold-floor dominates, so the effective attenuation is from 0.10 → 0.09 (not 0.10 → 0.030):

| Pulse | age (at this row's tick) | initialMagnitude | contribution at firing position (dist2D = 0) |
|-------|---|---|---|
| S1 (state-entry, within `FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW = 1.0 s` per C.1.1; full magnitude) | 0 s | 0.10 | 0.10 |
| S2 (hold-floor stationary) | 0 s at fire (2 s after S1) | 0.09 | 0.09 (plus S1's decayed 0.10 × exp(−2λ) ≈ 0.0955 = 0.1855 cumulative at this position) |
| S3 (hold-floor stationary) | 0 s at fire (4 s after S1) | 0.09 | 0.09 (plus ≈0.1771 cumulative from prior pulses ≈ **0.2671** cumulative — round-22 B4 correction: matches the series `S(3) = 1.993 − 1.893 × r² ≈ 0.267` and the direct sum `0.09 + 0.09r + 0.10r²`; the prior 0.2624/0.3524 cells were arithmetically inconsistent with this section's own pinned formula) |
| S4 (hold-floor stationary) | 0 s at fire (6 s after S1) | 0.09 | 0.09 (plus ≈0.2550 cumulative ≈ **0.3450** cumulative — round-22 B4 correction: matches `S(4) = 1.993 − 1.893 × r³ ≈ 0.345`) |
| **Steady-state asymptote** | ∞ | — | `S_inf = 0.09 / (1 − r) = 0.09 / 0.045158 ≈ 1.993 → clamps to 1.0 (Retreat-tier)` (S1's 0.10 contribution decays away in the limit; S_inf is the recurring-0.09-pulse accumulation) |

**Shared derivation constants** (pinned per B-R19-SD-1 / round-20 Session B; unchanged): `r = exp(−2λ) = 2^(−1/15) ≈ 0.954842`; `1 − r ≈ 0.045158`; `λ = ln(2)/30 ≈ 0.023105`.

**Series formula** (first pulse 0.10, recurring pulses 0.09): `S(N) = S_inf − (S_inf − MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE) × r^(N−1) = 1.993 − 1.893 × r^(N−1)`. The coefficient `1.893 = S_inf − MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE = 1.993 − 0.10` embeds the C.1.1 first-pulse exemption (S1 fires at full 0.10, not at 0.09), analogous to the old coefficient 0.5643 in the now-superseded 0.030 derivation.

**Time to first cross Hunt floor (t ≥ 0.65):** Solve `S(N) = 1.993 − 1.893 × r^(N−1) = 0.65`. `r^(N−1) = (1.993 − 0.65) / 1.893 = 1.343 / 1.893 ≈ 0.7095`. `N−1 = ln(0.7095) / ln(0.954842) ≈ (−0.3435) / (−0.04620) ≈ 7.43 pulses`. **Time-to-Hunt-floor ≈ 2 s × 7.43 ≈ ~15 s** (canonical figure for Section B 4th-bullet (a) cross-references; supersedes all prior ~160 s / ~165 s / ~159 s figures, which derived from the stale 0.030 initialMagnitude).

**Time to saturate to Retreat-tier (t ≥ 1.0 clamped):** `r^(N−1) = (1.993 − 1.0) / 1.893 = 0.993 / 1.893 ≈ 0.5246`. `N−1 = ln(0.5246) / ln(0.954842) ≈ (−0.6445) / (−0.04620) ≈ 13.95 pulses`. **Time-to-Retreat ≈ 2 s × 13.95 ≈ ~28 s** (canonical figure for Section B and H.PB1 question 4 watch-item timescale). **Pulse-count canonicality (round-23 B5/SD-1):** both canonical figures are pulse counts × the DEFAULT `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL = 2 s` and do NOT survive an interval retune — see the G.7 `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL` joint constraint (a retune re-derives these figures, every citing site, and the F.2a row-9 auto-release window).

**Sprint source lifetime at stationary hold-floor:** `ln(0.09 / 0.02) / λ = ln(4.5) / 0.023105 ≈ 65.1 s` (supersedes stale 17.55 s).

Without the stationary attenuation at all, steady-state at the same position would be `0.10 / (1 − 0.9548) ≈ 2.218` (clamps to 1.0 / Retreat) within ~25 s. With the `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR = 0.09` floor active, stationary sprinting escalates to **Retreat-tier** in **~28 s** — fast, loud, traceable to "you stood still while sprinting." This is the intended outcome (holding sprint stationary IS loud; Pillar 1 — Quiet Is Power). It is NOT "barely Hunt / recoverable." Players who hold sprint stationary reach Retreat in under 30 seconds; the correct response is to release sprint and let the field decay. Section B's traceability promise is preserved: the ~28 s Retreat-reach is traceable to "you held the sprint key without moving."

**Why this number changed across rounds 4 / 17 / 20 / 2026-06-19 (cross-reference for Section B 4th-bullet (a)).** Round-4 cited "~90 s" (wrong: missed first-pulse exemption + geometric series). Round-17 I16-5 cited "~165 s" (wrong: double-counted the first-pulse exemption). Round-20 B-CD18-2 corrected to "~160 s" (correct for the then-canonical 0.030 initialMagnitude: bare formula `2 s × 79.4 ≈ 159 s`). **2026-06-19 ED-yields ruling** replaces the 0.030 derivation entirely: the published stationary-sprint initialMagnitude is now `0.09` (PC `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR` dominates), yielding **~15 s to Hunt** and **~28 s to Retreat**. All prior ~160 s / ~165 s / ~159 s canonical figures in this GDD are superseded by the ~15 s / ~28 s figures at this published magnitude. H.PB1 question 4 should expect stationary-sprint Retreat-reach on a ~28 s timescale, not a ~2.5-minute timescale.

**Lantern stationary steady-state** (Q9 same-rule analysis, pulse interval 4s, attenuated 0.08 × 0.30 = 0.024):
- Per-pulse decay over 4s: exp(−0.02310 × 4) ≈ 0.9117
- Steady-state: 0.024 / (1 − 0.9117) ≈ **0.272** → Calm (just below Tense 0.30)

A stationary player with lantern raised settles in Calm tier — a base-camp crafter is not silently advertised as Hunt-tier. Resolves Q9.

**Gather escalation test** (1 medium Gather added to existing field at t=0.30; gather is point-in-time, no stationary attenuation; both gathers fire within the same field-update cycle so age ≈ 0 for both):
- Gather contribution at age=0, dist2D=0: 0.25 × 1.0 = 0.25
- New `fieldValue` = 0.30 + 0.25 = 0.55 (still Tense, approaching Hunt at 0.65)
- A second medium gather at the same spot in the same cycle: 0.55 + 0.25 = 0.80 (Hunt)

Two gathers at a medium node cluster on top of sprint noise reaches Hunt. Matches design intent.

**Beacon validation — active-defender case (single fresh Beacon, dist2D=0, age=0; squad has dispersed and is moving — cap NOT engaged per C.3.6 Beacon stationary-squad rule):**
- Contribution: 0.95 × 1.0 = 0.95 → Hunt (just below Retreat at 1.00)
- 90-second half-life: Beacon stays at or above Hunt-floor (0.65) until age ≈ 49 s (`0.95 × exp(−0.00770 × age) = 0.65 → age ≈ 49.3 s`); drops below Tense (0.30) at age ≈ 150 s; expires (m ≤ MAGNITUDE_FLOOR) at age ≈ 501 s.
- Intentional: Beacon announces "come here," not "kill immediately." Predator's effective commit window at peak threat is ~49 s — the Predator AI GDD's pathfinding cadence must align with this window.

**Beacon validation — stationary-squad cap case (single fresh Beacon, dist2D=0, age=0; ≥51% of squad is within `BEACON_STATIONARY_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 16` studs of beacon AND each nearby player's `cumulativeXZ < BEACON_CUMULATIVE_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD = 6` studs over the past `BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW = 3` s — cap engaged per C.3.6 round-5 / Tier-1 #10; round-7a R7-B6 cumulative-distance redesign):**
- **Sub-mode (i) — low-ambient case (round-5 baseline; NO LONGER the representative stationary-squad case — see below):** Native beacon contribution would be 0.95; non-beacon ambient contribution at beacon-position is **0.10 (illustrative low-ambient anchor — held fixed for the downstream H.41 worked-example arithmetic; see the post-2026-06-19 note below: a realistic stationary-squad ambient is now ≈0.88 under PC's `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR`, so **sub-mode (ii) is the representative case** and sub-mode (i) describes only low-ambient scenarios — round-22 I18 at-site flag so a reader stopping at this first worked example is not misled)**.
  - Per the cap rule, `scaleFactor = (BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE - non_beacon_contribution_at_B) / beacon_native_contribution_at_B = (0.65 - 0.10) / 0.95 = 0.579`.
  - Capped contribution: `0.95 × 0.579 ≈ 0.55`; total fieldValue at beacon position: `0.10 + 0.55 = 0.65` (Hunt-floor exactly; clamped by the cap). Predator investigates but does not enter Retreat-tier attack run.
- **Sub-mode (ii) — `non_beacon > cap` (round-7 R7-B5 systems-designer F3 / 3-specialist convergence):** Ambient is heavy (e.g., recent gather sequence pushed `non_beacon_contribution_at_B = 0.70` while squad froze near beacon). The cap formula yields `scaleFactor = (0.65 - 0.70) / 0.95 = -0.053`, which the implementation MUST clamp to `0.0` (the formula's `[0.0, 1.0]` clamp at C.3.6 step 6). Capped beacon contribution = 0; total fieldValue at beacon position = `0.70 + 0 = 0.70` — **the cap CANNOT pull the field below ambient**, so the field remains above `BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE = 0.65` due to ambient alone. Predator escalates to Hunt on the basis of ambient + decayed beacon (which is still in live-source list with its raw `m(age)`, just contributing 0). Design intent in this sub-mode: the cap is a beacon-attenuation mechanism, NOT a global field-value clamp. The field is still allowed to exceed cap when non-beacon sources alone produce that elevation. Predator AI's Investigate→Hunt transition responds to the ambient elevation correctly.
- **Sub-mode (iii) — `beacon_native_contribution_at_B → 0` (denominator-zero protection; round-7 R7-B5 network-programmer F6):** As the beacon decays (~age > 200 s for `m(age) = 0.95 × exp(-0.00770 × age) → MAGNITUDE_FLOOR + ε`), its native contribution at its own position approaches the floor and the formula's denominator approaches 0, threatening NaN. The implementation MUST short-circuit: if `beacon_native_contribution_at_B < MAGNITUDE_FLOOR × 1.5` (i.e., the beacon is at or near its own expiry threshold; default 0.03 absolute), the cap rule does NOT apply for this beacon (the beacon will be culled within one maintenance pass anyway). This guards against `0/0` NaN propagation. Verified by H.41f (added round-7a; see Section H).
- Cap clears immediately on the next field-update pass where any one criterion fails (`nearby/connected ≤ 0.50`; `stationary_nearby/nearby ≤ 0.50` due to ≥1 player accumulating cumulative-distance ≥ 6 studs; or beacon expires). On the cap-clear pass: full 0.95 contribution returns; field rises to 0.95 + 0.10 = 1.0 (clamped to Retreat); predator's locked-target logic re-evaluates per the path-stability rule (C.3.3).
- **Design intent**: stationary-squad-near-beacon is reduced from guaranteed protection to uncertain investigation. Active-defender play (squad disperses to draw predator across the map) keeps the original ~49 s protection window. Pillar 1 (Quiet Is Power) preserved. **Sub-mode-specific notes**: (i) is the targeted case the cap was designed for; (ii) is a graceful failure where ambient alone justifies the elevation; (iii) is a safety guard against the denominator-zero defect, not a designed gameplay state.

**Beacon validation — single-player-near-beacon case (`connected = 1`, `nearby = 1`):**
- Step 3 evaluates `nearby / connected > 0.50 = 1/1 > 0.50 = true` (single connected player counts as 100% near beacon). Step 5 evaluates `stationary_nearby / nearby > 0.50` — if the player is stationary, `stationary_nearby = 1`, ratio = `1/1 = 1.0 > 0.50 = true`, cap engages exactly as in the squad case.
- In a 1-player session this means a solo player who activates the beacon and stands still nearby triggers the cap — the design intent is the same: forces movement at activation, even solo. Solo-active-defender play (place beacon, run away, predator chases beacon, player flanks) is the expected gameplay loop and the cap does not interfere with it.

**Beacon validation — endgame hold-window × sprint-holding defender (round-22 I13 closure — this intersection sits at the sole win condition, the Crafting GDD's beacon continuous-hold survival window, and was previously unexamined):**

Setup: an active fresh Beacon B during the endgame hold window; one or more defenders hold position near B "ready to flee," with sprint engaged while stationary (key held on PC/gamepad; tap-toggle left on for touch, PC C.1.3). Governing rules: C.1.1 stationary attenuation with PC's `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR` (published 0.09), the C.3.6 stationary-squad cap, and the PC-owned **stationary sprint auto-release** (F.2a row 9 in `ecological-disturbance-forward-obligations.md`, RATIFIED 2026-07-03: sprint auto-releases after ~8–10 s of near-zero XZ movement — strictly below the ~15 s Hunt crossing — on all input modes; deliberate re-press restarts the window). **Precondition (round-23 GD-3 — stated as a hard dependency, not background):** every conclusion below is CONDITIONAL on Player Controller actually authoring the row-9 auto-release — as of 2026-07-04 PC contains no such mechanic (row 9 is a ratified obligation, not shipped design). Until PC ships it, a stationary sprint-holder blows past Hunt (~15 s) and Retreat (~28 s) well inside the ~49 s hold window and this example's fairness read does NOT hold.

- **Auto-release bound (single defender, no re-press).** Pulses fire at t = 0, 2, 4, … s; the auto-release fires after ~8–10 s of zero movement, so at most N = 5–6 pulses land before sprint drops: `S(5) = 1.993 − 1.893 × r⁴ ≈ 0.42`; `S(6) = 1.993 − 1.893 × r⁵ ≈ 0.49` (shared derivation constants above; conservative — assumes sprint-state entry at the plant moment, so the full-magnitude 0.10 first pulse is included). **Peak co-located contribution ≈ 0.42–0.49: Tense-band, strictly below Hunt (0.65), with ≥ 0.16 of margin.** The residue decays below Tense (0.30) roughly 15–21 s after release (`ln(S/0.30) / λ`).
- **Cap interplay — why the win-window is not destabilised by bounded readiness noise.** While the cap is engaged, C.3.6 step 6 pins `fieldValue(B.position)` to exactly `non_beacon + 0.95 × (0.65 − non_beacon) / 0.95 = 0.65` for ANY ambient below the cap — the cap *absorbs* bounded sprint noise into the beacon's contribution headroom. A single auto-release-compliant defender co-located with B contributes at most ≈ 0.49 of non-beacon ambient at B, below `BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE = 0.65`, so the field at B stays pinned at the Hunt floor (sub-mode (i)) — the defender cannot *accidentally* convert the hold window into a field-driven Retreat-tier escalation. At 0.65 the predator is in FSM-**Hunt** — locked and pursuing per PA Core Rules 3/4 — but attack remains melee-range/LOS-gated (PA Core Rule 6 / PA's H.47), never field-value-gated; what the cap suppresses is the field-driven escalation toward Retreat-tier, not lock or attack capability (round-22 gate correction of the retired "Investigate-grade" framing — see F.2a row 6).
- **Escalation past the Hunt floor is intent-gated on every input method (B12 tie-in).** Pushing ambient at B above 0.65 (sub-mode (ii), field rises toward Retreat) as a lone defender requires sustaining the stationary pulse train past the ~15 s Hunt crossing — which the auto-release forecloses without deliberate re-press: ~1 re-press to reach the ~15 s Hunt crossing, ~2–3 to reach Retreat at ~28 s (escalation carries across re-presses because live sources persist ~65.1 s, per F.2a row 9). On touch the re-press is a deliberate tap, so the 2026-06-19 ruling's "holding sprint IS a choice" rationale now holds on the primary platform *at the win-con site*; a forgotten toggle or AFK defender is bounded at ≈ 0.49 (Tense) and can no longer silently drag the squad toward Retreat during the endgame.
- **Squad-scale loudness remains squad-traceable.** Four defenders simultaneously sprint-planting co-located on B sum ≈ 4 × 0.49 ≈ 1.96 of pre-clamp ambient before any re-press → sub-mode (ii) transiently: `scaleFactor` clamps to 0, `fieldValue(B.position)` clamps to 1.0 (Retreat) around t ≈ 8–10 s, then decays back under the 0.65 floor within ≈ 48 s absent re-presses (`ln(1.96/0.65) / λ`). This is maximal *coordinated* loudness at the win-con and reads as squad-caused (Section B FELLOWSHIP attribution), not an untraceable spike. At 16-stud dispersal the same four defenders contribute only ≈ 4 × 0.49 × 0.111 ≈ 0.22 < 0.65 and the Hunt floor holds (sub-mode (i)).
- **Per-cell density (B8 tie-in).** An auto-release-compliant defender leaves only ~5–6 co-located live sources per engagement; the ~35-sources-per-cell natural worst case (and H.38's updated ~40-source cluster fixture) is reachable only by deliberate re-press cycling, so the round-22 B8 fixture bound is unchanged by this case.
- **Design read — no rule change required.** The sole win condition admits neither a quiet sprint-camping posture (the cap + auto-release bound it) nor an unfair punishment of a ready-to-flee posture (bounded readiness noise is absorbed at the Hunt floor). Escalation beyond the floor requires repeated deliberate re-press or coordinated squad loudness — both traceable, preserving Section B and Pillar 1 at the endgame. Active-defender movement (disperse, flank) remains the dominant endgame posture, per the cap's design intent above.
- **Adversarial-intent framing (round-23 RT-2 — USER-RULED 2026-07-04: SOCIAL-LAYER ACCEPT).** A deliberately hostile squadmate CAN weaponize the re-press cycle to force Retreat-tier during the hold window in ~28 s, with no mechanical squad counterplay (the field is purely additive; no per-player suppression, vote, or dampen mechanic exists in ED/PC/Crafting). **Ruled a social-layer / party-formation concern — accepted by design choice, as most co-op games ship; no anti-griefing mechanic will be authored.** HUD's per-player contribution display (F.2a-IMPORTANT H1, PENDING) is the legibility aid that makes the saboteur visible; it does not prevent the sabotage. This is a Resolved Design Tension, not an open question.
- **Boundary note (round-23 GD-4).** The endgame hold radius (`BEACON_HOLD_RADIUS ≈ 30` studs, game-concept/Crafting) exceeds `INFLUENCE_RADIUS = 24`: a defender legally holding at 26–30 studs from B contributes ZERO field at B's position regardless of behaviour — strictly safer than the 16-stud case examined above; the analysis here is the conservative inner-radius case.
- **Interval dependency (round-23 B5/SD-1).** The pulse arithmetic above assumes `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL = 2 s` (the default); see the G.7 joint constraint — at the knob's 1 s floor the auto-release window admits 9–11 pulses, Hunt arrives ≈ 6.7 s, and S(9) ≈ 0.75 (1 s-interval constants r₁ ≈ 0.9772 / S_inf,1 ≈ 3.94 — round-24 Gate-B2 F8 correction of the prior 2 s-constant reuse) — this example's margins do not hold.

**`non_beacon_contribution_at_B` derivation (round-7 R7-B5 systems-designer F3 / sub-mode (i) anchor):** the worked example uses `non_beacon = 0.10` as a representative low-ambient figure (its original round-7 basis was `~3.3 alive pulses × 0.030 attenuated Sprint magnitude ≈ 0.10`, ignoring spatial falloff for players within `BEACON_STATIONARY_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 16` studs ≪ `INFLUENCE_RADIUS = 24` studs). **⚠️ Post-2026-06-19 ED-yields note (follow-up, NON-breaking to this worked example):** PC's `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR = 0.09` supersedes the `0.030` attenuated magnitude AND raises the stationary-sprint source lifetime to `~65.1 s` (D.7), so a realistic stationary-squad ambient near a beacon is **materially higher than 0.10** and a stationary squad will more often land in **sub-mode (ii) (ambient ≥ cap)** rather than sub-mode (i). The `0.10` figure is therefore retained ONLY as the fixed illustrative anchor for the H.41 worked-example arithmetic (so those ACs do not silently shift); the cap formula + its `[0.0, 1.0]` clamp + the H.41 discriminant ACs are **value-agnostic and unaffected** by the actual ambient magnitude. **RESOLVED (Session C, 2026-06-19):** the realistic stationary-squad ambient at the published 0.09 hold-floor is `≈ 0.88` even in the conservative all-at-edge case (4 players at `d = 16` studs, falloff `(1 − 16/24)² = 0.111`, per-player steady-state `≈ 1.993 × 0.111 ≈ 0.221`, 4-player sum `≈ 0.88`) and exceeds 1.0 (field-clamped) for tighter clustering — in both cases **≥ the cap `BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE = 0.65`**, so `scaleFactor` clamps to `0.0` and the **representative stationary-squad case is now sub-mode (ii) (ambient-dominant)**, not sub-mode (i). Sub-mode (i) (cap suppresses below 0.65) now describes only low-ambient scenarios (few players, distant positioning, or a fresh beacon before sprint accumulates). The `0.10` anchor is retained as the fixed illustrative input for the H.41h/H.41i/H.41m worked-example arithmetic (those ACs test the cap *discriminant/formula*, which is value-agnostic — they do NOT shift). The cap formula handles all values via the `[0.0, 1.0]` clamp at step 6 (sub-mode (ii) for ambient > cap; sub-mode (iii) for beacon-near-floor).

## Edge Cases

**E.1 — Zero-magnitude emission (`initialMagnitude = 0`).** Server MUST reject any emission with `initialMagnitude ≤ 0` at the validation layer (same schema check that rejects missing fields per C.1.2). Prevents silent zero-entries accumulating in the live-source list. Not a graceful-handling case — it is a validation failure.

**E.2 — Emission published exactly at `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR` (`initialMagnitude = 0.02`).** `m(t, age = 0) = 0.02`. Source is live for one update pass; on the next maintenance pass it is culled (C.1.12). Contributes one negligible field evaluation at t=0 (D.2). No special handling needed.

**E.3 — Two emissions at exactly the same position from the same player on the same server tick.** Per C.1.5, no merge: both inserted as independent point sources. Their contributions stack at query time (D.3). Each carries its own `emissionId` (UUID v4) and is independently traceable. Correct behavior — rapid double-fire (e.g., two qualifying actions landing on the same tick) legitimately doubles local impact.

**E.4 — Player disconnects with live emissions.** Emissions are server-side plain Luau tables keyed by `emissionId`, not by `sourcePlayerId`. Disconnect does not expire them — they continue to decay normally per D.1 for their full natural lifetime. Predator can still follow the trail. If the same player reconnects before their emissions expire: attribution payloads referencing that `sourcePlayerId` still resolve correctly (C.1.11) because attribution reads from the emission record. If the player does not reconnect before expiry, the emissions are gone and `sourcePlayerId` is irrelevant — no dead player receives a death screen. No cleanup pass needed on disconnect.

**E.5 — `GetHottestHotspot` called when no live sources exist.** Returns `nil` per D.6. Predator AI falls back to patrol (C.3.3). No error.

**E.5b — `GetHottestHotspot` called with no candidates meeting `minimumTier`.** All candidates fail the `score >= tierFloor` gate in D.6. Returns `nil`. Predator AI receives the same nil fallback as E.5 — single return path handles both.

**E.6 — Player jitters across a tier boundary (PASSIVE alert hysteresis).** Field tier classification uses D.4 hysteresis (±0.03 band), so sub-second `t` oscillation near a boundary does not produce multiple `TierCrossedEvent` firings. For PASSIVE spike alerts (C.3.4), the "suppressed until exit and re-enter" rule requires the position to resolve to a *lower* tier before re-firing. Because tier classification uses D.4 with hysteresis, the position must drop below `boundary − 0.03` before exit is detected, and rise back above the boundary before re-entry is detected. Micro-oscillation within the ±0.03 band never produces an exit classification, so the PASSIVE alert cannot re-fire during jitter. **D.4 hysteresis is sufficient; PASSIVE alerts do not need a separate hysteresis mechanism.**

**E.7 — Two players at the same position emit on the same server tick.** Both emissions inserted as independent sources (C.1.5). Contributions sum at D.3. Each carries its own `sourcePlayerId` and `emissionId`. The `TierCrossedEvent` fires once if cumulative sum crosses a tier threshold — the event is position-based, not per-source. Attribution payload for a subsequent death correctly records both `emissionId`s as contributing (up to 3, per C.1.11).

**E.8 — Beacon activated while ambient field at the same position is ≥ 0.05 (such that Beacon + ambient ≥ 1.0).** `fieldValue(P) = min(1.0, ambient + beaconContribution)` per D.3. Sum ≥ 1.0 is clamped to 1.0 (Retreat). Excess is silently truncated — predator sees Retreat at that position. Design consequence is intentional: Beacon's guaranteed high disturbance is expressed as "position is at maximum aggression" rather than "position is at 1.27." Predator commits to attack run at Retreat (C.2). The Beacon emission's own `initialMagnitude` and decay track are unaffected — D.1 uses raw `initialMagnitude` (0.95), not the clamped field value.

**E.9 — Player dies in Calm tier with no emissions in the past 10 minutes.** No live sources exist with relevant `sourcePlayerId`. The predator's stored `topContributingEmissionIds` list at target-lock time is empty or contains expired `emissionId`s that no longer resolve. `DeathAttributionPayload` contains `killingEmissions: []` (empty array). Death screen displays fallback copy (e.g., "Attribution unavailable — no recent disturbance detected"). HUD must handle empty array without crashing — valid payload, not error. *Fallback copy ownership: HUD GDD + art bible §2.6 own the display.*

**E.10 — Chunk streams in with stale flora client-state.** When the server's StreamingEnabled chunk-load logic adds a chunk to a player's `loadedChunks` membership set (C.3.5 round-4 Option A), the server fires `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` to that player carrying current `t` for every flora in the chunk, computed from the live server-side source list. The client applies values to its local flora-state table before starting any visual tween (H.31 verifies state-before-tween). **If the RemoteEvent delivery is dropped (network packet loss; client mid-disconnect)**, the client's flora-state table for that chunk remains at whatever stale state it held; the next regular `FloraChunkUpdate` push for that chunk (≤ 200 ms at 5 Hz, also gated by server-side `loadedChunks` membership) carries any deltas that have crossed `FLORA_UPDATE_DELTA_THRESHOLD` since the snapshot would have been taken. In practice the recovery is nearly invisible because chunk-resident flora are unlikely to all sit within `FLORA_UPDATE_DELTA_THRESHOLD` of zero-delta on the next tick, so most flora self-correct on the immediate follow-up push. Persistent visual corruption is bounded to a single 5 Hz cycle in the worst case. **The round-1 client-pull `GetChunkFieldSnapshot` RemoteFunction was removed in round-2 and stays removed — no client-callable surface exists on this path** (C.3.5; F.4 RemoteFunctions-intentionally-absent rule; H.39b verifies).

**E.11 — Server update loop degrades to 3 Hz under load (below 5 Hz target).** Time-accumulator pattern (C.1.13) means update fires on actual elapsed time, not fixed tick count. At 3 Hz, field values still correct — only update interval stretches from 200ms to ~333ms. Flora saturation pushes lag; HUD meter pushes lag by up to 333ms. Decay continues accurately because `m(t, age)` uses `serverTime − emissionTime` (D.1), not tick count. Predator queries `fieldValue` synchronously on its own cadence (D.6) and receives correct values. **Graceful degradation: visual latency increases but no values corrupt.** Below 1 Hz, lag becomes perceptible; this is the lower bound for the update-cadence tuning knob (Section G).

**E.12 — Live source list grows unbounded (stuck emission loop).** Server maintenance pass (C.1.12, cadence 2–4 s) culls expired sources lazily. A bug producing emissions faster than decay expires them would cause `GetHottestHotspot` and `fieldValue` queries to grow in cost. **Safety valve**: implementation MUST enforce a hard cap `MAX_LIVE_SOURCES` (Section G, recommended starting value: 500). When cap is reached, new emissions from the offending `sourcePlayerId` are dropped (fire-and-forget contract per C.3.1 means callers are unaware). Drop is silent from caller's perspective but MUST be logged server-side with `sourcePlayerId` and `emissionType` for debugging. **A cap hit is an engineering red flag, not a design-intended state.** Eviction policy: drop-new-from-same-player is the recommended starting policy; alternatives (drop-oldest, drop-highest-emitting) to be confirmed when the `DisturbanceService` ADR is authored.

**E.13 — Player teleports while sprinting.** Next sprint pulse fires from the player's position at the moment Player Controller samples it (C.3.2). If teleport lands between two 2-second sprint-pulse intervals, the next pulse fires at the new position — old position receives no retroactive emission. `attributionChain` on the new pulse contains only the new emission's context; does not reference prior-position pulses. **No chain is broken** because attribution chains are per-emission, not per-player-movement-path. Teleport may create a spatial gap in the sprint-trail — correct behavior: the predator cannot follow a trail that was never laid.

**E.14 — Two players simultaneously cause a tier crossing at the same position.** If both emissions arrive on the same server tick and pre-tick field was below a boundary, server computes `fieldValue(P)` once after both emissions are inserted (update loop processes the full emission queue before running field evaluations). Per C.3.3's round-3 server-side per-pass coalescing rule, **`TierCrossedEvent` fires at most once for that position-boundary pair within the pass, carrying the `emissionId` of the highest-cumulative position's most-contributing emission** (NOT "last in insertion order" — that contradicts C.3.3 and is round-4-corrected here). For two co-located simultaneous emissions, the highest-cumulative-position is the same point and both emissions contribute; the most-contributing `emissionId` is the one with the larger individual `contribution(P, S)` after both are inserted (ties broken by enumeration order — see D.6 dedup snap-method). For death attribution, both `emissionId`s remain candidates in `topContributingEmissionIds` (D.6) — both players can appear on the death screen. The `TierCrossedEvent` single-`emissionId` slot is used only for the predator's threat-awareness activation signal, not for death attribution; the death screen correctly credits both via the top-3 mechanism.

**E.15 — Squad aggregate `t` evaluated with zero connected players.** Reachable during server start (before any `PlayerAdded` fires), simultaneous-disconnect frames, and single-player respawn-gap frames. Per D.5's empty-player guard, `squadT = 0.0` is returned. No `math.max()` call is made. The 5 Hz update pass that fires during this window pushes `OnMeterUpdate{playerT=0.0, squadT=0.0}` to no clients (none are connected) — the push is a no-op. Field-value math, emission decay, and the live-source list operate independently of player count and are unaffected. Verified by H.22b.

**E.16 — Emission with NaN, Infinity, or non-finite `position` coordinates.** Server MUST reject any emission whose `position.X`, `position.Y`, or `position.Z` is NaN, +Infinity, or −Infinity. Validation runs as part of the schema check (C.1.2).

**NaN / Infinity detection idiom (REQUIRED — round-7a R7-B11 gameplay-programmer F6).** The implementation MUST use the following Luau idiom for finite-checking each coordinate axis (no shorthand or library call substitution; Luau's standard library does not expose a documented `isfinite` helper):

```luau
local function isFinite(n: number): boolean
    -- NaN is the unique value not equal to itself (IEEE-754).
    -- Both +math.huge and -math.huge are the only Luau number values
    -- whose absolute value equals math.huge. Combining the two checks
    -- rejects all three non-finite cases in a single expression.
    return n == n and n ~= math.huge and n ~= -math.huge
end
```

The schema validator MUST call `isFinite(position.X) and isFinite(position.Y) and isFinite(position.Z)` and reject the emission if any axis fails. The same idiom MUST be applied to `initialMagnitude` (a NaN magnitude would propagate identically to a NaN coordinate; an Infinity magnitude would bypass the `> 0` check at E.1 because `math.huge > 0 = true`). Reuse of `n == n and math.abs(n) ~= math.huge` is a permitted equivalent formulation (the explicit `~= math.huge` and `~= -math.huge` separated form is preferred for grep-greppability against a CI lint rule that detects this idiom — see H.3b round-7a addition).

A NaN propagating through `dist2D()` would corrupt `fieldValue` for every query position the source touches and would silently invalidate D.6 candidate scoring without producing an obvious error symptom. Rejection is a validation failure (same path as E.1 zero-magnitude rejection), not graceful handling — the offending caller is logged with `sourcePlayerId`, `emissionType`, and the offending coordinate, and the emission is not inserted. Verified by H.3b (round-7a expanded to assert the canonical idiom is used). Vector3 with finite-but-extreme coordinates (e.g., 1e30) is permitted — D.2's `INFLUENCE_RADIUS` cutoff makes such positions contribute zero anywhere a player can stand, so they are harmless if implausible.

**E.17 — Player joins before their character spawns; first `OnMeterUpdate` push.** When `Players.PlayerAdded` fires, the player exists but `CharacterAdded` may not have fired yet — `player.Character` is nil and the player has no world position. The server's first `OnMeterUpdate` push for this player (within ≤ 200 ms of join per C.3.4) sends `{playerT = 0.0, squadT = currentSquadT}`. Once `CharacterAdded` fires and the player has a position, subsequent 5 Hz pushes carry the position-based `playerT`. No error, no skipped push — `playerT = 0.0` is correct semantics ("the player is not in the field yet"). Verified by H.29c.

**E.18 — Chunk streams out on a client while emissions in that chunk are live.** Per C.1.10, server-side emissions are independent of any client's StreamingEnabled state. When a chunk streams out on a client, the client drops the chunk's flora from its local table; the server retains the emission live-source list for that chunk and continues to decay it. If the chunk streams back in on that client (or any client) before the emissions expire, the server's `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` push (C.3.5) carries the current decayed `t` for every flora in the chunk — there is no visual "missed window" gap. The predator can still target the unloaded chunk's hotspot via `GetHottestHotspot` because the server-side state is unaffected by client streaming. Verified by H.32b.

**E.19 — Configuration error: `INFLUENCE_RADIUS = 0` (or negative), or `SPATIAL_GRID_CELL_SIZE < INFLUENCE_RADIUS`.** A misconfigured `INFLUENCE_RADIUS = 0` would produce `1 − dist/0 = NaN` for sources at `dist2D = 0` and propagate NaN through `fieldValue`. A misconfigured `SPATIAL_GRID_CELL_SIZE < INFLUENCE_RADIUS` (G.7 invariant) breaks C.1.6's 3×3-cell guarantee — query cost grows beyond a 3×3 neighborhood and H.36's 2 ms/cycle budget can be silently violated under load. The disturbance system MUST validate at server startup (`DisturbanceService:KnitInit`): (a) `INFLUENCE_RADIUS > 0`; (b) `SPATIAL_GRID_CELL_SIZE >= INFLUENCE_RADIUS`. Failure of either check refuses the service to start with a hard error identifying the misconfigured constant. This is a defense against a tuning misconfiguration, not against runtime input — the constants are set in code, not by player input. Verified by H.3d (radius) and H.3e (grid invariant).

**E.21 — Player rapid-disconnect during death-attribution push (round-3 addition; AC added round-4).** A player dies, `Humanoid.Died` fires, the call chain `Player Controller (or HUD) → DisturbanceService:GetDeathAttributionPayload → OnDeathAttributionPushed:Fire(deadPlayer, payload)` begins. The dead player rapid-disconnects (rage-quit; mobile app killed) before the `:Fire` executes. Roblox: a targeted Knit `RemoteSignal:Fire(player, ...)` on a departed `Player` is a silent no-op (round-22 B2-class correction: this edge case previously named `:FireClient`, which is not a Knit RemoteSignal method per C.3.4's round-5 API note; the N3 ADR empirical-verification obligation covers the actual `:Fire` semantics) — no error, no retry. This is intentional behaviour; no retry guard or fallback is required. The dead player's session is ending regardless. The `attributionMayBeIncomplete` flag is **NOT** set for this case (delivery failure is distinct from data incompleteness — the payload was constructible). The server-side ops-log entry **MUST** record the attempted-but-unfired delivery (`sourcePlayerId`, `deathTimestamp`, contributing `emissionId` list) so the 95% MVP-gate ("≥ 95% of deaths surface a legible cause") remains observable from the ops sink: deaths-with-construction-but-no-delivery are counted, distinguished from deaths with no constructible payload (E.9 case), and reported separately so they are not conflated with the design-failure case. **Verified by H.32c.**

**E.20 — Replay / duplicate `emissionId`.** The server-side `Emit()` path generates `emissionId` itself (via `HttpService:GenerateGUID(false)`) and never accepts a caller-supplied ID. There is no client-callable Emit path (C.1.8) so an attacker cannot replay or supply duplicate IDs. Internally, GUID collision probability is effectively zero for session-scale emission counts (~144,000 IDs per 20-min 4-player session vs. 2^122 ID space). The system does NOT explicitly check for duplicate IDs — collision is treated as unreachable. If a future bug produces a duplicate ID (e.g., a mocked GUID generator in a test that returns a constant), behaviour is undefined and a debug-build assertion in the live-source insertion path is recommended (not part of MVP requirements).

**E.22 — Chunk stream-out before `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` delivery (round-5 / Tier-1 #11).** When the server fires `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` to a player and the chunk streams out on that player's client BEFORE packet delivery completes (mid-flight stream-out is plausible on mobile or under network jitter), the following invariants hold: (a) the per-player `loadedChunks` membership-gate is checked at the fire-time, so a post-fire stream-out is NOT retroactively applied — the snapshot was authorised at the moment it fired and the in-flight packet is allowed to land; (b) the server's per-flora last-pushed-t cache reset for that chunk MUST occur AFTER the `:Fire(player, ...)` call returns to the caller, NOT before packet delivery completes (Roblox does not expose a per-packet delivery acknowledgment to the server, so "wait for ack before resetting cache" is impossible); (c) on the client, if the snapshot's RemoteEvent handler fires for a chunk the client has already dropped from its local table, the handler silently no-ops on the affected flora (membership-set check on the client side); (d) on the next chunk stream-in for that player, the server's `loadedChunks` membership set adds the chunk fresh and a new `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` fires — recovery is bounded to **one snapshot cycle**. The intermediate state (server cache says-pushed but client hasn't applied) is acceptable because the next stream-in cycle re-pushes a fresh snapshot reflecting current `t` values. Persistent visual corruption is bounded to a single 5 Hz cycle gap on the immediately-following stream-in. **Verified by H.32d** (round-5 addition; see Section H Edge Case Verification block — recovery within one snapshot cycle after stream-out / stream-in).

**E.23 — Predator-registration with expired `emissionId`s on respawn / state transition (round-5 / Tier-1 #14; round-7a R7-B3 + R7-B7 split-channel + grace-window updates).** A predator may register a `RegisterPredatorLock(predatorId, newList)` and then respawn (server-reset path), transition out of Hunt / Investigate / Disengage, or re-register with a refreshed top-3 (cadence: fixed 4 Hz per G.6 / PA Core Rule 2 — round-22 I7 refresh) with prior registrations still held. Without explicit cleanup AND without the merge+grace semantics introduced in round-7a, the registered `emissionId`s would either (a) extend the C.1.11 attribution archive's TTL indefinitely (retention condition (c) without bounded grace), or (b) drop attribution completeness silently when re-registration replaced the prior list (round-7 ai-programmer F2 finding). Resolution: `RegisterPredatorLock` is **merge-on-re-register, not replace** (round-7a R7-B7) — the new list is unioned with the predator's prior list, the union is pruned by archive-resolution (unresolvable IDs are silently dropped at union time, NOT treated as retention holds), and prior IDs no longer present in the new list enter a `PREDATOR_LOCK_RELEASE_GRACE_WINDOW` (G.4; default 5.0 s) before being removed. This bounds archive growth (grace ≪ `ATTRIBUTION_ARCHIVE_TTL`) while preserving attribution completeness across legitimate top-3 refresh cadence. The 5 s grace is comfortably above the 0.25–1.0 s typical re-registration interval and comfortably below the seconds-to-minutes archive TTL.

Additionally, `DisturbanceService` MUST listen for the predator's lifecycle events (predator respawn, transition out of Hunt / Investigate / Disengage) and **purge** all registry entries the predator had locked — including those still inside the release-grace window — at the moment of the transition. The predator's authoritative state machine lives in the Predator AI service — `DisturbanceService` exposes a server-internal `:ReleasePredatorLock(predatorId)` method that Predator AI calls on every state-out-of-Hunt-Investigate-Disengage transition. **Split-channel architecture (round-7a R7-B3 / CD adjudication D3):** `OnPredatorStateChanged` is the **server-internal BindableEvent** (owned by Predator AI service; payload `{predatorId, fromState, toState, timestamp}`) that DisturbanceService subscribes to during `KnitInit` for the defense-in-depth purge — it is the cross-service event channel and is never replicated to clients. `OnPredatorLockChanged` is a separate **client-facing Knit RemoteSignal** (also owned by Predator AI; F.2a row 3) that fires the per-player attribution payload to the targeted client for HUD consumption — DisturbanceService does NOT subscribe to it. The split is binding because Knit RemoteSignals fire OVER THE NETWORK to clients (Knit's intent), so a server-side subscriber on a Knit RemoteSignal cannot reliably serve as a cross-service server-event listener — round-5's pre-split design assumed it could, which silently broke the defense-in-depth purge (4-specialist convergence in round-7). `:ReleasePredatorLock` is the explicit server-side cleanup path (server-internal only — never exposed via `DisturbanceService.Client`); the BindableEvent purge is the defense-in-depth fallback when Predator AI omits the explicit call. **Round-9 DR-3 = Option B amendment (fire-ownership move):** `:ReleasePredatorLock` now also owns the `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(targetedPlayer, {locked = false, ...})` invocation as part of its body's yield-free sub-steps (4a)–(4c) per C.1.11 step 4. Both the explicit-call path (Predator AI invokes `:ReleasePredatorLock` directly) AND the BindableEvent-fallback path (DisturbanceService's `OnPredatorStateChanged` subscriber invokes `:ReleasePredatorLock` itself on observed state-exit) therefore guarantee the during-purge fire ordering — the registry remains the single source of truth for which `targetedPlayer`s receive `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(false)`, and Predator AI is no longer required to read or shadow that set. This eliminates the cross-service Knit-encapsulation hole that round-7c's wording implicitly required.

**Same-frame Fire(true)+Fire(false) ordering (round-13 I9 fold-in + cross-section consistency I-CS-1 closure — CI lint obligation, explicitly deferred forward).** A pathological code path in Predator AI could invoke `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(player, {locked = true, ...})` and `:ReleasePredatorLock(predatorId)` (which fires `:Fire(player, {locked = false, ...})` for the same player as part of sub-step 4b) within the same Luau frame — producing a same-frame `:Fire(true)` then `:Fire(false)` sequence on the same `targetedPlayer`. The HUD subscriber on the client side receives the two fires in network-arrival order (Knit RemoteSignal preserves invocation order on the wire), but if the implementation produces them within the same field-update pass, the client-side HUD's lock-indicator may flicker (lit-on-then-off) within a single render frame — a visible UI defect. **Round-13 IMPORTANT forward obligation status:** the H.39h CI lint AC is **explicitly deferred to a post-round-14 patch session** — round-13 records the obligation here AND in the R13-I cluster forward obligation list (see F.2a-IMPORTANT round-13 cluster) so round-14 reviewers applying the protocol check (c) "every normative MUST in prose has a falsifiable verification path in the ACs" will treat the gap as a tracked-and-acknowledged deferral rather than an authoring defect. The MUST in this paragraph (Predator AI MUST space the two fires across separate frames) is the prose normative claim; the falsifiable verification path is the H.39h CI lint that the qa-lead is mandated to author in the next patch session — until H.39h lands, the same-frame ordering is implementer-discipline-honor-system, and round-14 verdict review acknowledges this is a documented forward obligation. The H.39h lint's PASS criterion (when authored): zero matches in `src/server/services/PredatorAI/**/*.luau` for any code path where `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire` and `:ReleasePredatorLock` are invoked within the same lexical scope (same function body, no intervening `task.wait` or Heartbeat boundary); the lint can land alongside H.39e / H.39f as a CI-mechanical grep gate. The Predator AI GDD MUST author its state-machine transitions to space the two fires across separate frames (e.g., a state-into-Hunt transition fires `Fire(true)`; a subsequent same-target state-out-of-Hunt transition — itself an N+M frames later event under the predator's 4 Hz cadence (PA Core Rule 2; round-22 I7 refresh) — fires `:ReleasePredatorLock`'s `Fire(false)`).

**Round-11 B-R10-1c — same-frame Fire(true)+Fire(false) collision ordering contract on the BindableEvent fallback path.** A pathological case admitted by the round-9 split-channel + DR-3 = Option B architecture: in a single Luau frame, Predator AI may transition from Hunt-targeting-player-A to Hunt-targeting-player-B (e.g., the squad pivots and a new player becomes the highest-contribution target). Predator AI's state machine fires `OnPredatorStateChanged` (Hunt → Hunt-with-new-target — though most state-transition models would represent this as `Hunt-out then Hunt-in`, the BindableEvent firing semantics depend on Predator AI's internal model). DisturbanceService's `OnPredatorStateChanged:Connect` callback observes the transition and invokes `:ReleasePredatorLock(predatorId)` for the prior lock-set, which fires `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(playerA, {locked = false, ...})`. Predator AI's own state-into-Hunt-with-new-target code path fires `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(playerB, {locked = true, ...})`. Both fires are queued for delivery to clients in the SAME Luau frame — but Roblox `RemoteSignal:Fire` does not guarantee per-target ordering across distinct fire invocations within a single frame, AND the client-side HUD may receive Fire(playerB, true) BEFORE Fire(playerA, false), producing an HUD legibility defect (player A's HUD briefly shows "still locked" after the predator has actually pivoted to player B). **Resolution — explicit ordering contract:** when Predator AI's state machine transitions trigger BOTH a (false) clear (via `OnPredatorStateChanged` → DisturbanceService's `:ReleasePredatorLock` → sub-step (4b)) AND a (true) acquire (Predator AI's own state-into-Hunt-with-new-target fires `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(targetedPlayer, {locked = true, ...})`) within the same Luau frame, **Predator AI MUST sequence the (false) clear BEFORE the (true) acquire**. The contract is enforceable because BindableEvent `:Fire` is synchronous on the server: Predator AI's state-machine code path raises `OnPredatorStateChanged:Fire(...)` first; control transfers synchronously to DisturbanceService's subscriber callback; DisturbanceService invokes `:ReleasePredatorLock(predatorId)` which executes sub-steps (4a)–(4c) — including the `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(targetedPlayer, {locked = false, ...})` per-target fire loop — and returns control synchronously to Predator AI; Predator AI THEN invokes `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(newTargetedPlayer, {locked = true, ...})` for the new target. The (false) RemoteSignal fires are queued for delivery to clients before the (true) fire is queued, so client-side per-player ordering on the underlying RemoteEvent matches the design intent. **The BindableEvent fan-out + sub-step (4b) per-target loop's yield-free guarantee (round-11 step 4 sub-step (4b) sync-callback prohibition) is what makes this ordering contract enforceable** — without yield-free sub-step (4b), an interleaved Heartbeat boundary inside the for-loop could allow the (true) fire to slip in before all (false) fires have been queued. Predator AI's GDD MUST cite this ordering contract as a binding obligation on its own state-machine code path; DisturbanceService's role is to provide the synchronous BindableEvent fan-out that makes the contract structurally enforceable. **Verified by H.30c** (round-5 addition; round-7a updated to test merge-on-re-register + grace window + BindableEvent purge; round-9 updated to test the during-purge fire ordering, KnitInit/KnitStart connection-order determinism, and concurrent task.spawn invariant — sub-ACs (e)–(i); round-11 sub-AC (j) added to verify the same-frame Fire(true)+Fire(false) ordering contract under the BindableEvent fallback path; see Section H).

**E.24 — `_injectSyntheticEmission` minimum-invariant validation (round-5 / Tier-1 #12; round-7a R7-B12 upper-bound tightened from 1.05 to 1.00).** The test-only synthetic-injection seam (see H.39c, H.38b, Test Infrastructure Prerequisites) is documented as bypassing schema validation, Q9 stationary attenuation, and the cap-eviction policy — but it MUST still enforce a minimum-invariant set on its payload to keep the rest of the engine sound: (a) `position.X / Y / Z` finite (no NaN, +∞, −∞ — uses the same canonical idiom E.16 enforces on `Emit()`: `n == n and n ~= math.huge and n ~= -math.huge`); (b) `initialMagnitude` in the range `(0, 1.00]` — round-7a tightening: the round-5 upper bound was 1.05 to allow Beacon ceiling at 0.95 plus a 0.10 cushion, but round-7 systems-designer F6 identified that values in (1.00, 1.05] admit illegitimate magnitudes corrupting the cap-formula tests at H.38b (any individual source's contribution should not exceed `RETREAT_THRESHOLD = 1.00`, the field-value clamp ceiling, because the test fixture is constructing degenerate-clustered conditions and a >1.0 single-source value breaks the cap formula's `[0.0, 1.0]` clamp invariant). The new (0, 1.00] range still admits Beacon at 0.95 and provides 0.05 cushion to the Retreat ceiling; values in (1.00, 1.05] now reject. The seam preserves the legitimate test fixture's ability to construct boundary-condition payloads while closing the test-fixture-bug-corrupting-clamp-logic risk; (c) `emissionType` in the canonical enum `{"Gather", "Sprint", "Light", "Beacon", "Craft"}` (extended round-7a — Crafting GDD added `Craft` 2026-04-30; the canonical enum is owned by C.1.2 and the seam mirrors it). Violations of these invariants MUST cause the seam to raise a hard error (test failure). The seam's specific bypass of duplicate-`emissionId` checks, Q9 attenuation, and cap-eviction is explicitly out-of-scope by design — H.38b depends on those bypasses to fill a single grid cell to 200+ sources. Verified by H.31c (round-7a expanded to require three independent invocations — one per invariant — to prevent a plural-but-not-three escape).

**E.25 — Beacon stationary-squad cap edge cases (round-5 / Tier-1 #10 — Option A; round-7a R7-B4 + R7-B5 + R7-B6 updates).** The C.3.6 Beacon stationary-squad Hunt-floor cap rule has the following edge cases:
- **Zero connected players (`connected == 0`).** The cap rule does NOT apply — the strict `nearby / connected > 0.50` check at step 3 is undefined for a 0-divisor and the implementation MUST short-circuit to "cap not engaged" when `connected == 0` (parallel to D.5's empty-player guard). Verified by H.41a (round-5 addition).
- **Solo session (`connected == 1`, `nearby == 1`).** Step 3 evaluates `1 / 1 > 0.50 = true`; step 5 evaluates `stationary_nearby / nearby > 0.50` — if the single player is stationary, `stationary_nearby = 1`, ratio `1/1 > 0.50 = true`, cap engages.
