This is intentional: solo-active-defender play is the expected gameplay loop and the cap forces the player to move (run, flank) rather than stand at the beacon. Verified by H.41b.
- **Solo-with-under-filled-buffer (round-9 B-NS2 closure; `connected == 1`, `nearby == 1`, ring buffer has < 15 samples).** During the first 3.0 s after a solo player's join (or after their buffer is reset by the teleport-discontinuity rule above, or after server start), the player's ring buffer has fewer than `floor(BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ) = 15` samples and the C.3.6 step 4 round-7c B-7b-4 buffer-fill precondition treats the player as **moving** (per-pass `cumulativeXZ` for that player is `+∞`). Consequently `stationary_nearby = 0`, ratio `0/1 = 0 NOT > 0.50`, and the cap does NOT engage during this 3.0 s window even if the player is in fact perfectly stationary. **This is accepted-as-designed and not a defect:** the cumulative-distance integration is a statistical signal that requires a window's worth of samples to produce an exploit-resistant verdict (the shuffle-game exploit closure round-7a R7-B6 introduced is the load-bearing reason for the 3.0 s window). Allowing the cap to engage with an under-filled buffer would re-open the shuffle-game exploit AND would falsely classify a moving newcomer as stationary on the very first pass after join. The 3.0 s degradation is therefore the design's chosen trade-off. Player-facing consequence: a solo player who places a beacon and stands still on activation will see ~3.0 s of full-Retreat predator behaviour before the cap kicks in to clamp the beacon to Hunt-floor; this aligns with the design-intended "beacon commits to attack run for ~49 s" narrative anchor (D.7 Beacon active-defender case) and is the natural bootstrap window for the cap mechanic. The HUD GDD's three-state cap-cue (F.2a row 7) MUST handle the under-filled-buffer pre-cap-engagement window by displaying NO cap-cue (the cap is genuinely not engaged); the cap-cue State A appears only after the buffer fills and the cap actually engages. Verified by H.41n (round-9 addition; see Section H Beacon Stationary-Squad Cap).
- **Active-defender 2-of-4 case (round-7a R7-B4 / D1 — REGRESSION-CLOSURE EXAMPLE).** Squad-of-4 with all 4 within `BEACON_STATIONARY_PROXIMITY_RADIUS` of an active Beacon (so `nearby = 4, connected = 4, nearby/connected = 1.0 > 0.50`) and 2 of those 4 stationary while 2 actively flank/move (so `stationary_nearby = 2`). Step 5 with the round-7a denominator-fix evaluates `stationary_nearby / nearby = 2/4 = 0.50, NOT > 0.50` — cap does **NOT** engage; the active-defender play passes the gate. Under the round-6 mistaken `>=` operator OR the pre-round-7a `connected` denominator (`2/4 = 0.50`), the cap falsely engaged on this legitimate active-defender play; both defects are now closed. Verified by H.41g (round-7a addition).
- **Player teleports out of beacon proximity mid-window.** XZ-position-history ring buffer entries reflect the teleported player's pre-teleport position for `BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW` seconds after teleport. To prevent the teleported player from counting as "stationary" based on stale-sample-derived cumulative-distance after teleport, the implementation MUST mark the position-history ring buffer as **discontinuous** on any per-frame XZ-position delta exceeding `2 × BEACON_CUMULATIVE_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD` (default `2 × 6 = 12 studs in one 5 Hz pass = 60 studs/sec — clearly not natural movement on the iPhone SE walk-speed-class budget`; round-7a R7-B6 reformulation — the round-5 design used `4 × BEACON_STATIONARY_MOVE_DELTA = 16 studs`, which had no principled relation to the new cumulative-distance threshold; the new formulation is anchored to `2 ×` the threshold so the discontinuity heuristic scales with the threshold). On the next sample after a discontinuity, the player's stationary-status resets to "moving" (the per-pass `cumulativeXZ` for that player is treated as `+∞` for cap-engagement purposes) until the ring buffer fills with `BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW` seconds of post-teleport samples. Verified by H.41c.
- **Player disconnects mid-window.** The disconnected player is removed from `Players:GetPlayers()` and from the `nearby` count on the next field-update pass. The cap re-evaluates immediately. No retention of disconnected-player history beyond the Players: list change.
- **Beacon expires while cap is engaged.** Cap clears immediately on beacon expiry — there is no beacon to scale anymore. Field at the (former) beacon position decays naturally per the remaining live sources.
- **Beacon decayed near floor while cap engaged (round-7a R7-B5 sub-mode (iii) / network-programmer F6 — denominator-zero protection).** As the beacon's `m(age)` approaches `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR`, its native contribution at its own position approaches the floor and the `scaleFactor` formula's denominator approaches 0, threatening `0/0 = NaN`. The implementation MUST short-circuit: if `beacon_native_contribution_at_B < MAGNITUDE_FLOOR × 1.5 = 0.03`, the cap rule does NOT apply for this beacon — the beacon will be culled within one maintenance pass anyway. NaN propagation into `fieldValue` is prevented. Verified by H.41f (round-7a addition).
- **Ambient exceeds cap mid-window (round-7a R7-B5 sub-mode (ii) / systems-designer F3 — `non_beacon > BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE`).** Squad activates a beacon, then heavy ambient activity (e.g., a triggered gather sequence) pushes `non_beacon_contribution_at_B` to 0.70 (above `BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE = 0.65`). The formula `scaleFactor = (0.65 - 0.70) / 0.95 = -0.053` clamps to `0.0` per C.3.6 step 6, so the beacon's contribution becomes 0; total fieldValue at beacon position is `0.70 + 0 = 0.70`, **above the cap due to ambient alone**. This is correct behaviour, not a defect — the cap is a beacon-attenuation mechanism, not a global field-value clamp. Predator AI escalates to Hunt on the basis of ambient + 0 beacon contribution, which is the same response as if no beacon were present at high-ambient. Verified by H.41h (round-7a addition).
- **Two simultaneous beacons.** The cap rule applies independently per beacon. Both beacons may be capped simultaneously if the squad is stationary near both (unusual; would require the squad to straddle a 16-stud-radius cluster around two beacons). The combined `fieldValue` at any P contains each beacon's separately-capped contribution. **Order-dependence note (round-7 game-designer F9):** the per-beacon `non_beacon_contribution_at_B` calculation MUST NOT include the OTHER beacon's contribution at the cap-evaluation moment — otherwise the cap-engagement evaluation order influences the result (beacon A capped → beacon B sees lower ambient → beacon B may not cap → beacon A re-evaluates with higher ambient → ...). Implementation: compute `non_beacon_contribution_at_B(beacon_i)` as the field value at `beacon_i.position` summed over ALL live sources EXCEPT all live Beacon emissions (beacons are excluded from each other's ambient). Cap-engagement is then independent per beacon and order-free. Verified by H.41i (round-7a addition).

## Dependencies

### F.1 — Hard Dependencies (this system depends on)

**None.** This system is the Foundation Layer's central event bus + spatial signal field. It depends on no MVP-internal systems. Per `design/gdd/systems-index.md`:

> "Ecological Disturbance — pure event bus + signal definitions. Other systems publish events to and subscribe from it. Dependency-free."

### F.2 — Hard Dependents (systems that depend on this one)

| Dependent System | Direction | Interface | Owned by | Update cadence |
|------------------|-----------|-----------|----------|----------------|
| **Resource Node** | Publisher | `DisturbanceService:Emit("Gather", position, magnitude, sourcePlayerId)` (C.3.1) | Resource Node sets magnitude per node type | Per-gather completion |
| **Player Controller** | Publisher + Subscriber | Publishes `DisturbanceService:Emit("Sprint"\|"Light", position, magnitude, sourcePlayerId)` (C.3.2); **also subscribes (Session C) to the server-internal `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed(direction, newTier, timestamp)` squad-aggregate signal (C.3.3) for its world-response cue** | Player Controller owns state-transition detection + interval timer; ED owns the band-crossing producer | 2 s while sprinting; 4 s while lantern raised; band-crossing fires per field-update pass on a squad-tier change |
| **Predator AI** | Subscriber + Querier | Subscribes `TierCrossedEvent` (server-internal Knit signal); calls `GetHottestHotspot(callerPosition, searchRadius, minimumTier)` (C.3.3) supplying its own current world-space position as `callerPosition` (round-17 I16-11 — F.1 preservation; DisturbanceService never pulls Predator AI position via Knit.GetService) | Predator AI drives query cadence | Fixed 4 Hz decision tick (PA Core Rule 2; round-22 I7 refresh of the concept doc's 1–4 Hz estimate) |
| **HUD** | Subscriber | RemoteSignals `OnMeterUpdate` (5 Hz, FireClient-per-player), `OnDisturbanceAlert` (event-driven), `OnDeathAttributionPushed` (FireClient-to-dead-player-only on death) — see C.3.4 | HUD owns display; this system owns the data | 5 Hz meter pushes; event-driven for alerts; on-death for attribution |
| **Bioluminescent Flora** (rendering subsystem) | Subscriber (passive) | Receives `FloraChunkUpdate` raw RemoteEvent (steady-state) and `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` raw RemoteEvent (server-pushed on chunk stream-in) — see C.3.5 | Flora rendering owns the visual tween; this system pushes `targetT` only | 5 Hz pushes (chunk-batched, throttled by `FLORA_UPDATE_DELTA_THRESHOLD`); plus one initial snapshot per chunk-streamed-in event |
| **Crafting & Items** (Beacon emission only) | Publisher (partially TBD) | `DisturbanceService:Emit("Beacon", position, magnitude, sourcePlayerId)` (C.3.6) | Crafting & Items GDD owns Beacon trigger conditions + magnitude (TBD pending Crafting GDD) | One emission at activation |

**Resource Management BCT-accessor disclaimer (Session C, 2026-06-19 — reconciliation finding).** Resource Management reverse-cites a "BCT accessor" (its oxygen-drain bands BC1–BC4) and, in its own header, flagged it to "confirm with ED (Session B) + the Beacon charge model." **ED does NOT own this accessor.** RM's BC1–BC4 are **Beacon-Charge** bands tied to the Crafting beacon lifecycle (BCT1→BCT4), NOT ED's disturbance tiers (Calm/Tense/Hunt/Retreat) — ED tracks no beacon-charge state. The accessor RM needs (a current-BCT read on each Heartbeat to select its drain rate) is owned by **Crafting & Items**, which knows the active beacon-charge tier. ED's only beacon-related surface is accepting the `Emit("Beacon", …)` call + maintaining the resulting field (C.3.6). **Action:** RM's forward-pending ED row should be re-pointed ED → Crafting as the BCT-accessor owner; no ED authoring is owed. (Reconciliation finding only — does NOT reopen the APPROVED RM GDD; it resolves RM's own "confirm with ED" flag in the negative.)


### F.2a / F.2a-IMPORTANT / F.2b — Forward Obligations (relocated 2026-05-30)

> Relocated to **`ecological-disturbance-forward-obligations.md`** (decomposition by content type — relocation only). That file holds **F.2a** (Predator AI Deferred Contracts), **F.2a-IMPORTANT** (round-7a IMPORTANT cluster incl. N1–N9 ADR obligations + T-series), and **F.2b** (Forward-Obligations Registry). Internal cites elsewhere in this GDD to `F.2a` / `F.2b` resolve to that file.

### F.3 — Bidirectional Consistency Expectations

When the dependent GDDs are authored, each MUST list this system as a dependency in their own Section F. Specifically:

- **Resource Node GDD** must declare: "Publishes Gather emissions to Ecological Disturbance per ED.C.3.1"
- **Player Controller GDD** must declare: "Publishes Sprint and Light emissions to Ecological Disturbance per ED.C.3.2"
- **Predator AI GDD** must declare: "Subscribes to TierCrossedEvent and calls GetHottestHotspot per ED.C.3.3; stores top-3 contributing emissionIds for death attribution per ED.C.1.11"
- **HUD GDD** must declare: "Subscribes to OnDisturbanceAlert (active + passive sub-types) per ED.C.3.4; renders DeathAttributionPayload per ED.C.1.11"
- **Crafting & Items GDD** must declare: "Publishes Beacon emission with reserved magnitude range [0.85, 1.0] per ED.C.3.6"

**Cross-GDD value lock**: the tier boundaries `0.30 / 0.65 / 1.00`, hysteresis `±0.03`, half-lives `30 s / 90 s`, `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR = 0.02`, and `INFLUENCE_RADIUS = 24 studs` are owned by this GDD. Any dependent GDD that references these values must cite this GDD as the source of truth and must not redefine them.

### F.3a — Cross-GDD Author Checklist (round-3 addition)

When authoring a dependent GDD (Resource Node, Player Controller, Predator AI, HUD, Crafting & Items, Bioluminescent Flora rendering subsystem), the author MUST paste a copy of the relevant block below into their own Section F. This makes the bidirectional-consistency obligation explicit and prevents drift across five future GDDs.

```markdown
## Section F dependency — Ecological Disturbance (paste/adapt as appropriate)

This system depends on Ecological Disturbance per ED.[applicable C.3.x section].

- **Cross-GDD value lock cited**: I do NOT redefine TENSE_THRESHOLD, HUNT_THRESHOLD,
  RETREAT_THRESHOLD, HYSTERESIS_BAND, STANDARD_HALF_LIFE, BEACON_HALF_LIFE,
  MAGNITUDE_FLOOR, or INFLUENCE_RADIUS. ED is the source of truth.
- **Publishing path** (if this system emits): I publish via DisturbanceService:Emit(...)
  per ED.C.3.[1|2|6]. I do NOT bypass and write to the spatial grid directly. Magnitude
  per emission type is owned by [this GDD or ED.G.5].
- **Subscribing path** (if this system consumes): I subscribe via [TierCrossedEvent /
  RemoteSignal name] per ED.C.3.[3|4|5]. I do NOT request emission data via any
  client-callable RemoteFunction (this system MUST honour ED's zero-RemoteFunction-on-Client
  rule per ED.F.4).
- **Predator AI only — F.2a obligations resolved**: [list each row 1-5 from ED.F.2a
  with this GDD's resolution].
```

The DisturbanceService ADR also reverse-cites this GDD: it MUST specify the spatial-grid data structure (Roblox Region3 vs plain table-of-tables), the cap-eviction policy (Q3), the StreamingEnabled chunk-load detection API choice, and the test-fixture shapes (MockRemoteSignalRecorder, MockTweenService, `_setExpFn`).

### F.4 — Hard Dependencies on Engine

| Dependency | Required for | Notes |
|------------|--------------|-------|
| **Roblox `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`** | Monotonic server-time source for `emissionTime` and decay age (D.1) | `os.time()`, `tick()`, and `os.clock()` are forbidden — see D.1 clock specification (`os.clock()` is not formally guaranteed wall-clock-monotonic on Roblox game servers) |
| **Roblox `RunService.Heartbeat`** | Driving the 5 Hz update loop with manual time-accumulator (C.1.13) | `task.wait` loops are NOT used — they drift under load. Catch-up is graceful skip per C.1.13. |
| **Roblox `RemoteEvent` (raw)** | Server→client `FloraChunkUpdate` (steady-state chunk-batched flora targetT broadcast) and `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` (server-pushed initial snapshot on chunk stream-in) | Raw `RemoteEvent` for chunk-scoped broadcast efficiency; not routed through Knit RemoteSignal layer |
| **Knit framework + RemoteSignal** | `DisturbanceService` (server) + `DisturbanceController` (client). Three named `Service.Client` RemoteSignals declared via `Knit.CreateSignal()`: `OnMeterUpdate` (FireClient-per-player at 5 Hz, see C.3.4), `OnDisturbanceAlert` (FireClient-to-triggering-player on tier crossings, see C.3.4), `OnDeathAttributionPushed` (FireClient-to-dead-player-only on death, see C.3.4 + C.1.11). Payload Luau types in C.3.4. | Per `.claude/docs/technical-preferences.md`. All client-bound signal events go through Knit RemoteSignal for unified rate-limit + audit surface; the only raw RemoteEvent exceptions are `FloraChunkUpdate` and `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` (above) for chunk-broadcast efficiency. |
| **Roblox `StreamingEnabled`** + per-player chunk-load events | Server detects when a chunk newly enters a player's loaded region in order to push `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` (C.3.5). **Round-4 P0 mitigation (Option A): the server's own StreamingEnabled chunk-load events are the sole authority for the per-player `loadedChunks` membership set; `Player.ReplicationFocus` is NEVER read or watched by `DisturbanceService` (it is client-writable and therefore untrustworthy as a snapshot trigger).** | Emissions persist server-side independent of chunk lifecycle (C.1.10). Server-tracked membership is the security boundary for the snapshot path. |
| **Roblox `Humanoid.Died` subscription chain** (round-13 R11-I6 fold-in; round-11 DR-4 = Option B subscription monopoly) | DisturbanceService is the **sole** subscriber to `Humanoid.Died` in the entire server codebase per F.6 round-11 amendment. The subscription chain (`Players.PlayerAdded` → `Player.CharacterAdded` → `humanoid.Died`) is registered exclusively in `DisturbanceService:KnitInit` per C.1.11 step 5 and the (5-LC-a) per-respawn re-subscription rule. Server-internal only — no client surface. | Enforced by H.39e CI Lint (a) — single-subscriber regression rejected at PR-time. Cross-GDD revision mandates per F.6 (PC GDD T5, Crafting GDD C.8). |
| **Roblox `BindableEvent` (server-internal)** for `OnPlayerDied` fan-out (round-13 R11-I6 fold-in; round-13 I8 + I3 + I10 fold-in) | DisturbanceService declares a server-internal `OnPlayerDied` BindableEvent during `KnitInit`; subscribed services acquire the reference via `DisturbanceService:GetOnPlayerDiedSignal()` in their KnitStart. Synchronous fan-out at C.1.11 sub-step (5c). NOT exposed via `DisturbanceService.Client`; NOT replicated to clients. Payload schema `OnPlayerDiedPayload` defined in C.1.11 step 5. **Load-bearing platform assumption (round-13 I8 fold-in):** Roblox `BindableEvent:Fire(payload)` synchronous-callback semantics are load-bearing for the C.1.11 step 5 ordering contract — when `:Fire(payload)` is invoked, all `:Connect`-registered callback bodies execute synchronously WITHIN the `:Fire` call's lexical scope before `:Fire` returns control to its caller. This guarantee replaces the prior round-9 `Humanoid.Died:Connect` ordering risk class (which depended on undocumented Roblox `:Connect` registration-order semantics) with a documented Roblox `BindableEvent:Fire` synchronous-fan-out semantic. If a future Roblox release weakens the `BindableEvent:Fire` synchronous semantics, the C.1.11 step 5 ordering contract MUST be re-verified — the DevOps Engineer + N3 forward obligation captures this verification. **`GetOnPlayerDiedSignal()` return-value contract (round-13 I3 + I10 fold-in; round-13 cross-section consistency I-CS-2 closure — Luau type signature is the mechanical disambiguator):** the accessor's Luau type signature is `DisturbanceService:GetOnPlayerDiedSignal(): BindableEvent` (return type `BindableEvent`, NOT `RemoteEvent` — the Luau `--!strict` type system enforces this distinction at compile time; any implementation that returns a `RemoteEvent` instance fails the type check and refuses to compile). The accessor returns the raw `BindableEvent` instance that DisturbanceService owns (NOT a wrapper). Subscribers receive the same `BindableEvent` instance across all calls within a server session (identity guarantee — `DisturbanceService:GetOnPlayerDiedSignal() == DisturbanceService:GetOnPlayerDiedSignal()` always holds within a session; round-13 I10 closure). The PlayerControllerService's client-facing `OnPlayerDied` is a `RemoteEvent` (per PC GDD); the namespace + type-system distinction makes confusion mechanically rejectable, not just prose-prohibited. Subscribers MUST NOT invoke `:Fire(...)` on the returned BindableEvent — only DisturbanceService's `Humanoid.Died` handler is the legitimate fire-site (per C.1.11 step 5's (5c) sub-step). The `:Fire` invocation surface is exposed to subscribers because `BindableEvent` does not split fire-vs-subscribe interfaces in Roblox's API; subscriber-side `:Fire` invocations are an exploit class against the C.1.11 ordering contract. **Round-13 I3 closure: forward obligation on the qa-lead to author H.39g (or equivalent) — a CI grep lint that rejects any `:Fire(` invocation chained on a reference returned from `:GetOnPlayerDiedSignal()` in any service body other than `DisturbanceService:KnitInit(`'s body**. Until H.39g lands, the `:Fire`-invocation prohibition is honor-system among subscribing services (acceptable risk for MVP because all subscribers are first-party Knit services authored under code review; lint lands before any third-party Roblox-Marketplace code path can subscribe). | Distinct from PlayerControllerService's client-facing `OnPlayerDied` RemoteEvent (different namespace, different payload, different fire-site — see F.2a row 3 round-13 naming-collision note). H.39e Lint (b) enforces yield-free callback bodies on subscribers. |
| **Module-private cross-service push-registration accessors** (round-13 R12-B3 introduction; round-15 R14-B4 architectural relocation — supersedes the round-13 method-on-Service-internal-table form) | A separate handshake module at `src/server/services/DisturbanceService/RegistrationHandshake.luau` (binding path; see H.39c (c)) declares two module-local registration accessors that Predator AI invokes during PredatorAI's `KnitStart`: `RegisterPredatorLockChangedSignal(signal: RemoteSignal): ()` (push-injects PredatorAI's `OnPredatorLockChanged` Knit RemoteSignal handle into the handshake module's slot so DisturbanceService's sub-step (4b) `:Fire(...)` invocations can dereference it without a `Knit.GetService("PredatorAI")` pull) AND `RegisterPredatorStateChangedSignal(bindable: BindableEvent): ()` (symmetric push-injection for the server-internal `OnPredatorStateChanged` BindableEvent — the `:Connect` to the BindableEvent happens INSIDE this accessor's body via the round-15 R14-B2 register-time-subscribe pattern, NOT in DisturbanceService:KnitStart; see F.2a row 3 round-15 update for the ordering rationale). The accessors are **NOT methods on `DisturbanceService` and NOT methods on `DisturbanceService.Client`** — they are module-locals exported via `require()`. Predator AI imports the handshake module via `local handshake = require(<path>)` during its own `KnitStart` and invokes the two accessors through the imported reference. DisturbanceService imports the same handshake module during its `KnitStart` and (a) reads the registered handles via two read-side getters `getOnPredatorLockChangedSignal(): RemoteSignal?` and `getOnPredatorStateChangedBindable(): BindableEvent?` (returning `nil` if not yet registered; the State-channel getter's canonical name is `getOnPredatorStateChangedBindable` per F.2a row 3 + H.39c (b-iii) canonical 5-export surface — round-17 B16-2 closure of round-16 cross-section name drift; the prior round-15 form has been removed from this row entirely), AND (b) writes its own state-change handler into a slot via `setOnPredatorStateChangedHandler(handler: (payload) -> ())` so the register-time `:Connect` inside `RegisterPredatorStateChangedSignal` can register the handler the moment Predator AI invokes the registration accessor. **DisturbanceService's KnitStart invocation MUST follow the binding ordering rule at F.2a row 3 step 4 — getters (4-step-a, 4-step-b) executed lexically before handler-setter (4-step-c); H.39c (b-vi) grep-verifies this ordering at PR time** (round-17 B16-4 + B-CS-R17-2 closure — F.4 row cross-reference to the binding ordering rule, eliminating the round-17 cross-section gap surfaced by the qa-lead consistency subagent). Both registration accessors are idempotent (re-registration with the same handle is a no-op; with a different handle replaces the prior and re-registers any pending `:Connect`). | Resolves F.2a row 3 round-15 R14-B4 architectural commitment for module-private accessor placement (closes the round-13 R11-I5-class local-alias-injection threat structurally — there is no Service.Client surface to alias-back-to). F.1 "no MVP-internal hard dependencies" preserved (DisturbanceService never calls `Knit.GetService("PredatorAI")` and vice versa; the handshake module is the single shared dependency, imported via `require()` by both). H.39c (b)+(c) grep-verifies the non-Service / non-Service.Client placement AND the binding path AND the minimal-surface export rule (round-15 R14-B4 expansion of round-13 R12-B3 grep coverage). The Predator AI GDD MUST author the corresponding KnitStart-side `local handshake = require(...); handshake.RegisterPredatorLockChangedSignal(self.Client.OnPredatorLockChanged); handshake.RegisterPredatorStateChangedSignal(self._onPredatorStateChanged)` invocations. |

**GDD-level safeguard for chunk-load detection (round-5 / Tier-1 #9).** Roblox does not expose a documented clean server-side-only "chunk loaded for player X" event. The DisturbanceService implementation MUST use a server-only chunk-tracking mechanism for the `loadedChunks` membership set. **If the DisturbanceService ADR cannot identify a Roblox API that populates `loadedChunks` without reading any client-writable property** (including but not limited to `Player.ReplicationFocus`, `Player.Character.PrimaryPart.Position` polling, any `Player:GetPropertyChangedSignal` subscription on a client-influenceable property, or any `RemoteEvent` / `RemoteFunction` / `BindableEvent` driven by the client), **the design decision MUST be raised back to creative-director before sprint entry. The implementation epic for this GDD is BLOCKED until the ADR confirms a safe API.** This is a normative GDD-level requirement, not just an ADR action item — the safeguard is load-bearing for the round-4 P0 mitigation, and silently picking an unsafe API would re-introduce the chunk-enumeration / cross-map data-leak exploit that the round-2 redesign was specifically intended to eliminate. Verified by H.31b's expanded grep coverage (below).

**RemoteFunctions intentionally absent from the client surface.** This system exposes **zero** client-callable RemoteFunctions. The round-1 `GetCurrentMeterValue` was removed (server-pushes `OnMeterUpdate` on `PlayerAdded` instead), and the round-2 `GetChunkFieldSnapshot` is removed (server-pushes `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` on chunk stream-in instead). This is a deliberate exploit-surface reduction: clients cannot initiate any disturbance-related server work. Combined with C.1.8's no-client-Emit rule, the client's surface to the disturbance system is **read-only via server-pushed signals**. Adding any client-callable RemoteFunction or method to `DisturbanceService.Client` is a regression that reviewers MUST reject.

**Raw-RemoteEvent server-fire-only rule (round-3 addition).** `FloraChunkUpdate` and `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` bypass the Knit RemoteSignal layer for chunk-broadcast efficiency. The justification for the audit-bypass is that **these events are server-fire-only — no client action, RemoteFunction, RemoteEvent, or Knit Client method may cause the server to fire either of them, directly or transitively.** Both are triggered server-side: `FloraChunkUpdate` by the field-update pass when flora `t` changes by more than `FLORA_UPDATE_DELTA_THRESHOLD`; `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` by the server's StreamingEnabled chunk-load detection. If a future feature requires either to fire in response to a client action, the audit-bypass assumption MUST be re-evaluated and the events MUST be re-routed through the Knit audit surface. Adding a client-callable path that indirectly causes a server-side fire of these events is a regression that reviewers MUST reject (paired with C.1.8 / H.39 / H.39d for the Emit case).

### F.5 — Soft Dependencies (post-MVP / future)

- **Audio system** (when authored): may subscribe to `TierCrossedEvent` to drive ambient audio tier shifts. Pattern is identical to Predator AI subscription. No design change to this system required.
- **Cosmetic shop / Live ops** (post-launch): seasonal events that introduce new emission types (e.g., a "Halloween candle" item) hook in via the same `DisturbanceService:Emit()` API. Reserved 3 remaining enum slots per C.3.6 accommodate this without breaking the event bus contract (1 of original 4 consumed 2026-04-30 by `Craft`).
- **Map variants / additional predator variants** (post-launch v1): each additional predator may consume `GetHottestHotspot` with different `searchRadius` / `minimumTier` parameters. This system is parameter-agnostic and supports them without modification.

### F.6 — Out-of-Scope (NOT dependencies of this system)

These systems do NOT interact with the disturbance bus, by design:

- **Roblox `DataStoreService` / ProfileStore** — disturbance state is purely runtime; no persistence across sessions
- **Roblox built-in `Humanoid.Died`** — death event triggers Player Controller's death flow; Player Controller (or HUD) calls into DisturbanceService to retrieve `DeathAttributionPayload`. **(Round-11 DR-4 = Option B amendment, supersedes the round-9 "subscribes for snapshot path only" wording.)** DisturbanceService is the **sole subscriber** to `Humanoid.Died` in the entire server codebase. Other services that need death notification MUST subscribe to DisturbanceService's server-internal `OnPlayerDied` BindableEvent (acquired via `DisturbanceService:GetOnPlayerDiedSignal()` in their `KnitStart`) instead of subscribing to `Humanoid.Died` directly. DisturbanceService's `Humanoid.Died` callback (registered exclusively during `DisturbanceService:KnitInit` via the `Players.PlayerAdded` → `Player.CharacterAdded` → `humanoid.Died` chain) does three things and only three things, in the strict yield-free order specified by C.1.11 step 5: (5a) `_captureDeathLockSnapshot(deadPlayer)` — idempotent server-internal helper that stores `predatorWasLockedAtDeath` into `_deathLockSnapshot[deadPlayer]`; (5b) construct the `OnPlayerDied` payload; (5c) `_OnPlayerDied:Fire(payload)` — fan out synchronously to subscribed services. DisturbanceService does NOT drive death-attribution payload construction from the `Humanoid.Died` handler (Player Controller / HUD still own the call into `:GetDeathAttributionPayload`), and does NOT fire any client-facing RemoteSignal from the handler. The `Humanoid.Died` subscription's monopoly is enforced by the H.39e CI lint (round-11 addition) which rejects any `Humanoid.Died:Connect` chain in any service body other than `DisturbanceService:KnitInit(`. The round-9 KnitInit-vs-KnitStart connection-order rule that previously governed cross-service `Humanoid.Died` ordering is superseded — there is now exactly one subscriber, so connection-order is irrelevant. **Round-13 R12-B4 cross-GDD revision mandates (BLOCKING for downstream-GDD authors; the H.39e CI lint will reject the implementations of the listed downstream GDDs as currently written):**
   - **PC-T5 mandate:** `design/gdd/player-controller.md` Section C.4 transitions table row T5 (currently at line 247) declares `Humanoid.Died` as the trigger for the S1/S2 → S4 transition. Under H.39e Lint (a) the implementation as written would FAIL CI — PlayerControllerService cannot subscribe to `Humanoid.Died` directly. The Player Controller GDD MUST be revised to: (i) preserve the T5 transition's trigger semantics (the S1/S2 → S4 transition still occurs on death) but (ii) re-anchor the trigger to **DisturbanceService's server-internal `OnPlayerDied` BindableEvent acquired in PlayerControllerService's KnitStart via `DisturbanceService:GetOnPlayerDiedSignal()`** (NOTE: this is the server-internal BindableEvent, distinct from PlayerControllerService's own client-facing `OnPlayerDied` RemoteEvent — see F.2a row 3 round-13 naming-collision note for the namespace disambiguation; the two channels coexist and have distinct payloads + fire-sites + namespaces), AND (iii) re-author T5's "Side Effects" column so all server-side side-effects (stamina freeze, force T4, force T2, deduct squad oxygen, reset grace pulses, spectator camera, etc.) execute inside PlayerControllerService's `OnPlayerDied:Connect` callback per the deferred-queue pattern from H.39e Lint (b) (the synchronous callback body stashes the death payload onto a service-internal queue; a per-frame Heartbeat worker pops the queue and runs the side-effects yield-freely from outside the callback's lexical scope). The client-facing `OnPlayerDied` RemoteEvent that PC fires to the dead player's client is unaffected by this mandate (it remains owned by PlayerControllerService and is fired from the deferred Heartbeat worker, NOT from the server-internal `OnPlayerDied:Connect` callback body — and the client-facing RemoteEvent's name remains `OnPlayerDied` per F.2a row 3 round-13 naming-collision note; no rename obligation). The Player Controller GDD's revision MUST cite this F.6 row AND F.2a row 3 naming-collision note, AND MUST land before any PlayerControllerService implementation epic enters sprint planning.
   - **Crafting-C.8 mandate:** `design/gdd/crafting-and-items.md` Section C.8 (currently at line 161) declares: "Death mid-craft: detected via `Humanoid.Died`. Same path as disconnect — removes player from session; fires BT4 or BT5." Under H.39e Lint (a) the implementation as written would FAIL CI — CraftingService cannot subscribe to `Humanoid.Died` directly. The Crafting & Items GDD MUST be revised to: (i) preserve the BT4/BT5 transitions' trigger semantics (the death-mid-craft case still removes the dead player from the session and fires the appropriate transition) but (ii) re-anchor the trigger to **DisturbanceService's `OnPlayerDied` BindableEvent acquired in CraftingService's KnitStart via `DisturbanceService:GetOnPlayerDiedSignal()`**, AND (iii) re-author the death-mid-craft handler's logic to stash the payload via the H.39e deferred-queue pattern, with the BT4/BT5 transition firing from the per-frame Heartbeat worker outside the callback body. The Crafting & Items GDD's revision MUST cite this F.6 row and MUST land before any CraftingService implementation epic enters sprint planning.
   - **Generality clause:** any future downstream GDD that lists `Humanoid.Died` as a trigger or detection mechanism in its prose, formulas, or transitions tables MUST be revised on the same template — reroute through `OnPlayerDied` BindableEvent acquired in KnitStart, deferred-queue pattern for any non-yield-free side-effects. Cross-GDD reviewers MUST flag any new `Humanoid.Died`-direct-subscription wording at design-review time. The H.39e CI lint is the production-side enforcement; this F.6 row is the design-side enforcement.
   - **Crafting-MAX_BEACON mandate (round-15 R14-B11 closure of round-13 R13-I2 elevation; CD-recommended Option A from round-15 design ruling — scope clause + cross-GDD lock; bidirectional with H.36 PASS criterion's scope clause).** `design/gdd/crafting-and-items.md` MUST author a runtime constraint `MAX_ACTIVE_BEACONS = 3` and reject Beacon-emission publish-attempts at the Crafting publish-site BEFORE the emission reaches `DisturbanceService:Emit("Beacon", position, magnitude, sourcePlayerId)`. The constraint's specifics: (i) Crafting maintains a server-side counter `_activeBeaconCount: number` initialised to 0 at server startup; incremented on each successful Beacon-emission publish; decremented on Beacon-emission expiry detection (Crafting subscribes to a Beacon-expired signal — implementation-flexible; recommended: a server-internal Crafting accessor on DisturbanceService that fires when the beacon's `m(t, age)` decays below `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR`, OR a Crafting-side Beacon lifetime tracker per concept-doc cadence). (ii) Beacon-emission publish-attempts where `_activeBeaconCount >= 3` (or whatever value `MAX_ACTIVE_BEACONS` takes — round-15 default is 3, but the constant is a Crafting-owned tuning knob in the safe-range determined by Crafting's own safe-range analysis) MUST be REJECTED at the publish-site with a documented error code (recommended: `CraftingError.BEACON_LIMIT_REACHED` or equivalent); the rejection is observable to the player via Crafting's own UI surface (HUD GDD owns the visual treatment). (iii) Crafting MUST author an AUTO-UNIT AC verifying that emission-publish-attempts beyond cap are rejected with the documented error code — the AC is a Crafting-side AC, NOT this GDD's. (iv) The constant `MAX_ACTIVE_BEACONS = 3` is **bidirectional-locked** with this GDD's H.36 PASS criterion scope clause: any change to Crafting's `MAX_ACTIVE_BEACONS` MUST be reflected in this GDD's H.36 + H.36a in the SAME patch session that changes Crafting (cross-GDD value lock; see F.3 cross-GDD value lock semantics). Without the bidirectional lock, a Crafting GDD revision could silently raise `MAX_ACTIVE_BEACONS` to 4 or higher, invalidating H.36's measurement scope without triggering this GDD's R11-I8 grid-mandate elevation. The Crafting & Items GDD's revision MUST cite this F.6 row + this GDD's H.36 scope clause + R11-I8 forward obligation, AND MUST land before any CraftingService implementation epic enters sprint planning. **R13-I2 closure status:** R13-I2 is now CLOSED inline at this F.6 mandate AND at H.36's scope clause (see Section H Performance). The R13-I forward obligation tracking entry is updated to mark CLOSED (see F.2a-IMPORTANT round-13 cluster).
- **Roblox `PathfindingService`** — used by Predator AI for navigation but invisible to this system. The disturbance system supplies "where to go"; pathfinding handles "how to get there."
- **Roblox audio (`SoundService`, positional `Sound`)** — F.5 notes a future audio subscription, but at MVP this system is silent. The audio system reads downstream effects (flora pulses, predator behavior) on its own schedule.

## Tuning Knobs

Every designer-adjustable value lives here. Stories that reference any of these values cite this section as the source of truth; programmers MUST surface these as configurable constants in `DisturbanceService`, not magic numbers in code.

### G.1 — Tier Boundaries (locked by art bible §4.3 + ED.C.1.7)

| Knob | Default | Safe range | What breaks if too high | What breaks if too low | Cross-system impact |
|------|---------|------------|--------------------------|-------------------------|---------------------|
| `TENSE_THRESHOLD` | 0.30 | 0.20 – 0.40 | Players reach Tense rarely; the world feels static | Tense fires constantly from idle behavior; meter loses meaning | Locked by art bible flora tier table — change requires art-bible revision |
| `HUNT_THRESHOLD` | 0.65 | 0.55 – 0.75 | Hunt unreachable in normal play; predator never escalates | Hunt fires from light play; encounter rate spikes | Locked by art bible — see above |
| `RETREAT_THRESHOLD` | 1.00 | (clamped — not adjustable) | — | — | Hard ceiling defined by clamping in D.3 |
| `HYSTERESIS_BAND` | 0.03 | 0.02 – 0.05 | Stickier zones; Tense feels sticky-on, Hunt feels sticky-off | More boundary oscillation; flora pulse periods chatter | Affects PASSIVE alert fire rate (C.3.4) |

### G.2 — Decay and Lifetime

| Knob | Default | Safe range | What breaks if too high | What breaks if too low |
|------|---------|------------|--------------------------|-------------------------|
| `STANDARD_HALF_LIFE` | 30 s | 15 – 60 s | Disturbance lingers; predator follows stale trails; tense feel becomes constant. **⚠ Values above ~33 s (with other knobs at default) push the D.7 stationary-light asymptote above `TENSE_THRESHOLD` (`0.024/(1−exp(−4·ln2/33)) ≈ 0.298`; at 40 s it is already 0.358 = Tense) — see the G.7 stationary-light-asymptote joint constraint (round-22 B7)** | Disturbance evaporates; predator loses scent before squad has time to make decisions |
| `BEACON_HALF_LIFE` | 90 s | 45 – 180 s | Beacon dominates the field for too long; other emissions drowned out | Beacon spike fades before predator arrives; win condition becomes boring |
| `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR` | 0.02 | 0.01 – 0.05 | Sources cull early; predator loses long-tail signal; D.6 hotspot quality drops | Sources persist longer with negligible contribution; live-source list grows; query cost rises |

### G.3 — Spatial Behavior

| Knob | Default | Safe range | What breaks if too high | What breaks if too low | Note |
|------|---------|------------|--------------------------|-------------------------|------|
| `INFLUENCE_RADIUS` | 24 studs | 16 – 36 studs | Disturbance ripples too wide; flora respond from far away; hotspot resolution coarsens | Disturbance is too local; squad members 1 stud apart create independent hotspots | ~2× player lantern radius — intentional asymmetry |
| `HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE` | 4 studs | 2 – 8 studs | Predator targets lose precision; samples up to N studs from true peak | More expensive per-query (more candidate evaluations) | Used by D.6 only |
| `SPATIAL_GRID_CELL_SIZE` | 32 studs | max(`INFLUENCE_RADIUS`, 16) – 64 studs | Larger queries (more cells touched per query) | More cells in memory; insertion cost rises; **`SPATIAL_GRID_CELL_SIZE < INFLUENCE_RADIUS` violates G.7 invariant and breaks C.1.6's 3×3-cell guarantee — startup validation rejects (H.3e)** | Implementation hint per C.1.6 for cell size; existence of grid IS contractual |

### G.4 — Update Cadence and Performance

| Knob | Default | Safe range | What breaks if too high | What breaks if too low |
|------|---------|------------|--------------------------|-------------------------|
| `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ` | 5 Hz | 2 – 10 Hz | Server CPU + network rises; mobile frame budget pressured | Visible visual lag (>500ms); HUD meter feels disconnected; flora respond sluggishly. **⚠ round-23 B4:** every PERF-DEVICE PASS record (H.36/H.37/H.37b/H.38/H.38b) is pinned to the 5 Hz default — see the H.36 FIELD_UPDATE_HZ scope clause; re-measure before shipping any cadence change |
| `FLORA_UPDATE_DELTA_THRESHOLD` | 0.02 | 0.01 – 0.05 | More flora updates per tick; bandwidth rises | Flora visual tween steps become visibly chunky; "world responds" read degrades |
| `EXPIRY_MAINTENANCE_INTERVAL` | 3 s | 1 – 10 s | Expired sources accumulate slightly longer in the live list; trivial cost | Maintenance overhead rises; barely measurable on mobile |
| `MAX_LIVE_SOURCES` | 500 | 200 – 2000 | Allows pathological accumulation before the safety valve trips | Cap trips during legitimate 4-player squad play; emissions get silently dropped |
| `ATTRIBUTION_ARCHIVE_TTL` | 1200 s | 600 – 1800 s | Archive grows large; minor memory + GC overhead | Death attribution fails ("Attribution unavailable") for early-run emissions in a long session; concept doc 95% MVP gate at risk. **⚠ round-23 SD-5 joint constraint (G.7):** the TTL floor (600 s) is UNSAFE against `BEACON_HALF_LIFE` above ~110 s — a still-live beacon can outlive its own archive entry |
| `MAX_ATTRIBUTION_ARCHIVE_ENTRIES` | 4000 | 2000 – 8000 | Natural 20-min-session accumulation (~3,720 at sustained ~3.1 emissions/s) gets trimmed — attribution quality degrades on long loud sessions | Cap never binds; pathological/exploit archive growth unbounded (the round-23 B2 gap this knob closes). Eviction: FIFO-oldest, predator-lock-referenced entries exempt (C.1.11); every eviction ops-logged |
| `MAX_FLORA_PER_CHUNK` | 8 | 4 – 16 | Bandwidth burst on coordinated emissions; H.37 50 KB/s ceiling at risk; flora-target compute per cycle increases | Visual sparseness; flora-density read of "the world is alive" weakens | Anchored to art bible §6.5 default; level-design layer must respect this. Chunk dimensions are 64×64 studs (Roblox StreamingEnabled cell size). |
| `CHUNK_DIMENSIONS` | 64 × 64 studs | (Roblox StreamingEnabled-bound) | — | — | Documented for cross-system reference; not directly tunable in this GDD (StreamingEnabled is engine-set). |

### G.5 — Per-Emission-Type Magnitudes (D.7)

| Knob | Default | Safe range | What breaks if too high | What breaks if too low |
|------|---------|------------|--------------------------|-------------------------|
| `MAGNITUDE_GATHER_LIGHT` | 0.15 | 0.05 – 0.25 | Single gather pushes Calm→Tense; players never feel safe | Gathering feels free; loop has no consequence |
| `MAGNITUDE_GATHER_MEDIUM` | 0.25 | 0.15 – 0.40 | Medium gather alone reaches Tense; pacing breaks | Medium gather indistinguishable from light; node tiering loses meaning |
| `MAGNITUDE_GATHER_HEAVY` | 0.40 | 0.25 – 0.60 | Heavy gather near-instantly reaches Hunt; players avoid heavy nodes entirely | Heavy gather indistinguishable from medium; node tiering collapses |
| `MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE` | 0.10 | 0.05 – 0.15 | Sprint becomes a quick path to Hunt; movement becomes fearful | Sprint is free; "quiet beats loud" pillar weakens |
| `MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE` | 0.08 | 0.04 – 0.12 | Lantern raise becomes an exposure mechanic; players keep lights down constantly. **⚠ Values above ~0.088 (with other knobs at default) push the D.7 stationary-light asymptote above `TENSE_THRESHOLD`, breaking the Q9 Calm guarantee + the SQUAD_T anchor — see the G.7 stationary-light-asymptote joint constraint (round-22 B7)** | Lighting choice becomes invisible to the system |
| `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR` | 0.09 | (0.08, 0.10) **exclusive** | Stationary-sprint hold-floor approaches the moving-pulse 0.10 — the moving-vs-holding gradient collapses (PC G.7) | Drops to/below `MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE` (0.08) — a stationary sprint becomes quieter than a raised lantern, inverting the Pillar-1 emission hierarchy (PC [R13-F1]) |
| `MAGNITUDE_BEACON` | 0.95 | 0.85 – 1.00 | Beacon at 1.00 means instant Retreat — predator commits before squad readies | Beacon below 0.85 violates the reserved-band rule (C.1.2) |
| `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL` | 2 s | 1 – 4 s | Sprint registers as one-shot; sustained sprinting under-counted | Sprint creates spam; bandwidth + per-tick cost rises. **⚠ round-23 B5 joint constraint (G.7):** the canonical ~15 s Hunt / ~28 s Retreat figures AND the F.2a row-9 auto-release window are pinned to the 2 s default — any retune requires the full G.7-mandated re-derivation fanout |
| `LIGHT_PULSE_INTERVAL` | 4 s | 2 – 8 s | Lantern raise barely registers | Lantern creates spam; bandwidth rises |
| `EMITTER_MOVEMENT_DELTA_FLOOR` | 1 stud | 0.5 – 4 studs | Stationary-attenuation rarely triggers; near-stationary players still build steady-state field; Q9 fix weakens | Slow walking triggers stationary attenuation; mid-pace exploration under-emits; "Quiet Is Power" loses calibration |
| `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR` | 0.30 | 0.10 – 0.60 | Stationary attenuation weak; standing still still escalates rapidly; Q9 fix weakens. **⚠ Values above ~0.33 (with other knobs at default) push the D.7 stationary-light steady-state into Tense — a steady-state tier shift that breaks the named Q9 Calm invariant, not merely "rapid escalation"; see the G.7 stationary-light-asymptote joint constraint (round-22 B7)** | Stationary play is essentially silent; safe-base-camp incentive too strong; predator never finds idle squad |
| `FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW` | 1.0 s | 0.0 – 2.0 s | Multiple pulses on state-entry escape stationary attenuation; players can game the system by toggling sprint repeatedly | Even the first pulse on state-entry attenuates if the player was stationary just before; "instant Tense on first sprint after gather sequence" reappears |
| `BEACON_STATIONARY_PROXIMITY_RADIUS` | 16 studs | 8 – 24 studs | Cap engages from far away; cap fires when squad isn't really hiding behind beacon; "active defender" play penalised by movement requirements with no real proximity | Cap requires squad to be on top of beacon; small-radius play (e.g., crowded cave activations) cannot engage cap because squad is too spread out vertically/laterally |
| `BEACON_CUMULATIVE_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD` (round-7a R7-B6 / D2 — REPLACES `BEACON_STATIONARY_MOVE_DELTA`; **PROVISIONAL — round-7c I-7b-1**) | 6.0 studs (PROVISIONAL pending iPhone SE drift measurement per F.2a row 8 / Player Controller GDD obligation) | 2.0 – 12.0 studs (round-7c I-7b-7 — safe-range upper tightened from 24 → 12; the round-7a 24-stud upper extended into walking-defender territory, where a 4-player squad walking 4–8 studs/sec for 3.0 s accumulates 12–24 studs and the cap would falsely engage on legitimate active-defender movement at any tuner setting in (12, 24]) | Sustained mid-pace exploration counted as stationary; cap fires during legitimate active-defender play (a 4-player squad walking to a defensive position averages 4–8 studs/sec; over 3.0 s that's 12–24 studs of cumulative travel — the safe-range upper of 12 keeps the threshold strictly below the lower-bound of the walking-defender band) | Tiny shuffles + foot-tapping count as "moving"; degenerate shuffle-game strategy reopens (set the threshold strictly above maximum plausible per-window noise from idle-stick analog drift on iPhone SE per F.2a row 8 — current default 6.0 studs is ~2 studs/sec average, comfortably above 1-stud-class drift). **Round-7c I-7b-1 PROVISIONAL marker:** the 6.0-stud default ASSUMES iPhone SE touch-input idle-stick analog drift contributes ≤ ~1 stud/sec average noise per the F.2a row 8 deferred contract on the Player Controller GDD. If empirical measurement on iPhone SE-class hardware shows worst-case drift above ~2 studs/sec sustained, this default MUST be raised before the cap rule is shipped — touch players standing still would otherwise accumulate `cumulativeXZ ≥ 6 studs` over the 3.0 s window from drift alone and the cap would never engage on touch-input squads. The Player Controller GDD's iPhone SE drift measurement is the round-7c forward obligation that resolves the PROVISIONAL marker; until it lands, this default is a placeholder pending empirical data. H.41/H.41c MUST add an iPhone SE-variant test path measuring per-window drift accumulation. |
| `BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW` | 3.0 s | 1.0 – 8.0 s | Cap engages slowly; even with cumulative-distance redesign, a `BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW = 8.0 s` window admits 8 s of motion-buffer-fill before cap can engage on a freshly-stationary squad — at the high end of the safe range the cap response feels sluggish | Cap engages aggressively; momentary pauses (gather-completion idle, taking screenshots) trigger cap; player frustration. **Note (round-7a R7-B6):** the round-5–round-6 sampled-boolean exploit (game-designer F5) is closed by the cumulative-distance redesign at step 4; tuning this knob now affects only response latency, not exploitability. |
| `BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE` | 0.65 (Hunt floor) | 0.30 – 0.85 | Cap permits full Hunt-tier (or higher) at beacon under stationary-squad — degenerate strategy returns | Cap reduces beacon to Tense (or below) — beacon's design role as a guaranteed lure is broken |
| `PREDATOR_LOCK_RELEASE_GRACE_WINDOW` (round-7a R7-B7 — see C.1.11; round-7c I-7b-9 lower-bound tightening) | 5.0 s | 2.0 – 30.0 s (round-7c I-7b-9 — lower-bound raised from 1.0 → 2.0; the round-7a 1.0 s lower equalled the upper bound of the typical 0.25–1.0 s re-registration interval per G.6, exposing a race where a tuner setting at 1.0 s could shave the grace below the slowest legitimate re-registration cadence and trigger the same attribution-completeness data-loss window the grace was designed to prevent. Values ≤ 0.25 s are degenerate — they fall at-or-below the fastest legitimate re-registration interval and effectively disable the grace mechanism.) | Archive entries persist longer beyond predator's actual hold; minor memory pressure; archive TTL leak risk if grace exceeds session duration | Re-registration drops prior IDs from condition (c) before legitimate refresh cadence finishes (Predator AI re-queries at a fixed 4 Hz per G.6 / PA Core Rule 2 — round-22 I7 refresh — so the re-registration interval is ~0.25 s; the grace MUST exceed this interval by a comfortable margin to prevent attribution-completeness data-loss windows; 5.0 s is ~20× the re-registration interval, well above the safety floor; the 2.0 s lower bound — derived when the estimate was 1–4 Hz with a 1.0 s worst-case interval — is retained as-is, now preserving an ~8× margin instead of the original 2×) |
| `SQUAD_T_FAIL_FLOOR_THRESHOLD` (round-17 I16-1 closure of round-15 R14-B9 deferral; CD-recommended starting point per round-16 verdict) | **0.28** (calibration starting point within the round-20 Session B interim 0.276–0.285 band; Beta-playtest tuned — raised from the prior 0.20, which sat in the inert sub-band ≤ the D.7 ~0.272 asymptote and left criterion (iv) permanently inert; B-CD18-1 closure) | **(0.272, 0.30)** — runtime-meaningful safe range (round-20 Session B B-CD18-1 narrowing; **supersedes the prior type-level `0.05 – 0.29`**). **Interim recommended starting band: 0.276–0.285** (default 0.28). **Lower bound = D.7's stationary-light steady-state asymptote (~0.272, the Lantern-stationary floor)** — a threshold ≤ 0.272 produces an inert criterion (iv) because a stationary lantern-raised squad stabilises at ≈0.272 indefinitely, above the FAIL threshold for any value ≤ 0.272 (B-R19-SD-2: the prior `> MAGNITUDE_FLOOR + headroom` / "floor noise" rationale was conceptually wrong — the binding lower constraint is the D.7 asymptote, NOT floor noise). Upper bound strictly below `TENSE_THRESHOLD = 0.30` so a healthy Tense-decay-tail session cannot trigger FAIL. **Warning: any tuner value ≤ 0.272 silently defeats the permanent-Calm safety net.** **Round-22 I20 cross-reference:** the ~0.272 anchor is the stationary-**Light** asymptote specifically — after the 2026-06-19 ED-yields ruling, stationary-**Sprint** no longer produces a low sustained reading at all (the 0.09 hold-floor drives `squadT` to Hunt in ~15 s / Retreat in ~28 s per D.7), so a "permanent-Calm collapse" scenario in practice means a lantern-raised, non-sprinting stationary squad; the asymptote anchor and this threshold are unaffected by the ruling. The anchor is also coupled to four other knobs — see the G.7 stationary-light-asymptote joint constraint (round-22 B7). **RUNTIME safety floor — round-20 I-QA18-4 arithmetic clarification of round-18 inconsistency (CD-flagged round-18 B-CD18-1 BLOCKING; range narrowing applied in Session B):** D.7's stationary-light steady-state derivation yields a squad-aggregate floor of ~0.272 (the Lantern stationary steady-state, just below `TENSE_THRESHOLD = 0.30`); tuner values in the type-level safe range below 0.272 produce an inert criterion (iv) because stationary squads stabilise at ≈0.272 indefinitely, which is above the FAIL threshold for any tuner value < 0.272. The prior type-level range `0.05 – 0.29` was bounded-correct only as a type-system contract; the runtime-meaningful range is narrower and is now the published range. The BLOCKING B-CD18-1 narrowing **LANDED in round-20 Session B (2026-06-09):** the published safe range is now `(0.272, 0.30)` and the starting-point default is `0.28` (interim 0.276–0.285 band), superseding the prior `0.05 – 0.29` range and `0.20` default. The Beta-playtest milestone may further tune the default within `(0.272, 0.30)`; the F.2b registry (R17-I16-1 row) tracks the calibration return path. | Threshold ≥ Tense → criterion (iv) FAIL fires on healthy Tense-decay-tail sessions (false-positive failure on legitimate session-arc) | Threshold ≤ D.7 stationary-light asymptote (~0.272) → criterion (iv) FAIL never fires even on genuine permanent-Calm collapse (false-negative; the criterion's objective server-side floor is defeated because the criterion (iv) safety net designed to detect permanent-Calm has a hole shaped exactly like permanent-Calm — see B-CD18-1 round-18 closure rationale). Threshold near `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR` is a degenerate sub-case of this same failure mode. | **Calibration knob, NOT a primary balance knob.** Used ONLY by H.PB1-BETA criterion (iv) FAIL-iv-server condition (see Section H Player Fantasy Verification). The value is playtest-calibrated; the Beta-playtest milestone determines the final figure AND feeds back into a future patch session if the chosen starting point produces false-PASS or false-FAIL signal. F.2b registry tracks the calibration-tuning return path (Receiver: qa-lead + DevOps Engineer table). |

### G.6 — Predator Query Defaults (consumed by Predator AI GDD)

These are *defaults that Predator AI will likely use* but are owned by Predator AI's tuning, not this system. Listed here for cross-reference only.

| Knob | Default | Owner GDD | Note |
|------|---------|-----------|------|
| `PREDATOR_SEARCH_RADIUS` | 200 studs | Predator AI | Passed to `GetHottestHotspot`; recommended bound 32 ≤ radius ≤ 512 (see C.3.3) |
| `PREDATOR_QUERY_HZ` | 4 Hz (fixed — PA Core Rule 2; round-22 I7 refresh of the prior 1–4 Hz estimate) | Predator AI | Predator drives its own cadence; ED is cadence-agnostic |
| `PREDATOR_MIN_TIER_INVESTIGATE` | "Tense" | Predator AI | Floor for predator orientation |
| `PREDATOR_MIN_TIER_HUNT` | "Hunt" | Predator AI | Floor for active pursuit |
| `PREDATOR_PATH_STABILITY_MIN_DELTA` | 8 studs | Predator AI | Minimum bestPosition delta before re-invoking `PathfindingService:FindPathAsync()` (C.3.3 path stability rule). Decoupled from `HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE` so spatial-grid tuning does not silently affect chase responsiveness. |
| `RETREAT_DROPOUT_THRESHOLD` | 0.30 | Predator AI | `squadT` value below which the predator considers retreating. Read via `GetSquadAggregateT(excludeLockedPlayer)` — the round-23 B1 exclusion form (D.5/D.6b); PA's quiet-check excludes the locked target. |
| `RETREAT_SUSTAIN_DURATION` | 30 s | Predator AI | Required sustained-Calm duration before predator commits to retreat. Per concept doc escalation curve. **⚠ round-24 Gate-A note (F2):** the authored+APPROVED PA uses a canonical **20 s** sustain at every consuming site (PA:57, PA H.25/H.36, PA:166) and never adopts this row's 30 s — this row is a pre-PA placeholder and **PA's 20 s governs** (same yield rule as `INVESTIGATE_FALLBACK_DURATION` below); align the default at ED's next touch |
| `INVESTIGATE_FALLBACK_DURATION` (round-9 B-NS5 closure; resolves the F.2a row 1 + F.2a-IMPORTANT F6 forward obligation that round-8 flagged as a verification blocker for H.30c bootstrap) | 8.0 s | Predator AI | The sustained-nil duration of `GetHottestHotspot` returns after which the predator transitions Investigate→Patrol. Default 8.0 s = `2 × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ_RECIPROCAL × max(1, ceil(NIL_TOLERANCE_SAMPLES))` = 2 s per pass × 4 passes tolerance + 0 s buffer; covers ~4 sustained-nil queries at the predator's lower-bound 1 Hz cadence. Safe range: 4.0 – 30.0 s (lower bound 4.0 s ≈ 2× the predator's slowest query interval to avoid Patrol-fallback chatter from a single missed query; upper bound 30.0 s parallels `RETREAT_SUSTAIN_DURATION` to keep predator decision-cadence in the same temporal class). **This default is owned by the Predator AI GDD's tuning; the value 8.0 s is published here so that round-9 H.30c bootstrap-path verification can use a concrete number rather than a TBD reference.** When the Predator AI GDD is authored, this row's default may be overridden — the disturbance-system contract is parameter-agnostic; only the **existence** of a published default is normative for round-9 closure. |

### G.7 — Knob Interaction Warnings

Some knobs interact such that changing one in isolation can produce unintended effects:

- **`STANDARD_HALF_LIFE` × `MAGNITUDE_*`**: shorter half-life requires higher initial magnitudes to deliver the same total disturbance impression. Changing one without the other shifts the entire game's feel.
- **`HYSTERESIS_BAND` × `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ`**: at current Sprint (0.10) and Light (0.08) magnitudes, oscillation across a tier boundary in active play is nearly impossible — emissions push `t` by at least ~2.7× the hysteresis band per pulse (Sprint `0.10/0.03 ≈ 3.3×`; Light `0.08/0.03 ≈ 2.7×` — round-22 I3 correction of the prior blanket "3×" claim, which was false for Light). The warning becomes load-bearing only if future emission types are added with magnitudes below `HYSTERESIS_BAND × 2` (e.g., a "whisper" emission at 0.05). Pre-flight-check any future low-magnitude emission against this coupling.
- **`INFLUENCE_RADIUS` × `MAGNITUDE_*`**: doubling radius without lowering magnitudes effectively quadruples the affected area's total disturbance, AND shifts the falloff shape (a source 8 studs away contributes 0.025 at radius=16 vs 0.061 at radius=36 — distant sources become disproportionately louder). The two are coupled via the quadratic falloff in D.2.
- **`INFLUENCE_RADIUS` × `SPATIAL_GRID_CELL_SIZE`**: at upper bounds (radius=36, cell=64), per-query cell read count grows beyond a 3×3 neighborhood, increasing per-fieldValue cost. Recommended invariant: `SPATIAL_GRID_CELL_SIZE` ≥ `INFLUENCE_RADIUS` (current default 32 ≥ 24 ✓).
- **`MAX_LIVE_SOURCES` × `STANDARD_HALF_LIFE`**: longer half-life means more concurrent live sources. If half-life is doubled, the cap should be reviewed against expected source count under 4-player Hunt-state load (Section 8.11 Test 1 in the art bible).
- **`EMITTER_MOVEMENT_DELTA_FLOOR` × `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR`**: these two together define the Q9 stationary-attenuation strength. Lowering DELTA_FLOOR (more permissive) without raising FACTOR means slow-walking players accumulate at full magnitude AND stationary players still escalate. Tune as a coupled pair.
- **`MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE` × `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR` × `STANDARD_HALF_LIFE` × `LIGHT_PULSE_INTERVAL` — the stationary-light asymptote joint constraint (round-22 B7; BINDING).** These four knobs jointly determine D.7's stationary-light steady-state asymptote `(MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE × STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR) / (1 − exp(−λ × LIGHT_PULSE_INTERVAL))` — currently `0.024 / 0.088279 ≈ 0.272`. That asymptote is load-bearing TWICE: (1) it is the basis of the **Q9 "stationary lantern settles in Calm" guarantee** (D.7 — the asymptote must stay strictly below `TENSE_THRESHOLD`, itself tunable 0.20–0.40 per G.1); (2) it is the **lower-bound anchor of `SQUAD_T_FAIL_FLOOR_THRESHOLD`'s safe range `(0.272, 0.30)`** (this section + H.PB1-BETA criterion (iv)). Each of the four knobs has an *individually*-"safe" range that can break both dependents in combination: `MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE` at its 0.12 ceiling → asymptote 0.408 (Tense); `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR` at its 0.60 ceiling → 0.544 (Tense); `STANDARD_HALF_LIFE` at its 60 s ceiling → 0.531 (Tense). **Retuning ANY of `MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE`, `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR`, `STANDARD_HALF_LIFE`, `LIGHT_PULSE_INTERVAL`, or `TENSE_THRESHOLD` REQUIRES re-deriving the asymptote and re-validating both dependents (the Q9 Calm guarantee AND the SQUAD_T safe range/default) in the SAME patch session** — the per-row safe ranges are per-knob type bounds, not joint guarantees. The individual G.2/G.5 rows cross-reference this entry.
- **`MAX_FLORA_PER_CHUNK` × `FLORA_UPDATE_DELTA_THRESHOLD` × `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ`**: bandwidth cost is approximately `MAX_FLORA_PER_CHUNK × loaded_chunks × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ × per_flora_payload_size`. At defaults (8 × 4 × 5 × 44 bytes ≈ 7 KB/s), well within 50 KB/s ceiling. Doubling any factor approaches the ceiling — H.37 verifies.
- **`FIELD_UPDATE_HZ` × cap-cue persistence legibility (round-13 I15 fold-in):** the C.3.4 step 4 persistence rule's per-player counter is initialised to `math.ceil(1.0 × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ)` field-update passes (round-11 B-R10-3 cadence-derived initialisation), producing a fixed 1.0 s post-disengage attribution window across all G.3 safe-range cadences. **However, the in-window UI legibility — how many distinct frames the HUD has to render the post-disengage `nearby` / `stationary_nearby` populated state — varies by cadence:** at `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ = 2 Hz` the player observes 2 frames of populated state (one at 500 ms and one at 1.0 s post-disengage); at `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ = 10 Hz` the player observes 10 frames (one at 100 ms intervals). The 5× legibility variation between the safe-range bounds is a design decision, not implementation tuning. **Recommendation:** if a future tuner deviates from `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ = 5 Hz` and the legibility variation is observed in playtest as causing the post-disengage attribution UI to feel under-readable (at 2 Hz) or over-busy (at 10 Hz), the cap-cue persistence-window duration (`1.0 s`, hardcoded inside the `math.ceil(1.0 × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ)` expression) MAY need to be re-tuned independently of `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ` — at lower cadences toward 2.0 s for more frames; at higher cadences toward 0.5 s for fewer. This re-tuning would require a future patch session — round-13 documents the coupling as a knob-interaction warning here so future tuners are aware. **Minimum-cadence rule (round-13 I15 fold-in advisory):** the `BEACON_STATIONARY_PROXIMITY_RADIUS` proximity check at C.3.6 step 1 fires once per pass, so at `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ = 2 Hz` a player who walks across the proximity boundary at ~16 studs/sec walking speed will be sampled at most twice within the boundary — borderline detectable. At `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ ≤ 1 Hz` (outside the safe range) the proximity check would routinely miss transient boundary crossings; the safe range's lower bound of 2 Hz is therefore minimum-legibility-bounded, not just budget-bounded.
- **`SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL` × the canonical stationary-sprint timing figures × the F.2a row-9 auto-release window (round-23 B5/SD-1; BINDING).** D.7's canonical **~15 s Hunt / ~28 s Retreat** figures are PULSE COUNTS (≈7.43 and ≈13.95 recurring pulses past S1) multiplied by the DEFAULT `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL = 2 s` — they are NOT invariant under the knob's published 1–4 s safe range. At the 1 s floor (individually "safe"), the correct 1 s-interval constants are `r₁ = exp(−λ) ≈ 0.9772` and `S_inf,1 = 0.09/(1 − r₁) ≈ 3.94` — the default-interval constants (`r = 2^(−1/15)`, `S_inf ≈ 1.993`) EMBED the 2 s spacing and MUST NOT be reused at other intervals (round-24 Gate-B2 F8 correction: the prior "≈7.4 s / S(9) ≈ 0.685" figures reused the 2 s constants and UNDERSTATED the breach): Hunt arrives **≈ 6.7 s** (`N−1 ≈ 6.69` pulses × 1 s) — **BEFORE the F.2a row-9 auto-release window (8–10 s) fires** — and the D.7 I13 endgame example's 5–6-pulse auto-release bound becomes 9–11 pulses with `S(9) = 3.94 − 3.84 × r₁⁸ ≈ 0.75`, well past `HUNT_THRESHOLD = 0.65`, breaking the example's "strictly below Hunt" margin. **JOINT CONSTRAINT: any retune of `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL` away from 2 s MUST, in the SAME patch session, (a) re-derive the ~15 s/~28 s canonical figures and every citing site (Section B watch-item (a), D.7, H.PB1 Q4, the I13 example), AND (b) re-validate with Player Controller that the row-9 auto-release window remains strictly below the recomputed Hunt crossing** — the window is interval-dependent and PC-owned. Treat the sub-2 s half of the range as requiring the full fanout before use.
- **`FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW` × `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL` / `LIGHT_PULSE_INTERVAL` (round-23 SD-4).** JOINT CONSTRAINT: `FIRST_PULSE_GRACE_WINDOW` MUST be strictly less than the smallest pulse interval at the operating tune. At the individually-safe extremes (grace 2.0 s × sprint interval 1 s), TWO pulses fall inside the grace window and both fire at full magnitude — the grace-window row's own documented "toggle-gaming" failure mode, reachable with every knob inside its individual range. The defaults (1.0 s < 2 s) are safe.
- **`BEACON_HALF_LIFE` × `ATTRIBUTION_ARCHIVE_TTL` (round-23 SD-5).** JOINT CONSTRAINT: `ATTRIBUTION_ARCHIVE_TTL` MUST be ≥ the Beacon lifetime at the operating tune (`ln(0.95 / MAGNITUDE_FLOOR) / λ_beacon` ≈ 501 s at the 90 s default; ≈ 1002 s at the 180 s ceiling). At (half-life 180 s, TTL floor 600 s) — both individually safe — a still-LIVE beacon outlives its own archive entry by ≈ 400 s: a kill it drives after TTL expiry surfaces the E.9 "Attribution unavailable" fallback while the causal source is still present in the world, threatening the concept doc's 95% attribution MVP gate. The defaults (501 s < 1200 s) are safe.

### G.8 — Design Defaults vs Playtest Tuning

The defaults above are the design's starting position. They are **expected to change** during prototyping and playtesting per the concept doc's risk register entry on disturbance legibility. The values are deliberately published as constants so playtest builds can tune them without recompilation. Stories that introduce a knob change must update this section AND log the change in the version-controlled value record.

## Visual/Audio Requirements

This system has no direct visual or audio surface — it is an infrastructure event bus. The world's visual response to disturbance is owned by:

- **Art Bible §4.3** (Bioluminescent Disturbance Tiers) — flora `Color3.Lerp`, PointLight Brightness Lerp, and pulse-period selection per tier
- **Art Bible §6.4 Beat 3** — narrative beat that, per Q10 round-2 ruling, **must be re-authored to use diegetic in-world causes (props, lighting fixtures, narrative environment) rather than hand-set elevated flora `t` values.** The disturbance system renders one visual state — the live `t` from the field. Story moments use diegetic props, not field-state injection.
- **Bioluminescent Flora rendering subsystem** (downstream consumer per C.3.5) — owns the visual tween from received `targetT`

Audio response is deferred to the audio bible (not yet authored). Per F.5, the audio system can subscribe to `TierCrossedEvent` once authored, with no design change to this system required.

## UI Requirements

This system has no direct UI surface. All player-facing UI driven by disturbance state is owned by the **HUD GDD** (not yet authored). This system specifies the data contracts in C.3.4 (the three RemoteSignals `OnMeterUpdate`, `OnDisturbanceAlert`, `OnDeathAttributionPushed` with their published Luau payload types). Visual style for these elements is locked in **Art Bible §3.3, §4.5, §7.x**. The HUD GDD MUST implement:

- **Squad disturbance meter** — derives from 5 Hz `OnMeterUpdate.squadT` push; 0.25s `Sine/InOut` tween from current to target.
- **Per-player meter** — derives from 5 Hz `OnMeterUpdate.playerT` push (this player's own field value).
- **Active/Passive spike alerts** — distinct visuals per `OnDisturbanceAlert.alertSubtype` per art bible §7.3.
- **Persistent zone-presence indicator (NEW — round 2)** — a continuous HUD state shown while `playerT ≥ TENSE_THRESHOLD`, visually distinct from the spike-alert flash. Resolves the PASSIVE-alert dead-spot identified in round-2 review: the planet keeps a ledger continuously, not just on entry.
- **Predator-locked-on indicator (NEW — round 2 / Q11)** — a state shown to the targeted player while the predator's locked attribution resolves to that player's emissionId(s). Telegraphs "sprinting worsens signal" to teach Q11's no-escape-once-seen commitment. Source data is the Predator AI GDD's responsibility; the disturbance contract publishes attribution data via `OnDeathAttributionPushed` only on death.
- **Death screen** — populated from `OnDeathAttributionPushed.killingEmissions` per art bible §2.6; fallback copy when `killingEmissions` is empty per E.9 + Q1.

> **📌 UX Flag** — When the HUD GDD is authored, run `/ux-design` for the `disturbance-meter`, `zone-presence-indicator`, `predator-locked-indicator`, and `death-screen` UI elements before writing epics. Stories that reference UI must cite `design/ux/[screen].md`, not this GDD.

## Acceptance Criteria

Each criterion is labelled with its test type: **AUTO-UNIT** (TestEZ unit), **AUTO-INTEGRATION** (TestEZ multi-system, may need mock clients), **STATIC-LINT** (grep/AST source-analysis executed as a CI step outside TestEZ — round-23 QA-5 addition; previously these were mis-tagged AUTO-UNIT), **MANUAL-PLAYTEST** (Roblox Studio session with humans), **MANUAL-REVIEW** (two-engineer attested review, CI-evidence-gated), **PERF-DEVICE** (iPhone SE-class hardware with MicroProfiler).

### Emission Validity (Rules C.1.1 – C.1.2)

**H.1** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN the server receives an `Emit()` call with all seven required payload fields populated and `initialMagnitude > 0`, WHEN `DisturbanceService:Emit()` processes the call, THEN a new point source is inserted into the live-source list with `emissionId` matching the standard UUID 8-4-4-4-12 hex format (regex: `^[%x]{8}%-[%x]{4}%-[%x]{4}%-[%x]{4}%-[%x]{12}$`, version-agnostic), `emissionTime` equal to `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` sampled at insertion, and all other fields matching the call's arguments exactly.

**H.2** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN the server receives an `Emit()` call with any one of the seven required payload fields missing or null, WHEN the call is processed, THEN the call is rejected, no point source is inserted, and a server-side error log entry is written identifying the missing field.

**H.3** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN an `Emit()` call with `initialMagnitude = 0` or `< 0`, WHEN schema validation runs, THEN the emission is rejected (E.1) and no source is inserted, regardless of other fields being valid.

**H.3b** — AUTO-UNIT (round-7a R7-B11 expanded — canonical NaN/Infinity idiom enforcement) — GIVEN an `Emit()` call where `position.X`, `position.Y`, or `position.Z` is **(a)** NaN (constructed via `0/0`), **(b)** +Infinity (constructed via `math.huge`), or **(c)** −Infinity (constructed via `-math.huge`), AND a separate fourth invocation where `initialMagnitude` is NaN, AND a separate fifth invocation where `initialMagnitude` is +Infinity, WHEN schema validation runs on each invocation independently, THEN each emission is rejected (E.16), no source is inserted, no archive entry is written, and a server-side error log entry records `sourcePlayerId`, `emissionType`, and the offending coordinate axis (or `"initialMagnitude"` for the latter two cases). Each of the five cases (a)–(e) MUST be a separately-asserted invocation — a single payload that violates ALL five at once does NOT satisfy this AC, because the implementation could short-circuit on the first axis check while leaving downstream axis-checks dead-code (round-7a R7-B11 lesson: same plural-but-not-three escape pattern as H.31c). **Round-7a CI lint additional check:** the test fixture MUST also run a grep over `DisturbanceService` source for the canonical idiom `n == n and n ~= math.huge and n ~= -math.huge` (or the equivalent `n == n and math.abs(n) ~= math.huge`) and assert at least one occurrence in the schema-validation code path; this catches a regression where a programmer reverts to a non-canonical finite-check that misses NaN (e.g., `n > 0` alone — Infinity passes; e.g., `n < math.huge` alone — NaN passes because all comparisons with NaN return false).

**H.3c** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN an `Emit()` call whose `emissionType` is a string not in the canonical enum `{Gather, Sprint, Light, Beacon, Craft}`, WHEN schema validation runs, THEN the emission is rejected (C.1.2 enum constraint) and no source is inserted.

**H.4** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a player has been in continuous sprint state for the duration of `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL`, WHEN Player Controller issues the recurring sprint emission, THEN exactly one Sprint emission is published — not zero, not two — and each successive pulse carries a distinct `emissionId`.

**H.5** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a player raises their lantern while brightness exceeds the ambient threshold AND the test harness uses `DisturbanceService`'s injectable test-clock seam (test-only `_setTestClock(fn)` API on the service, declared at the service-internal layer — never exposed via `Service.Client`) to advance time deterministically, WHEN the test clock advances by exactly `LIGHT_PULSE_INTERVAL` without the lantern being lowered AND the player has moved more than `EMITTER_MOVEMENT_DELTA_FLOOR` in the interval, THEN a second `Light` emission is published at full magnitude at the player's then-current position; if the player has not moved that far, the emission is published at `MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE × STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR` per C.1.1; if the lantern is lowered before the test clock crosses `LIGHT_PULSE_INTERVAL`, no second emission is published. Test isolation: the clock seam exists in the service to make this AC deterministic; production code uses `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` per D.1.

**H.5b** — AUTO-UNIT (stationary-sprint published magnitude — UPDATED 2026-06-19, ED-yields ruling) — GIVEN a player has been in continuous sprint state for `SPRINT_PULSE_INTERVAL` AND has moved less than `EMITTER_MOVEMENT_DELTA_FLOOR` (default 1 stud) in that interval, WHEN Player Controller issues the recurring sprint emission, THEN the emission's `initialMagnitude` equals `max(SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR, MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE × STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR) = max(0.09, 0.10 × 0.30 = 0.030) = **0.09**` ± 0.001 — PC's `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR` (0.09) dominates ED's `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR` attenuation for Sprint (the test asserts the published value is the hold-floor 0.09, NOT the attenuated 0.030; a value of 0.030 FAILS this AC). The first pulse on sprint-state-entry is exempt and fires at full magnitude (`MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE` = 0.10) regardless of movement (C.1.1). `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR` still binds Light (no hold-floor) — see the D.7 "Lantern stationary steady-state" worked example (≈ 0.272, Calm); no `H.5c` AC exists (round-22 gate note removed the prior "H.5c if present" hedge). **Branch-coverage scope note (round-22 I21):** this AC exercises only the hold-floor branch of the `max()` because, under the current published safe ranges, the attenuated branch (`MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE × STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR ≤ 0.15 × 0.60 = 0.09`) cannot strictly exceed `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR`'s safe-range floor (> 0.08) at defaults — the untested branch is structurally unreachable, not untested-by-omission. **If a future retune widens `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR` or `MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE` such that their product can exceed `SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR`, this AC MUST be split to assert BOTH `max()` branches in the same patch session** — otherwise the attenuated branch silently loses coverage.

**H.6** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a Beacon emission is submitted with `initialMagnitude` below 0.85, WHEN schema validation runs, THEN the emission is rejected — values below 0.85 are reserved exclusively for non-Beacon types per C.1.2.

**H.6b** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a non-Beacon emission (`emissionType ∈ {"Gather", "Sprint", "Light", "Craft"}`) is submitted with `initialMagnitude > 0.85`, WHEN schema validation runs, THEN the emission is rejected — values above 0.85 are reserved for Beacon per C.1.2 (the reverse-direction check; H.6 covers Beacon-too-low; this covers non-Beacon-too-high; round-7a confirms `Craft` is subject to the same `> 0.85` reject rule as Gather/Sprint/Light — `MAGNITUDE_CRAFT_HEAVY = 0.35` is well below the reserved band).

### Decay (Formula D.1 / Rules C.1.4, C.1.12)

**H.7** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a Gather/Sprint/Light emission with known `initialMagnitude` and `emissionTime`, WHEN evaluated at `age = STANDARD_HALF_LIFE`, THEN `m(age)` equals `initialMagnitude × 0.5` ± 0.001.
