**H.7b** — AUTO-UNIT (regression for D.1 clock spec; round-4 scope correction) — GIVEN the test harness has installed a counter wrapping `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` AND `os.time()` AND `tick()` AND `os.clock()`, WHEN the test exercises (a) `DisturbanceService:Emit()` (where `emissionTime` is recorded), (b) the field-update pass enclosed by the `DisturbanceService_Update` `debug.profilebegin` scope per H.36a (where `serverTime` is read for `m(age)` evaluation), and (c) any code path computing `m(age)` per D.1 (decay-cache population, lazy expiry checks, post-hitch settle), THEN within those scopes `os.time()`, `tick()`, and `os.clock()` were called **zero times** and `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` was the sole clock source. **Out-of-scope (legitimate `os.time()` callers within DisturbanceService that this AC does NOT flag):** server-side ops-log timestamp formatting (E.21 / H.32c) and any human-readable error/diagnostic log lines — those use `os.time()` for wall-clock display and are not in the field-update / decay code path. The AC's counter scopes specifically to `Emit()`, the `DisturbanceService_Update` profilebegin region, and decay evaluations — NOT to `DisturbanceService` source globally. Catches the regression where a programmer reverts D.1's mandated clock source on the field-update / decay path; does NOT false-positive on logging.

**H.8** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a Beacon emission with known `initialMagnitude`, WHEN evaluated at `age = BEACON_HALF_LIFE`, THEN `m(age)` equals `initialMagnitude × 0.5` ± 0.001.

**H.9** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a point source whose `m(age)` has just dropped strictly below `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR` (i.e., `m < MAGNITUDE_FLOOR`), WHEN the next maintenance pass runs (within `EXPIRY_MAINTENANCE_INTERVAL` seconds), THEN the source is no longer present in the live-source list and contributes zero to subsequent `fieldValue()` queries. Per C.1.12, the expiry comparison is strict less-than: a source with `m == MAGNITUDE_FLOOR` exactly is NOT culled (it survives one more pass).

**H.9b** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN the test harness has installed a counter wrapping `math.exp` (via the `_setExpFn(fn)` injection seam on `DisturbanceService` — declared service-internal, never exposed via `Service.Client`) AND the live-source list contains 100 sources AND the field is queried at 50 distinct positions during a single update pass, WHEN the field-update pass completes, THEN `math.exp` was called at most 100 times during the pass — not 5,000. Verifies the per-source-per-pass caching mandate in C.1.13.

**H.9c** — AUTO-UNIT (forbidden-transcendental check) — GIVEN the test harness has installed counters wrapping `math.pow` and `math.sqrt` AND has a static-analysis pass (grep- or AST-level) over the inner contribution loop body in `DisturbanceService` source AND the live-source list contains 100 sources distributed such that exactly 30 sources fall within `INFLUENCE_RADIUS` of every query position used during the test (i.e., the 30 in-radius sources are the SAME 30 across all query positions; the test fixture controls source/query placement to ensure this), WHEN the field-update pass over Q query positions completes AND one `GetHottestHotspot` query (visiting C dedup-distinct candidate positions plus 1 additional `topContributorsAt(bestPosition, 3)` call which runs the inner loop one more time at `bestPosition`) completes, THEN: (a) `math.pow` was called zero times anywhere in the inner contribution loop body; (b) the static-analysis pass finds zero occurrences of the Luau `^` numeric operator anywhere in the inner contribution loop body (file-name and source-region scoped to `DisturbanceService`'s D.2 / D.3 helpers and the D.6 candidate-scoring inner loop — outside that scope `^` is unrestricted); (c) `math.sqrt` was called at most **`30 × (Q + C + 1)`** times (only for sources passing the squared-distance radius cull, never for culled sources; the `+1` accounts for `topContributorsAt`'s inner-loop call at `bestPosition`). For the canonical test setup (Q = 20 query positions, C = 5 hotspot candidate positions): expected upper bound is **`30 × 26 = 780`** sqrt calls. Verifies C.1.13 forbidden-transcendentals rule including the Luau `^` operator restriction (round-4 addition).

**H.10** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a source with `initialMagnitude = MAGNITUDE_FLOOR`, WHEN `fieldValue()` is queried at the source position immediately after insertion, THEN the source contributes its full (negligible) magnitude at age=0; on the next maintenance pass after `m` decays strictly below `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR` it is culled (E.2 + C.1.12).

### Spatial Field (Formulas D.2, D.3 / Rules C.1.3, C.1.5)

**H.11** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a single live source at S with current decayed magnitude `m`, WHEN `fieldValue()` is queried at P where `dist2D(P, S) = 0`, THEN the returned value equals `m` ± 0.001.

**H.12** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a single live source at S, WHEN `fieldValue()` is queried at P where `dist2D(P, S) = INFLUENCE_RADIUS`, THEN the returned value is exactly 0.0.

**H.13** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a single live source at S, WHEN `fieldValue()` is queried at P where `dist2D(P, S) > INFLUENCE_RADIUS`, THEN the returned value is exactly 0.0.

**H.14** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN two independent live sources S1 and S2 both within `INFLUENCE_RADIUS` of P, WHEN `fieldValue(P)` is computed, THEN the result equals `contribution(P, S1) + contribution(P, S2)` — sources stack additively (C.1.5). Holds even when S1 and S2 share the same `sourcePlayerId`.

**H.15** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN enough live sources near P that summed contributions exceed 1.0, WHEN `fieldValue(P)` is returned, THEN the value is clamped at 1.0; each individual source's stored `m(t, age)` is unaffected by the clamp.

**H.16** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN two sources at identical XZ but different Y (e.g., S1 at Y=0, S2 at Y=40), WHEN `fieldValue()` is queried at a coplanar point P, THEN both sources contribute based on XZ distance only — Y separation is irrelevant (C.1.3 2D rule).

### Tier Classification (Formula D.4 / Rule C.1.7)

**H.17** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a position whose `fieldValue` rises from below `TENSE_THRESHOLD` to exactly `TENSE_THRESHOLD`, WHEN `tierAt(P, "Calm")` is evaluated, THEN the returned tier is "Tense" (no upward hysteresis).

**H.17b** — AUTO-UNIT (first-call Hunt; resolves round-3 D.4 first-call ruling) — GIVEN a position whose `fieldValue = 0.85` (Hunt range, between HUNT_THRESHOLD and RETREAT_THRESHOLD) AND `previousTier = "Calm"` (first-call default), WHEN `tierAt(P, "Calm")` is evaluated, THEN the returned tier is "Hunt" — confirming the flat-threshold dispatch on the Calm branch (single-step ladder is bypassed only on first-call).

**H.17c** — AUTO-UNIT (first-call Retreat) — GIVEN a position whose `fieldValue = 1.00` (clamped, Retreat) AND `previousTier = "Calm"`, WHEN `tierAt(P, "Calm")` is evaluated, THEN the returned tier is "Retreat".

**H.18** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a position in "Tense" whose `fieldValue` falls to `TENSE_THRESHOLD − HYSTERESIS_BAND + 0.001`, WHEN `tierAt(P, "Tense")` is evaluated, THEN the returned tier remains "Tense".

**H.19** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a position in "Tense" whose `fieldValue` falls to strictly below `TENSE_THRESHOLD − HYSTERESIS_BAND`, WHEN `tierAt(P, "Tense")` is evaluated, THEN the returned tier is "Calm".

**H.20** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN `tierAt()` is called with `previousTier="Hunt"` and `fieldValue` at `HUNT_THRESHOLD − HYSTERESIS_BAND − 0.001`, WHEN evaluated, THEN the returned tier is "Tense" — Hunt→Tense hysteresis applies independently of Tense→Calm.

**H.21** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a position in "Retreat" whose `fieldValue` decays to `1.0 − HYSTERESIS_BAND − 0.001`, WHEN `tierAt(P, "Retreat")` is evaluated, THEN the returned tier is "Hunt".

### Squad Aggregate (Formula D.5)

**H.22** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a 4-player session with `fieldValue` at each player's position in {0.71, 0.34, 0.09, 0.02}, WHEN the server computes `squadT`, THEN the result is 0.71 — the maximum of the four values, not the mean or sum.

**H.22b** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN zero connected players (the `Players:GetPlayers()` set is empty), WHEN the server computes `squadT` per D.5, THEN the result is exactly 0.0, no error is raised, and `math.max()` is not called with zero arguments. Verifies the empty-player guard in D.5 (E.15).

**H.22c** — AUTO-UNIT (round-23 B1 — `GetSquadAggregateT` exclusion-form semantics; closes PA R5/OQ.10's ED-side contract) — GIVEN a 4-player session with `fieldValue` at the players' positions {P1 = 0.71, P2 = 0.34, P3 = 0.09, P4 = 0.02}, WHEN `GetSquadAggregateT(P1)` is called, THEN the result is 0.34 ± 0.001 (the max over the remaining three — the loudest player's own read is excluded); WHEN `GetSquadAggregateT(nil)` (or no argument) is called, THEN the result is 0.71 ± 0.001 (the unfiltered D.5 max — H.22 unchanged); AND GIVEN a 1-player session where the lone connected player P reads `fieldValue = 0.55`, WHEN `GetSquadAggregateT(P)` is called, THEN the result is 0.55 ± 0.001 — the empty-remainder branch returns the UNFILTERED max per PA H.57's solo semantics (NOT 0.0, NOT nil, no error); AND GIVEN zero connected players, WHEN called with any argument, THEN the result is exactly 0.0 with no `math.max()`-on-empty error (E.15 / H.22b parity); AND GIVEN the H.25c corrupted-read configuration in its SINGLE-connected-player form — P_probe is the ONLY connected player, so the exclusion form's empty-remainder branch re-reads the corrupted value (round-24 Gate-B2 F5: a multi-player fixture with clean other players would pass vacuously without the guard ever executing) — WHEN the exclusion form computes, THEN the D.5 finiteness guard applies identically (returns 0.0, never NaN/+Inf).

### Hotspot Query (Formula D.6)

**H.23** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN no live sources exist anywhere, WHEN `GetHottestHotspot(callerPosition, searchRadius, "Tense")` is called with any finite `callerPosition`, THEN the function returns `nil` and does not error (E.5).

**H.24** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN multiple live sources exist but none produce a `fieldValue` at or above the `tierFloor` for `minimumTier`, WHEN `GetHottestHotspot()` is called with that `minimumTier`, THEN the function returns `nil` (E.5b).

**H.25** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN three live sources within `searchRadius` where source A produces the highest `fieldValue` at its position, WHEN `GetHottestHotspot()` returns a result, THEN `result.fieldValue` matches `fieldValue(A.position)` ± 0.001 and `result.topContributingEmissionIds` contains A's `emissionId` as the first entry.

**H.25b** — AUTO-UNIT (round-7a R7-B10 — non-nil HotspotResult implies non-empty topContributingEmissionIds; gameplay-programmer F5 contract gap closure) — GIVEN any live-source configuration that causes `GetHottestHotspot()` to return a non-nil `HotspotResult`, WHEN the result is inspected, THEN `#result.topContributingEmissionIds >= 1` AND `result.topContributingEmissionIds[1]` is a valid `emissionId` (matches the H.1 UUID 8-4-4-4-12 hex regex) that resolves in the live-source list. **Negative path:** when `GetHottestHotspot` returns nil (E.5/E.5b), no topContributingEmissionIds value is exposed (the consumer never sees an empty list alongside a non-nil result). This AC closes the C.3.3 contract gap where the prior AC harness covered the "ordered by contribution descending" rule (H.25) but did not assert the minimum-length invariant; a regression that returned a non-nil HotspotResult with an empty `topContributingEmissionIds` would have been silently accepted, breaking attribution downstream (predator's stored `topContributingEmissionIds` would also be empty, and DeathAttributionPayload's `killingEmissions` would be empty too — a different failure mode than E.9 "no recent disturbance," and one that would erroneously pass H.30).

**H.25c** — AUTO-UNIT (round-22 B11 — non-finite disqualification guard coverage; a regression that deleted the D.6 in-loop guard or the D.5 squadT guard was previously test-invisible) — GIVEN the test harness constructs a live-source configuration whose D.3 evaluation at one candidate position yields NaN or `+math.huge` at the D.6 scoring loop — via `_setLiveSourceState` (the round-13 construction primitive, documented at H.39c (a) as having no invariant-enforcement layer, so it CAN construct a non-finite `initialMagnitude`; `_injectSyntheticEmission` CANNOT be used here because E.24/H.31c mandate it reject non-finite payloads) OR via `_setExpFn` injecting a function that returns a non-finite value for a targeted source — AND a second, legitimate finite candidate scoring 0.70 exists within `searchRadius`, AND (for clause (d)) a connected player P_probe is positioned within `INFLUENCE_RADIUS` of the corrupted source such that `fieldValue(P_probe.position)` is computed THROUGH the corrupted contribution — the D.5 `max()` input demonstrably routes through the non-finite value (round-23 B6: without this placement requirement, clause (d) was satisfiable by fixture placement alone with the D.5 guard deleted — the round-21 B11 class recurring), WHEN `GetHottestHotspot(callerPosition, searchRadius, "Tense")` is called, THEN: (a) the non-finite candidate is **disqualified** — it does not set `bestPosition`, does not increment `hotspotCount`, and is never returned; (b) the returned `HotspotResult` is the legitimate finite candidate (`fieldValue = 0.70 ± 0.001`, satisfying `fieldValue == fieldValue and fieldValue ~= math.huge` and meeting `minimumTier`); (c) with the legitimate candidate removed from the fixture, the same call returns `nil` (NOT a non-nil result with fabricated `fieldValue = 0.0` — the round-21 post-loop-guard failure mode); (d) under the same corrupted configuration WITH the P_probe placement from the GIVEN, the D.5 squad-aggregate path publishes `squadT = 0.0` (the D.5 round-22 finiteness guard's fail-quiet value), `GetSquadAggregateT()` returns the same in BOTH argument forms (nil and exclusion — H.22c), AND P_probe's `OnMeterUpdate` payload carries `playerT = 0.0` (the round-23 SD-2 playerT-guard fail-quiet value) — never NaN/+Inf on any of the three reads; **falsifiability control (round-23 B6):** the harness calls `fieldValue(P_probe.position)` DIRECTLY (the raw D.2/D.3 query exercised by H.11–H.16 — it carries no finiteness guard; round-24 Gate-B2 F1 names the read seam) and asserts the result is non-finite under BOTH corruption mechanisms — one NaN-flavoured run AND one `+math.huge`-flavoured run (round-24 Gate-B2 F3: a `value ~= value`-only guard passes a NaN-only test while leaking +Inf) — i.e., the corruption demonstrably reaches the D.5/playerT guard inputs, so a passing 0.0 publish can only be produced by the guards executing, never by fixture placement (this control is what makes clause (d) falsifiable rather than vacuously satisfiable). **Grep sub-AC (H.9c-style):** a static-analysis pass asserts the non-finite-disqualification idiom (`score ~= score or score == math.huge`, or equivalent) appears INSIDE the D.6 candidate-scoring loop body (before the tierFloor gate), and the DUAL-condition finiteness idiom (`v ~= v or v == math.huge`, or equivalent) appears in the D.5 squadT publish path AND in the per-player playerT publish path (round-23 SD-2; round-24 Gate-B2 F3 pins the dual idiom at these two sites to match the D.6 loop's — a NaN-only check MUST fail this grep) — catching silent deletion or half-deletion of any of the three guards (presence only; clause (d)'s falsifiability control is what proves effectiveness — round-23 B6).

**H.25d** — AUTO-UNIT (round-22 B3 — `hotspotId` stability + format; the contract Predator AI's tested AC H.8 tie-break/debounce depends on, closing PA OQ.11/FIX-13) — GIVEN a live-source configuration where dedup cell K1 wins `GetHottestHotspot` AND the harness advances the test clock such that the first-encountered source in K1 expires while ANOTHER source in the SAME cell K1 keeps the cell winning (the representative `position` within the cell shifts), WHEN two successive `GetHottestHotspot` calls are made (one before, one after the turnover), THEN: (a) both results carry the **identical** `hotspotId` (the winning cell is unchanged — identity is stable across representative-position turnover) even though `result.position` MAY differ between the calls; (b) `hotspotId` matches the canonical dedup-key format `string.format("%d_%d", math.floor(P.X / HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE), math.floor(P.Z / HOTSPOT_DEDUP_GRID_SIZE))` evaluated at the returned `position` (regex `^%-?%d+_%-?%d+$`); AND GIVEN a follow-up configuration where a DIFFERENT dedup cell K2 wins, WHEN queried, THEN the returned `hotspotId` differs from K1's. Verifies the C.3.3 `HotspotResult.hotspotId` contract; Predator AI's consume-side tie-break behaviour (epsilon + squared-distance + lowest stable id) remains a PA GDD AC (H.8), not asserted here.

### Cross-System Wiring (Rules C.3.1 – C.3.5)

**H.26** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN Resource Node completes a gather action by a connected player, WHEN the gather reward is granted, THEN a Gather emission has already been recorded in `DisturbanceService`'s live-source list with matching `sourcePlayerId` and `position` — confirming C.3.1's emit-before-reward ordering.

**H.27** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a player enters sprint state from a position with `fieldValue` baseline = 0.0 (clean field), WHEN the first sprint pulse fires AND the test queries `fieldValue()` at the player's position within the same update cycle (no decay applied yet, age = 0), THEN the returned value equals `MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE` ± 0.001 — confirming Player Controller→DisturbanceService wiring (C.3.2). The pre-sprint baseline is mandated to be 0.0 to remove ambiguity; if testing on a non-empty field is required, use a separate AC with explicit baseline capture.

**H.28** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN the disturbance field at a position rises above `TENSE_THRESHOLD` due to a new emission, WHEN the field update loop runs, THEN a server-internal `TierCrossedEvent` fires carrying `crossedTier = "Tense"`, the highest-cumulative position among same-pass crossings of that boundary, and that position's most-contributing `emissionId`; a subscribed mock Predator AI callback receives this event within `1 / FIELD_UPDATE_HZ` seconds.

**H.28b** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (round-3 coalescing) — GIVEN three positions cross `TENSE_THRESHOLD` in the same field-update pass AND a mock Predator AI subscribes to `TierCrossedEvent`, WHEN the pass completes, THEN exactly one `TierCrossedEvent` fires for `crossedTier = "Tense"` (not three), and the event's `position` corresponds to the highest-`fieldValue` position among the three crossings. Verifies server-side per-pass coalescing rule in C.3.3.

**H.28c** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (nil-on-query post-TierCrossedEvent — round-4 addition; round-5 constant-replaced-with-concrete-value) — GIVEN a `TierCrossedEvent` fires for `crossedTier = "Tense"` at position P (test-fixture sets up a single source at P with `initialMagnitude` chosen so `fieldValue(P)` settles at exactly `0.31` immediately after insertion — just above `TENSE_THRESHOLD = 0.30`, sufficient to trigger the event without overshooting Hunt) AND between the event firing and the next harness-controlled `GetHottestHotspot` call, the test harness advances the test clock (via `_advanceTestClock`) past `ln(0.31 / MAGNITUDE_FLOOR) / (ln(2) / STANDARD_HALF_LIFE) = ln(15.5) / (ln(2)/30) ≈ 119 s` (the time at which the triggering-sum decays strictly below `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR` and is culled by the maintenance pass), WHEN the harness calls `DisturbanceService:GetHottestHotspot(callerPosition, searchRadius = 200, minimumTier = "Tense")` (the harness supplies a representative finite Vector3 as `callerPosition` per round-17 I16-11), THEN the call returns `nil` (consistent with E.5/E.5b — no candidate meets `minimumTier`). The Predator AI's response to this nil return (remain in current state per C.3.3 nil-recovery rule) is a Predator AI GDD AC obligation, but this AC verifies the disturbance contract surface produces nil correctly when the activation signal's underlying field state has decayed away. **Without this AC, a regression that returned a non-nil stale-value `HotspotResult` after sources expired would be invisible to TierCrossedEvent-driven flows.** **Round-5 note:** the original wording invoked an undefined constant `MAGNITUDE_TENSE_TRIGGERING_SUM`. The replacement uses the concrete value `0.31` (a representative triggering-sum just above `TENSE_THRESHOLD`); the test fixture is responsible for arranging the live-source list to produce this value.

**H.28d** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (squad-aggregate band-crossing producer; Session C `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` contract — closes Player Controller F.4) — GIVEN a mock Player Controller subscriber is connected to the server-internal `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` signal AND the test harness drives `squadT` (via controlled emissions and the injectable test-clock) across the D.4 tier boundaries, WHEN the field-update pass runs, THEN: **(a)** exactly one `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed(direction, newTier, timestamp)` fires per field-update pass per squad-tier transition (per TRANSITION, not an unqualified per-pass ceiling — the clause (e) post-hitch cascade legitimately fires several in one pass, one per crossed boundary) — `direction = "up"` when `squadT` escalates across a boundary (e.g. Calm→Tense at `TENSE_THRESHOLD = 0.30`) and `direction = "down"` when it de-escalates (e.g. Hunt→Tense), `newTier` equals the freshly-classified tier, **the `timestamp` field is sourced from the same server clock as D.1 (`workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` via the injectable test-clock seam `_setTestClock` (Test Infrastructure Prerequisites, `ecological-disturbance-verification.md`; round-23 stray-cite fix — the prior "C.11" anchor did not exist) — the H.7b clock-source discipline applies to this read; round-22 I22)**, and the signal fires **zero times** on any pass where the classified squad tier is unchanged from `previousSquadTier`; **(b)** when `squadT` oscillates within the ±0.03 hysteresis band around a boundary (e.g. crossing 0.30 then falling to 0.285 within the band) without a confirmed classification change, **no** `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` fires — the D.4 hysteresis suppresses boundary chatter, the same mechanism `TierCrossedEvent` uses (H.28); **(c)** the signal has **no `DisturbanceService.Client` surface and no client-reachable input path** — a static/security assertion that `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` is never declared on `Service.Client` (it is a server-internal Knit signal / BindableEvent, never a RemoteEvent/RemoteFunction) and that `squadT` derives solely from server-authoritative player positions plus the server-owned emission field (no client-supplied value can forge a band-crossing; mirrors H.28's server-internal/zero-network property and the C.3.3 binding security clause); **(d)** (round-22 B10 — first-pass bootstrap) GIVEN the test harness drives `squadT` above `TENSE_THRESHOLD` (or above `HUNT_THRESHOLD`) BEFORE the very first field-update pass of the session runs (via the E.24/H.39c synthetic-injection seams), WHEN the first pass classifies `squadT`, THEN `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` fires **zero times** on that pass — `previousSquadTier` initialises to the pass's flat-dispatch classification per the C.3.3 first-pass rule (mirrors H.17b/H.17c's first-call convention) — AND a single correct fire occurs only when a SUBSEQUENT pass crosses a further boundary (or de-escalates). Verifies the C.3.3 `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` producer contract end-to-end (the squad-aggregate counterpart of the position-keyed `TierCrossedEvent` ACs H.28/H.28b); Player Controller's consume-side `direction == "up"` filter is a PC GDD AC obligation, not asserted here. **(e)** (round-23 SD-3 — post-hitch cascade parity) GIVEN the harness drives `squadT` from Retreat-range (≥ 1.0 pre-clamp) to Calm-range (0.20) between two passes (simulated hitch via direct live-source-list mutation at a CONNECTED PLAYER's position — squadT derives from player-position reads, so the drop must occur where a player stands; the H.34b construction — round-24 Gate-B2 F7 pin), WHEN the next field-update pass classifies, THEN `previousSquadTier` converges through the full ladder within that single pass (≤ 4 settle iterations) AND exactly THREE `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` fires occur in ladder order — (Retreat→Hunt), (Hunt→Tense), (Tense→Calm), each `direction = "down"` — NOT one skip-level Retreat→Calm fire and NOT a phantom intermediate tier persisting into the following pass (mirrors H.34b's positional cascade assertions).

**H.29** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (payload schema) — GIVEN the HUD controller has joined the session AND the test harness installs a `MockRemoteSignalRecorder` that records every `OnMeterUpdate` and `OnDisturbanceAlert` payload fired to that specific client, WHEN the server's field update pushes a player's `t` change, THEN the recorder captures a payload whose top-level keys are exactly `{playerT, squadT}` for `OnMeterUpdate` (or the documented schema in C.3.4 for `OnDisturbanceAlert`), and the recorder asserts that NO captured payload contains keys `sources`, `sourceList`, `emissions`, `otherPlayerPosition`, `position`, or any `Vector3`-typed field other than the alert's documented payload. The mock recorder is a test fixture in `tests/helpers/network_recorder.luau` that wraps Knit **`RemoteSignal:Fire` and `:FireAll`** for the duration of the test (round-22 B2 correction: the prior fixture spec named `:FireClient`/`:FireAllClients`, which per C.3.4's round-5 API note are raw-RemoteEvent methods that do NOT exist on Knit RemoteSignal handles — a recorder built against those names would intercept nothing and silently pass every privacy assertion).

**H.29b** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (routing assertion) — GIVEN four mock clients (P1, P2, P3, P4) each have a `MockRemoteSignalRecorder` installed AND each player is at a different `fieldValue` (P1=0.71, P2=0.34, P3=0.09, P4=0.02), WHEN the server's field update fires `OnMeterUpdate` for the cycle, THEN: P1's recorder captures exactly one payload with `playerT = 0.71`; P2's with `playerT = 0.34`; P3's with `playerT = 0.09`; P4's with `playerT = 0.02`; ALL four payloads carry the same `squadT = 0.71`; and `:FireAll` is captured zero times. Verifies C.3.4's targeted-fire-per-player routing rule (the wire-level "FireClient-per-player" effect). The mock recorder MUST be capable of distinguishing Knit `RemoteSignal:Fire(player, ...)` calls (recorded against the targeted player) from `:FireAll(...)` calls (recorded as broadcast) — round-22 B2 correction per C.3.4's round-5 Knit API note (`:FireClient`/`:FireAllClients` are not Knit RemoteSignal methods).

**H.29c** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (CharacterAdded race, E.17) — GIVEN a mock client joins via `Players.PlayerAdded` and has not yet had `CharacterAdded` fire when the next `OnMeterUpdate` cycle runs, WHEN the cycle pushes, THEN the mock client's recorder captures a payload with `playerT = 0.0` and `squadT = currentSquadT`, no error is raised, and no skipped push.

**H.30** — AUTO-INTEGRATION — GIVEN a player dies after the predator locked onto a hotspot driven by a specific `emissionId` AND the test harness installs `MockRemoteSignalRecorder` on all 4 clients, WHEN the server constructs the `DeathAttributionPayload`, THEN the payload contains that `emissionId` with `sourcePlayerId`, `emissionType`, `emissionTime`, and `positionLabel` populated AND is delivered exactly once to the dead player's recorder AND is delivered zero times to the other three players' recorders (C.1.9 / C.1.11 / C.3.4 targeted-fire-only routing). Knit `RemoteSignal:FireAll` for `OnDeathAttributionPushed` MUST register as zero captured events (round-22 B2 correction: `:FireAllClients` is not a Knit RemoteSignal method — see C.3.4's round-5 API note).

**H.30b** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (Beacon attribution chain) — GIVEN a Beacon emission published with placing player's `sourcePlayerId` and `attributionChain = ["BeaconPlaced"]` AND a player subsequently dies with the predator locked onto that Beacon, WHEN the `DeathAttributionPayload` is constructed, THEN the corresponding `killingEmissions` entry has `emissionType = "Beacon"`, `sourcePlayerId` matching the placing player, and the archive entry recorded the `attributionChain` containing `"BeaconPlaced"` (C.3.6).

**H.30c** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (predator-respawn / state-exit cleanup; round-5 / Tier-1 #14 + E.23; round-7c B-7b-3 ordering assertion + `predatorWasLockedAtDeath` flag) — GIVEN a mock predator has called `DisturbanceService:RegisterPredatorLock(predatorId, {emA, emB, emC})` AND `emA` and `emB` still resolve in the attribution archive while `emC` has been purged (its `ATTRIBUTION_ARCHIVE_TTL` elapsed) AND the live-source list and archive contain entries `emA` and `emB` with their original `emissionTime` values, WHEN the test harness simulates a predator state transition out of Hunt / Investigate / Disengage (e.g., the predator's mock state machine transitions to Patrol) AND advances the test clock past the `ATTRIBUTION_ARCHIVE_TTL` for `emA` and `emB` AND the maintenance pass runs, THEN: (a) `emA` and `emB` are purged from the archive on the maintenance pass following their TTL expiry (no extension by the predator's prior registration); (b) `emC` was silently dropped from the predator's registration set at the moment `RegisterPredatorLock` was first called (idempotent silent-drop semantics); (c) on the predator's transition to Patrol, all registrations for `predatorId` are cleared from `DisturbanceService` regardless of whether Predator AI explicitly called `:ReleasePredatorLock` (defense-in-depth observed-state-exit purge); (d) the live-source list does not retain any entry beyond its natural expiry as a result of the predator's prior registration. **Without this AC, `RegisterPredatorLock` could silently keep archive entries alive forever — a memory leak masquerading as long-tail attribution.** **Round-9 B-CC1 cluster ordering + flag assertions (sub-AC, MUST also pass; supersedes the round-7c (e) / (g) wording per DR-3 = Option B fire-ownership move):** (e) GIVEN the test harness installs a `MockOrderingRecorder` (see Test Infrastructure Prerequisites — round-9 addition) that timestamps each of: **(i)** DisturbanceService's `_captureDeathLockSnapshot(deadPlayer)` completion at C.1.11 sub-step (5a) (registered via DisturbanceService:KnitInit's **sole** `Humanoid.Died` subscription chain per C.1.11 step 5 round-11 DR-4 = Option B — verified by H.39e single-subscriber CI lint as the only `Humanoid.Died:Connect` chain in `src/server/**/*.luau`); **(i.5)** sub-step (5b) BindableEvent payload construction completion; **(i.75)** sub-step (5c) `_OnPlayerDied:Fire(payload)` invocation entry; **(ii)** `:GetDeathAttributionPayload(deadPlayer)` construction completion (which reads the cached snapshot, NOT the live registry); **(iii)** predator-state-out-of-Hunt processing for the death-as-trigger inside Predator AI's **`OnPlayerDied:Connect` callback** (round-11 DR-4 = Option B; supersedes the round-9 KnitStart-registered `Humanoid.Died` handler — Predator AI no longer subscribes to `Humanoid.Died` directly per the H.39e monopoly lint); **(iv)** `:ReleasePredatorLock(predatorId)` invocation entry inside DisturbanceService (typically invoked from inside Predator AI's `OnPlayerDied:Connect` callback as the death-as-trigger registry mutation); **(v)** sub-step (4a) `pre_purge_targets` computation completion; **(vi)** sub-step (4b) `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(targetedPlayer, {locked = false})` invocation for each target; **(vii)** sub-step (4c) atomic registry purge completion, AND simulates a player death within the `PREDATOR_LOCK_RELEASE_GRACE_WINDOW` of an active predator-lock chain, THEN the recorder captures the in-frame instruction-sequence ordering as the following call-stack nesting (round-13 R12-B5 nesting-notation rewrite; supersedes the round-11 flat strict-`<` predicate which round-12 found invited incorrect MockOrderingRecorder implementations that treated the eight events as peer-level when in fact (iii)–(vii) execute INSIDE the lexical scope of t_i.75's `:Fire` call and (v)–(vii) execute INSIDE t_iv's `:ReleasePredatorLock` call):

> **Nesting diagram (relocated round-22 AC-extraction; relocation only):** the sub-AC (e) call-stack nesting diagram is in **`ecological-disturbance-verification.md` § H.30c — call-stack nesting diagram**. The strict-precedence chain it encodes is restated normatively in the ordering-predicate paragraph immediately below.

The ordering predicate the `MockOrderingRecorder` MUST verify is the strict-precedence chain implied by this call-stack nesting: `t_i precedes t_i.5 precedes t_i.75 precedes t_iii precedes t_iv precedes t_v precedes t_vi precedes t_vii` in in-frame instruction-sequence order, AND `t_iii through t_vii are lexically nested inside t_i.75's :Fire call` (the recorder MUST capture this nesting via in-frame instruction-counter ascents and descents — every `:Fire` invocation entry pushes a "lexical depth" counter; every callback entry inside that `:Fire` runs at depth+1; depth returns to its prior value when `:Fire` returns), AND `t_v through t_vii are lexically nested inside t_iv's :ReleasePredatorLock invocation` (same depth-counting rule applied to `:ReleasePredatorLock`'s lexical scope). A flat-strict-`<` recorder that captures only sequence-numbers without depth-counters CANNOT distinguish a correct nested-call execution from a buggy peer-level execution; the depth-counted recorder is the load-bearing fixture choice (verify the prereq-table `MockOrderingRecorder` row's "interleaving-log assertion API" capability includes a depth-counter; round-13 forward obligation on the qa-lead to confirm). (ii) `:GetDeathAttributionPayload` may execute at any point AFTER (i) and is permitted to interleave with (iii)–(vii) on the call-site's choice — typically the HUD invokes it inside its own `OnPlayerDied:Connect` callback (which itself runs at depth+1 inside t_i.75's `:Fire` call, peer-level with t_iii but later in the per-callback dispatch order if Predator AI subscribed first; see (5-LC-c) read-and-clear atomicity for the consumer-side rules). **In-frame instruction-sequence semantics (round-11 BLOCK-2 red-team clarification).** Each `<` predicate denotes "occurs strictly before in the in-frame Luau-instruction sequence" — NOT wall-clock-time less-than. The `MockOrderingRecorder` MUST use a monotonic in-test counter that increments on each instrumented event capture (NOT `os.clock()` or `tick()` wall-clock reads), so that two events occurring in distinct in-frame instructions but the same wall-clock nanosecond are still captured with strictly-distinct counter values. Specifically: t_i.75 (`_OnPlayerDied:Fire(payload)` invocation entry) and t_iii (Predator AI's `OnPlayerDied:Connect` callback entry) occur in the same Luau frame but in distinct in-frame instructions — `:Fire` is invoked at t_i.75, then synchronously dispatches to its connected callbacks (Predator AI's callback enters at t_iii inside the same `:Fire` execution) before `:Fire` returns at some later instruction t_i.75-end (which is NOT separately tracked but is implicitly between t_iii's exit and the next instrumented event). The recorder's per-event counter MUST distinguish all these in-frame instructions so the strict ordering `t_i.75 < t_iii < t_iv < ...` is mechanically observable. Wall-clock-based recorders cannot satisfy this AC; the `MockOrderingRecorder` prereq table row MUST mandate the monotonic in-test counter (already specified in the round-9 prereq row as "per-event timestamp capture using a monotonic in-test counter (NOT `os.clock()` or `tick()`)" — BLOCK-2 verifies this requirement is the load-bearing one for sub-AC (e)'s strict-ordering testability) AND the recorder asserts no Heartbeat boundary, no `task.wait`, no `task.spawn`, no `task.delay`, no `:InvokeServer` / `:InvokeClient`, and no `coroutine.yield` appears between t_i and t_vii inclusive (closes B-CC1-E yield-free guarantee — sub-steps (5a)–(5c) AND sub-steps (4a)–(4c) execute within a single Luau frame; synchronous `RemoteSignal:Fire` and `BindableEvent:Fire` are permitted, but their connected callbacks MUST themselves be yield-free across the entire (5a)–(4c) window — the recorder's per-callback instrumentation hooks verify this; H.39e CI lint enforces yield-free callback bodies on `OnPlayerDied:Connect` and on the `OnPredatorLockChanged` server-side fire path). The recorder's instrumentation hook for sub-step (4b) MUST capture the `:Fire` invocation moment, NOT the corresponding client-side receipt; `RemoteSignal:Fire` is non-yielding on the server. (f) GIVEN the dead player's death-time predator-lock state in the snapshot matches the registry's entries for that player, the constructed `DeathAttributionPayload.predatorWasLockedAtDeath` is `true`; GIVEN the dead player's UserId is NOT a `targetedPlayer` of any live RegisterPredatorLock chain at death-time, the flag is `false`. (g) **Round-9 during-release atomicity (replaces round-7c grace-window race AC).** GIVEN a hypothetical scenario where a player's `Humanoid.Died` fires while another predator's `:ReleasePredatorLock(predatorId)` is mid-execution (the test harness uses the round-9 ring-buffer manipulation seam to construct interleaved Heartbeat-aligned conditions), THEN because sub-steps (4a)–(4c) are yield-free within a single Luau frame, the dying player's `Humanoid.Died` handler chain (DisturbanceService's KnitInit-registered handler followed by Predator AI's KnitStart-registered handler) MUST execute either entirely BEFORE sub-step (4a) of the in-flight `:ReleasePredatorLock` begins OR entirely AFTER sub-step (4c) completes — the yield-free guarantee makes the during-release window unobservable from any other Roblox callback. In the BEFORE case: snapshot reads pre-purge state (`predatorWasLockedAtDeath = true` if dead player was a target). In the AFTER case: snapshot reads post-purge state (`predatorWasLockedAtDeath = false`). The HUD's death-screen MUST present "decayed history" framing whenever `predatorWasLockedAtDeath == false` AND `killingEmissions` is non-empty (the AFTER case where attribution still resolves from the still-live archive); the "actively hunted" framing applies whenever `predatorWasLockedAtDeath == true`. (h) **Subscription-monopoly determinism (round-13 R12-B5 split into (h-static) + (h-runtime); supersedes the round-11 collapsed wording that round-12 found structurally tautological — the round-11 lint-only PASS criterion was guaranteed by Lua's BindableEvent semantics regardless of implementation correctness; the non-trivial runtime ordering invariant 'snapshot-before-fire' was no longer isolated and consequently was not load-bearing on a falsifiable test).** The sub-AC is split into two independent halves; both MUST pass.

**(h-static) — Static lint check, delegated to H.39e.** GIVEN the H.39e CI lint is invoked against `src/server/**/*.luau`, **PASS criterion:** H.39e Lint (a) reports exactly one `Humanoid.Died:Connect` (or equivalent — `humanoid.Died:Connect`, `:Connect` chained on a `humanoid.Died` reference) declaration in the entire codebase, AND that declaration is lexically nested inside `function DisturbanceService:KnitInit(`'s body. Zero subscriptions in any other service body. AND H.39e Lint (b) reports zero `task.spawn` / `task.wait` / `task.delay` / `coroutine.yield` / `:InvokeServer` / `:InvokeClient` / `RunService.Heartbeat:Wait` (etc., per the H.39e canonical forbidden-primitive list) inside any `OnPlayerDied:Connect` callback body. Zero counter-examples permitted. (h-static) is **delegated** to H.39e — the verification path is the H.39e CI gate; H.30c does not duplicate the H.39e check, it only declares that H.39e's PASS state is required for sub-AC (h) to evaluate true. (h-static) is the structural closure of B-CC1-B: no connection-order race is possible because only one subscriber exists.

**(h-runtime) — Runtime 100-trial behavioural harness with snapshot-before-fire as the SINGLE load-bearing assertion.** GIVEN the test harness simulates 100 session-startups in which DisturbanceService and Predator AI's Knit init-order is randomized (using `MockOrderingRecorder`'s `RandomizeKnitInitOrder(seed)` helper with seed = trial-index for reproducibility) AND for each session a player dies while a predator has them locked AND the test harness instruments DisturbanceService's `_captureDeathLockSnapshot` and `_OnPlayerDied:Fire` invocations with `MockOrderingRecorder` per the (e) sub-AC instrumentation rules, **THEN in 100 of 100 sessions the `MockOrderingRecorder` captures the snapshot-before-fire ordering predicate**: `t_capture(_captureDeathLockSnapshot completion at sub-step 5a) < t_fire(_OnPlayerDied:Fire invocation entry at sub-step 5c)`. **Single load-bearing assertion** — sub-AC (h-runtime)'s pass criterion is exactly this one ordering predicate, NOT the broader (e) ordering chain (which is sub-AC (e)'s responsibility). The 100-trial harness produces zero counter-examples; one counter-example fails (h-runtime). **Why this predicate is non-trivial (round-13 R12-B5 design rationale):** Lua's BindableEvent semantics guarantee synchronous-fan-out from `:Fire(payload)` to connected callbacks regardless of implementation correctness, so the `t_fire < t_callback` portion of (e)'s ordering chain is platform-guaranteed and consequently not falsifiable as a regression target. The `t_capture < t_fire` predicate IS falsifiable because it tests an implementation-side invariant: that the `Humanoid.Died` handler's body executes sub-step (5a) BEFORE sub-step (5c) within the same Luau frame. A buggy implementation that re-orders the (5a)/(5b)/(5c) sub-steps OR that introduces a yield primitive between them (which would fail H.39e Lint (b) at static-check time but could be inserted via a non-`OnPlayerDied:Connect`-callback path that bypasses H.39e's grep scope — e.g., a refactor that moves `_captureDeathLockSnapshot` into a `task.spawn`-driven coroutine for "performance reasons") would produce counter-examples in (h-runtime) where `t_capture > t_fire` or `t_capture` is not captured at all (snapshot was deferred to a later frame). The single-assertion formulation makes the regression target explicit and unambiguous.

**Plus secondary assertion (h-runtime-flag):** for each of the 100 sessions where `t_capture < t_fire` holds, the captured `predatorWasLockedAtDeath` flag in the resulting `DeathAttributionPayload` MUST be `true` (the dying player was locked by a predator at death-time per the test setup). This is a flag-correctness check consequent on the ordering, not the load-bearing ordering check itself; failures here indicate a bug in the snapshot-construction path (e.g., wrong registry-state read) rather than an ordering defect. (h-runtime-flag) MUST also pass for sub-AC (h-runtime) to evaluate true overall, but the single load-bearing assertion is the ordering predicate per the round-13 R12-B5 split rationale.

**Why this two-part split closes round-12's structural-tautology finding:** the round-11 wording's "Plus integration sub-test" presented the ordering check as secondary to the H.39e lint, structurally implying that the lint-passing state was the primary pass condition. Round-12 found this tautological because the lint-passing state is platform-guaranteed by BindableEvent semantics regardless of implementation correctness — the lint cannot fail on any implementation that uses `:Fire`, even a buggy one. Round-13 separates the two checks into independently-meaningful primary halves: (h-static) is the structural-architecture verification (only one subscriber exists), (h-runtime) is the implementation-behaviour verification (snapshot precedes fire within the frame). Each half is independently falsifiable; the combined sub-AC (h) is the conjunction. (i) **Concurrent task.spawn invariant + yield-free regression backstop (round-9 closes B-CC1-C; round-11 IMPORTANT 2-spec convergent reframe — relabel from AUTO-INTEGRATION-only to MANUAL-REVIEW-primary + AUTO-INTEGRATION-regression-backstop).** Round-10 ai-programmer F5 + qa-lead F5 convergent finding: the AUTO-INTEGRATION test as written is tautologically true under the (4a)–(4c) yield-free contract — IF the implementation is yield-free, no concurrent reader can observe a mid-purge state, period. The test cannot fail on a correct implementation, so it does not catch the meaningful regression class: a future PR that ADDS a yield primitive (e.g., `task.spawn` for performance reasons) inside (4a)–(4c). Round-11 reframes the sub-AC as a MANUAL-REVIEW yield-free regression test (primary) with the AUTO-INTEGRATION concurrent-reader test as a regression backstop (secondary).

**Primary — MANUAL-REVIEW yield-free regression check.** GIVEN any PR touching `src/server/services/DisturbanceService/**` AND specifically the `:ReleasePredatorLock` method body or the `_OnPlayerDied:Fire` dispatch path (sub-step (5c)) or any code path lexically nested in those bodies, two engineers independently inspect the changed code paths AND attest in PR-blocking review that ZERO yield primitives appear in sub-steps (4a)–(4c) AND (5a)–(5c) — specifically verifying absence of: `task.wait`, `task.spawn`, `task.delay`, `coroutine.yield`, `:InvokeServer`, `:InvokeClient`, `RunService.Heartbeat:Wait`, `.Stepped:Wait`, `.RenderStepped:Wait`, `:Wait` on any signal-or-event, and any other Roblox primitive that surrenders control. **Round-13 red-team B2 closure: the round-11 wording's "(except as the tail-call exception per H.39e lint (b))" parenthetical is REMOVED — round-13 R12-B1 closed the tail-call exception in H.39e Lint (b) absolutely (no exceptions, no AST inference); this MANUAL-REVIEW check inherits the same no-exceptions semantics. `task.spawn` is forbidden in the listed code paths without qualification.** Both attestations MUST be recorded in `production/qa/evidence/H30c-i-yield-free-review-[sprint-id].md` in the H.39d evidence-file format. The H.39e CI lint (round-11; lint (b) and lint (c)) provides AUTO grep-level detection of the most common forbidden primitives, but lexical grep cannot catch subtle cases (e.g., a `:Wait` on a custom signal type that wraps `coroutine.yield`); the two-engineer review fills the grep gap.

**CI gate trigger path filter — recursive, NOT inherited from H.39d (round-15 R14-B10 closure of round-13 R13-I4 elevation; CD-elevated to BLOCKING per the severity-floor rule for server-authoritative primary write seams — `:ReleasePredatorLock` is the primary write-side seam for the `_predatorLockRegistry` server-state purge that H.30c sub-AC (i) is verifying; honor-system inheritance from H.39d's Client-only filter violates project Forbidden Patterns rule on server-authoritative seams).** The CI gate that triggers this MANUAL-REVIEW MUST have path filter `src/server/services/DisturbanceService/**/*.luau` (recursive — covers the entire DisturbanceService source tree, NOT just the `Client/` subdirectory). The DevOps Engineer MUST author a CI gate parallel to H.39d's CI gate (the gate's grep-pattern: any modified file path under `src/server/services/DisturbanceService/**/*.luau` that includes any of `:ReleasePredatorLock`, `_OnPlayerDied:Fire`, `_predatorLockRegistry`, `_captureDeathLockSnapshot`, OR any function declaration whose body lexically contains `_OnPlayerDied:Fire`) AND the gate demands the H.30c-i evidence file `production/qa/evidence/H30c-i-yield-free-review-[sprint-id].md` is present with the correct two-reviewer attestation format per the H.39d evidence-file model. **The implementation epic is BLOCKED on this CI gate's presence per round-15 R14-B10 binding ruling**, parallel to H.39d's round-7a R7-B16 binding ruling AND H.39e's round-11 binding ruling AND H.39f's round-13 binding ruling. Without the CI gate, a future PR could touch `:ReleasePredatorLock`'s body or `_OnPlayerDied:Fire` dispatch path without producing the required evidence file, and the H.30c sub-AC (i) MANUAL-REVIEW would be honor-system on the project's server-authoritative primary write-side seam — a project Forbidden Patterns rule violation. The CI gate is the structural enforcement that makes the MANUAL-REVIEW load-bearing on server-state-purge correctness, NOT just on Client-callable surfaces (which is H.39d's scope).

**Why the path filter MUST be recursive (round-15 R14-B10 design rationale; round-23 B7 note: H.39d's own gate has now ALSO been converted to the recursive-filter + content-grep model — the Client-only-filter contrast described below is historical).** `:ReleasePredatorLock` is declared on DisturbanceService's main service table (server-internal accessor), NOT on `DisturbanceService.Client`. The H.39d CI gate's path filter `src/server/services/DisturbanceService/Client/**` does NOT cover `src/server/services/DisturbanceService/Init.luau` or `src/server/services/DisturbanceService/PredatorLockRegistry.luau` (or wherever the registry's purge logic actually lives — implementation-flexible per the ADR), so the H.39d gate WOULD NOT FIRE on a PR that touches `:ReleasePredatorLock`'s body alone. R13-I4 surfaced this as a forward obligation; round-15 R14-B10 closes inline because deferring an honor-system on the server-authoritative primary write path violates the project's Forbidden Patterns rule (per `.claude/docs/technical-preferences.md`: "Trusting client-supplied values for gameplay-critical state (always validate server-side)" — the analog at the implementation-side is "Trusting honor-system reviewers on server-authoritative state purges (always CI-mechanically gate the path)"). The recursive filter is the only filter shape that covers every reachable location for the `:ReleasePredatorLock` body across the DisturbanceService source tree's evolution.

> **Enforcement (relocated round-22 AC-extraction; relocation only):** the normative CI-gate YAML (`h30c-i-evidence-gate.yml`), the H.30c sub-AC (i) evidence-file template, the required-check clauses, and the R13-I4 / B16-3 closure-status notes are in **`ecological-disturbance-verification.md` § H.30c-i evidence gate**. The binding rules stand unchanged: the CI gate MUST be a required check on PRs touching `src/server/services/DisturbanceService/**/*.luau` whose changed files contain the protected references, AND the implementation epic is BLOCKED on the gate's presence (round-15 R14-B10 + round-17 B16-3 binding rulings).

**Secondary — AUTO-INTEGRATION concurrent-reader regression backstop.** GIVEN the test harness drives Predator AI's 4 Hz query loop (PA Core Rule 2 fixed decision tick — round-22 I7 refresh; the harness's mock loop-driver mechanism is fixture-flexible) concurrent with a player death (the predator's query loop is mid-iteration when the player's `Humanoid.Died` fires), AND the test harness records every read from DisturbanceService's predator-lock registry across the death window via `MockOrderingRecorder`'s interleaving log, THEN any registry read interleaved with the death's eventual `:ReleasePredatorLock` invocation observes EITHER the full pre-purge state (all `pre_purge_targets` entries present) OR the full post-purge state (no entries for that `predatorId` remain) — never a mid-purge state — because sub-steps (4a)–(4c) execute in a single Luau frame with no yield primitives. The interleaving log MUST contain zero observations of a partial-purge registry state across the entire 100-trial harness. **The AUTO-INTEGRATION test is a regression backstop:** under a correct yield-free implementation it tautologically passes (proving no defect); if a yield is silently added by a future PR that bypasses both the H.39e lint AND the two-engineer review, the test would observe a partial-purge state on at least one of 100 trials. Both halves of the sub-AC (primary MANUAL-REVIEW + secondary AUTO-INTEGRATION) MUST pass for sub-AC (i) closure. Together with sub-AC (h), this completes Cluster 1 (B-CC1-A through B-CC1-E) closure verification. **Round-11 sub-AC (j) — same-frame Fire(true)+Fire(false) ordering contract on the BindableEvent fallback path (closes B-R10-1c).** GIVEN the test harness drives Predator AI's state machine through a Hunt-targeting-player-A → Hunt-targeting-player-B transition within a single Luau frame (constructed via `_runFieldUpdatePass()` + the harness's mock-Predator-AI driver) AND `MockRemoteSignalRecorder` is installed on both player A's and player B's clients to capture per-target `OnPredatorLockChanged` payloads in arrival order, AND `MockOrderingRecorder` timestamps the server-side `:Fire` invocations: **(j-i)** Predator AI's state-out-of-Hunt-targeting-player-A code path raises `OnPredatorStateChanged:Fire(...)` first; **(j-ii)** DisturbanceService's `OnPredatorStateChanged:Connect` callback runs synchronously and invokes `:ReleasePredatorLock(predatorId)`; **(j-iii)** sub-step (4b) fires `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(playerA, {locked = false, ...})`; **(j-iv)** sub-steps (4c) atomic registry purge completes; **(j-v)** Predator AI's state-into-Hunt-targeting-player-B code path THEN fires `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(playerB, {locked = true, ...})`. THEN: (a) the recorder captures the strict server-side ordering `t_(j-i) < t_(j-ii) < t_(j-iii) < t_(j-iv) < t_(j-v)`; (b) `MockRemoteSignalRecorder` captures `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(playerA, {locked = false, ...})` queued for delivery BEFORE `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(playerB, {locked = true, ...})` is queued (both fires are within a single Luau frame, but the queue ordering matches the server-side fire-invocation ordering); (c) no Heartbeat boundary, no `task.wait`, no other yield primitive intervenes between (j-i) and (j-v); (d) verified via `MockOrderingRecorder`'s yield-primitive detection. Verifies the round-11 B-R10-1c ordering contract codified in E.23 — Predator AI MUST sequence the (false) clear (via `OnPredatorStateChanged` → `:ReleasePredatorLock`) BEFORE the (true) acquire on its own state-machine code path. Failure mode if the contract is violated: client-side HUDs receive `Fire(playerB, true)` before `Fire(playerA, false)`, producing a brief mid-frame "still locked on player A" misread on player A's HUD even though the predator has pivoted. The contract is enforceable structurally because BindableEvent dispatch is synchronous on the server AND sub-steps (4a)–(4c) are yield-free. **Negative sub-test (round-11 red-team I-6 closure — verify the AC fails on a contract-violating implementation, not just passes on a correct one):** GIVEN the test harness drives Predator AI's mock state machine to fire `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(playerB, {locked = true, ...})` BEFORE raising `OnPredatorStateChanged:Fire(...)` (constructing the contract-violating ordering on purpose), THEN: (a) `MockOrderingRecorder` captures the server-side ordering as `t_(violation-Fire-true) < t_(j-i)` — the (true) fire precedes the `OnPredatorStateChanged` dispatch in the in-frame instruction sequence; (b) `MockRemoteSignalRecorder` captures `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(playerB, {locked = true, ...})` queued for delivery BEFORE `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(playerA, {locked = false, ...})` (the contract-violating queue order); (c) the test harness asserts this scenario FAILS sub-AC (j)'s ordering predicate AND surfaces the failure with a clear error message identifying the contract violation. Without the negative sub-test, sub-AC (j) only verifies that a correct implementation passes; the negative sub-test verifies the AC actually catches the contract violation it claims to catch. The negative sub-test runs alongside the positive sub-test; both MUST pass for sub-AC (j) closure (the positive run produces the correct ordering and the AC asserts strict ordering; the negative run produces the wrong ordering and the AC asserts the strict-ordering check FAILS — both halves are required to prove the AC is bidirectional).

**H.41j** — AUTO-UNIT (`connected` requires spawned Character — round-7c B-7b-5 / round-7b systems-designer adversarial finding; round-9 B-CC2-D harness-mechanism specification) — GIVEN three connected players where one (P_void) has just joined via `Players.PlayerAdded` and has NOT yet had `CharacterAdded` fire (`P_void.Character == nil`; the harness constructs this state via the round-9-added "Characterless-player harness mechanism" — see Test Infrastructure Prerequisites — using `Players.CharacterAutoLoads = false` set BEFORE `PlayerAdded` and never invoking `:LoadCharacter()` for P_void during the sub-test) while two (P1, P2) have spawned characters, an active Beacon B in the live-source list with P1 and P2 both within `BEACON_STATIONARY_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 16` studs of B AND each player's position-history ring buffer is fully populated AND each player's `cumulativeXZ < BEACON_CUMULATIVE_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD = 6.0` studs over the past 3.0 s, WHEN the field-update pass runs, THEN: (a) `connected` evaluates to `2` (P_void excluded from the denominator per C.3.6 step 2 round-7c B-7b-5 rule); (b) `nearby` evaluates to `2` (P1 and P2 only — P_void has no Character to query position from); (c) the ratio `nearby / connected = 2/2 = 1.0 > 0.50 = true` AND `stationary_nearby / nearby = 2/2 = 1.0 > 0.50 = true`; (d) the cap engages on B; (e) repeat the test with P_void as the SOLE connected player AND no spawned-Character squad-mates: `connected = 0` → cap rule does NOT apply per E.15 short-circuit (parallel to H.41a). Without the round-7c B-7b-5 fix, sub-test (e) would evaluate `connected = 1` (P_void counted as connected) AND `nearby = 0` (P_void has no XZ), ratio `0 / 1 = 0 NOT > 0.50` — cap NEVER engages on a characterless solo session regardless of any beacon configuration; the asymmetry between `connected` (UserId-based) and `nearby` (XZ-based) is silent and unreachable through any tuning. Verifies the round-7c B-7b-5 patch at C.3.6 step 2 + E.17 cross-reference.

**H.41k** — AUTO-UNIT (cumulativeXZ buffer-fill precondition — round-7c B-7b-4 / round-7b systems-designer adversarial finding) — GIVEN four connected players where three (P1, P2, P3) have fully-populated position-history ring buffers (≥ 15 samples at default 3.0 s × 5 Hz) and the fourth (P_new) joined within the past 1.0 s and has only 5 samples in their ring buffer AND all four are within `BEACON_STATIONARY_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 16` studs of an active Beacon B AND P1, P2, P3 each have `cumulativeXZ < 6.0` studs over their populated 3.0 s windows AND P_new's 5-sample buffer would naively compute `cumulativeXZ ≈ 0.5` studs (under-filled, would naively evaluate as stationary), WHEN the field-update pass runs, THEN: (a) P_new is treated as **moving** for cap-engagement purposes (per-pass `cumulativeXZ` for P_new is treated as `+∞` per C.3.6 step 4 round-7c B-7b-4 rule); (b) `stationary_nearby = 3` (P1, P2, P3 — NOT P_new); (c) `nearby = 4`, ratio `stationary_nearby / nearby = 3/4 = 0.75 > 0.50 = true` — cap engages anyway because the strict majority of nearby players are still stationary; (d) repeat the test where ONLY P1 and P_new are nearby (`nearby = 2`) AND P1 is stationary AND P_new's buffer is under-filled: `stationary_nearby = 1` (P1 only), ratio `1/2 = 0.50 NOT > 0.50` — cap does NOT engage; (e) drive 12 deterministic field-update pass invocations via the round-9-added `DisturbanceService:_runFieldUpdatePass()` seam (`_advanceTestClock(0.2)` between each call to advance the clock by exactly one 5 Hz interval — `_advanceTestClock` alone advances clock reads but does NOT execute the pass body, so the ring buffer would not get appended; `_runFieldUpdatePass` is the round-9 seam that runs the pass body deterministically without yielding to Heartbeat — see Test Infrastructure Prerequisites). After 12 invocations P_new's buffer holds 17 samples ≥ 15. AND P_new's now-fully-populated `cumulativeXZ < 6.0`: re-evaluate sub-test (d) — cap NOW engages with `stationary_nearby = 2, nearby = 2, ratio = 1.0 > 0.50`. Without the round-7c B-7b-4 fix, sub-test (d) would falsely engage the cap immediately on P_new's join (under-filled buffer's naive `cumulativeXZ = 0.5 < 6.0` triggers the false-stationary read). Verifies the round-7c B-7b-4 patch at C.3.6 step 4.

**H.31** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (server-pushed initial snapshot, replaces round-1 client-pull AC) — GIVEN a chunk newly streams into a client with stale flora state (all flora at `t = 0` in the client's local table) AND a `MockTweenService` records every `TweenService:Create` call (the mock fixture lives in `tests/helpers/tween_recorder.luau`), WHEN the server detects the chunk-load and fires `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` to that client, THEN for every flora node in the snapshot the client's flora-state table is updated to the snapshot's `targetT` value ± 0.001 BEFORE any `TweenService:Create` call fires for that flora, AND the first subsequent tween's `StartValue` equals the snapshot's `targetT` (not 0). Deterministic state-assertion replaces the round-1 visual-pop wording.

**H.31b** — STATIC-LINT (round-23 QA-5 retag from AUTO-UNIT — pure source-scan) (no-`ReplicationFocus` / no-client-property regression check; round-5 addition; verifies C.3.5 + F.4 round-4 P0 mitigation AND the round-5 chunk-load API safeguard) — GIVEN the `DisturbanceService` source code AND a grep / AST-level static-analysis pass scoped to all code paths reachable from `DisturbanceService:KnitInit()`, the field-update pass, the `loadedChunks` membership update path, and any subscription handlers registered during init, WHEN the static-analysis pass runs, THEN: (a) zero occurrences of the literal string `"ReplicationFocus"` in any context (subscription, property read, comment, or string concatenation); (b) zero occurrences of the call pattern `Player:GetPropertyChangedSignal("ReplicationFocus")` regardless of variable naming; (c) zero direct reads of `Player.ReplicationFocus` or any expression matching `\.ReplicationFocus`; (d) zero `Player:GetPropertyChangedSignal(...)` subscriptions on any client-writable Player property within the chunk-load detection code path (per the F.4 row 5 GDD-level safeguard); (e) zero `Player.Character.*.Position`-polling loops (e.g., `RunService.Heartbeat:Connect(function() ... player.Character.PrimaryPart.Position ... end)`) within the chunk-load detection code path. Mirrors H.39's grep-based pattern. **This AC is BLOCKING — failure indicates the round-4 P0 mitigation has been silently regressed.**

**H.31c** — AUTO-UNIT (`_injectSyntheticEmission` minimum-invariant enforcement; round-5 / Tier-1 #12; round-7a R7-B12 + R7-B13 — three-independent-invocations + tightened upper bound) — GIVEN the test-only `DisturbanceService:_injectSyntheticEmission(payload)` seam, WHEN the test fixture executes **three independent invocations**, each violating exactly ONE of the minimum invariants while keeping the other two valid — (a) `position.X / Y / Z` non-finite (NaN, ±Infinity) with valid `initialMagnitude` and valid `emissionType`, (b) `initialMagnitude` outside `(0, 1.00]` with valid finite position and valid `emissionType` (round-7a R7-B12 — upper bound tightened from 1.05 to 1.00 because Retreat-ceiling clamp invariance requires `initialMagnitude ≤ 1.00`), (c) `emissionType` not in the canonical enum `{"Gather", "Sprint", "Light", "Beacon", "Craft"}` with valid finite position and valid `initialMagnitude` — THEN each of the three invocations independently raises a hard error (test failure) AND no source is inserted into the live-source list AND each error message identifies the specific invariant violated. **Round-7a R7-B13 binding clarification (qa-lead F1 escape-hatch closure):** the **three invocations MUST be separate** — a single payload that simultaneously violates ALL three invariants does NOT satisfy this AC. Reason: the implementation could short-circuit on the first invariant check while leaving the second and third invariant checks dead-code; a single-payload-violates-all-three test would pass while the dead-code paths silently accept singly-invalid payloads. The same plural-but-not-three escape pattern that affected H.3b (round-7a R7-B11) applies here. Three independent test invocations, one per invariant, each with the other two invariants valid, is the only formulation that exercises each invariant check end-to-end. **Round-7a additional (b)-boundary checks:** invocation (b) MUST cover both extremes of the rejected range — one with `initialMagnitude = -0.01` (below floor; degenerate negative case parallel to E.1) AND one with `initialMagnitude = 1.01` (above 1.00 ceiling; round-7a R7-B12 tightening regression-closure). **Round-7c NC-f — exact-strict boundary semantics (no epsilon).** The `(0, 1.00]` upper-bound check MUST be implemented as exact-strict `> 1.00`, with no epsilon tolerance: `initialMagnitude = 1.0000001` (= `1.00 + 1e-7`, just barely above the bound) MUST reject; `initialMagnitude = 1.00` exactly MUST accept. The implementation MUST NOT introduce an epsilon-cushion (e.g., `initialMagnitude > 1.00 + 1e-9`) that would silently admit values nominally above the ceiling — the ceiling enforcement's purpose is to preserve the `[0.0, 1.0]` clamp invariant on the cap formula's output, and even a 1e-9 epsilon admits values that mathematically violate the clamp. Test fixtures MUST assert both `initialMagnitude = 1.00` (accepts) AND `initialMagnitude = 1.0000001` (rejects with hard error) as separate invocations to prove the boundary is strict. The same exact-strict semantics apply at invocation (b)'s lower bound: `initialMagnitude = 0.0` MUST reject (violates `> 0`); `initialMagnitude = 1e-9` MUST accept (smallest representable positive). Bypassing schema validation in this seam is for stress-testing the live-source-list ceiling under H.38b clustered conditions, NOT for testing with arbitrary garbage payloads — the seam preserves the minimum invariants needed to keep the rest of the engine sound. The seam still bypasses Q9 stationary attenuation, the duplicate-`emissionId` collision check, and the cap-eviction policy — those are explicit out-of-scope by design. Verified by inspection of the seam's body in `DisturbanceService` source. (See E.24 for the prose mirror of this AC, also updated round-7a.)

> **AC-numbering note (round-22, B1-residual; regrouped in the round-22 AC-extraction pass):** H.31b and H.31c historically lived at the end of the **Security / Anti-Exploit Regression** block, where the non-contiguous numbering (this block ran H.31 → H.32) led two independent round-21 reviewers to conclude the ids were dangling (round-21 B1 — a verified false positive). The AC-extraction pass regrouped both ACs to sit here, immediately after H.31; a pointer remains at the Security block. Ids are unchanged — all existing H.31b / H.31c cites remain valid.

### Edge Case Verification

**H.32** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a player disconnects with live emissions in the source list, WHEN the server continues to run, THEN those emissions continue to decay per D.1 and remain queryable by `GetHottestHotspot()` — disconnect does not trigger early expiry (E.4).

**H.32b** — AUTO-UNIT (cross-chunk persistence, E.18) — GIVEN a chunk streams out on the only client that had it loaded AND the chunk contains live emissions, WHEN the server's update loop continues, THEN the live emissions in that chunk continue to decay per D.1 and remain queryable by `GetHottestHotspot()` from any predator within `searchRadius` of those positions; AND when the chunk later streams in on a client, `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` carries the current decayed `targetT` for every flora in the chunk.

**H.32c** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (E.21 departed-player Fire — round-4 addition; round-5 Knit-API correction) — GIVEN a player dies AND `DisturbanceService:GetDeathAttributionPayload(deadPlayer)` constructs a valid `DeathAttributionPayload` AND the player's session terminates (`Players.PlayerRemoving` fires) before `OnDeathAttributionPushed:Fire(deadPlayer, payload)` executes AND the test harness installs `MockRemoteSignalRecorder` on all four player sessions (the surviving three plus a recorder for the departed player that captures attempted fires after departure), WHEN the server invokes `:Fire(deadPlayer, payload)` after `PlayerRemoving` has fired, THEN: (a) the call returns to the caller without error or exception; (b) the constructed payload's `attributionMayBeIncomplete` field is `false` (the payload was constructible — delivery failure is distinct from data incompleteness); (c) the server-side ops-log captures one entry tagged `disturbance.attribution.delivery_failed_player_departed` containing `sourcePlayerId`, `deathTimestamp`, and the contributing `emissionId` list — observable through the standard ops-log sink; (d) the surviving three players' `MockRemoteSignalRecorder`s capture **zero** `OnDeathAttributionPushed` payloads (no broadcast fallback; per-player `:Fire` routing per C.3.4 / C.1.9 preserved); (e) no retry of `:Fire` is attempted by `DisturbanceService` for this dead player. Verifies E.21 documented behavior on the 95%-MVP-gate observability path.

**H.32d** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (E.22 chunk stream-out before snapshot delivery; round-5 / Tier-1 #11; round-7a R7-B14 — server-observable transitions only) — GIVEN a mock client has chunkA newly added to its server-side `loadedChunks` membership set AND the server fires `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot:Fire(player, payload)` for chunkA AND the test harness simulates the chunk-out / chunk-in race **using only server-observable state transitions** (NOT "before packet delivery completes" — Roblox does not expose per-packet delivery acks to the server, so any AC formulation that depends on observing in-flight delivery is unverifiable from server Luau; round-7a R7-B14 / qa-lead F2 fix): the harness removes chunkA from the server-side `loadedChunks[player]` set within the SAME field-update pass as the `:Fire` call (simulating a stream-out that happens between fire and the next pass — the server-observable signal of "in-flight" is the membership-set state at the next pass's evaluation moment), then re-adds chunkA to `loadedChunks[player]` on the SUBSEQUENT field-update pass (simulating stream-in), WHEN the third field-update pass runs after stream-in, THEN: (a) a fresh `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot:Fire(player, ...)` is invoked for chunkA carrying current decayed `t` for every flora in the chunk (verified by `MockRemoteSignalRecorder` capturing exactly one new fire); (b) the per-flora last-pushed-t cache for chunkA is reset such that the next regular `FloraChunkUpdate` push correctly applies the `FLORA_UPDATE_DELTA_THRESHOLD` gate against the snapshot's values, NOT against the pre-stream-out values (verified by setting flora `t` values to slightly-changed magnitudes between snapshots and asserting the next `FloraChunkUpdate` carries deltas measured against the snapshot's reset cache, not against the pre-stream-out cache); (c) recovery is bounded to **one field-update pass** after the stream-in pass — the server's snapshot fire and cache reset both occur within the stream-in pass itself; the next regular `FloraChunkUpdate` push (one pass later, ≤ 200 ms at default 5 Hz) carries any post-snapshot deltas correctly. **The server-observable invariants tested:** (1) `MockRemoteSignalRecorder` captures the snapshot-fire count = 2 (one pre-stream-out, one post-stream-in); (2) the cache reset is observable via the recorder's next-pass `FloraChunkUpdate` payload comparing against snapshot values; (3) no third snapshot is fired in the absence of further stream-in/out cycles. The harness's "in-flight window" is operationalised as "between the first `:Fire` invocation and the same-pass `loadedChunks` removal" — both server-observable. **Without this AC's server-observable formulation, the round-5 wording 'before packet delivery completes' was unverifiable from Luau code (no Roblox API surfaces packet ACKs to the server) and the AC was honor-system rather than mechanically-verifiable.**

**H.3d** — AUTO-UNIT (E.19 INFLUENCE_RADIUS validation) — GIVEN `INFLUENCE_RADIUS` is configured to 0 or a negative value, WHEN `DisturbanceService:KnitInit()` runs at server startup, THEN the service refuses to start and raises a fatal error identifying the misconfigured constant. Defense against tuning misconfiguration corrupting `fieldValue` via 0/0 NaN propagation.

**H.3e** — AUTO-UNIT (E.19 grid-cell-size invariant — round-3 addition) — GIVEN `SPATIAL_GRID_CELL_SIZE` is configured strictly less than `INFLUENCE_RADIUS` (e.g., cell=16, radius=24), WHEN `DisturbanceService:KnitInit()` runs at server startup, THEN the service refuses to start and raises a fatal error identifying the violation of the G.7 invariant `SPATIAL_GRID_CELL_SIZE >= INFLUENCE_RADIUS`. Defense against silently breaking C.1.6's 3×3-cell-neighborhood guarantee.

**H.33** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a Beacon is activated at a position where ambient `fieldValue` is already 0.10 (Beacon + ambient > 1.0), WHEN `fieldValue()` is queried at that position immediately after, THEN the returned value is 1.0 (Retreat). The Beacon's stored `initialMagnitude` (0.95) is not reduced; only the clamped `fieldValue` reflects the ceiling (E.8).

**H.34** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a position oscillates across a tier boundary such that `fieldValue` alternates between `TENSE_THRESHOLD + 0.01` and `TENSE_THRESHOLD − 0.01` across four consecutive update ticks, WHEN tier classification runs on each tick with hysteresis applied, THEN `TierCrossedEvent` fires at most twice (one upward cross, one downward cross that clears `HYSTERESIS_BAND`) — not four times (E.6).

**H.34b** — AUTO-UNIT (post-hitch multi-tier cascade — round-4 addition) — GIVEN a position P with `previousTierAtPosition[P] = "Retreat"` AND between the previous and current field-update passes a server hitch occurred (simulated by directly mutating the live-source list to drop `fieldValue(P)` from 1.00 → 0.20 in a single step) AND a mock Predator AI subscribes to `TierCrossedEvent`, WHEN the field-update pass completes its post-hitch settle loop per the D.4 cascade rule, THEN: (a) `previousTierAtPosition[P]` is set to `"Calm"` after the loop converges (NOT `"Hunt"` — that would be the result of a single-step `tierAt` call without the cascade loop); (b) exactly three `TierCrossedEvent`s fire for P in this pass — one each for `crossedTier ∈ {"Hunt", "Tense", "Calm"}` — subject to round-3 coalescing (if other positions also cross those same boundaries this pass, only one event per `crossedTier` value fires, with `position` selected by highest cumulative as in H.28b; here we assume P is the sole crosser of all three); (c) the cascade loop terminates in at most four iterations (worst-case Retreat→Calm cascade executes the loop body 4 times: Retreat→Hunt→Tense→Calm→stop, then stops because `result == prev`). Verifies the D.4 post-hitch multi-tier cascade rule. **Without this loop, a single-step `tierAt(P, "Retreat")` call with `t = 0.20` returns `"Hunt"` — a phantom tier that would silently drive Predator AI / HUD into Hunt-state response on a position that is actually Calm.**

**H.35** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN two emissions from different players arrive on the same server tick and their combined contributions push a position from below `TENSE_THRESHOLD` to above it, WHEN the update loop processes that tick, THEN `TierCrossedEvent` fires exactly once for that position-boundary pair, and both `emissionId`s are eligible to appear in a subsequent death attribution payload's top-3 list (E.14).

**H.35b** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN exactly `MAX_LIVE_SOURCES` live sources in the system (live-source list at capacity), WHEN an additional `Emit()` call arrives from any `sourcePlayerId`, THEN per E.12 the emission is dropped (no source added to the live-source list, no archive entry written), the call returns to the caller without error (fire-and-forget contract per C.3.1 preserved), and a server-side log entry is written with the `sourcePlayerId`, `emissionType`, and the cap-hit timestamp. The drop policy is "drop-new" by default (eviction policy alternatives are deferred to the `DisturbanceService` ADR per Q3).

**H.35c** — AUTO-UNIT — GIVEN a `MAX_LIVE_SOURCES` cap drop occurred during a player's contribution window AND that player subsequently dies, WHEN the `DeathAttributionPayload` is constructed, THEN the payload's `attributionMayBeIncomplete` field is `true`. Cap drops outside the dead player's contribution window do NOT set the flag.

### Beacon Stationary-Squad Cap (Tier-1 #10 — round-5 addition)

**H.41** — AUTO-UNIT (cap engagement, 4-player squad — round-7a R7-B6 cumulative-distance + R7-B4 nearby-denominator) — GIVEN four connected players with XZ positions all within `BEACON_STATIONARY_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 16` studs of an active Beacon B (so `nearby = 4, connected = 4, nearby/connected = 1.0 > 0.50`) AND each player's `cumulativeXZ` integral (sum of per-sample displacements) over the past `BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW = 3.0` seconds is < `BEACON_CUMULATIVE_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD = 6.0` studs (so `stationary_nearby = 4, stationary_nearby/nearby = 1.0 > 0.50`), WHEN the field-update pass runs, THEN the cap engages on B, the beacon's effective contribution at B.position is reduced such that `fieldValue(B.position) == HUNT_THRESHOLD = 0.65` ± 0.001 (clamped to Hunt floor exactly), and the beacon's underlying `m(t, age)` and decay-track are unmodified (D.1 still uses raw `initialMagnitude = 0.95`).

**H.41a** — AUTO-UNIT (cap does not engage with zero connected players) — GIVEN zero connected players AND an active Beacon B in the live-source list, WHEN the field-update pass runs, THEN the cap does NOT engage (the implementation short-circuits the strict `nearby / connected > 0.50` check on `connected == 0`); `fieldValue(B.position)` reflects B's full unmodified contribution. Verifies E.25 zero-player edge case.

**H.41b** — AUTO-UNIT (cap engages with single-player solo session) — GIVEN exactly one connected player whose XZ-position is within 16 studs of an active Beacon B AND that player's `cumulativeXZ` over the past 3.0 s is < `BEACON_CUMULATIVE_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD = 6.0` studs, WHEN the field-update pass runs, THEN the cap engages — step 3 evaluates `nearby/connected = 1/1 > 0.50 = true` AND step 5 evaluates `stationary_nearby/nearby = 1/1 > 0.50 = true`. Verifies E.25 solo-session edge case.

**H.41c** — AUTO-UNIT (teleport discontinuity resets stationary-status — round-7a R7-B6 reformulation) — GIVEN a player teleports such that their per-frame XZ-position delta exceeds `2 × BEACON_CUMULATIVE_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD = 12` studs in a single field-update pass AND their position-history ring buffer's prior samples would otherwise indicate stationary (low cumulativeXZ within the `BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW`), WHEN the next field-update pass runs after the teleport, THEN the player's ring buffer is marked discontinuous, the player's `cumulativeXZ` for cap-engagement purposes is treated as `+∞` (effectively "moving") until the ring buffer fills with `BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW` seconds of post-teleport samples, and the player does NOT count toward the `stationary_nearby` total during the discontinuity-recovery window. Verifies E.25 teleport-discontinuity edge case.
