**H.41d** — AUTO-UNIT (cap clears on movement — round-7a R7-B4 + R7-B6 update) — GIVEN the cap is engaged on Beacon B per H.41 setup, WHEN any one nearby player's next field-update pass position-sample causes their `cumulativeXZ` over the `BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW` to reach or exceed `BEACON_CUMULATIVE_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD = 6.0` studs (so that player's stationary-status flips to moving) AND the strict `stationary_nearby / nearby > 0.50` check at step 5 is no longer satisfied, THEN the cap clears on the next field-update pass: B's full contribution returns AND `fieldValue(B.position)` rises to its uncapped value (typically `0.95 + ambient ≥ 1.0` clamped to Retreat). Verifies the no-hysteresis cap-clear semantics in C.3.6 with the round-7a denominator-fix.

**H.41e** — MANUAL-PLAYTEST (degenerate-strategy closure verification — round-7a R7-B15 facilitator-directed redesign) — GIVEN a 6-minute 4-player playtest session run TWICE (back-to-back; same participants) where the **first session is a normal play session** (squad attempts whatever strategy they prefer at the win condition; cap-engagement is observed naturalistically) AND the **second session is a facilitator-directed segment** in which the facilitator instructs the squad to deliberately attempt the previously-degenerate strategy ("activate the beacon, fan out within 16 studs of it, and stand still — try to hide behind the beacon's protection") for ≥ 30 s of the win-condition window, AND server-side logging records every cap-engagement event in both sessions AND a debrief survey asks each participant the same question after each session: "When the squad stood still near the activated beacon, did the predator (a) commit to attack the squad, (b) circle / investigate without committing, (c) ignore the beacon altogether?", WHEN both sessions end, THEN: (i) **cap-engagement is logged in the facilitator-directed session** for at least one extended interval (≥ 5 s); the cap mechanism is confirmed firing in the targeted scenario regardless of whether the first naturalistic session reached this state. The naturalistic session's cap-engagement frequency is captured but is informational only (round-7 qa-lead F3: with a working cap, naturalistic sessions may NOT trigger the degenerate strategy because the squad pre-emptively learned to disperse — the facilitator-directed segment is the falsifiability harness for the cap mechanism); (ii) among **facilitator-directed-session participants** (denominator-anchored to a population guaranteed to exist by the protocol), ≥ 60% report (b) "circle / investigate without committing" — confirming the cap holds the beacon at the Hunt floor where the predator pursues without the field-driven Retreat-tier attack-run commitment — the player-perceived "circling / investigating" read; attack remains melee/LOS-gated per PA's H.47 (round-23 NP-1 rewording of the retired framing; the survey option's player-perception label is unchanged); (iii) among **all participants across both sessions**, ≥ 70% report that they perceived a tactical reason to disperse (active-defender play) rather than huddle (passive-hide-by-beacon). Failures route to game-designer review. **Anchored to the round-5 verdict on Decision-1 (Option A); H.41 / H.41a–i verify the mechanism, H.41e verifies the playtest outcome. Round-7a R7-B15 fix:** the previous H.41e formulation evaluated (ii) and (iii) over a denominator that was undefined when no participant attempted the degenerate strategy — a working cap would inverse-falsify the AC by suppressing the precondition. The facilitator-directed segment guarantees a non-zero denominator for (ii) and decouples (iii) from the suppressed natural-play denominator.

**H.41f** — AUTO-UNIT (denominator-zero protection — round-7a R7-B5 sub-mode (iii)) — GIVEN an active Beacon B in the live-source list whose age is sufficient that `m(B.age) × falloff(0) < MAGNITUDE_FLOOR × 1.5 = 0.03` (i.e., B's native contribution at its own position has decayed near the floor; for a 0.95-magnitude Beacon at 90 s half-life, this occurs at age ≈ `ln(0.95/0.03) / (ln(2)/90) ≈ 449 s`) AND four nearby stationary players (cap-engagement preconditions all satisfied per H.41 setup), WHEN the field-update pass runs, THEN the cap rule is short-circuited for B (the implementation detects `beacon_native_contribution_at_B < 0.03` and skips the `scaleFactor` calculation), no NaN propagates into `fieldValue`, and B is allowed to expire on the next maintenance pass per C.1.12. Verifies E.25 beacon-near-floor edge case.

**H.41g** — AUTO-UNIT (active-defender 2-of-4 case — round-7a R7-B4 / D1 regression closure) — GIVEN four connected players ALL within `BEACON_STATIONARY_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 16` studs of an active Beacon B (so `nearby = 4, connected = 4`) AND exactly two of those four have `cumulativeXZ < 6.0` studs over the past 3.0 s while the OTHER two have `cumulativeXZ ≥ 6.0` studs (active-defender play; 2 stationary near beacon, 2 actively flanking nearby), WHEN the field-update pass runs, THEN step 5 evaluates `stationary_nearby / nearby = 2/4 = 0.50, NOT > 0.50` and the cap does NOT engage; `fieldValue(B.position)` reflects B's full unmodified contribution. **This AC is the round-6→round-7 regression-closure test:** under round-6's mistaken `>=` operator OR the pre-round-7a `connected` denominator (`2/4 = 0.50` against either rule = engaged), the cap falsely engaged on this legitimate active-defender configuration; the round-7a fix is verified by this AC failing under the regressed code AND passing under the corrected code.

**H.41h** — AUTO-UNIT (`non_beacon > BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE` — round-7a R7-B5 sub-mode (ii)) — GIVEN an active Beacon B AND ambient (non-beacon) field at B.position of 0.70 (above `BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE = 0.65`; produced by a test-fixture constructed gather sequence near B) AND cap-engagement preconditions satisfied per H.41 setup, WHEN the field-update pass runs, THEN: (a) `scaleFactor` is computed as `(0.65 - 0.70) / 0.95 = -0.053` and clamped to `0.0` per C.3.6 step 6; (b) the beacon's contribution at B.position equals 0; (c) `fieldValue(B.position) = 0.70 + 0 = 0.70` — above the cap due to ambient alone. The cap is a beacon-attenuation mechanism, NOT a global field-value clamp; the field is allowed to exceed the cap when non-beacon sources alone produce that elevation. Verifies E.25 ambient-exceeds-cap edge case.

**H.41i** — AUTO-UNIT (two-beacon order-independence — round-7a game-designer F9 IMPORTANT-elevated-to-AC) — GIVEN two simultaneous active Beacons B1 and B2 with positions ≥ `INFLUENCE_RADIUS = 24` studs apart (so neither beacon's contribution reaches the other's position) AND a squad-of-4 stationary near both AND the test harness evaluates the cap engagement for both beacons in two passes — first computing B1's cap with B2 evaluated first, then computing B1's cap with B2 evaluated second — WHEN the field-update pass runs, THEN B1's cap-engagement verdict and `scaleFactor` are identical regardless of B2's evaluation order; the per-beacon `non_beacon_contribution_at_B(B1)` excludes ALL live Beacon-emission contributions (including B2's) per C.3.6 implementation note; cap-engagement is therefore a function only of B1's own decayed magnitude and the ambient non-beacon field, with no inter-beacon coupling. Verifies E.25 two-beacons order-independence rule (positions ≥ 24 studs apart). For the round-9 co-located case (positions < 24 studs apart), see H.41l.

**H.41l** — AUTO-UNIT (round-9 B-NS1 — co-located beacons within INFLUENCE_RADIUS; round-11 B-R10-4 worked-example clean-up) — GIVEN two simultaneous active Beacons B1 and B2 with positions < `INFLUENCE_RADIUS = 24` studs apart AND a squad-of-4 stationary near both (cap-engagement preconditions all satisfied per H.41 setup) AND zero ambient (no other live emissions; the test fixture uses `_setLiveSourceState` per the round-9 R9-I6 sub-stud beacon-placement helper to construct an ambient-zero baseline), WHEN the field-update pass runs, THEN per C.3.6 step 6's round-9 co-location amendment (DR-2 = Option B): the per-beacon `non_beacon_contribution_at_B(B1)` denominator-input is computed by INCLUDING B2's contribution at B1.position whenever B2 is co-located within `INFLUENCE_RADIUS` of B1 (overrides the round-7c NC-c all-beacons-excluded rule, which applies ONLY when beacons are non-co-located per H.41i). The cap formula then resolves to clamp B1's effective contribution to zero whenever B2's co-located contribution alone exceeds the `BEACON_STATIONARY_CAP_FIELD_VALUE = 0.65` floor.

**Three sub-tests, all of which MUST pass:**

- **Sub-test (a) — d = 4 studs (most-pathological co-location, maximally inside `INFLUENCE_RADIUS = 24` AND inside `BEACON_STATIONARY_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 16`).** D.2 falloff at d=4: `(1 - 4/24)² = (5/6)² ≈ 0.6944`. B2's contribution at B1.position with native magnitude 0.95: `0.95 × 0.6944 = 0.6597` (rounded display: 0.66). Round-9 amended `non_beacon_contribution_at_B(B1) = 0.6597` (B2's contribution; ambient is zero). Cap formula: `scaleFactor = (0.65 - 0.6597) / 0.95 ≈ -0.0102`, clamped to 0.0 per C.3.6 step 6's `[0.0, 1.0]` clamp. B1's contribution at its own position is zeroed (cap-zeroed). `fieldValue(B1.position) = ambient + B2_contribution + B1_capped_contribution = 0 + 0.6597 + 0 = 0.6597` — just above Hunt floor 0.65 (Hunt-tier; design intent preserved). **PASS criterion (round-13 R12-B7 symmetric IEEE-754 tolerance closure; supersedes round-11 red-team I-1 one-sided window which excluded valid single-precision rounding-down on iPhone SE; round-15 R14-B5 IEEE-754 derivation closure — anchors the ±0.001 tolerance in cited single-precision error analysis):** the boundary-test asserts `fieldValue(B1.position) ∈ [0.6597 − 0.001, 0.6597 + 0.001] = [0.6587, 0.6607]` (symmetric ±0.001 tolerance — the 0.001 magnitude is anchored in IEEE-754 binary32 forward error propagation on the cap-formula's specific operation chain, derived below). The strict-clamp-to-zero check on `scaleFactor` is verified by the side-assertion `scaleFactor == 0.0` exactly — Roblox `math.clamp` produces a deterministic 0.0 for clamped-from-below inputs, no epsilon-cushion.

  **±0.001 tolerance derivation (round-15 R14-B5 IEEE-754 anchor; Goldberg 1991 + IEEE-754-2008 binary32 standard).** The iPhone SE-class device's GPU shader pipeline operates in IEEE-754 binary32 single-precision (23-bit mantissa); CPU-side Luau `number` operations on Roblox are double-precision (53-bit mantissa) but the `Vector3` arithmetic that produces the position-delta inputs to D.2's falloff is single-precision per Roblox's `Vector3` storage contract. The cap-formula's operation chain on the d = 4 studs case proceeds through these elementary operations (each operation introduces at most 0.5 ULP forward error per IEEE-754 round-to-nearest semantics; cf. Goldberg, "What Every Computer Scientist Should Know About Floating-Point Arithmetic," ACM Computing Surveys 23(1), 1991, §1.2 "Error and Error Analysis"):

  | Step | Operation | Magnitude | ULP at this magnitude (binary32) | Forward error bound |
  |------|-----------|-----------|----------------------------------|---------------------|
  | 1 | Position-delta `B1 - B2` (Vector3 subtract; X/Y/Z components) | O(4) | ~5e-7 absolute | 0.5 ULP per component → ~3 × 5e-7 = 1.5e-6 absolute on the delta vector |
  | 2 | Magnitude `‖delta‖` (sqrt of sum of squares) | O(4) | ~5e-7 absolute | 1.5 ULP cumulative (sum + sqrt) → ~7.5e-7 absolute |
  | 3 | Normalize `d / INFLUENCE_RADIUS = d / 24` | O(0.16667) | ~2e-8 absolute | 0.5 ULP → ~1e-8 absolute |
  | 4 | Subtract `1 - d/24` | O(0.83333) | ~1e-7 absolute | 0.5 ULP → ~5e-8 absolute |
  | 5 | Square `(1 - d/24)²` | O(0.6944) | ~8e-8 absolute | 0.5 ULP → ~4e-8 absolute |
  | 6 | Multiply by native magnitude `0.95 × falloff` | O(0.6597) | ~8e-8 absolute | 0.5 ULP → ~4e-8 absolute |
  | 7 | Cap-formula numerator `0.65 - non_beacon` (subtract; near-cancellation) | O(0.01) | ~1e-9 absolute | The local 0.5 ULP after cancellation alone is ~5e-10 absolute, BUT the cancellation amplification factor `\|x\|/\|x-y\|` ≈ 0.65/0.01 ≈ 65× operates on the **carry-forward forward-error from upstream steps 1–6** (~4e-8 absolute at the `non_beacon` operand, dominated by step 6's multiply-chain bound), NOT on the local 0.5 ULP — Higham §3.1 condition-number analysis. Cumulative bound: 4e-8 × 65 ≈ **~2.7e-6 absolute** (round-17 I16-4 closure of round-16 IEEE-754 derivation gap; the round-15 wording mistakenly applied the 65× factor to the post-cancellation local rounding error rather than to the upstream-carried error). |
  | 8 | Divide by native magnitude `numerator / 0.95` | O(0.01) | ~1e-9 absolute | 0.5 ULP → ~5e-10 absolute |
  | 9 | Clamp `[0.0, 1.0]` (via `math.clamp`) | exactly 0.0 (clamped-from-below) | exact (deterministic) | 0 — the clamp produces exact 0.0 for negative inputs; no rounding |
  | 10 | Multiply `0.95 × scaleFactor (= 0)` | exactly 0.0 | exact | 0 |
  | 11 | Final sum `ambient + B2_contribution + B1_capped_contribution = 0 + 0.6597 + 0` | O(0.6597) | ~8e-8 absolute | 0.5 ULP per addition × 2 additions → ~1.6e-7 absolute, dominated by the carry-forward from step 6's ~4e-8 |

  **Cumulative forward error bound on `fieldValue(B1.position)` for sub-test (a):** dominated by the position-delta + magnitude + falloff chain (steps 1–6) at ~1e-6 absolute, plus the cap-formula path (steps 7–10) which contributes ~2.7e-6 absolute via the cancellation-amplified subtraction at step 7 (round-17 I16-4 corrected from the round-15 ~3e-8 figure; the cancellation factor amplifies upstream-carried error, not local rounding) — **but** the cancellation amplification IS bounded because the clamp at step 9 immediately collapses the entire downstream chain to exact 0.0; the amplified relative error on the negative pre-clamp value does NOT propagate to the final `fieldValue`. Round-trip-conservative cumulative bound on the actual final `fieldValue` (where the clamp absorbs the amplified intermediate error): **~1.5e-6 absolute on `fieldValue(B1.position)`** (unchanged from round-15; the ~2.7e-6 corrected step-7 figure is the bound on the pre-clamp scaleFactor numerator, not on the post-clamp final value).

  **Tolerance choice.** The chosen ±0.001 = 1e-3 tolerance is **~670× the forward-error bound** (~1.5e-6) — generously over-margined, NOT pinch-pointed at the noise floor. The over-margin admits two additional sources of variance the forward-error analysis above does not capture: (i) Roblox-platform-specific quirks in the `Vector3` arithmetic implementation (the `Vector3.magnitude` accessor's exact algorithmic choice — fast-rsqrt approximation vs IEEE sqrt — is not documented in Roblox's API surface; ±1e-5 absolute is a conservative engineering allowance); (ii) test-fixture position-construction precision via `_setLiveSourceState`'s Vector3 assembly (round-11 prereq table mandates sub-stud-tolerance ±0.05 studs, but the actual placement may quantise via Roblox's serialisation pipeline; ±5e-5 absolute is a conservative allowance for placement-quantisation downstream effects). Sum of (i) + (ii) ≈ ~6e-5 absolute, still well below the chosen ±0.001 tolerance. **The ±0.001 tolerance is therefore safely-wide for IEEE-754 binary32 forward-error propagation (FEP) AND for the cumulative platform-variance allowance — chosen to admit two-orders-of-magnitude safety factor over the worst-case combined error.** Tightening to ±0.0001 would still be conservative-correct against the FEP analysis above (sub-test (a) FEP bound is ~1.5e-6, far below ±1e-4) but would risk false-fails on platform-variance edge cases (the fast-rsqrt allowance ~1e-5 already approaches ±1e-4); the ±0.001 choice is the lowest-precision stable tolerance per round-15 design intent.

  **Could ±0.001 be too WIDE?** At ±0.001, the assertion admits values like 0.6587 (just above Hunt floor 0.65). At 0.6587, the implementation passes the AC despite producing a value that is mathematically ~0.001 below the analytic 0.6597. Could this admit a tier-misclassification regression (Hunt vs Tense)? **No** — the Hunt threshold is at 0.65 exactly; 0.6587 is still 0.0087 above Hunt, well within Hunt-tier. A tier-misclassification regression would require the value to drop below 0.65 (Tense-tier), which would be 0.0097 below the analytic value — well outside the ±0.001 tolerance. The ±0.001 tolerance therefore does NOT admit a tier-misclassification false-PASS, AND does NOT admit a clamp-violation false-PASS (the strict-clamp side-assertion `scaleFactor == 0.0` exactly is the load-bearing clamp invariant and is checked separately; it is exact-strict, no tolerance).

  **Citation chain.** (1) IEEE-754-2008 binary32 single-precision: 23-bit significand, exponent range [-126, +127], round-to-nearest-ties-to-even default rounding. (2) Goldberg, D., "What Every Computer Scientist Should Know About Floating-Point Arithmetic," ACM Computing Surveys 23(1):5-48, 1991 — §1.2 forward-error analysis; §1.4 catastrophic cancellation amplification factor for subtractions of nearly-equal operands. (3) Higham, N. J., "Accuracy and Stability of Numerical Algorithms," 2nd ed. SIAM 2002, §3 norm-wise forward-error bounds for compositions of elementary operations. (4) Roblox `Vector3` API documentation — components stored in single-precision per Roblox-core specification (verify against current Roblox docs at architecture-phase per F.4 row 5 GDD-level safeguard for unspecified platform behaviours). The cumulative ULP bound derivation in the table above is a conservative application of (2)+(3) to the cap-formula's specific operation chain. **Regression-failure mode (informational; what the round-9 amendment closes):** under the round-7c NC-c rule alone (without DR-2), B2 would be EXCLUDED from `non_beacon_at_B1`, giving `non_beacon_at_B1 = 0` → `scaleFactor = 0.65 / 0.95 ≈ 0.684` → B1's effective contribution = `0.95 × 0.684 ≈ 0.65` AND B2's contribution still compounds in the field-value sum → `fieldValue(B1.position) = 0 + 0.66 + 0.65 = 1.31` → clamps to `RETREAT_THRESHOLD = 1.00` (Retreat-tier; predator commits to attack run, violating the cap's Hunt-floor design intent). The round-8 B-NS1 systems-designer finding flagged a more pathological co-location (d ≈ 1 stud, where B2's contribution ≈ 0.87) producing the `0.65 + 0.87 = 1.52` headline failure mode; the d=4 case is the test fixture's chosen worst-case representative because (i) it sits maximally inside both `INFLUENCE_RADIUS` and `BEACON_STATIONARY_PROXIMITY_RADIUS` boundaries while remaining a realistic squad-co-placement distance, and (ii) the d=4 contribution (0.66) is the smallest co-location contribution that still exceeds the cap floor 0.65, so it is the boundary case where the round-9 amendment's clamp-to-zero behaviour is load-bearing.

- **Sub-test (b) — d = 23.9 studs (just inside the `INFLUENCE_RADIUS = 24` boundary; round-9 amendment FIRES).** D.2 falloff at d=23.9: `(1 - 23.9/24)² = (0.1/24)² ≈ 1.74e-5`. B2's contribution at B1.position: `0.95 × 1.74e-5 ≈ 1.65e-5` (negligible). Round-9 amended `non_beacon_contribution_at_B(B1) ≈ 1.65e-5` (B2's near-zero contribution; ambient is zero). Cap formula: `scaleFactor = (0.65 - 1.65e-5) / 0.95 ≈ 0.684`. B1's effective contribution = `0.95 × 0.684 ≈ 0.65`. `fieldValue(B1.position) ≈ 0 + 1.65e-5 + 0.65 ≈ 0.65` — exactly Hunt floor (design intent preserved; the round-9 amendment fires but its effect is computationally negligible at this distance, as expected for a continuous falloff function near the radius boundary). The boundary-test asserts `fieldValue(B1.position) ∈ [0.65 − 0.001, 0.65 + 0.001] = [0.649, 0.651]` (symmetric ±0.001 tolerance — round-13 R12-B7 IEEE-754 closure; the round-11 wording's one-sided `[0.65, 0.65 + 0.001]` window was R12-B7-flagged on the same single-precision rounding-down rationale as sub-test (a) above; the symmetric window admits both rounding directions equivalently) AND that the cap-engagement code path executed the round-9 co-location branch (verified via `MockOrderingRecorder` instrumentation hook on the C.3.6 step 6 cap-evaluation entry — the harness records whether the `dist < INFLUENCE_RADIUS` predicate evaluated true).

- **Sub-test (c) — d = 24.1 studs (just outside the `INFLUENCE_RADIUS = 24` boundary; round-9 amendment does NOT fire — cap reverts to round-7c NC-c rule per H.41i).** B2 is outside B1's influence radius; D.2 returns `0` for **B2's contribution at B1.position** (this is the cross-beacon contribution, NOT B1's own native contribution) by the radius-cull rule. **B1's own `beacon_native_contribution_at_B(B1) = 0.95 × falloff(0) = 0.95`** — B1 is evaluated at its own position (d=0) and the falloff function returns 1.0 at d=0; this value is well above the `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR × 1.5 = 0.03` State C threshold, so State C does NOT fire and the cap rule's math runs (round-13 red-team I3 disambiguation closure — clarifies that the two distinct "0" values in the cap-formula derivation are **B2's contribution at B1.position = 0** AND `non_beacon_contribution_at_B(B1) = 0` (i.e., the denominator-input excluding B1 itself, which is also zero because B2 is excluded per NC-c and ambient is zero by setup), NOT `beacon_native_contribution_at_B(B1) = 0` which would mis-trigger State C). Round-7c NC-c `non_beacon_contribution_at_B(B1) = 0` (no ambient, all beacons excluded — including B2 because the round-9 amendment's `dist < INFLUENCE_RADIUS` predicate evaluates false). Cap formula: `scaleFactor = (0.65 - 0) / 0.95 ≈ 0.684`. B1's effective contribution = `0.95 × 0.684 ≈ 0.65`. `fieldValue(B1.position) = 0 + 0 + 0.65 = 0.65` — exactly Hunt floor (design intent preserved; no compounding because B2 is too distant to contribute). The boundary-test asserts `fieldValue(B1.position) ∈ [0.65 − 0.001, 0.65 + 0.001] = [0.649, 0.651]` (symmetric ±0.001 tolerance — round-13 R12-B7 verification confirms sub-test (c) was already symmetric, re-expressed in `[low, high]` interval form for notational consistency with sub-tests (a) and (b)) AND that the cap-engagement code path took the round-7c NC-c branch (`MockOrderingRecorder` records that the `dist < INFLUENCE_RADIUS` predicate evaluated false for B2 at this distance). Verifies that the round-9 amendment is correctly scoped to `dist < INFLUENCE_RADIUS` (strict less-than) and does NOT spuriously apply at-or-beyond the boundary.

The 23.9 / 24.1 boundary pair is the load-bearing test for the round-9 DR-2 = Option B amendment's `dist < INFLUENCE_RADIUS` predicate (round-9 R9-I6 forward-obligation). The harness's source-placement helper MUST support sub-stud placement tolerance (±0.05 studs) per the R9-I6 prereq table row; integer-stud-precision placement would make sub-tests (b) and (c) unsoundable. Verifies the round-9 DR-2 = Option B co-location cap-rule amendment closes the silent design-intent violation across the entire `[0, INFLUENCE_RADIUS)` co-location range.

**H.41m** — AUTO-INTEGRATION (round-9 B-NS9 / B-CC3-B — three-state cap-state discriminant server compute + push verification) — GIVEN four mock clients with `MockRemoteSignalRecorder` installed AND four test-fixture-constructed scenarios covering each discriminant outcome per the C.3.4 cap-state discriminant rules:
- **Scenario 1 (no-cap-cue):** active beacon B; squad-of-4 within 16 studs of B; all 4 players actively walking (`cumulativeXZ ≥ 6.0` over their 3.0 s windows); cap NOT engaged.
- **Scenario 2 (State A — cap-suppressing beacon):** active beacon B; squad-of-4 within 16 studs of B; all 4 players stationary (`cumulativeXZ < 6.0`); ambient `non_beacon_contribution_at_B = 0.10` (well below cap = 0.65); cap engaged with `scaleFactor ≈ 0.579`.
- **Scenario 3 (State B — cap fired but ambient dominates):** active beacon B; squad-of-4 within 16 studs of B; all 4 players stationary; ambient `non_beacon_contribution_at_B = 0.70` (above cap = 0.65; constructed via test-fixture gather sequence); cap engaged with `scaleFactor` clamped to 0.0 per C.3.6 step 6.
- **Scenario 4 (State C — beacon near expiry):** active beacon B at age = 449 s such that `m(B.age) × falloff(0) < MAGNITUDE_FLOOR × 1.5 = 0.03` (E.25 denominator-zero protection short-circuits the cap rule); squad-of-4 stationary nearby.

WHEN the field-update pass runs in each scenario AND `OnMeterUpdate` fires for each player, THEN every captured payload's `beaconCapState`, `nearby`, and `stationary_nearby` fields match the C.3.4 discriminant decision tree:
- **(a) Scenario 1:** `beaconCapState = nil` AND (if no prior cap engagement within past 1.0 s) `nearby = nil`, `stationary_nearby = nil`. If a prior cap engagement transitioned within past 1.0 s, `nearby` and `stationary_nearby` carry their CURRENT live values per the F.2a row 7 persistence rule (`math.ceil(1.0 × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ)`-pass decay counter — round-11 B-R10-3 supersedes the round-9 hardcoded 5-pass anchor).
- **(b) Scenario 2:** `beaconCapState = "A"`, `nearby = 4`, `stationary_nearby = 4`.
- **(c) Scenario 3:** `beaconCapState = "B"`, `nearby = 4`, `stationary_nearby = 4`.
- **(d) Scenario 4 (round-11 B-R10-2 nil-on-State-C closure; round-11 red-team I-2 test-isolation closure):** `beaconCapState = "C"`, `nearby = nil`, `stationary_nearby = nil` — assuming the test scenario constructs Scenario 4 from cold (no prior cap engagement within the past `math.ceil(1.0 × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ)` passes; the harness uses `_setRingBufferState` to construct fresh per-player buffers and no prior State A/B transition). **Test-isolation precondition (round-11 red-team I-2 closure):** the test fixture MUST explicitly reset `_capCueDecayPassesRemaining[player]` to 0 (or nil — implementation choice; nil and 0 produce identical behaviour because the persistence rule decrements only when the entry is > 0) for ALL players at fixture setUp, BEFORE constructing Scenario 4's preconditions. The harness exposes a `DisturbanceService:_resetCapCueDecayPassesRemaining(): ()` test seam (server-internal only; H.39c grep-verifies non-Client per round-13 R12-B3 + R12-B2 expansion; the seam is REQUIRED for sub-AC (d)'s test-isolation guarantee — without it, residual persistence state from a prior sub-test contaminates the cold-start assertion AND sub-AC (d) FAILS spuriously even on a correct implementation). The seam may be implemented as `function DisturbanceService:_resetCapCueDecayPassesRemaining() self._capCueDecayPassesRemaining = {} end` or equivalent. The seam is mechanically tracked in the Test Infrastructure Prerequisites table per its dedicated row (round-13 R12-B2 closure of the round-11 forward-obligation deferral that did not execute in-session). The C-state takes evaluation-order priority over A and B per the C.3.4 step 3 strict if/elseif/else chain because the short-circuit happens BEFORE the cap rule's `scaleFactor` math runs — but per round-11 B-R10-2 the populations are nil unless the persistence rule fires (the round-9 wording populated them unconditionally on State C, producing the "we're capping it" HUD-read defect on a state where the cap NEVER engaged; round-11 closes this). **Sub-AC (d-persistence) — separate sub-test for the prior-cap-engagement-into-State-C case (round-11 B-R10-2 persistence-rule verification):** GIVEN a setup where Scenario 2 (State A active) runs for at least one pass producing a populated payload, then the harness aged the beacon directly via `_setLiveSourceState` to push `beacon_native_contribution_at_B` below `MAGNITUDE_FLOOR × 1.5 = 0.03` on the next pass (transitioning State A → State C between passes N and N+1), THEN on pass N+1 the captured payload has `beaconCapState = "C"`, `nearby = 4`, `stationary_nearby = 4` (populated via the persistence rule because the prior pass had State A within the persistence window) AND on pass N+1+`math.ceil(1.0 × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ)` (after the persistence counter has decremented to 0) the captured payload has `beaconCapState = "C"`, `nearby = nil`, `stationary_nearby = nil`. Both halves of the (d-persistence) sub-test MUST pass.

PLUS persistence sub-AC (e): GIVEN Scenario 2 transitions mid-test from `stationary_nearby = 3, nearby = 4` (cap engaged at ratio 3/4 = 0.75 > 0.50) to `stationary_nearby = 2, nearby = 4` (cap disengages because 2/4 = 0.50 NOT > 0.50), THEN the next `N = math.ceil(1.0 × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ)` field-update passes' captured payloads have `beaconCapState = nil` AND `nearby = 4`, `stationary_nearby = 2` populated (post-hoc attribution per F.2a row 7 "past 1.0 s" rule); the (N+1)-th and subsequent passes have all three fields nil. **Cadence-tracking sub-test (round-11 B-R10-3 closure; round-13 I2 fold-in expands triplet to cover the safe-range upper bound):** the test MUST be parameterised against `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ` and run at THREE distinct cadences spanning the full G.3 safe range — at the default `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ = 5 Hz` the count is `math.ceil(1.0 × 5) = 5` passes (1.0 s persistence), at the safe-range lower bound `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ = 2 Hz` the count is `math.ceil(1.0 × 2) = 2` passes (1.0 s persistence), AND at the safe-range upper bound `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ = 10 Hz` the count is `math.ceil(1.0 × 10) = 10` passes (1.0 s persistence). All three sub-runs MUST pass; a hardcoded-5 implementation passes at 5 Hz but FAILS at 2 Hz (would persist for 5 / 2 = 2.5 s instead of the contracted 1.0 s) AND FAILS at 10 Hz (would persist for 5 / 10 = 0.5 s instead of 1.0 s). The 10 Hz upper-bound case is the round-13 I2 fold-in closure — the round-11 wording covered the lower-bound failure mode but not the upper-bound failure mode (under-persistence at high cadence is symmetric to over-persistence at low cadence; both break F.2a row 7 post-hoc attribution UI). The cadence-tracking sub-test is the load-bearing regression closure for round-10 B-R10-3 / systems-designer F2 + performance-analyst F5 convergent finding AND the round-12 I2 / qa-lead + performance-analyst convergent finding.

PLUS routing sub-AC (f): GIVEN Scenario 2 with one player (P_far) located at >24 studs from beacon B (outside `INFLUENCE_RADIUS`), WHEN `OnMeterUpdate` fires, THEN P_far's payload has `beaconCapState = nil`, `nearby = nil`, `stationary_nearby = nil` (no in-range beacon for P_far); the other three players' payloads carry the cap-state fields populated. Verifies the per-player affecting-beacon scan in C.3.4 is correctly scoped.

PLUS bandwidth sub-AC (g) — MANUAL-REVIEW (round-11 IMPORTANT cluster — 4-spec convergent on wording / measurement gap; relabelled from AUTO-INTEGRATION because Lemur-headless and Studio-local-server measurement of per-RemoteSignal serialised payload size is not exposed by Roblox's API surface, so the original AUTO formulation was unimplementable): GIVEN two engineers independently inspect the `OnMeterUpdate` `MeterUpdatePayload` Luau type definition (C.3.4) AND the C.3.4 cap-state-discriminant computation block AND a sample-trace of the field's serialisation form (engineer captures via the `MockSerializer` proxy described below or via Luau-side serialised `string.byte`-counted approximation), THEN both engineers attest that: (a) the per-payload **increment** (NOT total — round-11 wording fix; "total" was the round-10 4-spec finding) attributable to the round-9 DR-1 = Option A-added three fields (`beaconCapState`, `nearby`, `stationary_nearby`) — measured as the difference in per-payload serialised byte count between (i) a payload with all three fields populated AND (ii) a payload with all three fields nil — does not exceed **25 bytes/payload per player** (the 20-byte target with 5-byte headroom for framing variance). The measurement is **per-player**, NOT server-aggregated (the round-10 4-spec ambiguity finding); each player's `OnMeterUpdate` payload is constructed independently and the increment is per-payload regardless of how many players are connected. (b) The per-second **per-player incoming** bandwidth attributable to the increment does not exceed `25 bytes × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ = 125 bytes/s` at the default 5 Hz cadence, scaling proportionally with `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ` (e.g., at 10 Hz the increment is `≤ 250 bytes/s`; at 2 Hz `≤ 50 bytes/s`). Both engineers' attestations MUST be recorded in `production/qa/evidence/H41m-g-bandwidth-review-[sprint-id].md` in a **bandwidth-attestation-shaped evidence-file format (round-13 I11 fold-in; supersedes the round-11 wording that borrowed H.39d's call-graph-traversal format which is wrong-shape for bandwidth attestation)**. Required fields (the CI gate parses the file via grep — same gate-style as H.39d but the field set is different):

> **Evidence-file template + gate-parse rules (relocated round-22 AC-extraction; relocation only):** the H.41m sub-AC (g) bandwidth-attestation evidence-file template, the CI gate's numeric-field parse rules, and the optional MockSerializer AUTO-INTEGRATION-upgrade mechanism are in **`ecological-disturbance-verification.md` § H.41m-g bandwidth review**.

Verifies the round-9 DR-1 = Option A data-surface that closes Cluster 3 / B-CC3-A and B-CC3-B.

**H.41n** — AUTO-UNIT (round-9 B-NS2 — solo + under-filled buffer + stationary → cap does NOT engage during first 3.0 s; cap DOES engage on/after 3.0 s) — GIVEN exactly one connected player (`connected = 1`) within `BEACON_STATIONARY_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 16` studs of an active Beacon B (`nearby = 1`) AND the player's ring buffer has 0 samples at the start of the test (just-joined or just-buffer-reset state) AND the test harness uses `_setRingBufferState(player, {})` to ensure the empty-buffer precondition AND the player's actual XZ-position remains within `BEACON_CUMULATIVE_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD / 4 = 1.5 studs` of their initial position throughout the test (genuinely-stationary play), WHEN the test harness invokes `_runFieldUpdatePass()` 15 times in succession with `_advanceTestClock(0.2)` between each (advancing real time by exactly `15 × 0.2 = 3.0 s = BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW`), THEN: (a) on passes 1–14 (buffer has 1–14 samples; under-filled per the round-7c B-7b-4 precondition): `stationary_nearby = 0`, ratio `0/1 = 0 NOT > 0.50`, cap does NOT engage; `MeterUpdatePayload.beaconCapState = nil` per C.3.4 discriminant; `fieldValue(B.position)` reflects B's full unmodified contribution (Retreat-tier); (b) on pass 15 (buffer has 15 samples; fully-populated): `cumulativeXZ < BEACON_CUMULATIVE_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD = 6.0` (genuinely-stationary play accumulates < 6.0 studs over the 3.0 s window); `stationary_nearby = 1`, ratio `1/1 = 1.0 > 0.50`, cap engages; `beaconCapState = "A"` (assuming ambient < cap; otherwise State B); `fieldValue(B.position) = HUNT_THRESHOLD = 0.65` exactly. The 3.0 s under-filled-buffer window is verified as accepted-as-designed (cap does NOT engage; this is the round-7c B-7b-4 precondition's intended outcome); the 3.0 s+ post-fill window is verified as cap-engaging (the round-9 B-NS2 design-clarification AC). PLUS sub-AC (c): repeat the test with the player ACTUALLY moving (XZ-displacement > 6.0 studs cumulative over 3.0 s); cap MUST NOT engage at pass 15 either (`stationary_nearby = 0` because cumulativeXZ ≥ 6.0; ratio `0/1 NOT > 0.50`). Sub-AC (c) verifies the buffer-fill precondition does not falsely classify a moving newcomer as stationary even after the buffer fills.

**H.41o** — AUTO-UNIT (round-9 B-NS6 — `Character.PrimaryPart` nil-guard on ring-buffer write) — GIVEN a connected player whose `player.Character ~= nil` (CharacterAdded has fired) but whose `player.Character.PrimaryPart == nil` (PrimaryPart has not yet replicated to the server; the test harness uses the round-9 characterless-player harness mechanism extended to allow `Character = mockCharacter` where `mockCharacter.PrimaryPart = nil`, simulating the rare-but-reachable race window between CharacterAdded firing and PrimaryPart-assignment replicating) AND an active Beacon B in the live-source list with the player within 16 studs of B's nominal position, WHEN `_runFieldUpdatePass()` runs, THEN: (a) the ring buffer for that player is NOT appended this pass (per the C.3.6 step 4 round-9 nil-guard idiom); (b) no `attempt to index nil with 'Position'` runtime error is raised; (c) `--!strict` type-check on the source code passes (verified via static analysis grep over the C.3.6 step 4 buffer-write code path showing the canonical `local primaryPart = character.PrimaryPart; if not primaryPart then return end` idiom — or its functional equivalent that performs the nil-check before any `.Position` access — is present); (d) the player is excluded from `nearby` at C.3.6 step 1 (the 16-stud proximity check cannot evaluate against a nil PrimaryPart) AND from `connected` at step 2 IF the implementation chooses to extend the round-7c B-7b-5 character-spawned filter to also require non-nil PrimaryPart (recommended; the C.3.6 step 4 nil-guard makes the behaviour identical regardless of whether step 2's filter is extended); (e) on the subsequent pass where the test harness sets `mockCharacter.PrimaryPart = mockBasePart` (PrimaryPart "replicates"), the buffer resumes appending normally and the player rejoins `nearby` if their position is within 16 studs of B's position. Verifies the round-9 B-NS6 nil-guard idiom prevents the silent runtime crash that round-8 flagged.

### Performance

**H.36a** — AUTO-UNIT (instrumentation requirement; round-9 Cluster 4 / B-CC4-A scope expansion + B-NS8 discontinuity-detection cadence fold-in) — GIVEN the implementation under test, WHEN a code search for `debug.profilebegin("DisturbanceService_Update")` and `debug.profilebegin("DisturbanceService_HotspotQuery")` runs (grep- or AST-level), THEN both tags are present and balanced with `debug.profileend()` calls. The `DisturbanceService_Update` region MUST enclose ALL of the following per-pass workloads (round-9 expanded to capture every workload added by round-7c heavy patches and the round-9 cap-state discriminant — the round-7c wording omitted four of these and admitted an implementation that left them OUTSIDE the profile fence, silently violating H.36's 2 ms budget without measurement detection):
1. The per-source decay cache (`m(age)` evaluation per live source per pass; D.1).
2. The field-value evaluation at all flora positions in active chunks (D.2/D.3 on each `MAX_FLORA_PER_CHUNK × loaded_chunks` flora).
3. The field-value evaluation at all connected players' positions (D.2/D.3 on each connected player's XZ).
4. The tier-classification + `TierCrossedEvent` firing (D.4 with post-hitch settle loop; round-3 server-side coalescing).
5. **(Round-9 fold-in for round-7c B-7b-4 / B-7b-5 ring-buffer additions.)** Per-player XZ-position-history ring-buffer maintenance: append the player's current XZ to the buffer (1 entry per player per pass at 5 Hz default), evict oldest sample if buffer exceeds `floor(BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ) = 15` capacity, and compute `cumulativeXZ` (sum of inter-sample 2D displacements) on each per-player buffer for any pass where any beacon is in cap-evaluation range. Per-pass cost: O(connectedPlayers × 15) sums in worst case (all 4 players have full 15-sample buffers and a beacon is active) — at default tunings this is 60 vector subtractions + magnitude computations per pass.
6. **(Round-9 fold-in for round-7c B-7b-4 / NC-c cap-evaluation additions.)** Per-beacon cap-state evaluation: for each currently-active Beacon emission B (defined per C.3.6 step's beacon-detection rule), execute C.3.6 steps 1–7 — count `nearby`, `connected`, `stationary_nearby`; evaluate engagement predicates; compute `non_beacon_contribution_at_B` per the round-7c NC-c all-beacons-excluded rule and the round-9 DR-2 = Option B co-location amendment; compute `scaleFactor` and clamp to `[0.0, 1.0]`; apply E.25 sub-mode (iii) denominator-zero short-circuit. Per-pass cost: O(activeBeacons × connectedPlayers + activeBeacons × liveSources) bounded by `activeBeacons × 4 + activeBeacons × MAX_LIVE_SOURCES` (round-23 B3: this ceiling — 3 × 500 = 1,500 unaccelerated contribution evaluations per pass — is now MEASURED by the extended H.38b fixture; no prior fixture exercised it).
7. **(Round-9 addition for C.3.4 cap-state discriminant per DR-1 = Option A.)** Per-player cap-state discriminant computation: identify the affecting beacon (per-player O(activeBeacons) scan), apply the C.3.4 discriminant decision tree, populate `MeterUpdatePayload.beaconCapState` / `.nearby` / `.stationary_nearby` (or carry over from the per-player `_capCueDecayPassesRemaining` counter for the F.2a row 7 "past 1.0 s" persistence rule), decrement the counter for any player whose `beaconCapState` transitioned to nil this pass.
8. **(Round-9 fold-in for round-7a R7-B6 / E.25 teleport-discontinuity rule per B-NS8.)** Per-player E.25 teleport-discontinuity detection: compute the per-player per-frame XZ-position delta (the magnitude of the 2D displacement between the player's position at this pass and at the previous pass) and compare against `2 × BEACON_CUMULATIVE_DISTANCE_THRESHOLD = 12 studs` per pass. **The cadence is the field-update cadence (5 Hz default), NOT Heartbeat (60 Hz)**, because the discontinuity-check's only consumer is the ring-buffer-mark-discontinuous logic at C.3.6 step 4 / E.25, which itself runs at field-update cadence per the round-9 specification. Running the discontinuity-detection at 60 Hz Heartbeat would consume Heartbeat budget unnecessarily AND would not catch additional cases (the field-update pass at 5 Hz is the only consumer; intermediate Heartbeat readings are discarded). Per-pass cost: O(connectedPlayers) — 4 vector subtractions + magnitude computations per pass.
9. The `OnMeterUpdate` per-player targeted `RemoteSignal:Fire` invocation (C.3.4 routing rule) — non-yielding per Roblox `RemoteSignal:Fire` semantics; cost is O(connectedPlayers) network-frame writes.
10. **(Round-22 I16 — the Session-C C.3.3 addition, folded into the instrumentation list per this AC's own completeness rule.)** The squad-aggregate band-crossing check: classify the (already-cached, workload-3-derived) `squadT` via D.4, compare against `previousSquadTier`, fire `OnDisturbanceBandCrossed` on a confirmed change, update `previousSquadTier` (C.3.3). Per-pass cost: O(1) — one classification + one comparison reusing the D.5 per-pass cache; measured rather than assumed, per the round-9 B-CC4-A discipline.
11. **(Round-23 B2/PF-1.)** Attribution-archive maintenance: the C.1.11 three-condition purge scan (TTL / all-referencing-players-left / predator-lock-referenced) PLUS `MAX_ATTRIBUTION_ARCHIVE_ENTRIES` cap enforcement. Runs on the `EXPIRY_MAINTENANCE_INTERVAL` cadence (not every pass), but on the passes where it runs it MUST execute INSIDE the `DisturbanceService_Update` profile fence. Per-scan cost: O(archiveEntries), bounded by `MAX_ATTRIBUTION_ARCHIVE_ENTRIES` (4000) — previously this workload was wholly unfenced and unbounded by count.

The `DisturbanceService_HotspotQuery` region MUST enclose: the `GetHottestHotspot` body (D.6 — candidate enumeration via the spatial grid, dedup, scoring, top-3 contributor scan via `topContributorsAt`).

**Round-9 Cluster 4 / B-CC4-A binding clarification (round-22: expanded 1–9 → 1–10; round-23 B2: → 1–11):** any code path that performs work in the categories 1–11 above (decay cache, field-value evaluation, tier classification, ring-buffer maintenance, cap evaluation, cap-state discriminant, teleport-discontinuity detection, OnMeterUpdate fire, squad-aggregate band-crossing check, attribution-archive maintenance) MUST execute INSIDE the `DisturbanceService_Update` profile fence — placing any of these workloads outside the fence (e.g., as a separate `RunService.Stepped` callback to "save Heartbeat budget") violates this AC and silently invalidates H.36's measurement scope. The grep / AST verification MUST detect any of these workloads outside the fence: a successful pass requires ALL eleven workloads to be lexically nested between `debug.profilebegin("DisturbanceService_Update")` and the matching `debug.profileend()` in the source file. This is a precondition for H.36 — it is the implementation requirement that PERF-DEVICE testing depends on.

**H.36** — PERF-DEVICE (round-9 Cluster 4 / B-CC4-A — TWO fixture variants required) — GIVEN a 4-player Hunt-state session per art bible §8.11 Test 1 conditions (60 seconds elapsed; mixed Gather/Sprint/Light/Light-pulse emissions) AND H.36a's instrumentation is in place AND **the test fixture drives BOTH of the following variants in sequence (Variant 1 first, then Variant 2; each in its own 5-second MicroProfiler sample window):**

**Variant 1 — Beacon-inactive worst-case (the round-5 baseline).** The worst-case upper-bound scenario for source count: continuous sprint + steady lantern across all 4 players, producing `~207 live sources at the 60-second mark` (round-5 point estimate; derivation: 4 players × 30 sprint pulses/min × ~1.16 min Sprint lifetime [`ln(MAGNITUDE_SPRINT_PULSE / MAGNITUDE_FLOOR) / λ = ln(5) / (ln(2)/30) ≈ 69.7 s`] + 4 players × 15 light pulses/min × ~1.00 min Light lifetime [`ln(MAGNITUDE_LIGHT_PULSE / MAGNITUDE_FLOOR) / λ = ln(4) / (ln(2)/30) ≈ 60.0 s`] + ~8 gathers [round-22 I17 — derivation for the previously-bare term: assumes ~1 medium-node gather per player per minute during Hunt-state coordinated play (art bible §8.11 Test 1 cadence); per-player steady-state = 1/min × ~1.82 min Gather-medium lifetime (`ln(0.25/0.02)/λ ≈ 109 s`) ≈ 1.8 ≈ 2 concurrent; 4 players × 2 ≈ 8. If playtest shows a materially higher coordinated-gather rate, this term and the 207 ceiling MUST be re-derived] ≈ 207 sources); full bioluminescent saturation in two loaded chunks (`MAX_FLORA_PER_CHUNK = 8` per art bible §6.5 → 16 flora query positions per cycle); predator active in-chunk; **NO beacon active** during the sample window. This variant exercises decay cache + field-value evaluation + tier classification + per-player ring-buffer maintenance (no cap path because no beacon) + teleport-discontinuity detection + OnMeterUpdate without cap-state discriminant. The H.36a workloads 1–5 + 8 + 9 are exercised; workloads 6–7 are exercised only minimally (the per-pass cap-evaluation loop iterates over zero beacons). **Archive pre-seed (round-23 B2; procedure pinned round-24 Gate-B1 F1.1):** the fixture pre-seeds the attribution archive to `MAX_ATTRIBUTION_ARCHIVE_ENTRIES` (4000) BEFORE the sample window. A single synthetic burst CANNOT do this — an injection batch is bounded by `MAX_LIVE_SOURCES` (500), and cap-dropped emissions write NO archive entry (C.1.11 / H.35b) — so the pre-seed MUST use the **batch-and-decay cycle**: inject ≤ 500 synthetic emissions, advance `_advanceTestClock` + `_runFieldUpdatePass` past their live-list expiry (their archive entries persist under the 1200 s TTL), repeat ~8 cycles; the harness MUST then **ASSERT the archive count == 4000 before the MicroProfiler window opens** (a silently under-filled pre-seed reproduces the exact measured-vs-assumed defect PF-1 closed, one level down). If the implementation instead adds a dedicated archive-seeding seam, it is server-internal and H.39c-listed like its peers.

**Variant 2 — Beacon-active worst-case (round-9 addition; B-CC4-A closure — measures the cap path; FIXTURE POPULATION RESTATED round-22 B9).** *The prior "Same as Variant 1 plus … all 4 players stationary" wording was self-contradictory and unreproducible as written: Variant 1's ~207-source population derives from continuous MOVING sprint+lantern at full magnitudes, while the stationary precondition that engages the cap forces the hold-floor population (~138, per the lower-bound derivation below) — the two cannot coexist in one natural fixture.* **Normative Variant-2 fixture:** all 4 players stationary (`cumulativeXZ < 6.0` in their 3.0 s windows, sprint held, lanterns raised) for the entire sample window, producing the **~138-source stationary steady-state population** (hold-floor Sprint stream ~130 + attenuated Light stream ~8; see the lower-bound derivation), **clustered near the beacon** (all 4 players within `BEACON_STATIONARY_PROXIMITY_RADIUS = 16` studs of Beacon B — this clustering, not the total count, is what stresses the cap path and the per-cell density; see the round-22 B8 per-cell note below). The cap rule is engaged on B for every pass; the per-pass workloads include the full cap-evaluation chain (H.36a workload 6 — `non_beacon_contribution_at_B` summation over all live sources excluding beacons; clamp computation) AND the cap-state discriminant computation (H.36a workload 7 — per-player affecting-beacon scan + State A/B/C decision tree + `_capCueDecayPassesRemaining` counter management). The fixture also includes a SECOND beacon B2 placed > 24 studs from B (so H.41i order-independence holds) AND a THIRD beacon B3 co-located within 4 studs of B (so the round-9 DR-2 = Option B co-location amendment is exercised). Total active beacons: 3 (per-pass cap-evaluation iterates 3 times). This variant exercises EVERY H.36a workload 1–10 at the worst-case cap-path burden the contract admits; **its PASS criterion is measured against this ~138-clustered population** — the 207-source total-count worst case is Variant 1's job (cap path minimally exercised there because no beacon is active). A harness MAY additionally pre-seed synthetic sources toward 207 near the beacon as an adversarial extension, but the normative PASS/FAIL fixture is the naturally-forced ~138-clustered population above.

AND **the device has been running the test scenario for ≥ 10 minutes prior to each Variant's sample window** (sustained-thermal steady-state — see thermal derating policy below) AND **MicroProfiler captures a 5-second sample window per Variant** in a Studio local-server session on the iPhone SE-class device (production-deploy MicroProfiler is not available; this is a Studio-local-server PERF-DEVICE test), THEN for BOTH Variant 1 and Variant 2: the average `DisturbanceService_Update` frame budget does not exceed `2 ms / 1.4 ≈ 1.43 ms` per cycle (Tier-2 #18 thermal derating: ≥ 1.4× margin on the 2 ms target accounts for sustained-load ARM CPU throttling) and the 99th-percentile budget does not exceed `4 ms / 1.4 ≈ 2.86 ms` per cycle. **A failure on EITHER Variant fails the AC.** Variant 1 establishes the no-cap baseline; Variant 2 verifies the cap path stays within budget under the round-9 expanded H.36a profile-fence scope. **This AC is achievable only with the C.1.6 spatial grid implemented for both `fieldValue()` queries AND `GetHottestHotspot` candidate enumeration.**

**Scope clause — `activeBeacons ≤ 3` (round-15 R14-B11 closure of round-13 R13-I2 elevation; CD-recommended path Option A from round-15 design ruling).** This AC's PASS criterion is implicitly bounded by `activeBeacons ≤ 3` per concept-doc constraint (max 3 crafting stations active at once per session). Variant 2's worst-case fixture exercises the 3-beacon ceiling explicitly. **The PASS criterion is VALID ONLY when the production constraint `activeBeacons ≤ 3` holds.** If any production scenario admits `activeBeacons > 3` — whether via Crafting GDD design change, live-ops seasonal content, or an unintended emission path — H.36's PASS criterion is INVALIDATED and the R11-I8 grid-mandate forward obligation BECOMES BINDING (must elevate from "deferred forward obligation" to "in-AC mandate") BEFORE H.36 can be re-validated against the new ceiling. The dependency between Crafting's `MAX_ACTIVE_BEACONS` constraint and this GDD's H.36 budget proof is BIDIRECTIONAL — the dependency is locked at both ends:

- **Crafting GDD-side lock (round-15 R14-B11 cross-GDD mandate; see F.6 cross-GDD mandates below).** The Crafting & Items GDD MUST author a runtime constraint `MAX_ACTIVE_BEACONS = 3` and reject Beacon-emission publish-attempts at the Crafting publish-site BEFORE the emission reaches `DisturbanceService:Emit("Beacon", ...)`. The constraint MUST be tested by a Crafting-side AUTO-UNIT verifying that emission-publish-attempts beyond cap are rejected with a documented error code. Without this lock, the H.36 PASS criterion is vulnerable to a silent Crafting GDD revision that admits `activeBeacons > 3` AND would silently invalidate H.36's measurement scope.
- **DisturbanceService-side defence-in-depth (informational, not normative for this round).** The DisturbanceService ADR MAY add a defence-in-depth `activeBeacons ≤ 3` check inside `DisturbanceService:Emit` that drops Beacon emissions beyond the cap with an ops-log entry — parallel to the existing `MAX_LIVE_SOURCES` cap-drop policy at E.12. The ADR's choice on whether to add this defence-in-depth is left open; the round-15 design ruling chose Option A (scope-clause + Crafting-side cross-GDD lock) over Option C (DisturbanceService-side runtime cap), so the DisturbanceService-side check is non-binding. If the ADR chooses to add the check, H.35b's drop-policy AC formulation is the template.
- **R11-I8 forward obligation status:** R11-I8 (workload 7 grid-mandate) remains a forward obligation but is now PRECONDITIONALLY BINDING: it becomes binding the moment any production change admits `activeBeacons > 3`; until then, the linear-walk-over-beacons cost is bounded by `activeBeacons × {connectedPlayers, liveSources}` with `activeBeacons ≤ 3` and the grid-mandate is non-load-bearing.
- **R13-I2 closure status:** R13-I2 is now CLOSED inline at this AC's scope clause. The R13-I forward obligation tracking entry is updated to mark CLOSED (see F.2a-IMPORTANT round-13 cluster).

**Re-validation trigger note.** If a future round of design or implementation reveals an `activeBeacons > 3` scenario, the proper sequence is: (1) elevate R11-I8 to in-AC mandate in this GDD; (2) update Variant 2's fixture to include the new beacon count; (3) re-measure H.36 against the expanded fixture on iPhone SE class device; (4) update the PASS criterion's scope clause to reflect the new ceiling. Skipping any step risks silent budget violation in production. The CD-recommended sequence is the only valid path; abbreviated paths (e.g., "we'll just add another beacon and re-run H.36") are insufficient because R11-I8's grid-mandate is the architectural enabler for higher beacon counts; without it, the measurement scope is invalid.

**Scope clause — `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ = 5` (round-23 B4/PF-3; parallel to the `activeBeacons ≤ 3` clause above).** The PASS criteria of H.36, H.37, H.37b, H.38, and H.38b are VALID ONLY at the default `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ = 5 Hz`. Per-cycle cost does NOT stay flat across the knob's 2–10 Hz safe range: workload 5's ring buffer sizes with the cadence (`floor(BEACON_STATIONARY_WINDOW × FIELD_UPDATE_HZ)` samples/player — per-cycle cost doubles at 10 Hz while per-second cost quadruples), and every per-pass workload's per-second total scales linearly with the cadence. G.4's own escape hatch ("visible visual lag → raise the cadence") therefore exits the measured envelope: **any retune of `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ` INVALIDATES all PERF-DEVICE PASS records and requires re-measurement at the new cadence on the iPhone SE-class device BEFORE the retune ships.** The G.4 row carries the cross-reference flag.

**Lower-bound scenario (informational, not a separate AC; round-7a R7-B17 corrected per CD adjudication D4; UPDATED 2026-06-19 — ED-yields ruling: the hold-floor removes Sprint attenuation, so the stationary lower-bound rises ~40 → ~138).** A fully stationary 4-player squad with sprint held settles around **~138 sources**. Derivation:
- **Sprint stream lifetime (hold-floor, NOT attenuated — 2026-06-19):** the published stationary-sprint magnitude is `max(SPRINT_HOLD_FLOOR 0.09, 0.030) = 0.09`, so `ln(0.09 / 0.02) / (ln(2)/30) ≈ 65.1 s`. Per-player live count: `65.1 s / 2 s ≈ 32.5 pulses`. 4-player total: `4 × 32.5 ≈ 130 sources`. (The prior ~17.55 s / ~35-source figure assumed the now-superseded 0.030 attenuated magnitude.)
- **Light stream lifetime (attenuated — unchanged; Light has no hold-floor):** `ln(0.024 / 0.02) / (ln(2)/30) ≈ 7.89 s`. Per-player live count: `7.89 s / 4 s ≈ 2.0 pulses`. 4-player total: `4 × 2.0 ≈ 8 sources`.
- **Combined:** Sprint + Light ≈ 130 + 8 ≈ **~138 sources**.

Naturalistic 4-player play falls in the corridor between ~138 (stationary, sprint held) and ~207 (continuous moving-sprint + lantern, all moving). The H.36 PASS criterion is anchored to the 207-upper-bound (worst-case CPU budget); the lower-bound figure is documented for cross-reference and PR-discussion only and does not gate any AC. **Budget is UNAFFECTED on the TOTAL-count axis:** ~138 < 207 and per-player-per-type moving strictly dominates stationary in source count (moving magnitudes ≥ stationary magnitudes for both Sprint 0.10 ≥ 0.09 and Light 0.08 ≥ 0.024, and lifetime is monotonic in magnitude), so NO mixed moving/stationary squad profile can exceed the ~207 total; H.36/H.38 PASS criteria and `MAX_LIVE_SOURCES` (500) totals are unchanged. **⚠ Per-CELL density axis (round-22 B8 — the axis the prior "UNAFFECTED" claim did not cover):** H.36a workloads 2/3/6 and the D.6 hotspot scan are cost-driven by LOCAL per-cell source density, not the global total, per C.1.6's clustered-source caveat — and the hold-floor materially raised the worst NATURAL per-cell density: a single stationary sprint-holding player (no squad required) accumulates `65.1 s / 2 s ≈ 32.5` co-located Sprint sources + ~2 Light ≈ **~35 sources in one 32-stud grid cell** (pre-ruling: ~10.75). This exceeded H.38's prior "no single cell holds more than ~30 sources" distributed-fixture assumption — H.38's fixture is updated this round to include a natural-stationary-cluster cell (~40 sources) so the distributed-path budget is measured at ≥ the natural per-cell maximum. The pathological bound is unchanged: H.38b's synthetic 200+-sources-in-one-cell stress case (4 ms ceiling) still dominates the natural ~35 by >5×, so the budget CLAIM survives — but via H.38b's stress ceiling plus the updated H.38 fixture, not via the total-count comparison alone. **History:** the round-5 ~103 figure mistakenly applied the Sprint unattenuated lifetime (~70 s) to the Light stream; round-7 PA-F1's ~51 mistakenly applied the Sprint attenuated lifetime (~17.55 s) to the Light stream; round-7a's ~40 was correct *for the then-canonical 0.030 attenuated Sprint magnitude*; the 2026-06-19 ED-yields ruling supersedes that magnitude (0.030 → 0.09 hold-floor), raising the Sprint-stream lifetime to ~65.1 s and the lower-bound to ~138. H.37 + H.38b downstream costs that propagate from this figure are NOT affected because they are anchored to the upper-bound 207, not the lower-bound — the lower-bound is informational only. **Round-7a forward note:** the DisturbanceService ADR's source-decay re-derivation (an obligation carried forward to ADR phase per round-7 PA pair-check) MUST re-verify these arithmetic anchors against the implemented decay code.

**Thermal derating policy (round-5 / Tier-2 #18).** PERF-DEVICE tests MUST run at thermal steady-state — the iPhone SE-class device MUST execute the test scenario for at least 10 minutes prior to the MicroProfiler 5-second sample window. The ≥ 1.4× margin applied to the nominal 2 ms / 4 ms budget accounts for ARM CPU throttling under sustained mobile load; without this margin, a non-thermally-soaked test on a fresh device can pass the AC while production play (where players sustain longer sessions) silently exceeds the budget.

**H.37** — PERF-DEVICE — GIVEN the same 4-player Hunt-state session as H.36 — but with an uplifted **4 loaded chunks** (deliberate worst-case bandwidth uplift above H.36's 2-chunk CPU scenario; 4 chunks better matches steady-state StreamingEnabled neighbourhood at session midpoint) receiving field updates AND a documented baseline-engine-replication budget **assumption** (assumed ≤ 30 KB/s for Roblox engine state replication — Humanoid, Character, physics — leaving ≤ 20 KB/s headroom for disturbance signals on the 50 KB/s per-client total ceiling), WHEN the server fires `FloraChunkUpdate` RemoteEvents over the 5-second sample, THEN total disturbance-signal outbound bandwidth per client (sum of `FloraChunkUpdate`, `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot`, `OnMeterUpdate`, `OnDisturbanceAlert`, and `OnDeathAttributionPushed`) does not exceed 20 KB/s. **Note**: H.36 uses 2 chunks for the CPU budget (matching art-bible §8.11 Test 1); H.37 uses 4 chunks for the bandwidth budget — the chunk-count uplift is intentional and conservative for H.37. Desk-calculated worst case at defaults, summed **per-client** (round-22 B6 correction — the prior total mis-summed by using the server-wide meter aggregate instead of the per-client figure computed in this same sentence): 8 flora × 4 chunks × 5 Hz × 44 bytes ≈ 7.04 KB/s flora; per-client incoming `OnMeterUpdate` = 5 Hz × ~70 bytes ≈ **0.35 KB/s** (round-9 payload: ~50 bytes baseline + ~20 bytes for the `beaconCapState` / `nearby` / `stationary_nearby` cap-state discriminant fields; the server-wide OUTBOUND aggregate across 4 players is 4 × 0.35 ≈ 1.4 KB/s, but that figure is not a per-client budget input); alert events ≤ 0.5 KB/s; mid-session `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` re-fires ≈ 0.28 KB/s (chunk-membership churn at an ASSUMED ≤ 1 chunk-churn / 10 s per client × ≈ 2.8 KB re-snapshot burst — the N2 ADR obligation MUST measure the real churn rate under coordinated movement; round-23 PF-4); `OnDeathAttributionPushed` ≈ negligible (≤ 1 per player-death, ~0.3 KB each, amortized < 0.01 KB/s); **per-client total ≈ 7.04 + 0.35 + 0.5 + 0.28 ≈ 8.2 KB/s** (round-23 PF-4 completes the 5-signal itemization — the round-22 "≈ 7.9" figure was a 3-term sum omitting the snapshot-churn and death-attribution terms; the earlier "≈ 8.9" had mis-summed the server-wide meter aggregate) — still well within the 20 KB/s sub-budget.

**Round-5 / Tier-2 #19 — engine-replication baseline is an assumption requiring profile validation.** The 30 KB/s engine-replication figure is a **ceiling assumption pending Roblox-side profile validation at architecture phase**, NOT a measured target. The DisturbanceService ADR MUST run a baseline profile (4 connected players, coordinated movement at near-max sprint speed, in StreamingEnabled-active chunks, no disturbance traffic — measured via the Roblox Studio network analyser or equivalent) and record the actual character / Humanoid / physics replication rate. **If the profiled baseline exceeds 30 KB/s under coordinated 4-player movement, the disturbance-signal sub-budget MUST be lowered such that engine-replication-baseline + disturbance-signal-budget ≤ 50 KB/s per client.** The lowered sub-budget propagates back to G.4: `FLORA_UPDATE_DELTA_THRESHOLD` would need to be raised, OR `MAX_FLORA_PER_CHUNK` lowered (anchored to art-bible §6.5; coordinate with art-director), OR `FIELD_UPDATE_HZ` dropped from 5 → 4 Hz. The architecture-phase profile-validation ruling is required before the H.37 AC's PASS criterion is locked in; until then, the 20 KB/s sub-budget is provisional and the AC is "informational-pass" rather than binding-pass.

**H.37b** — PERF-DEVICE (join-burst peak — round-3 addition) — GIVEN four mock clients connect simultaneously (within a 100 ms window) AND each client streams in 8 chunks AND each chunk contains `MAX_FLORA_PER_CHUNK` flora, WHEN the server fires `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` for each (client × chunk) pair, THEN the total burst of `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot` traffic per client completes delivery within 1000 ms of `Players.PlayerAdded` AND the burst-second outbound disturbance-signal bandwidth per client does not exceed 30 KB/s instantaneous (allows a 1.5× over the 20 KB/s sustained budget for the one-off join handshake; subsequent seconds MUST return below 20 KB/s per H.37). Desk-calc: 8 flora × 8 chunks × 44 bytes ≈ 2.8 KB per client snapshot burst — well within the instantaneous allowance.

**H.38** — PERF-DEVICE (fixture updated round-22 B8) — GIVEN `MAX_LIVE_SOURCES` live sources are distributed across the map such that (a) no single grid cell holds more than **~40** sources AND (b) **at least one cell holds ~40 sources — the natural-stationary-cluster case** (a single stationary sprint-holding player accumulates ~35 co-located sources under the 2026-06-19 hold-floor: `65.1 s / 2 s ≈ 32.5` Sprint + ~2 Light; the fixture's ~40 bounds it with margin; the prior "~30" assumption predated the hold-floor and was already exceeded by ordinary single-player behaviour) — AND a mock predator calls `GetHottestHotspot(callerPosition, searchRadius=200, minimumTier="Tense")` at 4 Hz over a 5-second window **with `callerPosition` centered on the ~40-source cluster cell for at least half the window** (the mock predator supplies a representative search-center as `callerPosition` per round-17 I16-11), WHEN MicroProfiler captures the sample, THEN the average `DisturbanceService_HotspotQuery` region cost does not exceed 1 ms per call and the 99th-percentile does not exceed 2 ms per call. (The pathological ≥200-per-cell case remains H.38b's job.)

**H.38b** — PERF-DEVICE (clustered-source worst case) — GIVEN the test harness uses **synthetic injection** (direct server-internal `Emit()` calls bypassing Q9 stationary attenuation and the normal pulse-cadence emitters) to fill ONE 32-stud grid cell with 200+ live sources at varied ages within `STANDARD_HALF_LIFE`; total live-source count across the map is `MAX_LIVE_SOURCES` (500); a mock predator queries `GetHottestHotspot` at 4 Hz, WHEN MicroProfiler captures a 5-second sample, THEN the average combined `DisturbanceService_Update + DisturbanceService_HotspotQuery` per-frame cost does not exceed 4 ms per cycle (under stress; 2× the H.36 nominal budget). **Synthetic injection is required because Q9 stationary attenuation + magnitude-floor culling reduce live-source count under any naturalistic 4-player coordinated-sprint scenario — the natural-play scenario cannot reach 200 sources in one cell. The test harness's synthetic-injection seam is server-internal (NOT exposed via `DisturbanceService.Client`); H.39c verifies the seam is not Client-callable.** If H.38b fails, the implementation MUST add per-cell source-count limits or lazy hot-cell sub-indexing — either fix is part of the DisturbanceService ADR. **If a per-cell-limit fix is introduced to pass H.38b, H.38 (distributed case) MUST be re-run as a regression** to confirm the fix did not degrade the distributed path's budget.

**Fixture extension (round-23 B3/PF-2 — the 500-sources × 3-beacons worst case, previously exercised by NO fixture):** the H.38b fixture ADDITIONALLY includes THREE active Beacon emissions (the production ceiling per H.36's `activeBeacons ≤ 3` scope clause) with the stationary-squad cap engaged on at least one (4 mock players stationary within 16 studs of B1, constructed via `_setRingBufferState`), so the C.3.6 step-6 `non_beacon_contribution_at_B` summation — which scans the FULL live-source list, NOT the spatial grid — is measured at its H.36a workload-6 ceiling (3 × 500 = 1,500 contribution evaluations per pass) INSIDE the same 4 ms stress ceiling, alongside the clustered D.6 scan. Desk-model: ~1,500 evals × ~12 float ops ≈ 18k ops/pass ≈ ~0.7 ms at conservative interpreted-Luau ARM throughput — roughly half the H.36 derated budget from this workload alone, which is exactly why it must be measured, not assumed (the round-19 "fits comfortably" disposition rested on a 621-eval model that the round-22 fixture split superseded). **If the combined cost exceeds the ceiling, the DisturbanceService ADR MUST route the step-6 summation through the C.1.6 spatial grid** (making its cost local-density-bounded rather than global-N-bounded) — the same class of fix this AC already names for the D.6 scan.

### Player Fantasy Verification (Section B)

**H.PB1** — MANUAL-PLAYTEST — **DIRECTIONAL SIGNAL ONLY (round-5 / Tier-2 #15 — full CD package adopted)**: this AC produces a directional signal at vertical-slice playtest scale, NOT a pass/fail authority. The pass/fail authority for Section B's traceability commitment is **H.PB1-BETA** (round-5 addition; see below) which runs during Beta playtest milestone with `N ≥ 30` participants. Reasoning: at vertical-slice scale (8–12 participants), the criteria below produce confidence intervals of ±27–32 percentage points — too wide for a load-bearing pass/fail gate. H.PB1 is run during vertical-slice as an early-warning signal; a failure on (i)–(iii) below routes to game-designer review and (if pattern is severe) to creative-director, but does NOT block sprint progression.

GIVEN a 5-minute 4-player playtest session on a vertical-slice map with mixed gather/sprint/lantern actions AND each participant is briefed only on the basic controls (no explanation of the disturbance system) AND each participant completes the 6-question post-session survey below; AND server-side logging records every stationary-sprint event (a sprint-state-active interval ≥ 6 s during which the player's XZ-position remained within `EMITTER_MOVEMENT_DELTA_FLOOR` of its starting sample) per playtest session so question-4 responses can be cross-referenced against actual occurrences; AND the session sample size is at least 8 participants (two 4-player sessions or equivalent), WHEN sessions end, the following directional signals are computed (each with a minimum-affected-participant floor; see below):

**Question 1.** "Did you notice the world brighten or dim during play?" (Yes / No)

**Question 2.** "If yes to Q1: can you describe what action you or a teammate did just before that change?" (free-form)

**Question 3.** "If no to Q1: did the world look different in different areas? If yes again, what did you think was causing the difference?" (free-form)

**Question 4.** *(verifies Section B 4th-bullet (a) stationary-sprint Hunt-reach watch-item; round-22 B12 — responses MUST be recorded with the respondent's input method).* "During the session, did you ever experience a moment where the world went into a high-brightness ('Hunt-tier', escalating to the top 'Retreat' state within ~28 s) while you or a teammate was sprinting **without moving** (stuck against terrain, AFK with the key held, or — on touch — the sprint toggle left on)? If yes: did the visible brightness feel like a fair consequence of 'you stood still while sprinting,' or did it feel surprising / unfair?" (Free-form, then 5-point scale: 1 = very unfair → 5 = very fair / felt earned). **The survey instrument MUST capture each respondent's primary input method for the session (keyboard/mouse, gamepad, touch) alongside the Q4 response** — per PC C.1.3 sprint is a tap-toggle on touch, so the "holding is a choice" framing this question tests differs by platform, and pooled data would mask a touch-specific fairness failure.

**Question 5.** *(round-5 redesign — CD package; de-primes blame-attribution by removing cause-naming wording).* "At some point during the session the world may have gone into a high-brightness state. What was your read on what caused it?" (Multiple choice — single select):
- **(a)** "Our squad caused this together — multiple actions stacking." (FELLOWSHIP)
- **(b)** "A specific teammate's action set it off." (SPECIFIC-TEAMMATE — note: this is NOT inherently BLAME; the intent attribution is captured in the free-form follow-up below)
- **(c)** "Mixed — partly the squad collectively, partly something specific." (MIXED)
- **(d)** "Don't know / didn't notice the cause." (UNKNOWN)
- **(e)** "I didn't experience a high-brightness state during this session." (NOT-EXPERIENCED)

**Question 5 follow-up** *(free-form, only shown if Q5 answer is (b))*: "When you noticed the specific teammate's action set off the high-brightness state, did that read as 'we're in this together' (positive squad-support), 'this teammate made things harder for me' (negative blame-attribution), or 'neutral information about who did what'?" (Three-option select: POSITIVE-SUPPORT / NEGATIVE-BLAME / NEUTRAL — plus a free-form box for the participant to elaborate)

**Question 6.** "Anything else about the world's response to your actions that surprised you, confused you, or felt unfair?" (free-form retention question — kept for traceability to ad-hoc concerns)

**Directional signals** (computed from response data; each gated by a minimum-affected-participant floor):

- (i) **Q1 + Q2/Q3 traceability signal**: among participants with Q1 = Yes (the affected sub-population), compute the share who identify a specific squad action as the cause in Q2. **Minimum floor: ≥ 3 participants with Q1 = Yes.** Below floor → INCONCLUSIVE for this signal (run additional sessions or accept that the population was too quiet for the signal to be measurable). Above floor: target ≥ 80% identify-cause; falling below 60% is a stronger directional concern.
- (ii) **Q4 stationary-sprint fairness signal (Section B 4th-bullet (a))**: among participants who experienced one or more stationary-sprint Hunt-reach events (verified by server-side logs), compute the average Q4 5-point fairness score. **Minimum floor: ≥ 3 participants with at least one logged event.** Below floor → INCONCLUSIVE; either re-run the test on a friction-prone first-zone level design (deliberately recreating the watch-item scenario), or accept that the level design successfully avoided the pattern in this session. Above floor: target ≥ 70% score 4 or 5; falling below 50% is a stronger directional concern. **Round-22 B12 — report this signal SPLIT by input method (touch vs hold-input) in addition to pooled**: a touch-only failure MUST NOT be masked by pooled PC/gamepad data (per PC C.1.3 sprint is a toggle on touch; the fairness rationale differs by platform). The per-platform sub-floors may individually read INCONCLUSIVE at vertical-slice scale — the split is still mandatory so the Beta instrument (H.PB1-BETA) inherits it with real statistical power.
- (iii) **Q5 + Q5-followup positive squad-support signal (Section B 4th-bullet (b))** — *replaces the round-4 BLAME ≤ 50% criterion with a positive-fellowship criterion per CD package*: among participants whose Q5 response is (a) FELLOWSHIP, (b) SPECIFIC-TEAMMATE, (c) MIXED, or (d) UNKNOWN (i.e., excluding (e) NOT-EXPERIENCED), compute the share who report **squad-support** experience: any of `Q5 == (a) FELLOWSHIP`, `Q5 == (c) MIXED with positive-affect free-form`, or `Q5 == (b) SPECIFIC-TEAMMATE with Q5-followup == POSITIVE-SUPPORT`. **Minimum floor: ≥ 3 participants whose response is in the affected set (excludes (e)).** Below floor → INCONCLUSIVE; the squad max-aggregation propagation pattern was not observed during sessions, so the fellowship-vs-blame dynamic could not be measured. Above floor: target ≥ 60% report squad-support (positive criterion); falling below 40% is a stronger directional concern.

**This AC verifies Section B's load-bearing fantasy claim AND its two playtest-watched fantasy edges as a directional signal.** Failure of (i) routes to game-designer review (the traceability promise is not landing — investigate per-emission visual-cue legibility). Failure of (ii) routes to level-design (revise first-zone friction terrain) AND/OR Player Controller GDD (revisit `EMITTER_MOVEMENT_DELTA_FLOOR` / `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR`). Failure of (iii) routes to HUD GDD (consider per-player contribution display in the squad meter UI per Section B 4th-bullet (b) mitigation path). Below-floor INCONCLUSIVE on any signal triggers a redesigned scenario (e.g., a vertical slice deliberately constructed to surface the relevant watch-item) rather than a pass/fail call.

**H.PB1-BETA** — MANUAL-PLAYTEST (Beta-milestone confirmation gate; round-5 addition / Tier-2 #15) — GIVEN the same 6-question survey instrument as H.PB1 above AND a Beta playtest milestone with at least **N = 30** participants across at least 8 distinct sessions (mix of 2-, 3-, and 4-player squads to surface single-player and partial-squad patterns) AND server-side logging configured per H.PB1, WHEN sessions end, **THEN all of the following must hold** for the H.PB1-BETA verdict to be PASS:
- (i) **Q1 + Q2/Q3 traceability:** ≥ 80% of participants who report observing brightening (Q1 = Yes) identify a specific squad action as the cause in Q2; AND ≤ 5% of all Q3 free-form responses contain "the world brightened but I have no idea why" or equivalent semantic.
- (ii) **Q4 stationary-sprint fairness:** EITHER zero stationary-sprint Hunt-reach events occurred across all sessions (verified against server-side logs), OR among participants who experienced one or more such events: ≥ 70% rate the experience 4 or 5 on the Q4 5-point scale — **evaluated BOTH pooled AND split by input method (round-22 B12): the ≥ 70% bar MUST hold independently for the touch sub-population (if its affected count ≥ 5) and for the hold-input sub-population; a pooled pass with a touch-specific fail is a criterion FAIL. **Sub-floor rule (round-23 QA-3):** if the touch-affected count is 1–4 (nonzero but below the ≥ 5 floor), record the raw touch-specific rate for transparency but do NOT apply it as an independent FAIL condition — the pooled result governs for that Beta cycle, and the qa-lead flags the sub-floor population size for the next Beta iteration.** **Frequency-shift re-validation note (round-22 I14):** this criterion's 70% bar and participant floors were calibrated when the trigger required ~160 s of sustained stationary sprint (a rare event); the 2026-06-19 ruling makes it reachable in ~28 s, so the event's Beta base rate will be materially higher — the qa-lead MUST re-examine at Beta whether the bar/floors remain appropriate for a now-frequent event (a frequent unfair-feeling event is worse than a rare one at the same percentage) and record the re-validation in the H.PB1-BETA evidence file.
- (iii) **Q5 + Q5-followup positive squad-support:** ≥ 60% of participants in the affected set (Q5 ∈ {a, b, c, d}, excluding (e) NOT-EXPERIENCED) report squad-support experience per the H.PB1 (iii) computation rule.
- (iv) **Session-arc closure against permanent-Calm attractor (round-9 B-NS7 closure / round-7c R7c-I1 elevation; round-11 IMPORTANT 3-spec convergent — pre-segment activity rubric added to address Hawthorne effect).** D.7's worked example shows a fully Q9-attenuated 4-player stationary squad settles at `fieldValue ≈ 0.272` (Lantern stationary steady-state, just below `TENSE_THRESHOLD = 0.30`) AND Predator AI's `RETREAT_DROPOUT_THRESHOLD = 0.30` exactly matches the Tense floor. A squad that achieves zero-emission play (everyone stationary, no gathers, no sprint, lantern raised but stationary-attenuated) admits a permanent-Calm attractor where the predator's Disengage timer never trips because `squadT` hovers at 0.272 (below the 0.30 threshold) AND Hunt-floor never reaches because the cap mechanic doesn't apply (no beacon active to engage cap). The 5-min minimum session arc commitment from the concept doc is unenforced against this play pattern. **AC criterion:** at the Beta playtest milestone, at least 4 of the 8 distinct sessions MUST include an extended-stationary-squad observation segment (≥ 60 s of all 4 players stationary, no emissions, no beacon active — facilitator-directed if necessary, parallel to H.41e's facilitator-directed segment design). Server-side logging records the squad's `fieldValue(squadT)` and `predator.state` across the segment.

**Server-side FAIL telemetry contract (round-15 R14-B9 closure of round-13 R13-I1 elevation; CD-elevated to BLOCKING per the severity-floor rule for server-authoritative primary write seams).** In addition to the PASS paths below, the criterion has an **objective server-side FAIL condition** that fires unconditionally regardless of subjective participant response.
