The FAIL condition closes the round-13 R13-I1 surfaced gap that subjective-feel can be falsely-positive when participants rate a session "fine" even though the field collapsed; the server-side FAIL is the objective floor that prevents the criterion from passing on subjective optimism alone.

- **(FAIL-iv-server) Permanent-Calm collapse detection.** GIVEN the server-side log captures `fieldValue(squadT)` and `predator.state` at every field-update pass during the extended-stationary segment, THEN: in ANY of the 4-of-8 affected sessions, if `fieldValue(squadT) < SQUAD_T_FAIL_FLOOR_THRESHOLD` (the threshold value is deferred per round-15 — see threshold-deferral note below) AND `predator.state == "Patrol"` BOTH SUSTAINED for the FULL 60-second extended-stationary segment without interruption, the criterion FAILS UNCONDITIONALLY for that session AND the H.PB1-BETA verdict for criterion (iv) is FAIL regardless of how many of the 4 affected sessions reached the (a) / (b) / (c) PASS paths AND regardless of any participant subjective debrief responses. The FAIL is unconditional because a sustained-permanent-Calm-collapse with the predator in Patrol throughout demonstrates the design's stationary-arc claim has empirically failed at the server-state level — the field genuinely collapsed below Tense AND the predator genuinely lost interest, both for the entire 60-second observation. No subjective-feel rating can recover an objectively-collapsed session.

- **Telemetry contract (binding).** The server-side log MUST record the following fields per field-update pass during each affected session's extended-stationary segment, into the dedicated evidence file `production/qa/evidence/HPB1-BETA-iv-server-telemetry-[session-id].csv` (or equivalent CSV-shaped artifact; the exact extension is implementation-flexible but the field set is binding):
  - `pass_index: int` — the field-update pass counter from segment-start (0 at segment-start; increments by 1 per pass).
  - `pass_timestamp_seconds: number` — `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()` at the pass, in seconds (relative to segment-start).
  - `squad_t: number` — the squad-aggregate `fieldValue(squadT)` value at the pass, in `[0.0, 1.0]`.
  - `predator_state: string` — the predator's state-machine state at the pass, in `{"Patrol", "Investigate", "Hunt", "Disengage"}`. **Read-source contract (round-15 red-team I-RT-3 closure):** DisturbanceService reads the predator's state from a service-internal cached field `self._cachedPredatorState: PredatorState` (default `"Patrol"` at server startup) that is updated synchronously inside the existing `OnPredatorStateChanged:Connect` callback (registered via the round-15 R14-B2 register-time-subscribe pattern in the handshake module's `RegisterPredatorStateChangedSignal` accessor body). The cached field is the F.1-preserving read source — DisturbanceService NEVER calls `Knit.GetService("PredatorAI"):GetState()` or any equivalent pull-style API; the push-via-BindableEvent path that already exists for the C.1.11 / E.23 purge is the same data surface this telemetry consumer reads. If the cached field is `nil` because Predator AI has not yet fired any `OnPredatorStateChanged` event (e.g., session-start before any state-machine transition), the telemetry row writes `"Patrol"` as the safe-default value (Predator AI's design contract is that the predator initialises in Patrol on every server-start; the safe default matches the design intent). The DisturbanceService ADR MUST author the `_cachedPredatorState` field initialisation, the OnPredatorStateChanged callback's update logic, AND the telemetry-writer's cached-read in the SAME implementation epic as the H.PB1-BETA criterion (iv) FAIL-iv-server contract — without all three, the FAIL contract is unverifiable in production.
  - `pre_segment_profile: string` — the activity rubric Profile assigned to the session ("A", "B", or "C" per the rubric below).
  - `session_id: string` — the unique session identifier.

  The DisturbanceService implementation MUST emit these telemetry rows during the extended-stationary segment (segment-start is signalled by a server-side flag the facilitator's harness can set via a server-internal accessor `DisturbanceService:_setExtendedStationarySegmentActive(active: boolean)` — H.39c grep-verifies the seam is server-internal-only AND the prereq table at the end of Section H records the new seam as a sprint-backlog item; the seam is required for the H.PB1-BETA test fixture to bracket the segment's telemetry capture without polluting the rest of the session's logs). The evidence file's CSV format MUST parse cleanly with standard CSV libraries (`HttpService:JSONEncode` per-row alternative is acceptable for the implementation if CSV is awkward in Luau; the qa-lead's analysis pipeline determines the format choice).

  **Seam invocation mechanism in Beta-playtest builds (round-20 I-QA18-2 closure of round-18 production-deploy IPC testability gap; round-20 red-team R20-RT-4 reframing — paragraph clarifies EXISTING enforcement posture, does NOT add new mechanism).** At the Beta playtest milestone, the `_setExtendedStationarySegmentActive(active)` seam is invoked by the facilitator's test-harness fixture from a Roblox Studio test-mode server-context script (NOT from a deployed client UI; NOT from a Knit RemoteSignal client surface — both are mechanically prohibited by the seam's server-internal placement per H.39c grep-verification, parallel to all other server-internal test seams `_setTestClock`, `_setExpFn`, `_injectSyntheticEmission`, `_runFieldUpdatePass`, `_setRingBufferState`, `_captureDeathLockSnapshot`, `_setLiveSourceState`, `_resetCapCueDecayPassesRemaining`). The enforcement posture of `_setExtendedStationarySegmentActive` matches that of all other test seams: H.39c's grep verifies non-Client placement (no client-callable surface); the seam's server-side reachability in production-deploy builds is governed by the same Roblox build pipeline that ships full server source for all services — Roblox does not natively support build-time-strip-out of named server-side functions, so the seam's body ships in production-deploy builds alongside all other test seams. This is an accepted enforcement-posture limitation common to all test seams across the codebase, NOT a new gap introduced by `_setExtendedStationarySegmentActive` specifically. The legitimate invocation paths are exactly two: (a) Studio command-bar invocations from a server-context script run by the facilitator (the harness MAY be packaged as a Studio plugin or as a local server-side script loaded by the Beta-playtest place-file), OR (b) the H.PB1-BETA AUTO-INTEGRATION fixture's setup / teardown helpers running under TestEZ in Studio local-server mode. **A future stronger-enforcement option** (not required for round-20 closure) would be runtime-guarding the seam body via `RunService:IsStudio()` or an equivalent build-flavour check; this would prevent invocation in production-deploy builds even from server-side exploit paths (e.g., a compromised server-side plugin). The DisturbanceService ADR's first revision MAY adopt the runtime-guard as part of the seam-implementation pattern across all seams; round-20 documents the option for ADR scope but does NOT mandate it at the AC level. The fixture's runtime (Studio plugin vs server-side harness module vs TestEZ runner) is implementation-flexible per the qa-lead's owned harness authoring; the binding contract is that no client surface reaches the seam in any build flavour AND the seam is grep-verified server-internal in every CI run via H.39c. The Test Infrastructure Prerequisites table at the end of Section H records the harness module as a sprint-backlog item; the implementation choice between Studio-plugin and server-side-harness-module is the qa-lead's call, deferred to first sprint-backlog story authoring.

- **Threshold starting-point note (round-17 I16-1 origin; REWRITTEN round-22 B5 — the round-17 text at this site survived the round-20 Session B narrowing and contradicted G.5, re-opening the exact inert-criterion defect B-CD18-1 closed for any reader calibrating from this AC alone).** The threshold `SQUAD_T_FAIL_FLOOR_THRESHOLD = **0.28**` (interim recommended band 0.276–0.285) is published as the calibration starting point in **G.5 — the SOLE canonical source; this note is a cross-reference, not a second definition.** Its safe range is **`(0.272, 0.30)` exclusive** (round-20 Session B B-CD18-1 narrowing): the lower bound is **D.7's stationary-light steady-state asymptote (~0.272)** — a threshold ≤ 0.272 produces a permanently-inert criterion (iv) because a stationary lantern-raised squad stabilises at ≈0.272 indefinitely, above the FAIL threshold (B-R19-SD-2: the earlier "`MAGNITUDE_FLOOR` plus headroom / floor noise" rationale that previously appeared at this site was conceptually wrong — the binding lower constraint is the D.7 asymptote, not floor noise); the upper bound is strictly below `TENSE_THRESHOLD = 0.30` (a threshold ≥ Tense would FAIL healthy Tense-decay-tail sessions per (b)'s PASS path). The value is **playtest-calibrated within `(0.272, 0.30)`**, not design-mandated; the Beta-playtest milestone determines the final figure (F.2b registry row R17-I16-1 tracks the calibration return path). *Historical note: the round-17 starting point was 0.20 with a floor-noise rationale — both superseded by round-20 Session B; any implementation or fixture wired to 0.20 sits in the inert sub-band and MUST be re-pointed to G.5's current default.*

- **R13-I1 closure status:** R13-I1 is now CLOSED inline at this AC. The R13-I forward obligation tracking entry is updated to mark CLOSED (see F.2a-IMPORTANT round-13 cluster).

**Pre-segment activity rubric (round-11 IMPORTANT 3-spec convergent — game-designer F4 + systems-designer F4 + qa-lead F6 finding; addresses Hawthorne effect that facilitator-directed segments admit on PASS paths (a) and (b)).** Round-10 review identified that paths (a) "predator-Hunt residue" and (b) "squad-Tense decay-tail" depend on emission-decay residue from pre-segment squad activity — but the round-9 wording did NOT specify what activity must occur in the pre-segment window, leaving facilitator skill as the only variable controlling whether (a) or (b) is reachable. A facilitator who runs a hot pre-segment (~60 s of continuous sprint + lantern + gathers, building substantial decay residue) makes paths (a) and (b) trivially achievable; a facilitator who runs a quiet pre-segment (minimal emissions before the stationary observation begins) makes (a) and (b) unreachable in principle, collapsing the criterion onto path (c) participant-debrief alone — which is highly subject to the Hawthorne effect because participants are mid-facilitator-directed segment AND know they are being observed. To make criterion (iv) measurable independent of facilitator skill, each affected session's pre-segment window MUST execute a normalised activity rubric: **(rubric-1)** in the 60-second window IMMEDIATELY preceding the stationary-observation segment, the squad MUST execute one of three normalised activity profiles, selected by the facilitator from the rubric (NOT by the participants — participants are not informed of the rubric until debrief), and recorded in the session's metadata: **Profile A — high-activity pre-segment:** all 4 squad members sprint continuously AND raise lanterns continuously AND complete at least 3 gather actions across the squad in the 60 s window. Yields decay residue spanning roughly 2-half-life decay (~60 s post-emission for `STANDARD_HALF_LIFE = 30 s`); paths (a) and (b) are reachable if the design contracts hold. **Profile B — moderate-activity pre-segment:** all 4 squad members run (not sprint) AND lanterns are raised AND 1 gather action across the squad in the 60 s window. Yields modest decay residue; paths (a) and (b) are at-risk-but-not-unreachable. **Profile C — minimal-activity pre-segment:** squad walks slowly, lanterns lowered, no gathers in the 60 s window. Yields near-zero decay residue; paths (a) and (b) are unreachable in principle and the criterion falls back to path (c) only. **(rubric-2)** Across the 4-of-8 affected sessions, the facilitator MUST run AT LEAST 2 sessions on Profile A AND AT LEAST 1 session on Profile C; the remaining 1 session is facilitator's-choice (Profile B recommended for variety). The Profile-C sessions are the load-bearing ones for the rubric — they isolate path (c)'s standalone reachability and surface whether the design's stationary-arc claim holds even without pre-segment decay residue. **(rubric-3)** The session-debrief question for path (c) MUST be administered BEFORE the facilitator reveals which Profile the session ran (counters the Hawthorne effect: participants do not know whether their session was the "no-residue control" or a "high-residue prepared" condition until after they answer the debrief). PASS criterion (iv) is now: across the 4-of-8 affected sessions, EITHER (a)/(b) holds in at least the 2 Profile A sessions AND ≥ 1 Profile B session, OR (c) holds in at least one Profile C session AND at least 60% of all participants across all 4-of-8 affected sessions rate the experience 4 or 5 on the 5-point scale. The path (c) standalone-Profile-C requirement is the Hawthorne-counter: if path (c) reads ≥ 60% under Profile C blind-debrief, the design's stationary-arc claim is verified; if it reads below 60% under Profile C, the criterion fails REGARDLESS of (a)/(b) reaching threshold under Profile A — because the failure indicates the design is depending on pre-segment residue rather than holding the stationary arc on its own merits. **PASS criteria (round-11 reformulated):** in the affected segments, EITHER (a) the predator's `state == Hunt` for at least 30 cumulative seconds within the 60-second segment (the predator continues to investigate from prior emissions' decay tail, Hunt-tier is reachable) AND held under Profile A or B, OR (b) the squad's `fieldValue(squadT)` reaches Tense (≥ 0.30) for at least 10 cumulative seconds via emission-decay residue from pre-segment activity (the squad's pre-stationary actions left a long-tail decay that keeps the field above Tense across the segment) AND held under Profile A or B, OR (c) ≥ 60% of participants across all 4-of-8 affected sessions (NOT 70% within affected segments — round-11 lowered the bar because Profile C makes the per-Profile-C-session sample size small and the across-Profile pooling more robust) rate the experience "I felt the threat was still present even though we were standing still" (4 or 5 on a 5-point scale) on a per-segment debrief question, AND under Profile C specifically the path (c) read is ≥ 50% (Hawthorne-counter floor — Profile C's path (c) read MUST be at least 50% even though the overall pool's threshold is 60%, ensuring the design holds when residue is removed). Any single PASS path satisfies (iv); a session that fails ALL THREE fails the criterion. **Failure routes:** failure of (iv) routes to game-designer review (revisit Q9 stationary-attenuation factor — raise `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR` from 0.30 toward 0.50; OR revisit `RETREAT_DROPOUT_THRESHOLD` ownership in Predator AI to ensure it sits comfortably above the stationary steady-state). Without this criterion + the round-11 pre-segment activity rubric, a working game can ship with the permanent-Calm attractor unenforced AND a Hawthorne-effect-induced false-PASS hiding the permanent-Calm risk, breaking the 5-min minimum session arc silently on zero-emission play.

**H.PB1-BETA is the load-bearing pass/fail gate for Section B's fantasy commitment AND the round-9-added round-7c R7c-I1 session-arc commitment.** At N ≥ 30 the confidence intervals on each criterion are ≤ ±18 percentage points (vs ±27–32 at vertical slice), tight enough to support a binding ruling. Failure of any of the four criteria blocks Release. Failure routes through creative-director for design adjustment per the H.PB1 routing rules above.

**H.PB2** — MANUAL-PLAYTEST (Q11 teaching) — GIVEN a 30-minute first-time-player session with a Calm-tier first zone authored per the Q11 teaching beat (Player Controller GDD), WHEN the player encounters their first predator-Hunt event after the teaching zone, THEN ≥ 70% of first-time players, on debrief, correctly identify "stop sprinting" as a survival response (not "sprint faster" or "run away"). Verifies Q11 ruling delivers in-game teaching.

### Security / Anti-Exploit Regression

**H.39** — STATIC-LINT (round-23 QA-5 retag from AUTO-UNIT — pure source-scan) (no-client-Emit lint, direct; round-9 B-NS3 grep-pattern expansion) — GIVEN the `DisturbanceService` source code, WHEN a grep-level code search runs for ALL of the following declaration forms — **(i)** `function DisturbanceService.Client:` (Lua method-call sugar form: `function Service.Client:Name(args)`); **(ii)** `function DisturbanceService.Client\.` (Lua dot-form function declaration: `function Service.Client.Name(self, args)`); **(iii)** `DisturbanceService\.Client\.[%w_]+\s*=\s*function` (assignment form: `Service.Client.Name = function(...)` — round-9 B-NS3 closure for the round-8 finding that the round-7c grep missed assignment-form RemoteFunction declarations); **(iv)** `DisturbanceService\.Client\[["'][%w_]+["']\]\s*=\s*function` (table-index form: `Service.Client["Name"] = function(...)` — pathological but valid Luau) — AND for `:Emit(` invocations within any code body matching any of forms (i)–(iv), THEN: (a) the only methods declared on `DisturbanceService.Client` via any of forms (i)–(iv) are RemoteSignals declared via `Knit.CreateSignal()` — exactly the three named in F.4 (`OnMeterUpdate`, `OnDisturbanceAlert`, `OnDeathAttributionPushed`) — no more, no fewer; and (b) zero direct `:Emit(` calls appear within any client-method body matching any of forms (i)–(iv). Verifies C.1.8 no-client-Emit rule for direct call paths across all syntactic forms.

**H.39d** — MANUAL-REVIEW (no-client-Emit, transitive call-graph; round-3 split from H.39; round-5 evidence-path enforcement / Tier-2 #20) — GIVEN the `DisturbanceService` source code, WHEN two engineers independently inspect the call graph reachable from each `DisturbanceService.Client` RemoteSignal handler (or any client-callable code path that handlers transitively invoke), THEN no reachable code path leads to `self:Emit(...)` (or any equivalent live-source-list insertion) without first passing through a named server-side authority validator (e.g., `ResourceNodeService:ValidateGather`, `PlayerControllerService:ValidateSprintTransition`). Both reviewers' sign-offs MUST be recorded in the PR. This AC is MANUAL-REVIEW because the judgment it requires is SEMANTIC — whether a reachable path's validator is genuinely a server-side authority validator — not merely syntactic reachability (round-23 QA-6 re-grounds the original "grep cannot traverse call graphs" rationale, which the project's later AST-tooling investment for H.39e/H.39f superseded as a blanket claim). H.39 catches the direct case mechanically; H.39d catches indirect chains AND judges validator legitimacy. Once the R15-I1/R15-I2 AST tooling lands, the unambiguous zero-intervening-call subset MAY be auto-flagged as a pre-filter; the two-engineer semantic review remains the load-bearing check.

**Evidence artifact (round-5 enforcement; round-7a R7-B16 — CI grep gate replaces honor-system PR-template-checklist).** Each H.39d review MUST produce a named evidence file at `production/qa/evidence/H39d-[sprint-id]-call-graph-review.md` containing: (a) the date of the review, (b) the GitHub usernames of **both** reviewers (no shared accounts; two distinct human reviewers), (c) a one-paragraph summary of the call-graph traversal each reviewer performed, (d) explicit attestation by each reviewer that no client-handler-reachable code path leads to `self:Emit(...)` without a named server-side authority validator, (e) any flagged paths the reviewers identified for follow-up (or "none" if the path is clean).

**PR enforcement (round-7a R7-B16 — qa-lead F5 honor-system fix).** The round-5 wording said "the evidence file cannot be silently omitted because the PR template surfaces it" — round-7 qa-lead F5 identified this as honor-system: PR-template checklists are advisory, can be ticked-without-doing, and "cannot be silently omitted" was a false claim. The round-7a fix introduces a **CI gate** that mechanically enforces the evidence file:

> **Enforcement (relocated round-22 AC-extraction; relocation only):** the normative CI-gate YAML (`h39d-evidence-gate.yml`), the H.39d evidence-file template, and the required-check clause are in **`ecological-disturbance-verification.md` § H.39d evidence gate**. The binding rules stand unchanged: the CI gate MUST be a required check on PRs touching `src/server/services/DisturbanceService/**/*.luau` whose changed files touch the `Service.Client` surface (round-23 B7/QA-1 correction — the prior `Client/`-subdirectory-only trigger could structurally never fire under the standard single-file Knit layout, silently disabling the P0 client-Emit review gate; the YAML now uses a recursive filter + content-grep narrowing step, the H.30c-i model), AND the DevOps Engineer MUST land it before any DisturbanceService.Client method body is introduced or modified — the implementation epic is BLOCKED on the gate's presence (round-7a binding ruling).

**H.39b** — STATIC-LINT (round-23 QA-5 retag) (no client RemoteFunctions on DisturbanceService; round-9 B-NS3 grep-pattern expansion) — GIVEN the `DisturbanceService` source code, WHEN inspected via the SAME four-form grep-pattern set as H.39 (forms (i)–(iv) — method-sugar, dot-function, assignment-form, table-index-form), THEN `DisturbanceService.Client` contains zero RemoteFunctions across all four forms (the round-7c wording of "zero methods declared as `function DisturbanceService.Client:MethodName(...)`" caught only form (i); the round-9 expansion to forms (i)–(iv) closes the assignment-form / table-index-form escape hatch identified by round-8 B-NS3) AND the only declared signals are exactly `OnMeterUpdate`, `OnDisturbanceAlert`, `OnDeathAttributionPushed` (presence-positive assertion: no more, no fewer). Per F.4 round-2 design: this system exposes only RemoteSignals (server→client push) — never client→server RemoteFunctions. Catches accidental reintroduction of `GetCurrentMeterValue` or `GetChunkFieldSnapshot` patterns AND silent removal of a required signal AND the assignment-form regression where a programmer writes `DisturbanceService.Client.GetSomething = function(self, player, ...)` instead of `function DisturbanceService.Client:GetSomething(player, ...)` (functionally equivalent in Luau; the grep MUST catch both).

**H.39c** — STATIC-LINT (round-23 QA-5 retag) (test seam + server-internal accessor non-Client enforcement; round-9 expanded for new Cluster 1 + Cluster 2 seams; round-13 R12-B2 + R12-B3 expansion folds in `_resetCapCueDecayPassesRemaining`, `_setLiveSourceState`, `RegisterPredatorLockChangedSignal`, `RegisterPredatorStateChangedSignal`; round-15 R14-B4 architectural change relocates the two `Register*Signal` registration accessors from DisturbanceService's method table into a separate module-private handshake module) — GIVEN the `DisturbanceService` source code AND the round-15-mandated handshake module source (path: `src/server/services/DisturbanceService/RegistrationHandshake.luau` per the round-15 R14-B4 architectural commitment; the path is binding, not advisory — H.39c's grep target depends on a fixed canonical path), WHEN inspected, THEN:

**(a) Test-seam non-Client placement.** ALL of the following test-only seams AND non-registration server-internal accessors are declared on the service's server-internal table (e.g., `function DisturbanceService:_setTestClock(...)`) and **not** on `DisturbanceService.Client`: `_setTestClock`, `_advanceTestClock`, `_setExpFn`, `_injectSyntheticEmission`, **`_runFieldUpdatePass`** (round-9 addition; H.41k sub-test (e), H.34b cascade verification, H.41i two-beacon order-independence), **`_setRingBufferState`** (round-9 addition; H.41k sub-tests (a)–(e), H.30c sub-AC (g) construction), **`_captureDeathLockSnapshot`** (round-9 addition; the production code path invokes this from the KnitInit-registered Humanoid.Died handler per C.1.11 step 5, but it is also test-callable as a server-internal helper for harness-driven snapshot construction in H.30c sub-ACs (e), (h), (i)), **`_setLiveSourceState`** (round-13 grep-sweep retrofit; round-11 prereq-table row added the seam without updating H.39c — round-13 closes the omission; the seam bypasses Q9 stationary attenuation and schema validation parallel to `_injectSyntheticEmission` and would expose a catastrophic exploit surface if Client-callable), **`_resetCapCueDecayPassesRemaining`** (round-13 R12-B2 closure; H.41m sub-AC (d) test-isolation precondition; the seam clears `self._capCueDecayPassesRemaining = {}` to ensure cold-start preconditions for cap-state discriminant Scenario 4; if Client-callable, a malicious client could clear other players' persistence state mid-session, breaking F.2a row 7 post-hoc attribution UI), AND **`_setExtendedStationarySegmentActive`** (round-15 R14-B9 + red-team B-RT-3 closure; the H.PB1-BETA criterion (iv) FAIL-iv-server telemetry contract requires a server-side flag that the facilitator's Beta-playtest harness can set via this seam to bracket the per-pass telemetry-row capture window; if Client-callable, a malicious client could spuriously activate the flag mid-session AND pollute the evidence-file logs with non-segment-window data, OR conversely deactivate the flag during a legitimate segment AND silently drop the FAIL-trigger detection). Test seams MUST NOT be exposed via Knit's client surface. Catches the regression where a test-only seam is mis-declared as Client-callable. (The `_injectSyntheticEmission` seam in particular bypasses Q9 stationary attenuation and schema validation; the `_setRingBufferState` seam bypasses the natural pulse-cadence buffer-population path; both expose catastrophic exploit surfaces if Client-callable. H.31c verifies the synthetic-emission seam's minimum-invariant enforcement; the round-9-added and round-13-added seams have no equivalent invariant-enforcement layer because they are construction primitives — H.39c's grep guarantee is the only defence.)

**(b) Registration accessors are module-private — NOT methods on DisturbanceService at all (round-15 R14-B4 architectural closure; round-15 red-team B-CS-2 cross-section closure — five canonical exports identical across F.2a row 3 + F.4 row + this H.39c (b)).** The two registration accessors `RegisterPredatorLockChangedSignal` and `RegisterPredatorStateChangedSignal` (round-13 R12-B3 introduction; round-15 relocation) MUST NOT be declared as methods on `DisturbanceService` (the service's server-internal table) AND MUST NOT be declared on `DisturbanceService.Client`. Instead, the two accessors are declared as **module-local functions** inside the handshake module file `src/server/services/DisturbanceService/RegistrationHandshake.luau`. The handshake module's `return` statement exports a table containing exactly five canonical entries (the two registration accessors, the two read-side observation hook accessors `getOnPredatorLockChangedSignal()` and `getOnPredatorStateChangedBindable()` for DisturbanceService's own internal use, AND the write-side handler-setter `setOnPredatorStateChangedHandler` per the round-15 R14-B2 register-time-subscribe pattern below) — five exports total, **NOTHING ELSE** — no leaked references, no helper functions, no debug surfaces. Predator AI imports the handshake module via `require(<absolute path to RegistrationHandshake.luau>)` during its own `KnitStart` and invokes the two registration accessors directly. DisturbanceService imports the same handshake module during its `KnitStart` and reads from it via the two `get*Signal` accessors AND registers the `:Connect` to the `OnPredatorStateChanged` BindableEvent inside the registration accessor's body itself (round-15 R14-B2 register-time-subscribe pattern — closes the KnitStart ordering race; see F.2a row 3 round-15 update). H.39c's grep verifies all of the following:

- (b-i) The string `RegisterPredatorLockChangedSignal` appears NOWHERE in any `function DisturbanceService:...(` declaration AND NOWHERE in any `function DisturbanceService.Client:...(` declaration AND NOWHERE in any `DisturbanceService.Client.<name> = function(...)` assignment-form declaration AND NOWHERE in any `DisturbanceService.<name> = function(...)` assignment-form declaration. Symmetric grep for `RegisterPredatorStateChangedSignal`.
- (b-ii) The two accessor names appear ONLY in the handshake module `src/server/services/DisturbanceService/RegistrationHandshake.luau`, declared as module-local functions: `local function RegisterPredatorLockChangedSignal(signal: RemoteSignal): ()` and `local function RegisterPredatorStateChangedSignal(bindable: BindableEvent): ()` (the function-name-local-form is binding; declarations as keyed table entries `local module = {RegisterPredatorLockChangedSignal = function(...) ... end}` are equivalent ACCEPT-form).
- (b-iii) The handshake module's `return` statement exports EXACTLY five canonical names (`RegisterPredatorLockChangedSignal`, `RegisterPredatorStateChangedSignal`, `setOnPredatorStateChangedHandler`, `getOnPredatorLockChangedSignal`, AND `getOnPredatorStateChangedBindable`) — no more, no fewer. Round-15 R14-B4 + red-team B-RT-1 + B-RT-2 closure: this list is identically specified at F.2a row 3 step 2, at F.4 row "Module-private cross-service push-registration accessors", and here at H.39c (b-iii) — round-15 closed the export-COUNT drift (4→5) AND introduced the canonical name `getOnPredatorStateChangedBindable` at F.2a row 3 step 2, but the round-15 patch did NOT eliminate the prior round-15-first-pass form `getOnPredatorStateChangedSignal` from the F.4 row + this AC body, leaving a residual cross-section name fanout drift surfaced by the round-16 verdict. **Round-17 B16-2 closure of round-16 partial-close (red-team I-RT17-3 closure of self-contradiction in the round-17-first-pass closure annotation)** — the F.4 row name drift is now corrected (the prior-form `getOnPredatorStateChangedSignal` is removed from F.4's canonical surface entirely; only this historical narrative AND the F.4 row's own brief one-clause acknowledgement of the round-17 rename retain the prior form, both clearly-annotated). The canonical 5-export list above is the only authoritative surface; `getOnPredatorStateChangedBindable` is the precisely-typed name given the return type is `BindableEvent?`; `setOnPredatorStateChangedHandler` is the explicit 5th export. Adding a sixth export is a regression that reviewers MUST reject (the handshake module is a minimal-surface API by design; expanding it re-introduces the local-alias-injection class that B4 closure mechanically forecloses). Re-introducing the prior-form name on any canonical-surface section (F.2a row 3, F.4 row, or this AC body) is also a regression — the cross-section-consistency CI check (round-17 R17-I1 forward obligation; see round-17 IMPORTANT cluster below) per-canonical-name greps F.2a + F.4 + H.39c (b-iii) and rejects any drift. **Cross-reference (round-17 I-CS-R17-2 closure):** the F.4 row referenced for the round-17 B16-2 closure is the row "Module-private cross-service push-registration accessors" in the F.4 Hard Dependencies on Engine table — the cross-reference is row-name-anchored rather than line-number-anchored to remain stable as the document evolves.
- (b-iv) The handshake module's source contains zero `Knit.GetService("PredatorAI")` invocations AND zero references to the Knit `Service.Client` surface of any service — the handshake module is the boundary between DisturbanceService and PredatorAI; it is NOT a Knit service itself and it does NOT bridge through Knit's namespace. Direct `require()` is the only acquisition path; F.1's "no MVP-internal hard dependencies" is preserved (DisturbanceService's main service body never `Knit.GetService`s PredatorAI; PredatorAI's main service body never `Knit.GetService`s DisturbanceService — both `require()` the handshake module instead).
- (b-v) Predator AI's code body MUST acquire the handshake module's accessors via `local handshake = require(<path to RegistrationHandshake>)`'s standard `require` form. Local-aliasing the `handshake.RegisterX` accessor (e.g., `local registerLock = handshake.RegisterPredatorLockChangedSignal; ... registerLock(self.Client.OnPredatorLockChanged)`) is permitted (the alias resolves to the module-local function, NOT to a Service.Client method, so the local-alias-to-Client-method injection class is mechanically foreclosed at the architecture level — there is no Service.Client method for the accessor to be aliased back to). H.39c does NOT need to scan for local-alias-injection patterns on these accessors because the architectural change has removed the Service.Client surface entirely; round-15 R14-B4 closes the local-alias-injection threat structurally rather than via an AST-lint expansion.
- (b-vi) **DisturbanceService:KnitStart's handshake-import block follows the F.2a row 3 step 4 binding ordering rule (round-17 B16-4 closure of round-16 BLOCKING — intra-KnitStart ordering race; round-17 red-team B-RT17-1 + B-RT17-2 closure — local-variable-name independence + corrected failure-mode locus).** Locate the `function DisturbanceService:KnitStart(` body's lexical scope. Within that scope, find the three handshake-module call sites by their **method-name suffixes** (NOT by a specific local-variable prefix): `.getOnPredatorLockChangedSignal(`, `.getOnPredatorStateChangedBindable(`, AND `.setOnPredatorStateChangedHandler(`. The grep MUST verify that BOTH `.getOnPredatorLockChangedSignal(` AND `.getOnPredatorStateChangedBindable(` appear lexically EARLIER in the function body than `.setOnPredatorStateChangedHandler(`. The leading dot anchors the suffix to a member-access form on any local variable holding the handshake module reference; the implementer MAY name the local `handshake`, `rh`, `ds_handshake`, or any other identifier — the grep matches against the method-name suffix only, so the rule is independent of local-variable naming choices (round-17 B-RT17-1 closure of the round-17 red-team naming-dependency gap finding). The two getters may appear in either order relative to each other (their slot-writes are order-independent); only the handler-setter's last-place ordering is binding. **Failure mode if violated (round-17 B-RT17-2 closure of the round-17 red-team mechanical-incoherence finding):** if the handler-setter `.setOnPredatorStateChangedHandler(...)` is invoked before the State-channel getter `.getOnPredatorStateChangedBindable()` has been called, the round-15 R14-B2 register-time-subscribe queue-drain triggers synchronously inside the setter — each drained `:Connect` callback attempts to subscribe to the **handshake module's `_onPredatorStateChangedBindable` slot** (NOT DisturbanceService's `self._on*` field; the slot lives inside the handshake module per round-15 R14-B4 module-private architecture). If Predator AI's KnitStart has not yet run `RegisterPredatorStateChangedSignal(bindable)`, that handshake-module slot is nil AND the `:Connect` is queued in the handshake module's pending-registrations list per round-15 R14-B2; the queue is drained later when the slot is populated. The actual failure under the wrong-ordering case is NOT a nil-dereference at the bindable layer — it is that DisturbanceService's `self._onPredatorStateChangedBindable` (the service-internal cached field, populated by `.getOnPredatorStateChangedBindable()` at sub-step 4-step-b) is nil at the moment the queue-drained callback executes (because 4-step-b has not yet run), so any callback body that reads `self._onPredatorStateChangedBindable:<...>` for purge-related operations dereferences nil. Both orderings (getters-then-setter vs setter-then-getters) eventually populate the slots, but only the getters-then-setter ordering guarantees the slots are populated WHEN the queue-drained callbacks fire. The grep is the structural enforcement of F.2a row 3 step 4's binding rule. Implementation note: a static-analysis pass that recognises Luau lexical-precedence within a function body is sufficient (no full call-graph traversal required); the AST-tooling shared dependency from R15-I1 / R15-I2 / R11-I3 is the natural implementation site, but a simpler grep-based check that finds line numbers of the three method-suffix call sites and asserts `line(.setOnPredatorStateChangedHandler) > max(line(.getOnPredatorLockChangedSignal), line(.getOnPredatorStateChangedBindable))` within the KnitStart body is also acceptable for the round-17 closure (the grep-only formulation may produce false-positives if the function body is restructured into multiple statements per line, but the false-positive rate is bounded by single-line-per-statement coding style). **Multi-statement-line edge case (round-20 I-QA18-3 closure of round-18 false-positive note).** If a future programmer writes all three handshake calls on a single source line — e.g., `local lock = handshake.getOnPredatorLockChangedSignal(); local state = handshake.getOnPredatorStateChangedBindable(); handshake.setOnPredatorStateChangedHandler(handler)` — the grep's line-number comparison treats the three call sites as being on the SAME line, so the predicate `line(.setOnPredatorStateChangedHandler) > max(line(.getOnPredatorLockChangedSignal), line(.getOnPredatorStateChangedBindable))` returns `false` (equal line numbers do not satisfy strict `>`), producing a false-positive FAIL even though the runtime semantic ordering is preserved (Luau executes statements left-to-right within a line, so the setter still runs after both getters). The qa-lead's chosen implementation MUST either (a) tokenise within the line to compare offset-within-line column positions for the three method-suffix calls, OR (b) escalate to AST-tooling per R15-I1 / R15-I2 shared dependency. The grep-only line-number-based implementation is acceptable for round-17/round-20 closure ON THE ASSUMPTION that the codebase observes single-line-per-statement coding style; if a programmer violates this style at this specific call-site cluster, the false-positive is documented + fixable by re-formatting the call cluster across three lines, AND is NOT a structural escape hatch (the underlying runtime ordering invariant is preserved). **Nested-call form residual gap (round-20 red-team R20-RT-1 closure of I-QA18-3 patch's grep-completeness overclaim).** Both the grep-only line-number formulation AND the column-offset-tokenised formulation (a) above are defeated by a pathological-but-syntactically-valid nested-call form: `handshake.setOnPredatorStateChangedHandler(handshake.getOnPredatorLockChangedSignal() and handshake.getOnPredatorStateChangedBindable())`. Here the setter is the outer call site (its method-suffix token appears first in source-order, at lower column AND lower or equal line number), AND the getters are argument-position calls within the setter's argument list (their method-suffix tokens appear at higher column positions but the same OR earlier line numbers depending on line-wrapping); the predicate `position(setter) > max(position(getter1), position(getter2))` returns false regardless of whether `position()` reads line numbers OR column offsets — because the setter's source position IS lexically earliest. The nested-call form is the ONE structural escape hatch that grep-or-column-offset cannot detect: only AST-level analysis distinguishing call-site-position from argument-position handles it. **Resolution:** the nested-call form is acknowledged as a residual gap that ONLY AST-tooling resolves; the qa-lead's chosen implementation path (a) or (b) above is normatively-equivalent for round-20 closure purposes, **but path (a) tokenised-column-offset MUST add an AST-level secondary check OR the H.39c (b-vi) AC body MUST add an explicit prohibition on the nested-call form (the rationale: the discriminant tree's runtime ordering invariant is preserved by simple sequential-statement calls but is NOT preserved by nested-call construction because the getters' return-values would be discarded into the `and`-expression's boolean coercion path)**. Reviewers MUST reject any nested-call form construction during code review; the AC's grep gate is best-effort detection for the sequential-statement case. The qa-lead authors the chosen implementation; either path is normatively-equivalent for round-17/round-20 closure purposes for the sequential-statement case, AND the nested-call form gap is an explicit AC residual recorded as the round-20 red-team R20-RT-1 forward obligation for AST-tooling at R15-I1 / R15-I2.

**Why architectural relocation, not AST-lint expansion (round-15 R14-B4 design rationale).** Round-13 introduced the registration accessors as methods on DisturbanceService's server-internal table, defended by H.39c's grep that they are not on Service.Client. Round-14 surfaced (CD-elevated round-15 R14-B4) that the grep cannot catch the local-alias injection pattern: `local accessor = DisturbanceService.RegisterPredatorLockChangedSignal; ... DisturbanceService.Client.SomeNewMethod = function(self, player, payload) accessor(DisturbanceService, payload) end`. The pattern is grep-undetectable because the violating `DisturbanceService.Client.SomeNewMethod` declaration is syntactically-distinct from the accessor's declaration and the alias `accessor` is a local that resolves to a Service-internal function. AST-level alias-resolution lint COULD catch the pattern (R11-I5 forward obligation), but the chosen approach — relocate the accessors out of DisturbanceService entirely — eliminates the threat surface mechanically: a wrapper function that calls a module-private function declared in another file does not have the same exploit surface (the malicious-Client method cannot grant access to a function it could not have invoked directly anyway, because module-private functions are reachable only via `require`-chains that have no Client surface). The architectural change is the strongest closure available to the round-15 ceiling problem.

**Round-15 R14-B2 register-time-subscribe rule (closes the KnitStart inter-service ordering race).** Inside the handshake module's `RegisterPredatorStateChangedSignal(bindable: BindableEvent)` accessor body, the function MUST execute `bindable:Connect(<DisturbanceService's onPredatorStateChanged callback>)` (or equivalent — the callback registration is part of the accessor's effect, NOT a separate step in DisturbanceService's KnitStart). The callback handle is read from a module-local-private slot that DisturbanceService writes to during its own KnitStart's call into a setter accessor (e.g., `setOnPredatorStateChangedHandler(handler: (payload) -> ())`). The handshake module's slot-writer + register-time-subscribe pattern means: regardless of whether DisturbanceService's KnitStart or Predator AI's KnitStart runs first, the `:Connect` happens on the Predator AI side at the moment Predator AI invokes `RegisterPredatorStateChangedSignal(bindable)` — by which time DisturbanceService's KnitStart has either (a) already run AND set the handler slot, in which case the `:Connect` registers the live callback immediately, OR (b) not yet run, in which case the handshake module DEFERS the `:Connect` until DisturbanceService writes the handler slot (the deferral is implemented as a small queue inside the handshake module: pending registrations are stored if no handler is set; when DisturbanceService eventually writes the handler slot, the queue is drained and all queued `:Connect` calls execute). This pattern eliminates the round-9 KnitStart inter-service ordering risk class structurally — Knit's KnitStart-after-all-KnitInits guarantee combined with the handshake module's slot-write-or-queue pattern produces deterministic subscription regardless of inter-service KnitStart order. H.39c (b-iii) verifies the handshake module's surface includes the deferral queue as a module-local NOT exported (the queue is an implementation detail; only the five canonical exports are externally visible — the slot-writer `setOnPredatorStateChangedHandler` IS one of those five exports per the round-15 red-team B-CS-2 cross-section closure).

**(c) Handshake module path is binding (round-15 R14-B4 architectural commitment).** The handshake module's location at `src/server/services/DisturbanceService/RegistrationHandshake.luau` is binding for H.39c's grep target. Relocating the handshake module to any other path is a regression that reviewers MUST reject; if a future architectural reason genuinely requires relocation, the H.39c AC text MUST be updated in the SAME PR that relocates the module — silent relocation breaks the grep AND the BLOCKING-on-CI-gate-presence rule. The DisturbanceService ADR's source-tree layout MUST place the handshake module at this path; the path is part of the architectural commitment, not an implementation detail.

**H.39e** — STATIC-LINT (round-24 Gate-B2 F10 retag — the round-23 QA-5 batch missed this sixth pure source-scan; H.30c (h-static) itself calls it a "Static lint check") (round-11 B-R10-5 / DR-5 = Option A — `Humanoid.Died` subscription-monopoly + `OnPlayerDied`/`OnPredatorLockChanged` yield-primitive CI lint; closes the C.1.11 step 5 cross-reference orphan AND the B-R10-1b sub-step (4b) sync-callback prohibition) — GIVEN the entire server codebase under `src/server/**/*.luau`, WHEN a CI grep gate (running on every `pull_request` AND every `push: branches: [main]` per the round-9 B-NS4 trigger expansion model) executes the following three pattern matches:

- **Lint (a) — `Humanoid.Died` subscription monopoly.** Pattern: any of `Humanoid\.Died:Connect`, `humanoid\.Died:Connect`, or any `:Connect\(` invocation chained on a previously-bound `humanoid\.Died` reference (e.g., `local d = humanoid.Died; d:Connect(...)` — the lint MUST follow simple variable aliasing within a single function body via a static-analysis pass; if a future Luau migration introduces a `Died.Connect`-style declaration, the lint MUST be updated to match all syntactic forms). The set of all matches returned by the lint, regardless of declaration form, MUST contain exactly ONE entry, AND that entry MUST be lexically nested inside the body of `function DisturbanceService:KnitInit(`. Zero matches anywhere else in the codebase. Closes C.1.11 step 5 round-11 DR-4 = Option B subscription-monopoly rule structurally.

- **Lint (b) — `OnPlayerDied:Connect` callback yield-primitive ban + call-graph reachability (round-11 BLOCK-3 red-team grep-only formulation; round-15 R14-B1 call-graph-reachability extension).** Pattern: locate every `OnPlayerDied:Connect(` invocation in the codebase (any service that subscribes to DisturbanceService's `OnPlayerDied` BindableEvent — typically Predator AI, HUD, Player Controller). For each match, locate the callback function body's lexical scope (from the opening `function (...) ... end` after the `:Connect(` to its matching `end`). Within that scope, the lint MUST find ZERO occurrences of: `task\.wait`, `task\.spawn`, `task\.delay`, `coroutine\.yield`, `:InvokeServer`, `:InvokeClient`, `RunService\.Heartbeat:Wait`, `\.Stepped:Wait`, `\.RenderStepped:Wait`, or any other Roblox primitive that surrenders control to a Heartbeat boundary (the canonical forbidden-primitive list is maintained as a CI configuration constant; new yield primitives added by future Roblox releases MUST be added to the list).

**Round-15 R14-B1 call-graph-reachability extension (closes the lexical-scope-only gap surfaced by round-14 ai-programmer + qa-lead 2-spec convergent finding).** The yield-free contract on the `Humanoid.Died` handler chain spans sub-steps (5a)→(5b)→(5c) per C.1.11 step 5 — NOT just the `OnPlayerDied:Connect` callback bodies on subscribing services, and NOT just DisturbanceService's own `Humanoid.Died` callback body. Private helpers invoked from any of those callback bodies — for example DisturbanceService's `_captureDeathLockSnapshot(deadPlayer)` invoked from sub-step (5a), the payload-construction helper invoked at (5b), Predator AI's `_handleDeathSync(payload)` if it chooses the same yield-free invocation pattern, etc. — MUST themselves be yield-free across their entire call graphs reachable from the `Humanoid.Died` handler entry. A round-14-flagged regression class: a future programmer refactors `_captureDeathLockSnapshot` to introduce a `task.wait(0)` "for performance reasons" (e.g., to defer some non-critical bookkeeping), believing that `_captureDeathLockSnapshot` is "outside the callback" and therefore exempt from H.39e Lint (b). Lexical-scope-only grep does NOT catch this — `_captureDeathLockSnapshot`'s body is lexically separate from the `humanoid.Died:Connect` callback that invokes it. **The lint MUST therefore operate on the call graph reachable from each `:Connect`-registered callback entry**, NOT just the callback's immediate lexical body.

**Call-graph traversal rules (binding for the lint's static-analysis pass):**

1. **Roots.** The traversal starts at every `:Connect`-registered callback that subscribes to either DisturbanceService's `OnPlayerDied` BindableEvent OR DisturbanceService's `Humanoid.Died` chain (DisturbanceService's own callback inside `KnitInit`). Each root is a lexical function body.
2. **Edges.** For each function body F in the traversal frontier, the pass identifies every direct call site `<callee>(<args>)` or `<obj>:<method>(<args>)` lexically inside F's body. For each call site, the pass attempts to resolve the callee to a function definition: (a) module-local function declarations `local function foo(...)` and `function Service:foo(...)` are resolvable to their bodies; (b) calls to module-local helpers reached via local-aliasing (`local helper = self._captureDeathLockSnapshot; helper(deadPlayer)`) are resolved by the same static-analysis pass H.39e Lint (a) uses for `humanoid.Died`; (c) calls into non-DisturbanceService modules (e.g., a `require("ReplicatedStorage.Shared.Util").doThing()`) are followed iff the called function is in the codebase under `src/**/*.luau`; calls to library/framework code (Roblox built-in `Instance.new`, `task.*`, `coroutine.*`, `:Fire`, `:Connect` itself, `string.format`, `table.insert`, `math.*`, `os.*`) are NOT followed — those are the LEAVES of the traversal AND are the primitives the lint actually checks for. **(d) Table-dispatch call sites (round-15 red-team I-RT-1 closure).** Calls where the callee is a table-index expression — `t[k]()` with dynamic `k`, OR `t.k()` where `t` is itself a parameter or local whose value is set non-constantly — are flagged for HUMAN-REVIEW as ambiguous edges. The traversal records the call site location AND the table reference's source (parameter origin, local-binding origin, etc.) AND emits a non-blocking warning in the lint's output requiring reviewer annotation in the PR description. Pure-static cases (`t = {foo = function() ... end}; t.foo()` where `t`'s definition is lexically obvious in the same function) MAY be resolved automatically if the AST tooling supports it, but the conservative default is to flag and require reviewer sign-off — silent traversal-skip would mis-fire as a clean PASS on a code path with a hidden yield primitive. The reviewer's annotation MUST attest the called function (or any function the table-dispatch could resolve to at runtime) is yield-free if it is reachable from a `Humanoid.Died` handler root. **Calls to library/framework yield primitives are the violations the lint detects;** non-yield-primitive library/framework calls (e.g., `Instance.new`, `string.format`, `table.insert`, `math.exp`, `:Fire`, `:Connect`) are leaves but not violations.
3. **Reachable set.** The traversal builds the closure of all function bodies reachable from any root via the resolved-call edges. The reachable set may include private helpers, public methods called from private helpers, and any chain of intra-codebase function calls.
4. **Forbidden-primitive check.** For every function body F in the reachable set, the pass checks F's lexical body for occurrences of the forbidden-primitive list (`task.wait`, `task.spawn`, `task.delay`, `coroutine.yield`, `:InvokeServer`, `:InvokeClient`, `RunService.Heartbeat:Wait`, `.Stepped:Wait`, `.RenderStepped:Wait`, etc.). Any match is a violation.
5. **Cycle handling.** The traversal MUST NOT loop on recursive calls or mutually-recursive call graphs; each function body is visited at most once. **Visited-set keying granularity (round-17 B16-5 closure of round-16 BLOCKING — visited-set keying ambiguity).** The visited-set MUST be keyed by the **`(module-path, function-name)` pair**, NOT by `module-path` alone AND NOT by `function-name` alone. Module-path-only keying produces silent false-negatives on shared utility modules: e.g., a utility module `src/server/services/Shared/Util.luau` exporting two functions `Util.foo` and `Util.bar` would have only one entry in a module-path-only visited-set, so a traversal that reaches `Util.foo` first would mark the entire module visited AND skip `Util.bar` even when reached by a different call-graph branch — `Util.bar`'s body is never inspected for forbidden primitives. Function-name-only keying produces collisions across modules (every module's `init` function would map to the same key). The `(module-path, function-name)` pair is the only keying granularity that visits each distinct function body exactly once across the entire codebase. **Implementation note (binding):** for module-local function declarations `local function foo(...)` the function-name component is the lexical name `foo`; for method-table assignments `function Service:foo(...)` the function-name component is `Service:foo` (the method-on-table name); for assignment-form declarations `Service.bar = function(...)` the function-name component is `Service.bar`; for anonymous function literals passed inline (e.g., `:Connect(function(...) ... end)`) the function-name component is a synthetic location-based key `<anonymous-at-line-N-of-module-path>` that the AST tooling MUST generate deterministically per call-site. The forbidden-primitive check runs once per visited (module-path, function-name) pair.
6. **Out-of-scope edges (deferred-queue pattern).** A function body F that contains a `RunService.Heartbeat:Connect(...)` call site is NOT considered to invoke the Heartbeat callback synchronously — the Heartbeat callback's body is OUT of the call graph reachable from F's perspective (it's a separate root that fires from a Heartbeat boundary, not from F's invocation). This is the structural enabler of the deferred-queue pattern below: the synchronous root callback can `RunService.Heartbeat:Connect(asyncWorker)` and the `asyncWorker` body is NOT reachable from the root's call graph.

**No exceptions, no AST inference required for the forbidden-primitive check itself (round-11 BLOCK-3 closure of the round-11 red-team finding that an "AST tail-`task.spawn` recognition" exception was untestable as an AC — `task.spawn` is forbidden inside `OnPlayerDied:Connect` callback bodies absolutely AND, per round-15 R14-B1, inside any function body in the call-graph reachable from those bodies, absolutely).** The call-graph traversal IS an AST-level pass (call-site identification requires AST recognition of `<expr>(<args>)` and `<expr>:<method>(<args>)` patterns plus local-alias resolution); the forbidden-primitive check on each visited body is grep-level (one regex pass per file).

**Subscribers that need to perform async work after receiving a payload MUST use the deferred-queue pattern** documented in this row: the synchronous callback body stashes the payload onto a service-internal per-frame queue (e.g., `table.insert(self._pendingDeaths, payload)`), AND a separate `RunService.Heartbeat:Connect` handler (registered in the same service's `KnitStart`, NOT inside the `OnPlayerDied:Connect` callback) pops queue entries and processes them asynchronously. The queue-stash + Heartbeat-worker decoupling preserves the synchronous receipt phase's yield-free guarantee while admitting async post-processing. The deferred-queue pattern is the structural exit from the call-graph reachability check: the async-worker function (registered via `Heartbeat:Connect`) is a separate root for the call-graph traversal, NOT part of the synchronous callback's reachable set per traversal rule (6) above. **The async-worker root is NOT subject to H.39e Lint (b)'s yield-free check** — it MAY freely use yield primitives because it runs from the Heartbeat boundary, not synchronously from `OnPlayerDied:Fire`. Reference implementation pattern (informational, not normative — the implementation choice is the service author's):

> **Reference implementation (relocated round-22 AC-extraction; relocation only):** the informational deferred-queue reference pattern (`_pendingDeaths` queue-stash + Heartbeat worker) is in **`ecological-disturbance-verification.md` § H.39e deferred-queue reference implementation**.

**Why grep-only for the forbidden-primitive check, but AST-level for call-graph traversal (round-11 BLOCK-3 design-decision rationale; round-15 R14-B1 extension rationale):** the round-11 author's first formulation included a "tail-`task.spawn`" AST exception that was AC-testability-defective — no specific AST tool was named, no exact pattern was specified, and the lint authors had no falsifiable target. The grep-only forbidden-primitive check (per-body, no exceptions) closes that AC defect; the deferred-queue pattern provides an alternative that is grep-detectable AND functionally equivalent in async-work expressivity. **Round-15 R14-B1 adds an AST-level call-graph traversal LAYER on top of the grep-only forbidden-primitive check** because the round-14 ai-programmer + qa-lead 2-spec convergent finding established that lexical-scope-only grep cannot detect yield primitives in private helpers reachable from the `:Connect` callback (e.g., `_captureDeathLockSnapshot`'s body lexically outside the callback but called from inside it). The traversal pass IS AST-level by necessity (call-site identification requires AST recognition of `<expr>(<args>)` patterns and local-alias resolution), but the forbidden-primitive check applied to each visited body remains grep-level (a single regex pass per file). The two-layer architecture preserves the round-11 grep-only AC-testability for the forbidden-primitive check itself while closing the lexical-scope-only gap. **AST-tooling choice and shared dependency (round-15 R14-B1 forward-obligation tracking entry: R15-I2):** the call-graph traversal pass requires the same `luau-lsp` (or equivalent) AST tooling that R11-I3 / R11-I5 / round-15 R14-B3 (H.39f alias-resolution) already track as a shared cross-AC dependency. The qa-lead and DevOps Engineer are the canonical owners of the AST-tooling decision; if the chosen tool does not natively support call-graph reachability across `local function` declarations and method-table assignments, the qa-lead MUST author a one-time AST-pass implementation (or contract one out) BEFORE H.39e Lint (b)'s call-graph extension can land mechanically. Until the AST-tooling lands, the round-15 R14-B1 closure is honor-system at the call-graph level (the lexical-scope-only grep still runs and catches the most-common case); the closure becomes structural when the AST pass lands. The forward-obligation tracking is required because round-14 verdict review acknowledged the round-13 protocol layer (cross-section consistency) closed prose-level coherence but did NOT close implementability micro-defects of this exact class (round-14 mechanical-translation ceiling pattern); round-15 closes it inline with an AST-tooling forward-commit rather than deferring to a future round.

- **Lint (c) — `OnPredatorLockChanged` server-side fire-path observer ban (round-11 B-R10-1b sub-step (4b) sync-callback prohibition).** Pattern: any `:Connect(` invocation chained on `OnPredatorLockChanged` server-side (specifically: any `OnPredatorLockChanged.Event:Connect(...)` access against the underlying RemoteEvent's `.Event` signal, OR any BindableEvent fan-out helper that mirrors `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(...)` invocations into a server-internal BindableEvent and accepts synchronous subscribers — the lint detects this pattern by grep-matching `local <name>BindableEvent = Instance.new("BindableEvent")` followed within the same scope by a `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(...)` call AND a `<name>BindableEvent:Fire(...)` call — flag for human review even if the pattern is benign), OR any pattern that allows a server-side observer to receive synchronous notification of `OnPredatorLockChanged:Fire(...)` invocations during the (4b) per-target for-loop. Zero matches. Steady-state client-side Knit RemoteSignal subscription via `OnPredatorLockChanged:Connect` (Knit RemoteSignal client-side — runs in `LocalScript` / `ModuleScript` under `src/client/**/*.luau`) is permitted and explicitly OUT of this lint's scope; the lint runs against `src/server/**` only.

THEN: (a) the CI gate exits 0 (PASS) when lints (a), (b), and (c) all return zero unexpected matches per their criteria above; (b) the CI gate exits non-zero (FAIL) on any lint violation, with an error message naming the violating file path, line number, and the specific lint that failed (the message MUST be specific enough that the violation is fixable without re-running the lint); (c) the gate is a REQUIRED check on PRs that modify any file under `src/server/services/DisturbanceService/**` OR any file containing a `Humanoid.Died` reference OR any file containing `OnPlayerDied` / `OnPredatorLockChanged` references, AND fires on direct push to main per the round-9 B-NS4 trigger model. **The gate MUST land before any DisturbanceService.Client method body or any BindableEvent subscription path is introduced or modified — the implementation epic is BLOCKED on the gate's presence per round-11 binding ruling, parallel to the round-7a R7-B16 H.39d evidence gate's binding ruling. The DevOps Engineer is the canonical owner of the gate's authoring per the H.39d gate precedent.**

**Why this AC is BLOCKING (round-11 closes the round-10 B-R10-5 cross-reference orphan).** C.1.11 step 5 cited "the H.39e CI gate operationalises this lint" as a load-bearing closure for the B-R10-1a Roblox `:Connect` ordering concern, but the H.39e AC body did not exist in Section H — round-10 gameplay-programmer F3 finding. Round-11 DR-5 = Option A authors the AC explicitly. Without H.39e, the C.1.11 step 5 monopoly rule would be honor-system: a programmer could add a second `Humanoid.Died:Connect` chain in a non-DisturbanceService file and the round-9 KnitInit-vs-KnitStart connection-order race would silently re-emerge (as B-R10-1a established, the round-9 race rested on undocumented Roblox API guarantees that the BindableEvent monopoly architecture replaces). The H.39e CI lint is the structural enforcement that makes the monopoly rule load-bearing.

**H.39f** — STATIC-LINT (round-23 QA-5 retag) (round-13 I4 fold-in; round-15 R14-B3 alias-resolution closure — N5 `OnServerEvent` non-subscription guarantee CI enforcement; closes the F.2a-IMPORTANT N5 forward obligation that previously had only ADR-level verification) — GIVEN the entire server codebase under `src/server/**/*.luau`, WHEN a CI grep gate executes the pattern match against the `OnServerEvent` access on the two raw RemoteEvent instances `FloraChunkUpdate` and `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot`, THEN zero matches anywhere in the server codebase. **Round-15 R14-B3 closure — alias-resolution required (parallel to H.39e Lint (a)'s static-analysis pass).** The lint MUST detect ALL of the following syntactic forms via the same static-analysis pass H.39e Lint (a) uses for `humanoid.Died`:

- **(i) Direct method-sugar form:** `FloraChunkUpdate.OnServerEvent:Connect(...)`, `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot.OnServerEvent:Connect(...)`.
- **(ii) Local-alias-on-the-RemoteEvent form:** `local rev = FloraChunkUpdate; rev.OnServerEvent:Connect(...)` (or any chain of single-function-body local-aliases that resolves to one of the two raw RemoteEvent instances). The pass MUST follow `local <name> = <expr>` declarations within a function body and resolve each alias's rvalue back to its original RemoteEvent reference; if the resolved reference is `FloraChunkUpdate` or `FloraChunkInitialSnapshot`, the subsequent `<name>.OnServerEvent:Connect` call is a match.
- **(iii) Local-alias-on-the-OnServerEvent form:** `local sig = FloraChunkUpdate.OnServerEvent; sig:Connect(...)` (or any chain of aliases on the `.OnServerEvent` member directly). The pass MUST also follow alias chains that capture the `.OnServerEvent` member itself, not just the parent RemoteEvent.
- **(iv) Table-index form:** `FloraChunkUpdate["OnServerEvent"]:Connect(...)` and equivalent square-bracket-indexed accesses (defeats simple dot-access grep patterns).
- **(v) Reflection form:** any `:GetPropertyChangedSignal("OnServerEvent")` or `:FindFirstChildOfClass("RBXScriptSignal")`-style reflection chain that ends at `OnServerEvent` on either raw RemoteEvent. Reflection chains are flagged for human review even if benign; pure grep cannot fully reason about reflection-derived references and the AST pass MUST emit a "manual-review" warning rather than silent-pass for any such pattern.

Catches the regression where a programmer accidentally registers an `OnServerEvent` callback on either raw RemoteEvent — Roblox's raw RemoteEvents allow client-fired `:FireServer(...)` invocations to invoke any server-side `OnServerEvent` callback, so a registered callback is itself the exploit surface (a malicious client could fire arbitrary `FloraChunkUpdate` payloads and cause the server-side handler to do whatever it does in response, even if the handler is "harmless" — at minimum it consumes server CPU; at worst it could be repurposed for DoS or for state corruption). The F.4 raw-RemoteEvent server-fire-only rule mandates zero `OnServerEvent` callbacks; H.39f is the CI-mechanical enforcement. The lint runs on `pull_request` AND `push: branches: [main]` per the round-9 B-NS4 trigger model. Same FAIL-loud-with-named-paths semantics as H.39e. Same DevOps-Engineer-owned ownership as H.39d / H.39e per the BLOCKING-on-CI-gate-presence rule. **Round-15 R14-B3 forward-obligation tracking entry: R15-I1** (recorded in the F.2a-IMPORTANT round-15 IMPORTANT cluster) — the static-analysis pass for forms (ii)–(v) is a shared dependency with H.39e Lint (a) and H.39c's local-alias coverage (round-15 R14-B4 closure on H.39c); the AST tooling choice (`luau-lsp` or equivalent) is owned by the qa-lead and the DevOps Engineer; if the chosen tool does not natively support form (v) reflection-chain warnings, the qa-lead MAY route the (v) pattern to a separate manual-review CI gate parallel to H.30c sub-AC (i)'s evidence-file model. The same forward obligation (R11-I5 — "Four-form grep doesn't catch local-alias") that originally tracked this concern at the H.39 / H.39b / H.39e level is now PARTIALLY CLOSED inline at H.39e Lint (a) (via the static-analysis pass already specified there), at H.39c (via the round-15 R14-B4 module-private accessor architectural change — see H.39c body), AND at H.39f here; the residual scope (any future `OnServerEvent`-class lint) inherits this same alias-resolution AC.

> **H.31b / H.31c** (no-`ReplicationFocus` client-property regression check; `_injectSyntheticEmission` minimum-invariant enforcement) — **regrouped to follow H.31** in the Cross-System Wiring block (round-22 AC-extraction; relocation only). Both ACs remain part of this Security / Anti-Exploit Regression suite; their ids are unchanged and all existing cites remain valid.

### Resolved (round 2 review, 2026-04-29)

- **Q9 — Stationary-emitter design intent.** Resolved: stationary escalation is a Player-Fantasy violation (untraceable to a specific squad action). Fix applied via C.1.1 stationary-emitter attenuation (`EMITTER_MOVEMENT_DELTA_FLOOR` + `STATIONARY_EMISSION_FACTOR` in G.5); D.7 worked examples updated to publish both moving and stationary steady-state numbers. Section B's traceability promise is preserved.
- **Q10 — Section B traceability vs Art Bible §6.4 Beat 3.** Resolved: GDD wins. Pre-disturbed flora moments must be authored via diegetic in-world causes (props, lighting fixtures, narrative beats), NOT by hand-setting flora to elevated `t` values. The flora-rendering subsystem renders one visual state — the live `t` from the disturbance field. **Action item: Art Bible §6.4 Beat 3 must be revised before any flora-rendering implementation work begins.** Section B is unchanged.
- **Q11 — Sprint-to-flee feedback loop.** Resolved: "No escape once seen" is the intentional commitment. Mitigation is teaching, not mechanical: Player Controller GDD adds a low-stakes Calm-tier first-zone teaching beat where sprint visibly raises the meter without consequence; HUD GDD displays a "predator locked — sprinting worsens signal" indicator while attribution lock targets this player. The disturbance system itself does NOT implement chase-state suppression — Sprint emissions fire normally regardless of predator state.
